## Chapter 0: Introduction

**¶1** ⟨p.1⟩
(1) [The Father of the Light unveiled hidden wisdom in] light [to his own] which he has unveiled [and he taught] his disciples [so that they might comprehend] entire [a fullness] of wisdom [and he uttered the] word in the beginning [primal] concerning the separation [instruction] the heights [from] to the heights [down] and they spread out [through the aeons, unti]il they should make war [in the] kingdom [against the]ight [of the l] in the heights [o]rom all the aeons [there remained above all,] the light, the one that no [falsity can dwell] in it.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opens with the impersonal cosmological source: the Father as uncreated Light. Corresponds to 'Divine disclosed wisdom that had been hidden'.
> *¶1 #2 [tentative]:* Bridges to 'light … which he has unveiled' and anticipates disclosure to learners; keeps oldest-layer register (impersonal bestowal).
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* Connects unveiling with instruction of disciples (receivers). The substrate tends to state transmission as teaching rather than preaching.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* Follows the spiritual sense: disclosure formed full understanding in those taught.
> *¶1 #5 [moderate]:* Matches 'entire … of wisdom' and the spiritual sense 'full understanding'.
> *¶1 #6 [moderate]:* Introduces the 'word in the beginning' in an impersonal cosmological way (utterance as creative ordering).
> *¶1 #7 [moderate]:* Constrains 'word in the beginning' toward first truth, not narrative quotation.
> *¶1 #8 [moderate]:* Fits the didactic tone of oldest layer: 'set forth the primal truth'.
> *¶1 #9 [strong]:* Needed for 'the separation … the heights'. Separation proceeds from higher degrees.
> *¶1 #10 [moderate]:* Pairs with 'from … to the heights' as an influx schema: higher to lower, then extension.
> *¶1 #11 [moderate]:* Restores the temporal-extension formula: truths extended 'through the aeons' until opposition arose.
> *¶1 #12 [strong]:* Introduces arena of opposition: war 'in the kingdom'.
> *¶1 #13 [strong]:* War is directed; matches later explicit 'darkness has made war against the light'.
> *¶1 #14 [strong]:* Completes '...ight' as 'light' in 'kingdom … light'.
> *¶1 #15 [strong]:* Single-letter completion for 'f]rom' → 'from'.
> *¶1 #16 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: above contests remained uncreated wisdom itself.
> *¶1 #17 [moderate]:* Concludes: in the unmingled Light no falsity can be. Chooses 'falsity' (structural opposite of truth) over moralizing terms.

**¶2** [But as for] the darkness, the world [of] their root [was in] which poured forth [into the depth,] the one that the earth has [filled the whole earth with] darkness [and it became] it is far from the light [because]

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* Sets contrast: from Light to Darkness.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Grammatically anchors 'the darkness, the world of …'.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* Introduces the root-state/origin of denial as a condition.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Corresponds to pouring forth into the lowest degree.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Matches spiritual sense: denial poured into the lowest, filling with obscurity.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* Leads to 'darkness … it is far from the light'.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* Causal tie: distance from Light explains the state of obscurity.

**¶3** This is the first [great teaching] concerning the eternality; he [he has disclosed] to you, for each one of them exists [steadfastly;] they are divided from one another by their [own measure] in an eternality since the [matter] which I have written for you; this is, that [that] (4) the darkness has made war against the light, desiring that it would reign over an essence not its own. It spread a quarrel [and] it disturbed [among the powers of the soul;] came up, it [need] need.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Aligns with 'first instruction' and the didactic, impersonal register.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Matches spiritual sense: 'he set forth'.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* Marks permanence of discrete degrees (eternality).
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Degrees distinguished by measure of reception; keeps correspondential language of degree/measure.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Refers back to 'this is, that … darkness has made war…' as the essential point.
> *¶3 #8 [tentative]:* Spiritual sense: dissension cast into ordered faculties; chooses 'powers' over ecclesial terms.
> *¶3 #9 [strong]:* Completes the phrase: 'in this great need' already partially present; keeps as the crisis-state enabling uprising of opposition.

**¶4** The power came forth from [the Father, and the power] is [First]st Man. He [went forth and clothed himself with] the armour of light [and he encountered] all [the enmity]. He crucified it in [the boundary of the world,] the armour [and he bound it fast.]

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The 'Power' proceeds from the highest source; links to First Man as first combatting good.
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* Completes editor's partial; accepted.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: invested itself with Divine truth in outermost strength; armour imagery retained.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Meets hostility directly.
> *¶4 #5 [strong]:* Accepted as fitting the binding of opposition.
> *¶4 #6 [tentative]:* Corresponds to fixing opposing force in a restrained state in ultimates (a boundary).
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* Directly matches spiritual sense: held under restraint by invested truth.

**¶5** [Afterwards] he brought the sun [into its station,] the light aeons [and the] Living Spirit spread out the universe [and the] the earth [and he separated] the light and the darkness in the beginning [their forms] and the garments. He gave [to each according to its kind.] He set in order the [courses of the luminaries] and the wheel of the course [in their revolutions] in the heights, that they would [draw away the power] of the darkness. He stood firm [and]ixed. He constructed the foundation [of the whole structure;] all of it was set up.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* Signals next cosmogonic ordering step.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Sun as central governing clarity set into ordered place.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Introduces Living Spirit’s action; matches the sequence.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Core act: distinguishing light and darkness in the beginning.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* Garments correspond to outer forms of truth; oldest-layer prefers structural 'forms'.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* Assigning function/order without moral exhortation; fits 'He gave them …'.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* Wheel of the course = cycles of states represented by heavenly motions.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* Extends 'wheel of the course' to orderly alternations.
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: draw off and weaken what belonged to falsity (darkness).
> *¶5 #11 [strong]:* Completes 'f]ixed' → 'fixed'.
> *¶5 #12 [moderate]:* Summarizes: foundation constructed and all set up.

**¶6** Underneath [the heavens he placed] all of them; and also above he [three]e earths, they being conjoined. He enclosed the earth in [f]our mountains and three vessels [that are set] in its seas and its mountains [set in] order upon it. This universe stood firm [as] a bond for the evil powers [lest the li]ght that they joined with the darkness [again.]

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* Maintains cosmological architecture: beneath/above ordering of degrees.
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Completes 'thre]e earths' as 'three'.
> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* Single-letter completion for 'f]our mountains' → 'four'.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Qualifies the 'three vessels' as containing forms.
> *¶6 #5 [strong]:* Accepted; matches 'set in order'.
> *¶6 #6 [strong]:* Leads into function clause: universe stood firm as a bond.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Restores the preventative purpose: lest the light join with darkness.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* Closes the protective aim: prevent renewed mixture.

**¶7** Then the Third Ambassador! [He purified the light] by his image. He became [redeemer of] all things.

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Accepted; corresponds to mediation purifying what can be saved.
> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* Accepted but understood in oldest-layer sense: deliverer, not devotional title.

**¶8** (5) [For] they are in all the [ordered u]niverse, and the fire blazes in it, and it is devoured [by it,] all [impurity from] of the light also, and he becomes purifier, and goes up to his essence. Conversely, the darkness which is in [what is freed] out, and it is gathered in to a bond [the body of the world] unveil [and they are made manifest,] and they go in to him and become [a sweet pe]rfume [is]et up [there, an]d they reign for ever, and a single God comes to be [ov]er the totality, being above the totality. You find no opponent from this time on against the Father, the King of the Light and the [Wisdom.]

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* Introduces general law of the ordered universe.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* Completes 'all the … universe' as 'ordered universe' (structure emphasized).
> *¶8 #3 [tentative]:* Fire devours by its action; keeps terse.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: impure consumed and removed from what can receive heaven.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* Liberated good and truth are purified.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* Darkness remaining in the outward level: expressed as 'body of the world' (natural ultimate).
> *¶8 #7 [tentative]:* Unveiling of purified elements before conjunction.
> *¶8 #8 [moderate]:* Prepares 'perfume' as correspondential delight of purified goods and truths.
> *¶8 #9 [strong]:* Links 'perfume is set up'.
> *¶8 #10 [tentative]:* Locative establishing of permanence.
> *¶8 #11 [strong]:* Completes 'over' already indicated; keep letters only per bracket.
> *¶8 #12 [moderate]:* Pairs with 'Father, King of the Light' as love and wisdom reigning; avoids later Christian epithets.

**¶9** [These are the states] which they occur in, from the beginning [before the end,] they are mixed and joined with one another.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces explanation of early mingling in the same arena.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: in earliest states before completion opposites appear together.

**¶12** (6) The first great lesson [is this,] which in the [outward life] that walks in it because of the twelve [path] in it, which in the cities and the dwelling places found in the [measures] because of the doors that are opened in them [ways] upon the earth, which in the four corners [it is a trial in completeness for the whole mind] and my pains. For also [the Divine labours] and my afflictions [bearing] to them [to know] suffices for the person [who desires to become] wise person.

> *¶12 #1 [strong]:* Formula introducing lesson content.
> *¶12 #2 [strong]:* Matches spiritual sense: one who lives in the outward life experiences complexity.
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* Completes 'that walks in it' as a way or path.
> *¶12 #4 [moderate]:* Twelve as fullness of arranged truths; 'measures' keeps correspondential, non-ecclesial tone.
> *¶12 #5 [moderate]:* Many openings by which influences enter in various habitual states.
> *¶12 #6 [moderate]:* Restores the sense: outward life as field of trial touching all aspects of natural mind.
> *¶12 #7 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: Divine undergoes labor in restoration; impersonal phrasing retained.
> *¶12 #8 [moderate]:* Links to 'my pains… my afflictions' as endured states in the work.
> *¶12 #9 [moderate]:* Perception of this even in part.
> *¶12 #10 [moderate]:* Closes: suffices for the person … wise person; keeps volitional condition without exhortation.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are recoverable at the level of correspondential structure (source of Light, discrete degrees, war of Darkness, First Man as first resisting power, Living Spirit ordering cycles, enclosure to prevent mixture, Third Ambassador purifying, final unopposed rule, early mingling, and the ‘first great lesson’ about the outward life). Exact surface wording remains uncertain in several places (especially ¶1-2 and ¶6, ¶8-12), but the required cosmological functions tightly constrain the semantic content; restorations therefore aim for oldest-layer impersonal cosmological register rather than later devotional or institutional phrasing.

## Chapter 1: Concerning the Ad[vent] of the Apostle.

**¶3** Understand, O my beloved ones: All the apostles who are on occasion sent to the world resemble farmers; while their churches, which they choose, are like Parmuthi and Paophi. For, the way Parmuthi occurs not in all the months of the year; nor does Paophi [occur] in all of them. Rather, the season [is appointed] for it alone, as they know [the season of] its vegetables [and] the time they are cropped; also the vegetables and the [and] season; and they tend and ripen the pickings, and they [fruit-bearing] to Paophi.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* Paophi is treated as a seasonal state like Parmuthi; the clause requires the same verb: it does not ‘occur’ in all months.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The season is not random but ordered and fixed in its own time; correspondentially, states of reception are appointed in order.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The farmer knows the proper state for each implantation; ‘season of’ introduces the state appropriate to that crop.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* Connects the farmer’s knowing to what follows (the vegetables).
> *¶3 #5 [strong]:* Balances the parallel items: vegetables and their time of cropping.
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* Opposes ‘vegetables’ with what belongs to Paophi (later harvest, fruits). The substrate register prefers ‘fruit-bearing’ over moralized terms.

**¶4** Of course, now that farmer ⟨p.10⟩ [has not yet reached] Parmuthi; in that, if he will come and sow some corn seed there [before its season, it will not become] cereal. And he shall be involved from the beginning [in planting] a corn seed and toil for it. Yet, when the summer comes each year, and his corn comes to the season of its harvest; then the farmer comes forth and harvests it. Even the gardener from the beginning [he shall take] shall toil for his fruits; and he tends them and he [of his vineyard] and they ripen; and he comes forth and picks them from the tree.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The point is: if he starts before the proper state (Parmuthi), effort is premature and cannot yield effect.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially: truths implanted before reception cannot become living understanding and use; the natural image is seed not becoming cereal.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Specifies the beginning-labour: the initial implantation.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Matches the farmer’s act: taking seed and labouring for it from the outset.
> *¶4 #5 [tentative]:* Gardener image shifts from grain to fruit; ‘vineyard’ is a standard oldest-layer figure for an ordered recipient that yields fruit.

**¶6** ⟨p.11⟩
Also the way [an apostle is sent] many years before [he appears in] the world, as he journeys below to the [heights] makes the form of his church free, and [he releases it from the bonds] of the flesh, whose forms he had made free [from the bonds]

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Sets the general law: preparation precedes manifestation in the world.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The emissary is manifested ‘in the world’ only after interior preparation.
> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* The spiritual translation specifies preparation in the higher degree before descent.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondentially: freeing the church-form from domination by outward life; ‘bonds’ fits the slavery image of flesh.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Second clause parallels the first: freeing from ‘the flesh’ and its bondage.

**¶7** Like the farmer, for when he will [turn to sow] that moment he shall begin [to work and] tend [it, and] it reaches Parmuthi also, he may harvest it [then] toil for the fruits of Paophi [also he shall]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* Moment of turning to the service initiates the tending work.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Parallel to ‘begin … tend’; expresses continuous ordered labour.
> *¶7 #3 [strong]:* Connects tending with the advance toward the Parmuthi state.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Marks the transition from tending to harvesting when the state arrives.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* Parallelism: as he toils for corn in Parmuthi, so also for fruits in Paophi.

**¶8** Again, this too is [how] the apostles [have acted] from the beginning of the moulding of humanity [that it is formed above first], as I have told you, that when they [make it] before every thing he shall [make it] free above, first.

> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* States the continuous practice ‘from the beginning’.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: before anything is in ultimates, it is first made free and established in the interior degree.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* The apostles ‘make’ the church-form free above first; verb required by the spiritual logic of formation.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* Reinforces the rule: first freedom above, then birth below.

**¶9** Yet, when it [will be] born in the flesh, he shall come down to it and at once [he will release] it in the [world] which comes out from the [heights] is raised up to the greatness. He shall continue in the world at this time [truth] him, corresponding to the season when the world will come to his [until the season will come for] when the season will mature he is raised up from the world [need] and he leaves his church {behind} and goes forth. Still, he [and he is raised up] and he helps and aids his church. Whoever comes out from their body after him, he shall become for them a support and a leader; and he goes on before them [yet he does not abandon it], excepting the others who are set firmly in the flesh. He is the one who will become for them a helper and a guardian; he shall not take [the authority] in secret, until his church [is able to] change it [then it shall] when his church [is perfected] he was raised up after it, and went into the land of glory. And he rejoices and rests at the last [in the consummation]

> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Descent is for immediate liberation and elevation of the formed church in ultimates.
> *¶9 #3 [strong]:* The operative field is the lower order (world).
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* What proceeds ‘out from the heights’ is interior truth flowing down to raise the church.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* The raising is by what proceeds from interior truth, not by exhortation; this matches the oldest substrate’s impersonal mechanism.
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* He remains as long as the state requires; the text wants a temporal-state clause.
> *¶9 #7 [moderate]:* The church’s state ‘calls for’ or ‘needs’ his presence.
> *¶9 #8 [moderate]:* When the state matures, withdrawal follows: raised from the world.
> *¶9 #9 [moderate]:* Key correspondential point: interior conjunction remains after outward withdrawal.
> *¶9 #10 [moderate]:* He does not seize rulership covertly; this keeps the mechanism impersonal and state-governed.
> *¶9 #11 [moderate]:* He waits until the church is able to change state/order.
> *¶9 #12 [moderate]:* Introduces the consequent change once readiness is present.
> *¶9 #13 [moderate]:* The change occurs when the church’s form is completed for that state.
> *¶9 #14 [moderate]:* ‘At the last’ points to completion of the cycle of use: consummation of that state, not an apocalyptic ending.

**¶10** Like the farmer who shall be glad of heart in [his house, because] he was bringing in (the harvest). Or like the merchant who shall come up from a country with the doubling of his great cargo; and the riches of his trading.

> *¶10 #1 [tentative]:* Natural image: joy at home upon bringing in the harvest; spiritually: delight in gathered uses.

**¶11** When the apostle will be raised up from the world, he and his church [remain conjoined] for every apostle [remains] in the [heights] Since [upon a] tree, that still is laden with its first fruits [and], and they are picked; they shall propagate other ones there, and they mature to the season to bring them forth. There is not any time the tree is bare of fruit! Rather, instead, those about to ripen as they pick them shall propagate other ones there. When they bear these [fruits; when they pick] them on the tree; they come up and are brought forth upon its branches. They shall pick from it those that mature and ripen. The farmer does not rest at any time from the labour and the pain. At what hour may he cease? When he can finish harvesting the corn in Parmuthi! [when they cast] them in the winter, the fruits of the summer shall [again] grow up and be picked in the month of Paophi.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual translation: emissary and church remain conjoined in interior life.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* Every apostle ‘remains’ in the spiritual degree with his formed recipient.
> *¶11 #3 [strong]:* The locus of conjunction after withdrawal is the higher degree.
> *¶11 #4 [moderate]:* Introduces the tree image: ‘Since upon a tree…’.
> *¶11 #5 [strong]:* Links ‘first fruits’ with their picking and renewal.
> *¶11 #6 [moderate]:* The cycle: uses gathered, new beginnings propagated simultaneously.
> *¶11 #7 [tentative]:* Winter corresponds to a lower, harder state; sowing or casting seed continues even then.
> *¶11 #8 [moderate]:* Completes the seasonal parallel: what begins in winter will ‘again’ grow up in Paophi.

**¶12** ⟨p.12⟩
The apostles are like this also. Now, when the apostle will be raised up to the heights, he and his church, and they depart from the world; at that instant another apostle shall be sent to it, to another church [to which he shall serve] it [and make free]. Yet, first, he shall make the forms of his church free in the heights, as I have told you. When [the season will come]; again, he too shall come down and appear [in the flesh] and he releases his church and saves it from the flesh [of sin and death]

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* Another apostle is sent to another church ‘to serve’ it; impersonal functional vocabulary.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* The preparatory act is always freedom and ordering in the heights.
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* Transition from preparation above to manifestation below is state-governed (‘when the season comes’).
> *¶12 #4 [moderate]:* He appears in the ultimate degree: in fleshly manifestation.
> *¶12 #5 [tentative]:* Oldest-layer cosmological register prefers the paired bondage: sin as the falsifying power in flesh, death as its ultimate effect.

**¶20** ⟨p.14⟩
Like a tree will be plucked, and the fruits on it taken away. And it remains behind without fruit.

**¶23** Yet, when the church assumed the flesh, the season arrived to redeem the souls; like the month of Parmuthi that cereal shall ripen in, to be harvested.

**¶24** He unveiled to me the hidden mystery, the one that is hidden from the worlds and the generations, the mystery of the depths and the heights. He unveiled to me the mystery of the light and the darkness; the mystery of the calamity of conflict, and the war, and the great [tumult], the battle that the darkness spread about. Afterwards, he unveiled to me also: How the light [subdued] the darkness, through their mingling this universe was set up [and overcame]

> *¶24 #1 [moderate]:* The list escalates: conflict, war, great tumult—cosmic upheaval spread by Darkness.
> *¶24 #2 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: wisdom (Light) subdues falsity (Darkness), not moral persuasion.
> *¶24 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the action pair: subdue and overcome, leading into mingling and the set-up of the universe.

**¶25** He opened my eyes also to the way that the ships were constructed; to enable the gods of light to be in them, to purify the light from creation. Conversely, the dregs and the effluent [are cast down] to the abyss. The mystery of the fashioning of Adam, the first man. He also informed me about the mystery of the tree of knowledge, which Adam ate from; his eyes saw.

> *¶25 #1 [strong]:* Opposite motion to purification: rejected residues are cast down to the abyss.

**Assessment:** Most gaps could be restored to a coherent oldest-layer mechanism: ordered seasons as discrete states of reception; preparation ‘in the heights’ before manifestation ‘in the flesh’; withdrawal without abandonment via interior conjunction; perpetual propagation of uses. Several fills remain tentative where the natural imagery (vineyard vs orchard; winter action; exact phrasing of ¶10) is underdetermined by the surviving syntax, but the correspondential constraints still fix the direction (state-governed cultivation, harvest, and renewed propagation).

## Chapter 2: The Second, concerning the Parable of the Tree.

**¶5** Great is [the man who magnifies himself] by his own mouth [and teaches] bad [that which is] The sects [have arisen from him] Yet, this that they explain about the two trees, as they reckon them, [and they are many] the trees [they confound] in an explanation [are one likeness in the sight] because [they set] tree, as it grows on this composite earth, [not every] date palm, because [so is the], for not absolutely every part of the date palm is used!

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense begins with a self-exalting teacher speaking from self-derived understanding.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Needed to link “by his own mouth” to what proceeds from that speech.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Bridges to “bad” as predicate of what he teaches.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Sects correspond to divisions in doctrine arising from such teaching.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Sects plural; the passage contrasts their multiplicity with the true two principles.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* They attempt to explain the two trees but mix them in the natural degree.
> *¶5 #7 [tentative]:* Correspondentially: judging spiritual realities by external appearance.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* They “set the trees in an explanation”, i.e., propose an illustrative case.
> *¶5 #9 [strong]:* Leads into “tree, as it grows on this composite earth” and the point about mixture.
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* Introduces the example “date palm” as a single instance taken for the whole.

**¶6** ⟨p.18⟩ [For they take the date palm and speak of] their child, as it strikes [its fruit with] while first one [and pierces] in them [is sweet] at the end there is one [there is bitterness] it occurs in its fibres and its branches [and then] There is a bad dust in them [that is sour] date palm [and again] before the tree that [and in its bark] bad; because they give the decision [which clings] the one that they name [and therefore they judge the] the type [as wholly] name it: tree [is] is sweet [from the taste] writing [good] they shall establish [Sin] man [of this world] it is sweet [which] seed of the [of life] and it is used for their nourishment [and they say it] will explain [according to their] may not join to the [that] mankind, as they are named [that it is for the outer]

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The argument in ¶6 presupposes they have chosen the date palm as their model.
> *¶6 #2 [tentative]:* Links “their child” to the palm’s production and effect.
> *¶6 #3 [tentative]:* Matches spiritual sense: one thing seems to strike and determine another in mixed states.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Sets up contrast: first one quality appears, later another; sweetness is the initial appearance.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Opposite quality emerging in the same subject over time in outermost degree.
> *¶6 #6 [strong]:* Temporal progression into “at the end there is one…”.
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* A second quality manifesting in fibres and branches; fits the palm analogy of mixed usefulness.
> *¶6 #8 [strong]:* Adds the third observation about fibres and branches.
> *¶6 #9 [tentative]:* Natural locus for “fibres and branches”; supports “bad dust in them”.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* Corresponds to “clinging obscurity” in the spiritual translation.
> *¶6 #11 [moderate]:* Transitions from observation to mistaken doctrinal decision.
> *¶6 #12 [moderate]:* They treat a composite as if absolute; matches “not absolutely every part… is used”.
> *¶6 #13 [strong]:* Needed before “bad; because they give the decision…”.
> *¶6 #14 [moderate]:* They decide from what feels pleasant to the natural mind.
> *¶6 #15 [moderate]:* They prefer what seems sweet and call it good; fits the error described.
> *¶6 #16 [tentative]:* Oldest-layer register uses personified evil principle; they misname by wrong principle.
> *¶6 #17 [moderate]:* Locates the misnaming in the mixed natural world.
> *¶6 #18 [strong]:* Links “the type” to “name it”.
> *¶6 #19 [moderate]:* They call something “tree of life” because it is sweet and nourishing outwardly.
> *¶6 #20 [moderate]:* Introduces their claim about sweetness.
> *¶6 #21 [moderate]:* Leads into “writing”; i.e., their textual proof.
> *¶6 #22 [strong]:* Syntax to introduce what “they shall establish”.
> *¶6 #23 [tentative]:* Correspondentially: they confuse nourishment of external man with internal.

**¶11** [Now behold] how I will reveal to you [I will show you] the expound[positer] the trees [concerning] the Father [who is from] all of them, as he [the Origin of] in the [who established] world [threefold] this good tree, the [of the] the light of this good tree [and he set] the Pillar [and its strength is]

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Opens the shift from their confused teaching to the true exposition.
> *¶11 #2 [strong]:* Matches “how I will reveal to you…”.
> *¶11 #3 [strong]:* Completes expound[er ...] = “expounder”.
> *¶11 #4 [strong]:* Connects expounder with “the trees”.
> *¶11 #5 [moderate]:* True exposition proceeds from the Divine origin, not self-derived speech.
> *¶11 #6 [tentative]:* Oldest-layer register for the Divine source in cosmological teaching.
> *¶11 #7 [moderate]:* Fits spiritual sense: provides all things by orderly influx.
> *¶11 #8 [tentative]:* Corresponds to degrees (celestial, spiritual, natural) implied by correspondences.
> *¶11 #9 [strong]:* Bridge into “world”.
> *¶11 #10 [moderate]:* Introduces the good tree as an instituted order of influx.
> *¶11 #11 [moderate]:* Leads into “the Pillar …” as upholding power in ultimates.

**¶12** ⟨p.20⟩ ... the intellectuals that are cloaked upon its body [and within are] this good tree. They are the light elements that are mingled, being compounded in the totality.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* Connects cloaking in body (outer form) to interior light-elements mingled in the whole.

**¶14** Behold, this is the good tree [and it is the Origin of life]

> *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* Summarizes: the good tree as heavenly origin manifested.

**¶15** [Again] I reveal to you [also] it has five limbs. [They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind.] Its consideration is the holy church. [Its counsel] is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. [st Man]st Man who dwells in the ship of living waters. Its thought is the Third Ambassador [who dwells in] the ship of living fire, that shines in [the ship of the day and the night] Also, the mind is the Father who dwells in greatness, who is perfect in the aeons of light.

> *¶15 #1 [strong]:* Continues exposition with a new disclosure of its internal structure.
> *¶15 #2 [strong]:* Smooths: “I reveal to you also… it has five limbs.”
> *¶15 #3 [strong]:* Retained editor fill; matches the fivefold series in both natural and spiritual senses.
> *¶15 #4 [strong]:* Retained; required by parallelism with the other limb-definitions.
> *¶15 #5 [strong]:* Completes “Fir]st Man”.
> *¶15 #6 [strong]:* Retained; required by the fixed mythic locatives (ships of waters, fire).
> *¶15 #7 [tentative]:* Spiritual sense distinguishes states of clarity and obscurity; oldest-layer cosmology pairs day and night as alternating states of reception.

**¶17** It is consideration that shall [gather them from below] they shall be raised up [to counsel, and] is counsel; they shall be raised up to insight, which is the First Man who dwells in the ship of the night; from insight they shall rise up to thought, which is the Ambassador who dwells in the ship of the day. And he too, great glorious thought, shall bring them in to mind; which is the Father, the God of truth, the great Mind of all the aeons of glory.

> *¶17 #1 [tentative]:* Consideration (external reception) lifts from mere natural life upward.
> *¶17 #2 [moderate]:* Provides the stepwise ascent through the five limbs.

**¶18** This is the good tree that shall give good fruit, [and whoever eats of it] is the entire life and light for ever [for it] occurs [in him] ever, it is blessed [for ever and]

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* Eating corresponds to appropriation and reception of life from the good origin.
> *¶18 #2 [strong]:* Leads into “is the entire life and light for ever”.
> *¶18 #3 [moderate]:* Reception occurs in the subject; “insofar as it is received it is blessed.”
> *¶18 #4 [strong]:* Pairs with “ever” in the line and stabilizes cadence.

**¶19** ⟨p.21⟩ [But there is also a tree that is] bad [called] happen in [and it makes] from [the same likeness] also [self-love] the way that it is [dominion] the good tree [and it turns] of the fire [into] they are the ruling-power [as is] heaven and the wheel [in the likeness] they are the five fleshes that walk [of this world] fruit [false]aching as of error, the mystery that [by]ich Satan appointed in the world.

> *¶19 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the bad tree as parallel opposite.
> *¶19 #2 [strong]:* Before “bad”.
> *¶19 #3 [moderate]:* It “happen” effects in the world; outwardly similar forms.
> *¶19 #4 [moderate]:* Bad origin appears by same outward forms; thus deceives.
> *¶19 #5 [strong]:* Spiritual translation: hellish origin works by self-love.
> *¶19 #6 [strong]:* Next clause: dominion from that love.
> *¶19 #7 [moderate]:* Leads to “the way that it is… the wheel”.
> *¶19 #8 [strong]:* Bridge to the perverted order (wheel).
> *¶19 #9 [tentative]:* Connects description of bad way to “the good tree” by contrast.
> *¶19 #10 [moderate]:* Bad mimics good in externals; “of the fire” follows as sensual burning lust.
> *¶19 #11 [moderate]:* Locates ruling-powers in the cosmic outermost (heaven and wheel).
> *¶19 #12 [strong]:* Completes “... te]aching as of error” = “false teaching”.
> *¶19 #13 [moderate]:* Completes “... wh]ich Satan appointed” = “by which…”.

**¶20** However, the taste of these bad fruits is these evil people, the sects [which]ich are bound in law after law, they and their teachers [are chained] the law of death; they taking taste for it, they being thirsty for it [make covenant with] the souls of death.

> *¶20 #1 [strong]:* Completes relative “sects … which are bound…”.
> *¶20 #2 [moderate]:* Corresponds to binding under rule after rule without inner life.
> *¶20 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation: covenant with what is dead, law of death.

**¶22** ⟨p.22⟩ [And now] reveal to you and you [shall understand] The bad tree has five limbs. They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind. Its consideration is the law of death from which the sects take instruction. Its counsel is transmigration [as well] in type after type. Also, its insight is the furnaces of fire [of]henna; which is full of smoke. Thought [is their] vessel. Its mind is the [in a] the lump, the last bond, the [of clay], they that Satan cast away [Abyss] the great [bond of] and they fall [seal] the darkness [from the Light] all [lump of] and they take away [darkness] are swallowed in to the lump, and they are bound [into] the fruit of darkness, the one that they [of] for ever and ever [truth] his face [in] will arrive at that place; and they have no repentance therein.

> *¶22 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the mirrored five-limbed exposition of the bad tree.
> *¶22 #2 [moderate]:* Needed after “and you …”.
> *¶22 #3 [strong]:* Balances: reveal of bad tree as well as good tree.
> *¶22 #4 [strong]:* Completes “ge]henna” phrase: “furnaces of fire of gehenna”.
> *¶22 #5 [tentative]:* Introduces “Thought … vessel” as containing structure for deception.
> *¶22 #6 [strong]:* Bridge to “vessel”.
> *¶22 #7 [tentative]:* Oldest-layer image for a formed container of falsity; matches “containing structure”.
> *¶22 #8 [tentative]:* Mind of bad tree as final state: the lump, last bond; abyssal fixation.
> *¶22 #9 [moderate]:* Connects “last bond” to what follows.
> *¶22 #10 [moderate]:* Final closure of interiors; “last bond, the seal”.
> *¶22 #11 [moderate]:* What Satan cast away: rejected from heaven.
> *¶22 #12 [moderate]:* Prepares “great …”.
> *¶22 #13 [moderate]:* Great lump of darkness as hardened mass of confirmation.
> *¶22 #14 [strong]:* They fall into darkness.
> *¶22 #15 [strong]:* Before “the darkness”.
> *¶22 #16 [moderate]:* What is taken away: whatever remains of truth.
> *¶22 #17 [strong]:* They are bound in the lump; required by syntax.

**¶23** [Therefore] this is the explanation of the bad tree, as I have explained to you, which you [have heard] to it [and have inclined] good fruit [appearing as] its fruit bad [yet is] in it, in its existent days [and in every time] in it.

> *¶23 #1 [strong]:* Conclusion marker after the long exposition of bad tree’s limbs and end.
> *¶23 #2 [tentative]:* “which you … to it” = you have heard and assented.
> *¶23 #3 [tentative]:* They may incline to it; fits warning function without pastoral overlay.
> *¶23 #4 [moderate]:* Bad tree’s fruit appears good outwardly.
> *¶23 #5 [strong]:* Contrast clause.
> *¶23 #6 [moderate]:* Matches spiritual sense: in every stage of existence it carries its end within it.

**¶24** Concerning this [word] and my beloved ones [hold fast] them in truth, it being changed from [its own origin] which lies in its good earth [of] and light; as death and darkness do not exist [within it] Also, the bad tree is Matter [and there is none besides] it, I understand it alone among the [that]ich occurs in its bad earth [and brings forth] darkness and death.

> *¶24 #1 [tentative]:* “Concerning this word” common substrate-style transition to doctrinal clarification.
> *¶24 #2 [moderate]:* Corresponds to “beloved are to be held in truth” without devotional tone.
> *¶24 #3 [moderate]:* Good changes from origin into manifestation in good earth; keeps discrete origins.
> *¶24 #4 [strong]:* Completes “[ ... life] and light” = “of life and light”.
> *¶24 #5 [moderate]:* Death and darkness do not exist within heavenly good.
> *¶24 #6 [tentative]:* Bad tree is Matter; it alone among the principles generates darkness and death.
> *¶24 #7 [strong]:* Completes “... wh]ich occurs” = “that occurs”.
> *¶24 #8 [moderate]:* Bad earth generates darkness and death.

**¶25** ⟨p.23⟩ Blessed is every one [who discerns] these two trees, and separates them one from another. He understands that they did not arise out of one another, nor did they come from one another. They did not come from one! The person who can separate them out may go up to the aeon of light, [shown] revealed it to you.

> *¶25 #1 [strong]:* Blessed is everyone who discerns the two trees and separates them.
> *¶25 #2 [strong]:* Completes “[ ... as I have] revealed” = “shown”.

**¶26** And he sees [that the good tree is pure] bad fruit did not arise from it [nor]; but whoever will not separate out [of the good] from one another, he falls to the land of darkness [and remains] until he arrives at the [last bond]

> *¶26 #1 [moderate]:* He sees heavenly good is not source of evil fruit.
> *¶26 #2 [strong]:* Links the two negations.
> *¶26 #3 [moderate]:* “separate out … from one another” = separate good from evil origins.
> *¶26 #4 [moderate]:* Falls to land of darkness and remains until final fixation.
> *¶26 #5 [moderate]:* Directly matches spiritual translation: final fixation of ruling love, “last bond”.

**¶27** Blessed is whoever can believe! He [is delivered from] darkness; which grows out of the [bad tree] for ever and ever.

> *¶27 #1 [moderate]:* Matches spiritual translation: delivered from falsity and evil life.
> *¶27 #2 [moderate]:* Evil life grows out of the bad tree (hellish origin), persisting to eternity.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae can be constrained at the level of doctrinal structure: two discrete origins, outward similarity in the natural degree, and the fivefold series for each tree with ascent and inversion. Exact phrasing at several early ¶6 and ¶19 gaps remains uncertain because the surviving natural text gives only short syntactic anchors; restorations there follow the correspondential necessities (mixture in ultimates, judgment from taste, self-love and dominion, wheel-compulsion, final lump and last bond) in the oldest impersonal cosmological register.

## Chapter 3: The Interpretation of Happiness, Wisdom and Power; what they signify.

**¶4** [teach]each you of another happiness [that is in the Father] and another wi]sdom [and another discernment] and together another power [that works] So, now, listen that I may reveal to you [how] it is [with] these three: [ha]ppiness, wisdom and power.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The triad is being re-stated across discrete degrees. In the oldest register, the first degree is explicitly “in the Father” (inmost source). This also balances the later clauses that locate the same triad “in the Living Spirit,” “in the ship,” and “in the elements.”
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation reads “discernment of truth” corresponding to wisdom as understanding. The oldest layer tends to define wisdom functionally (discernment) rather than scholastically.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Power here is operative force (efficacy in use). “that works” keeps the impersonal, structural register and matches the triad’s third term as effecting agency.

**¶5** [ess]ess of the glorious one is the Father, the God of [truth, who] is established in the great land of [light]. ⟨p.24⟩ His glorious wis[d]om is his Great Spirit that [proceeds to the places] below, which flows through all his aeons, and t[h]ey float therein. His great power is all the gods, the rich ones and the angels who were summoned from him as they [came forth, and] t]hey that are called aeons [as regions and orders]

> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Wisdom here is the Great Spirit as proceeding understanding that descends “below.” In the substrate logic this is not mere location but procession into lower degrees, i.e., to the ordered places that can receive it.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* Power is the array of derived forces “summoned from him.” The next clause resumes “they that are called aeons,” so a brief emergence marker (“came forth”) best preserves the emanative structure without importing later institutional phrasing.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* The aeons are being defined as organized domains (ordered communities). This matches the spiritual sense: “organized domains,” and keeps the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶6** [This is the great ship] which is called] the sun [that is called the sun, the ship of] living fire, [in which is the Third Amba]ssador, the second greatne[dor, the second greatness. The] glorious [happiness] is the [Living] Spirit, [and the wisdom] is the Mother of Lif[e; and great power] is [all] the gods, [the] rich o]nes and the angels who are within the ship.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The paragraph is introducing a new discrete vessel-degree (sun, living fire, Third Ambassador). The opening must name the complex as the ‘great ship’ before specifying its designation(s).
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* The syntax demands the apposition: the ship is called ‘the sun’ and is ‘the ship of living fire.’ This keeps the oldest cosmological naming (sun as vessel), not devotional language.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador is an indwelling ordering presence in this vessel-degree. The gap naturally introduces his presence “in which is…” before his name.
> *¶6 #4 [strong]:* Completes “Ambassador” and the title “second greatness,” which is structural (degree-language) consistent with the triadic mapping that follows.
> *¶6 #7 [strong]:* This clause lists the triad’s correspondents within this degree: happiness = Living Spirit; wisdom = Mother of Life; power = gods, rich ones, angels. A simple conjunction fits the enumerative style.
> *¶6 #8 [strong]:* Restores “Life” and provides the hinge into the third term of the triad. Keeps the impersonal, list-like structure of the substrate.

**¶7** Again, [h]appiness, wisdom and power exist in the s[ship of liv]ing waters [and in it they are established] the happiness [is the Mind of the Father] the Mind of the Father. Also, wisdom [is the Vir]gi]n of [Li]ght. And the power that is [i]n the ship is [all] the go[ds], the [ri]ch ones and the angels who are established i[n it].

> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* The ship of living waters is another vessel-degree; the triad is said to “exist in” it. The substrate regularly adds a stabilizing clause (established, fixed) to indicate equilibrium within a degree.
> *¶7 #4 [strong]:* The next line already gives “the happiness … the Mind of the Father.” The lacuna after “the happiness” needs exactly this identification to complete the triad mapping for this vessel.

**¶8** Again, these three exist in the elements: happiness, wis[d]o]m and power. The happiness is [the Pillar of Glory], the Perfect Man. Wisdom is the [five sons of the] Living Spirit; and great powe[r is the five]ve sons of the First Man [these, who are encl]osed and compounded in the totality, that [may not be broken,] while he supports the totality.

> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Accepted as-is: in the ultimate degree (elements), blessedness is the stabilizing manifested Human-form (“Perfect Man”), i.e., the Pillar that supports the whole.
> *¶8 #3 [strong]:* Accepted as-is: wisdom in ultimates is an arranged sufficiency of operative truths, traditionally enumerated as five sons proceeding from the Living Spirit.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Removes the editorial hesitation and restores a direct identification: power here is not vague but specifically the ‘five sons of the First Man’ as combatant goods in the first engagement with Darkness.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* The syntax requires a resumptive demonstrative before the relative clause: “these, who are enclosed…” This keeps the impersonal listing style of the oldest layer.
> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* The enclosed and compounded powers serve preservation of the totality in ultimates. “may not be broken” matches the spiritual sense: support and preserve the system under the weight of ultimates.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are short and tightly constrained by the chapter’s fixed triadic correspondential scheme across discrete degrees (Father, sun-ship of fire, water-ship, elements). The larger gaps were restored by preserving the emanation-and-containment logic: wisdom as proceeding understanding that descends and flows through aeons, and power as organized arrays of derived forces, culminating in the five combatant sons enclosed in the totality for preservation. A few medium gaps (¶4 #2–4, ¶5 #5, ¶5 #7–8, ¶7 #3) remain moderately constrained because multiple substrate-appropriate phrasings could express the same correspondential function.

## Chapter 4: Concerning the Four Great Days that have come forth from one another, together with the Four Nights

**¶2** The first great day is the Father, the God of truth, the fir[st principle that is a]idst the aeons of his greatness, in his [l]iving kingdom. The twelve hours of this great day are the twelve great rich Gods of greatness. These, who are the first evocations that he evoked (to mirror) his greatness, he spread out to the four climes, three by three before his face.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires “first principle abiding…”. In the substrate register this is expressed as the Father as the first principle “that is” within the aeons, not as a personal title. The surviving letters fir[st … … m]idst also constrain to “first … a … midst”.

**¶3** The second day is the Third Ambassador, the one who dwells in the light ship. His twelve hours are the twelve virgins that he evoked in his greatness.

**¶4** [The third day is the Pillar of Glory, the great porter that is greater than] all [po]rters; the one that supports all [things], those above and those below. Its twelve hours are the five sons of the First Man; the five sons of the Living Spirit, who support all the weights of the universe; together with the summons and the obedience, which are counted to their ten brethren. These are the twelve light hours of the third day.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* Given already in the core and matches the correspondential function: the universal boundary and support surpassing lesser safeguards.
> *¶4 #3 [strong]:* What the Pillar supports must be the totality in discrete degrees: “those above and those below”. “Things” fits the impersonal cosmological register and the structural spiritual sense (“holding together what is higher and what is lower”).

**¶5** The fourth day is Jesus the Splendour who [dwells in] his church. His twelve hours are the twelve wisdoms, which are his [light] h[o]u[rs].

**¶7** ⟨p.26⟩ [The apostle spoke to him]: For just as four day[s ex]ist, s[o] too four nights exist.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* This is likely a remaining layer-2 dialogue seam. But some speech-intro is demanded before the colon. Keeping it minimal and non-devotional; it simply introduces the structural axiom about nights corresponding to days.

**¶8** The first night is the land of Darkness. It has twelve black [shadows] therein, which are its dark h[o]ur[s]. The twelve shadows of the first night are the five elements of the land of Darkness, [which] poured forth from its five sense organs; and [the five kinds of smoke] which [are mixed and bound] in its [fi]ve elements and its five [sense organs]; the five [sp]irits that dwell in its elements [within them]. These are the twelve shadows and spirits of the first night.

> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* The twelve must be: 5 elements + 5 ‘spirits’ + 2 completing factors (as elsewhere: fire and lust). Here the text already later counts “the five spirits…”. So after the five elements, a second pentad is required: in Manichaean-darkness taxonomy this is “smoke” (a dark factor distinct from the elements) as a set of five. It matches the spiritual sense: “further disorderings arise within the same series… and their extensions.”
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* The spiritual sense calls for “further disorderings arise within the same series…”. The substrate way of saying this is that these dark products “mix” and “bind” within the elements and their organs.
> *¶8 #8 [strong]:* Completes the paired structure: “its five elements and its five sense organs”. This is demanded by the earlier clause “poured forth from its five sense organs.”
> *¶8 #10 [strong]:* The five spirits are said to ‘dwell’ in the elements; “within them” makes the indwelling explicit in the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶9** The second night is Matter, the sculptress [of the darknesses] who has sculpted [the bodies] and the entire ruling-power that is in the worl[s of darkness]. She has fashioned it in five sense organs, five [male] and five female, two per world; together with the fire and the lust [that] dwell in men and women, inflaming [them] inward to one another. These are the twelve spirits of this second night.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Matter as the sculptress belongs to and works from the dark realm; “of the darknesses” fits the next clause about sculpting the rulers in the “worlds of darkness.”
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* What Matter ‘sculpted’ in the oldest cosmological layer is the corporeal formations by which the rulers act. “Bodies” matches the spiritual sense (“delusive appearances” given form) without later moralizing.

**¶10** Indeed she, this Matter, the thought of death, ga[ve] the King of the realms of Darkness and his powers strength for the war and the fight, against the aeons of greatness. Yet, he was caught! They laid a snare for him through the Fi[rst M]an. [He] was ta[ken] t[to the height] through the powers [and he was bound] was brought up from the land of death and s[et to stand]ove and below in the [whole] universe; in the five par[t]s in the heavens above, as well as the five [parts] in the earths below; together with the heat and the cold that are the father and the mother, their fire and their l[u]st.

> *¶10 #5 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense: the ruling falsity is “removed… and compelled into fixed positions… in higher and lower degrees.” The narrative idiom is “taken… and brought up”. “To the height” supplies the upward transition before the next sentence “He was brought up…”.
> *¶10 #6 [tentative]:* After “through the powers …” the substrate expects the restraint motif: seized by contending powers and bound. This matches the spiritual sense “restrained… taken by means of contending persuasions.”
> *¶10 #7 [strong]:* Restores “set to stand above and below”, matching the Pillar-like fixing into positions of service and the spiritual sense “compelled into fixed positions… in higher and lower degrees.”

**¶11** This is the second night that is begotten from the first night. They laid a snare for her through the First M[a]n. She was brought up by the hand of the Living Spirit, and set in this composite univers[e and set] he[r], above and below. The [upper side is the sense] organs of males; the lower side [is the sense organs of] fe[mal]es.

> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The clause needs the fixing action in the composite order: “set in this composite universe and set her… above and below.” This aligns with the spiritual sense “fixed into this mixed order”.
> *¶11 #5 [strong]:* Completes the bipartite correspondential division demanded by the spiritual sense: upper aspect acting through perverted sensory forms; lower likewise. The surviving text already gives the male side explicitly next.

**¶12** ⟨p.27⟩ The thir[d night is the five] worlds of flesh [ten worlds of flesh,] male, and five female; that were begotten from the powers [abo]ve. They have fallen upon the earth, being displayed in wh[at is d]ry and what is moist; together with the fire and the lust [that d]we[l]l in them, which drives them inward to one another.

> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* The line requires the total “five male and five female” as a single cosmological set; restoring “ten worlds of flesh” makes the counting explicit in the oldest-layer cataloguing style.

**¶13** [Mat]te[r] herself, the thought of death, is the [mother] of them all; as among them she is named 'the night'. [The worlds] of male and female fleshes, together with the fire and the l[ust], shall [make up the] twelve hours of [this third] night. Again, [it] too, the third night that is in the worlds of fl[e]sh, was begotten from the second night; which the Living [Spir]it crucified in the uni[verse and set]ove and below in heaven [and in earth].

> *¶13 #6 [strong]:* The sentence needs a verb of constitution: these items “make up” the twelve hours, matching the spiritual sense “make up the full cycle of stages”.
> *¶13 #7 [strong]:* “twelve hours of … … night” points to naming the very night being described; “this third” is the structurally demanded identifier.
> *¶13 #11 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense: the Living Spirit “held it fixed throughout the universal order… in higher and lower degrees.” So the crucifixion motif here functions as fixing; “in the universe and set…” expresses that in the substrate register.
> *¶13 #12 [moderate]:* Balances “in heaven …” with the required lower degree, matching the persistent above-below schema.

**¶14** The fourth night is the law of Sin, which is the dark spirit who speaks in the twelve spirits, the twelve sects. They are the nakedne[ss]es, the twelve zodiacal signs of Matter. They are her thrones; she who is made public, as she sculpts and is displayed in the old man.

**¶15** And, also, the hours of this fourth night that is the old man, who reigns in the sects, are the twelve e[vi]l spirits; the [powers] of the old man, which [that proceed and]fferentiate the day and night [in the rhythm] death.

> *¶15 #2 [moderate]:* “the … of the old man” in this register is his operative forces proceeding from selfhood; “powers” matches the preceding talk of spirits and ruling-power without importing later moral exhortation.
> *¶15 #3 [tentative]:* The syntax needs a relative clause introducing what these spirits do; spiritually, they establish a dead discrimination. “Proceed and” prepares for “differentiate…”.
> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense explicitly: “establishing for itself a rhythm of spiritual death.” So the phrase before “death” should signal that measured alternation.

**¶16** This is the fourth night [and these are the nights] that have come forth of one another. One has [been begotten] just like the days of lig[ht and] they manifested one another. So als[o in turn] they did arise out of one another.

> *¶16 #1 [strong]:* The closing recapitulates the series: “This is the fourth night, and these are the nights that have come forth…” This matches the spiritual translation’s summary about successive arising.
> *¶16 #2 [strong]:* Fits “One has … just like the days of light…”. The verb required is generation in sequence; “been begotten” keeps the impersonal cosmological tone.
> *¶16 #4 [moderate]:* Completes “So also … they did arise out of one another.” “In turn” captures ordered succession by degrees, as in the spiritual sense.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are small and restore securely from immediate syntax. The substantial gaps occur in ¶8–¶11 and ¶15, where counting-logic (twelve by composition) and the chapter’s governing correspondence (above-below fixing; four days mirrored by four nights) constrain the restorations. A few places remain only moderately constrained because the Coptic Vorlage could have used several equivalent verbs for restraint and fixing; I selected the forms that best match the oldest-layer impersonal cosmological register and the spiritual progression described.

## Chapter 5: Concerning Four Hunters of Light and Four of Darkness.

**¶2** The first hunter is the First Man who was sent from the greatness. He threw himself down to the five storehouses of Darkness, he caught and siezed the enmity [with his powers;] his net also [he spread] out over all the children of Darkness [and cast it]. His ship is his four sons who are swathed over his body. The sea is the land of Darkness [in the abyss]; his net is [his righteousness] and his powers.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* After “caught and seized the enmity” the correspondential sequence requires the binding and constraining agency. In the substrate register the First Man restrains Darkness by the extension of his own powers, not by exhortation; this also anticipates the explicit “his net is … and his powers” at the close of ¶2.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The net, in the oldest layer, is something extended or cast forth over the whole domain; “spread” matches the spiritual sense of encompassing ultimates and sets up the next clause “out over all the children of Darkness”.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The verb needed is the completion of the fishing-hunting image: the net is cast out over the children of Darkness. This keeps the impersonal technical tone of the substrate.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The “sea” is then defined as the land of Darkness; the correspondential grounding is the lowest external plane. The natural term that coheres is “abyss”.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* In the oldest cosmological layer, the ‘net’ that restrains is the ordered truth in ultimates—often rendered in this tradition as “righteousness” (order, rightness) rather than later moralism. It pairs naturally with “and his powers”.

**¶3** The second hunter is the Third Ambassador. This one, for by his light image, which he revealed to the depths below, he hunted after the entire light that is in all things; as it is established in them. His net is his light image, [by which he seized] the whole universe and took it prisoner, to this likeness [and he brought] ship is his light ship. [The sea] is the universe [and the worlds] which were hunted after by his net [and were gathered by] his [glorious] image.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The light-image is the net; the next clause must state its operative function before “the whole universe and took it prisoner”. “By which he seized” preserves the hunting logic and the spiritual sense of comprehension and subjection.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* The broken phrase “to this likeness …” requires the act of conforming what is gathered to the heavenly form revealed. “And he brought” supplies the needed governing verb leading into the damaged “His ship…”.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The sea here is the external complex: “the universe … and the worlds” gives the manifold field within which the net operates, matching the spiritual translation’s “whole compass of the created order”.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* After “which were hunted after by his net” the sense demands the result: the hunted light is gathered and subordinated through the image. This is the same correspondence as “comprehended… and brought… into subjection”.

**¶5** The fourth hunter is the great counsel that [lives] that lives in the circuit [of the Light] ⟨p.29⟩ entire universe in it today. Yet, at the end, in the dissolution of the universe, this very counsel of life will gather itself in and sculpt its soul in the Last Statue. Its net is its Living Spirit, because with its Spirit it can hunt after the light and the life that is in all things; and build it upon its body. Its ship, in which it is established, is this light cloud whereby it itself travels in the five elements [of the worlds], the great fire that will burn all the buildings of [in the five elements; and at the end,] in its net is the light and the life. It can rescue and free it from all bonds and fetters.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The phrase “the great counsel that … that lives” needs a verb of being; the substrate often names this force as a ‘counsel’ that ‘lives’ in the circuit (the encompassing order).
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* “Circuit” in this layer is the encompassing order of the Light-realm that contains and sustains the universe. Adding “of the Light” keeps the cosmological (not ecclesial) register and matches the spiritual sense ‘encompassing order’.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* What is sustained “in it today” is the arranged cosmos; “entire universe” already appears, and “of the worlds” is the idiomatic substrate complement.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* This lacuna must bridge the ‘travel in the five elements’ to the eschatological burning. The counsel moves through the elemental states; then comes the final phase. The spiritual translation likewise pivots: ‘through a sufficient series… and in that final time…’.

**¶8** The first hunter is the King of they who belong to the Darkness, who hunted after the living soul with his net at the beginning of the worlds. His net is his fire and his lust that he has put upon the living soul, with which he has entangled it [at the beginning], through all his powers.

> *¶8 #1 [tentative]:* The net entangles the living soul; the clause needs the manner/result before “through all his powers”. “At the beginning” anchors the predation to the primal onset of the worlds, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘from the beginning of a new spiritual arrangement’.

**¶9** [The] second hunter is the evil counsel that lives in [the circuit of Darkness] that hunts after the light [that is mingled] and [drags it] up from [from all] the earths to heaven. It binds them with its powers, which bring them to the heavenly worlds above. Its net is [persuasion] whereby they are drawn up from the abyss [to the heights]hts.

> *¶9 #2 [strong]:* The evil counsel ‘lives in …’ should mirror ¶5’s ‘lives in the circuit…’ but in opposite sense: the encompassing order of the Dark realm. This fits the correspondential opposition and keeps the technical cosmological diction.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* It hunts after the light and must specify the light’s state within the mixed cosmos. “That is mingled” matches the substrate doctrine of light mixed in all things.
> *¶9 #4 [strong]:* The action is violent extraction upward from lowest states; “drags it up” is required by the hunting image and the spiritual translation ‘drags it from the lowest natural states’.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* The clause “up from … the earths to heaven” needs the distributive source: not one place but the whole range of lower domains. “From all” supplies that scope.
> *¶9 #6 [strong]:* The net here, correspondentially, is coercive persuasion. Using the bare noun “persuasion” preserves the oldest-layer tone and aligns with the spiritual translation’s ‘coercive persuasion’.
> *¶9 #7 [strong]:* Completes the bracketed word; exactly the opposite-ascent motif required here.

**¶10** The third hunter is lust [which] walks in every power of the flesh that walks [about] in the [members of the body,] the living souls [and it binds] them in its bodies, which [it makes them serve death].

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Introduces the defining relative clause for lust as an agent moving through the bodily powers.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The verb phrase needs a motion term: lust ‘walks…’ and ‘roams about’ in the powers of the flesh; “about” supplies the idiom without adding later moral rhetoric.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* The fragment “in the …” demands the natural seat of lust’s operation: the bodily members as ultimates of the natural self. This matches the spiritual translation ‘forms of the natural self’.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* The next action after pursuing is fastening and imprisoning. “And it binds” continues the predatory net logic and leads into “them in its bodies”.
> *¶10 #5 [tentative]:* The final damaged clause must state the inversion of life into dead use: the living faculties are made to serve what is dead. This is exactly the spiritual translation’s closing idea, rendered in substrate idiom without Christian overlay.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae can be restored to a coherent correspondential chain because each ‘hunter’ requires fixed counterparts (net, sea, ship, prey, action). Where wording is fragmentary (especially ¶3 #2 and ¶8 #1, ¶10 #5), the spiritual constraints give the direction (conforming to the image; primal predation; life inverted to death), but the exact surface phrasing remains less tight.

---

## Chapter 6: Concerning the Five Storehouses that have poured forth from the Land of Darkness since the [Beginning]; the Five Rulers, the Five Spirits, the Five Bodies, the Five Tastes.

**¶2** Five storehouses have arisen since the beginning in the land of darkness! The five elements poured out of them. Also, from the five elements were fashioned the five trees! Again, from the five trees were fashioned the five genera of creatures in each world, male and female. And the five worlds themselves have five kings therein, and five spirits, five bodies, five tastes; in each world, they not resembling one another!

**¶3** The King of the world of Smoke [is the ruler] who came up from the depth of darkness; this is he who is the head of all wickedness, and all malignity. The beginning of the spread of the war occured through him; all the battles, fights, quarrelling, dangers, destructions, fights, wrestling-contests! That is the one who first made arise danger and war, with his worlds and his powers. Afterwards, also, he waged war with the light. He pitched a battle with the exalted kingdom.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The passage immediately defines this king as the head-source of malice and the initiator of war. In the oldest cosmological register this is stated as a simple identification: the Smoke-king is the ruling principle rising from the depth.

**¶4** ⟨p.31⟩
Now, regarding the King of Darkness, there are five shapes on him! His head is lion-faced; his hands and feet are demon- and devil-faced; his shoulders are eagle-faced; while his belly is dragon-faced; his tail is fish-faced. These five shapes, the marks of his five worlds, exist on the King of the realms of Darkness!

**¶5** Now, there are five other properties in him. The first is his darkness. The second is his putridity. The third is his ugliness. The fourth is his bitterness, his own soul. The fifth is his burning, which burns like an iron () as if poured out from fire.

**¶6** [There are] also three others in him! The first, that his body is hard and very tough, even as she has formed him [in evil and] cruel-hearted; namely Matter, who is the thought of death, [o]ne who sculpted him from the nature of the land of darkness. This is the manner of the body of the ruler of Smoke. He is harder than every iron, copper and steel and lead; as there is no cleaver at all, nor any iron implement, can [break] him and cut him. For Matter, his fashioner, has formed him [exceedingly] strong and hard.

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The triad here describes (1) bodily hardness formed by Matter, (2) an interior quality of heart, and (3) (in ¶8) linguistic cunning. The second item must mark his interior as merciless in the active sense; pairing “evil and cruel-hearted” preserves the impersonal, descriptive substrate style and matches the subsequent attribution to Matter as ‘thought of death’.
> *¶6 #4 [strong]:* The sentence requires a verb meaning that no implement can do this to him ‘and cut him’. In the substrate logic this property signifies unyielding confirmed state; ‘break’ is the natural correspondent that pairs with ‘cut’.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* The closing restatement intensifies the fashioning by Matter: ‘has formed him exceedingly strong and hard’ fits the correspondential emphasis on confirmed rigidity.

**¶8** ⟨p.32⟩
The third, that the King of the realms of Darkness knows the converse and language of his five worlds. He understands every thing he hears from their mouths, as they address one another; each one of them in his language. [Every] design they will consider against him, every snare they debate with one another to bring upon him, he knows them! He can also understand the gesturing they signal between one another. Yet, his powers and his rulers, who are subject to him, can not understand his wordy converse. While all these things are unveiled to him; still, their heart is not manifested to him. He knows not their mind nor their thought; he can not ponder their beginning and their end. Rather, he only knows and apprehends what is before his eyes.

**¶9** Also, another different thing is found in the King of the realms of Darkness! For when it pleases him to move, he spreads all his limbs out and walks. When it comes to mind, he with[d]raws his limbs and takes them in, and is rolled (?) to his companions; and he falls to the ground like a grape and a great iron ball! He terrifies by his cry, he is frightful. He frightens his powers with his sound; because when he speaks, being like thunder in the clouds, he resembles the [crash] of the rocks [when they are split] When he cries out and [roars] and calls [aloud] over his powers, they shall tremble and to[tter a]nd fall under foot; even as some birds would be [driven by] the bird [hawk] and they fall down to the earth. Still, this thing only: he knows not what is far from him, he sees not who is at a distance, nor does he hear him. Rather, whoever is before his face he sees, hearing him and knowing him.

> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* Thunder-like speech is then compared to something ‘of the rocks’; the natural image required is the crashing or bursting sound that corresponds to overwhelming persuasion that drowns discernment.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the simile: thunder in clouds resembles the crash of rocks when split. This keeps the impersonal, physical correspondence imagery typical of the oldest layer.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* The sequence is: he cries out and does something and calls over his powers. The missing verb should be an intensified vocal action parallel to crying: ‘roars’.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* Completes ‘calls ... over his powers’ in the sense of a summoning shout; fits the terrification motif.
> *¶9 #7 [tentative]:* The bird-comparison describes weaker powers collapsing when swept by a stronger outcry; ‘driven by the bird …’ preserves the correspondence of being carried and cast down by a stronger force.
> *¶9 #8 [tentative]:* A predatory bird best fits the image of lesser birds falling when driven by a stronger one; ‘hawk’ keeps the substrate’s concrete natural imagery without later moralizing labels.

**¶10** These signs and these evil marks are found in the chief of the demons and fiends, the King of all the mountains of Darkness [who is the foremost of them,] to [whom] the land of darkness has given birth, begotten in its cruelty, in its wickedness and its wrath [and reared in its poison.]

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* This clause must resume the identification: the king is chief among demons and fiends; the lacuna bridges to ‘the one to whom… has given birth’. Restoring a superlative headship statement matches the correspondential summary in the spiritual translation.
> *¶10 #3 [tentative]:* The closing birth-formula in the oldest layer typically adds a nurturing image after ‘begotten in cruelty, wickedness, wrath’. ‘Reared’ keeps the structural cosmogonic tone; ‘poison’ corresponds to the domain’s corrupting nurture without importing later pastoral language.

**¶11** ⟨p.33⟩
... more than all his fellow rulers, who are in all his worlds.

**¶12** Gold is the body of the King of the realms of Darkness. The body of all the powers who belong to the world of Smoke is gold. And also, the taste of its fruits is salty.

**¶13** In contrast, the King of the worlds of Fire is lion-faced, the foremost of all the beasts. Copper is his body. Again, the body of all the rulers who belong to Fire is copper. Their taste is the sour taste that is in every form.

**¶14** Again, the King of the worlds of Wind is eagle-face. His body is iron. Also, the body of all they who belong to the Wind is iron. Their taste is the sharp taste that is in every form.

**¶15** For his part, the King of the world of Water is fish-face. His body is silver. All the other rulers who belong to Water, silver is their body. Also, the taste of their fruit is the sweetness of water, the sweet taste that is in every (form).

**¶16** Again, [the Kin]g of the world of Darkness is a dragon. His [body is lead] and tin. All the other rulers [who belong to the world of] Darkness, their body is lead and tin. And also, the taste of their fruits is bitterness. Again, the spirit who reigns in them is the spirit who speaks till today in the soothsayers, giving oracles; in the givers of portents, every type; in they who are possessed; and the other spirits that give oracles, every type.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are locally constrained by the chapter’s fixed descriptive schema: kings, bodies (metals), tastes, and the king’s three further properties (hardness, cruel heart, linguistic cunning). The hardest restorations are in ¶9 (the thunder-rock simile and the bird comparison) and ¶10’s closing nurture clause, where the correspondential direction is clear (overwhelming outcry; birth and corrupt nurture) but several natural images could fit, so those readings remain tentative.

---

## Chapter 7: The Seventh, concerning the Five Fathers

**¶2** Five fathers exist; they were summoned forth one of one. [Also], one did come out of another!

**¶3** The first Father is the Father of Greatness, the blessed one of glory, the [on]e who has no measure to his greatness; who also is the first only-begotten, the first eternal; who exists with fiv[e fa]thers for ever; the one who exists before every thing that has existed, and that will exist.

**¶4** Now he, the glorious Father, summoned from him three emanations. The [fi]rst is the Gre[at] Spirit, the first Mother, who came out of the Father. She appeared first. ⟨p.35⟩

**¶5** The seco[nd i]s the Belo[ved of] the Lights, the great glorious Beloved, [the one w]ho is honour[ed; w]ho came out of the Father. [He ma]nifested ou[t of h]im.

**¶6** The third father is the Thir[d Ambassador], the eldest of [a]]] the counsell[ors; who came out of] the first Father. He appeared.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Retained “Third Ambassador” as the oldest-layer function-name for the primary mediating revealer, matching the correspondential role: emissary from the inmost toward lower degrees.

**¶7** This is the first Father, the first power, the one from whom the three great powers came out. This is the first Father, the first eternal, the root of all the lights; from whom the three emanations came out. They have humiliated the Darkness. They have brought its heart's desire to naught. They have given themselves the victory. They have also given the victory to their aeons.

**¶8** The second father, who came out of the first Father, is the Third Ambassador, the model of the King of lights. And again, he too summoned and sent out of him three powers.

**¶9** One is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man; the one who bears up under all things; the great Pillar of blessing; the great porter, who is greater than all the porters.

**¶10** The second is glorious Jesus the Splendour; [the] one through whom shall be given life eternal.

**¶11** The third is the Virgin of Light, the glorious wisd[o]m; the one who takes away the heart of the [rul]ers and the powers by her image, as she fulfils the pleas[u]re of the greatness.

**¶12** The third father, who came out of the second father: he is glorious Jesus the Splendour. And, again, he too summoned three evocations after the pattern of the second father.

**¶14** The second power whom Jesus summo[n]ed is the gre[at Jud]ge, who gives judgement on all the souls of mankind, [his] dwelling being established in the atmosphere under [the twelve] wheel [and the] stars.

> *¶14 #4 [moderate]:* The Judge’s dwelling is “in the atmosphere” (the intermediate region of equilibrium). Correspondentially it is ordered under fixed knowledges and their determinate forms; in this register that is expressed as the zodiacal governance: the twelve-fold ordering beneath which the wheel of the heavens and the stars are ranged.
> *¶14 #5 [moderate]:* Connects the cosmological series demanded by the spiritual sense: atmosphere under the twelve, under the wheel, and under the stars as fixed patterns of knowledges.

**¶16** The fourth father is the Light Mind, the one who chooses all the churches. And, again, he too summoned three powers after the pattern of Jesus.

**¶20** And also the fifth father is this Light Form; the one who shall appear to everyone who will go out from his body, corresponding to the pattern of the image to the apostle; and the thr[ee] great glorious angels who are come with her.

**¶22** These are the five fathers who have come out of one another. They have appeared and man[ifes]ted through one another!

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations fixed by the surviving word-forms. The only substantial restoration is ¶14’s cosmological placement of the Judge; the correspondential constraints (equilibrium in the intermediate region under fixed patterned knowledges) support a twelvefold ordering (zodiac) followed by the wheel and the stars, but the exact phrasing remains moderately constrained rather than certain.

---

## Chapter 8: Concerning the Fourteen Vehicles that Jesus has boarded.

**¶3** The first vehicle is the light ship, since he received instructions from the Ambassador there.

**¶4** ⟨p.37⟩ The second carriage is the ship of the First Man, since his dwelling is established there.

**¶5** The third is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man, since he shone forth there. He came below, he appeared in the world.

**¶6** The fourth is the summons that he clothed upon his body. The fifth is the obedience that he swathed upon the summons.

**¶7** The sixth is the living air, and since he assumed it too, he received anointment there. The seventh is the living wind that blows. The eighth is the light that illuminates. The ninth is the living water. The tenth is the living fire that is above them all.
