**¶8** He was journeying in these ten carriages among all the orders and all the [aeons], those above and those below.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* The closing summary requires a comprehensive cosmological term parallel to “all the orders”: he journeys through the whole arranged heaven. In the oldest Iranian-substrate register, “orders” names ranked formations, while “aeons” names the great totalities or world-ages that contain those ranks. This matches the spiritual sense: progression through every arranged level of heaven and the church, in higher and lower degrees.

**Assessment:** Only one gap. The correspondential structure (ten vehicles spanning degrees) constrains the missing noun to a totalizing cosmological category paired with “orders.” “Aeons” best fits the oldest-layer register and the intended sweep (above and below), though the exact Coptic equivalent cannot be proven from syntax alone.

---

## Chapter 9: The Explanation of the Peace, what it is; the Right Hand; the Kiss; the Salutation.

**¶2** You have questioned me about these five lessons. Now, they are displayed in the world as miniatures, being made little; and yet indeed they are great and honoured. I am the one who can reveal to you their mystery! So, these five signs are the mystery of the First Man. He came forth with them from the aeon of light. Also, when he finished his contest, he went up with these good signs. They received him into the aeons of light.

**¶3** ⟨p.38⟩ The first peace is the one that the gods and the angels in the land of light gave to the First Man; when he comes out against the enemy. The gods and the angels were walking with him; escorting him, giving to him their peace and power, and their blessing and fortification. This is the first peace that the gods and the angels gave to the First Man; as he comes forth from the aeon of light.

**¶4** The first right hand is the one that the Mother of Life gave to the First Man; when he comes out to the contest.

**¶5** The first kiss is this one with which the Mother of Life embraced the First Man; as he separates from her, coming down to the contest. Even all the gods and the angels who are in the aeons of light were making to embrace him! [With her] all of them also, and the church tutelaries (?), and the kinsfolk who belong to the household of his people. They were making to embrace him as [he] separates from them, accompanying him out, embracing him with the kiss of affection and love.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence requires the resumption of the subject of the embracing: after ‘were making to embrace him!’ the text must specify that the Mother of Life (the Divine wisdom conjoining love to the descending Divine good) was doing so, and then extend it to all celestial societies (‘all of them also’).

**¶6** ⟨p.39⟩ The first salutation is the salutation with which the First Man made obeisance; when he comes out [to the depths] below. He was bending his knees, making obeisance to the God of truth, and all the aeons [of light] who belong to the household of his people; entreating them for a power to escort him, as he comes forth.

**¶7** The first laying on of hands is the one that the Mother of Life bequeathed upon the head of the First Man. She armed him and made him mighty. She laid hands on him, and sent him to the battle. He descended. He completed his struggle with the great powers and the enmity.

**¶8** By the [se] five signs and these five myst[e]ri[es] the First Man came forth from the aeons of light against the enemy. He humbled him. He was victorious against him by them.

**¶9** Also, when he ascended from the war, he came in to the kingdom of the household of his people by these five mysteries!

**¶10** The peace with which [he ascen]ded is the peace that the summons gave to him, [w]hen it was sent forth from the [Fa]ther of Life. It gave him the peace in the world of darkness. Also, that peace with which he descended is th[e sam]e as the one with which he ascended from the struggle! Therefore, appropriate to the my[s]tery of that peace, which the summons gave the First Man, did this peace here and now come about. It was named and heard among the powers of the Father.

**¶11** Again, this second right hand is the one that the Living Spirit gave the First Man, when he [brou]ght him upward out from the struggle. Appropriate to [the] mystery [of t]hat right hand this right hand has come about; the one that occurs in the [midst of] mankind, as they give it to their companions, [it b]eing honoured and [glorified]

> *¶11 #6 [moderate]:* The clause balances ‘being honoured and …’ and needs a second predicate expressing the outward valuation of the right-hand act among humans as an ultimate image of the spiritual ‘communication of strength and faithful support’. ‘Glorified’ fits the impersonal, cosmological register better than later moralising verbs.

**¶12** Also, this second kiss is the one with which the [Father o]f Life and the Mother of the living embraced the First Man, when he ascended from the struggle. Again, this kiss occurs among mankind, as they make to embrace their companions with it; whether then [they] go away from home and be far from their friends, or else [if] they approach one another, according to the mystery of (the First Man).

**¶13** ⟨p.40⟩ [The] second salutation with which the First Man made obeisance [in] time to the God of truth and [all] the blessed aeons and al[l] the aeons [in] whole [land] of light, whe[n] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living brought the Man upward from the abyss of the struggle. Appropriate to the mystery of the second salutation this salutation has come about; this one that occurs today in the midst of mankind, as they make obeisance to one another, doing an honour to their companions.

> *¶13 #2 [tentative]:* The sequence demands an adverbial qualifier: the salutation is rendered ‘this time’ in a fuller, restored state after deliverance. A simple connective (‘in’) supports the sense ‘in this time’ without importing later framing.
> *¶13 #3 [moderate]:* The obeisance is directed ‘to the God of truth and … the blessed aeons’; the spiritual logic requires inclusion of the total heavenly order, so a collective term (‘all’) is demanded before enumerating ‘the blessed aeons’.
> *¶13 #5 [tentative]:* The phrase ‘and … the blessed aeons’ needs a preposition indicating location within the Light-realm societies (‘in the blessed aeons’), matching the chapter’s constant ‘in the aeons of light’ diction.

**¶14** The second laying on of hands is this: When the Living Spirit brought the First Man upward from the war, he saved him from all the surging waves. He brought him upward, he gave him ease in the great aeons of light, which belong to the household of his people. He set [him] firmly before the Father, the Lord of the totality. He received this great laying on of hands that he may become leader of his brethren in the new aeon. Again, appropriate to the mystery of the second laying on of hands, this laying on of hands has come about; the one that occurs in the midst of mankind as they lay hands on one another, the great giving authority to the lesser.

**¶15** These five mysteries and these five signs came about first in the divine. Mankind has been taught them; and they have instituted them in their midst [in forms;] these mysteries were not at the beginning amongst the powers of darkness.

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* The contrast required is: instituted among humans in external ultimates (‘in forms;’) yet absent ‘at the beginning’ among Darkness-powers. This keeps the oldest-layer emphasis on correspondence: natural enactments are images of prior divine operations, not moral commands.

**¶20** Once again: At the time of their coming forth, the Light Form shall come forth before them; and she redeems them from the darkness to the light [and she makes them peaceful.] This Light Form calms the person, with the kiss and her quiet, from fear of the demons w[ho de]stroy his body. By her asp[ec]t and her image [the] heart of the elect one, who is come forth from his bo[d]y, shall be calm for [him]. Afterwards, the angel who h[o]lds the victory prize extends to him the right hand. And it draws him out of the abyss of his body, and accepts him in with the [k]iss and love. That soul shall make obeisance to its red[eem]er, who is this Light Form. And also, at the inst[instant when he makes obeisance,] he shall be perfected and increased according to [the ordinance of the living ones] in the household of the living ones, with the go[ds] and the angel[s] and all the apostles and the chosen. [An]d he receives the crown [and the robe of] glory in the life for ever.

> *¶20 #1 [moderate]:* After ‘she redeems them from the darkness to the light’ the spiritual sense requires the immediate effect of that παρουσία: pacification and removal of terror. This prepares the next sentence (‘This Light Form calms the person…’), avoiding later soteriological rhetoric while keeping the structural sequence: redemption → tranquillisation → protection.
> *¶20 #10 [moderate]:* The narrative sequence is: right hand extended → drawn out and received with kiss → soul makes obeisance → then, at that very moment, perfection and increase according to order. The lacuna must mark that transitional instant tied to obeisance, not introduce a new actor or later ecclesial frame.
> *¶20 #11 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires ‘according to heavenly order’ (not a named church rule). In the oldest substrate register this is expressed as an impersonal ‘ordinance’ belonging to the Living (the ordered societies of Light).
> *¶20 #15 [tentative]:* After ‘he receives the crown … glory’ the missing element is the victory-investiture in ultimate form. In this chapter the five signs are enacted as ultimates; thus alongside the crown the restored one receives a ‘robe’ (garment) as the correspondence of confirmed truth surrounding good, leading into ‘glory in the life for ever’.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are minor letter restorations and are secure. The main recoveries are ¶5 #1 (subject resumption for the universal embrace), ¶11 #6 (a second predicate matching the honoured right hand), ¶15 #1 (external ultimates contrasted with Darkness), and ¶20 #1, #10–11, #15 (the exit-sequence of the soul: redemption, pacification, obeisance, perfection according to order, investiture). These are constrained primarily by the chapter’s five-operation pattern and by the inside-to-outside correspondential movement: divine act first, human enactment as image.

---

## Chapter 10: Concerning the Interpretation of the Fourteen [Great Aeons] of Light, about which Sethel has spoken in [his Prayer]

**¶3** ⟨p.43⟩ Happen you know this, that the [seven praises] below abide in fourteen [great aeons of light]: the five elements, the sons of the First Man, together with the call and the hearing. They anoint them, living in them. These shall make seven, five plus two.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The list that follows totals seven (five elements + Call + Hearing). In the oldest layer these are counted as ‘praises’ or ordered acknowledgments residing in the fourteen aeons; ¶4 resumes explicitly with ‘fourteen praises’.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The fourteen here are the full interior ‘measures’ or heavens of Light within which the sevens abide; the register is cosmological, not devotional.

**¶4** [There are also the] f[ive s]ons of the Living Spirit who [support] all [the wei]ght of the universe; together with the [Li]vi[ng] Spirit [and the Fir]st Man, their fathers. These are the fourt[een p]raises that he has proclaimed, the praise that is made of fourteen pra[yers], by which the universe is established.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Carries forward the parallel structure: after the seven below, ‘also’ introduces the sustaining powers above/beneath as another complete ordering.

**¶5** [There are fourt]een aeons of light [of the great aeons of light] of Seth [e] ... ] about them in his prayer, for they are expl[ai]ned [in the land that is not revealed]nd that is not revealed. This land [which is established and made firm]lished. They bore up under the enemy [and nothing among them equals]ls them. The hidden plenitude that [is within them] the Ambassador who came from the Father, the [sent forth] He came and stood firm in the midst of these [Living Word]: he is called the good father of [all] the ae[ons and gods] who are holy and conjoined. He has become leader and a great king after the likeness of the first Father, the lord of all these cou[nse]ls. This universe is held together by their will [and their counsel and their thought are one] to the end of all things.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Matches the chapter’s constant ‘fourteen aeons’ framework: a complete interior ordering in Light before the mention of Seth’s confession.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The ‘unrevealed land’ is the hidden doctrine or interior church in the spiritual translation; the phrase completes the sense that they are ‘explained’ within what is concealed.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation stresses that the concealed doctrine ‘is made firm’; the substrate idiom is establishing the hidden land as a stable ordering.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* Corresponds to ‘nothing in the opposing force could prevail’; here expressed as the enemy having no equal to these ordered states.
> *¶5 #8 [tentative]:* Introduces the ‘hidden plenitude’ as an interior fullness residing within these states, consistent with the correspondential ‘interior fullness hidden within these things’.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* Sets up the descent of the Ambassador/Divine Truth: ‘the hidden plenitude … sent forth the Ambassador’. This matches the spiritual translation’s ‘was sent forth’.
> *¶5 #10 [tentative]:* Oldest-layer functional title for the envoy proceeding from the Father: Divine truth proceeding from Divine good. ‘Living Word’ fits the impersonal cosmological register better than later ecclesial titles.
> *¶5 #14 [tentative]:* The universe is held together by their will; correspondentially this is the conjunction of love and understanding acting as a single consent. The fill articulates the inner unity that grounds the ‘will’ maintaining order to the end.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are either trivial letter-joins or tightly constrained by the chapter’s fixed numeration (seven within fourteen) and by the later explicit phrases ‘fourteen praises’ and ‘unrevealed land’. The harder gaps are in ¶5 where the mythic envoy-title and the exact phrasing around ‘hidden plenitude’ are not recoverable from syntax alone; those were restored by correspondential function (interior fullness sending forth proceeding truth) in the oldest cosmological register.

---

## Chapter 11: [Concerning the Interpretation of] all [the] Fathers of [Light], who are distinguished from one another.

**¶2** The beginning of each blessing and every prayer is the Mother of [L]ife, the first Holy Spirit.

**¶3** Also, [the] beginning [of] every good [co]unsel is the T[h i]rd Am[bassad]or, the king of the glorious realm that lies in this [wo]rld, [the steward and ordainer] of the King of the lights.

> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador here is not an evangelical messenger but the governing truth that administers the lower realm of reception on behalf of the King of Lights. The gap should therefore name a function of rule and arrangement (ordering, stewardship), matching the correspondential sense: discernment begins from Divine Truth as regulator in the world of mixture.

**¶4** The beginning of [reverence and] each [hono]ur (?) is the Beloved of the Lights, who is honour[ed.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The series lists beginnings of inner acts: blessing and prayer, good counsel, then reverence and honour. The spiritual sense requires the pair to be an inward acknowledgment (reverence) and its outward due regard (honour). Thus the lacuna supplies the interior term that precedes 'each honour'.

**¶5** The beginning of a]ll [the trappers] and hunters is the First [Man.

**¶6** ⟨p.44⟩
... of] all fighters is [the] Living [Spirit], the Father of Life, who has distribu[ted about] his five sons from place to place.

**¶7** The beginning of all the archit[e]cts and builders is the Great Builder, who is glorious.

**¶8** Also, the first of all the porters of the gr[e]a[t]ness is the Pillar of Glory ... [who bears up the worlds]

> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* The Pillar of Glory is the ultimate support in the lowest plane by which higher things stand and cohere. Correspondentially it must be described by sustaining and bearing. 'bears up the worlds' keeps the impersonal cosmological register of the substrate (outermost support holding the ordered whole).

**¶9** The beginning of [all] the deliverers [is Jesus the Splendour]; or the one who delivers, as he frees whoever belongs [from the bonds of Darkness] from his words.

> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Deliverance in this layer is release of what belongs to Light from binding falsity and constraint. The clause 'as he frees whoever belongs ... from his words' demands a named contrary power from which release occurs; in the oldest cosmological register this is Darkness as the binding principle, with 'bonds' expressing constraint dissolved by the delivering utterance.

**¶10** The beginning of every wisdom of truth is the Virgin of Light, [.

**¶11** The beginning of] all [the great]est honoured ones [in the worlds] is the Light Mind; who is the awakene[r of they] who sleep, the gatherer in of the ones who are sc[att]ered.

> *¶11 #2 [tentative]:* The Light Mind is described as awakener and gatherer, i.e., enlightened understanding operating throughout the domain of reception. A brief locative expansion fits the cosmological style: the honoured states are 'in the worlds' (degrees of reception) rather than in a devotional assembly.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are trivial letter or short-word restorations fixed by the surviving context. The three major restorations were constrained by the chapter’s correspondential structure: (1) the Third Ambassador must be expressed as an ordering, administrative Divine Truth in the mixed world; (2) the missing paired term before 'each honour' must be interior reverence preceding outward honour; (3) the deliverance clause requires a binding contrary power, best expressed as the bonds of Darkness. Two minor expansions remain less tight (¶11 #2), since the extant line already carries the needed spiritual function.

---

## Chapter 12: Concerning the Interpretation of the Five Words that are proclaimed in the Universe

**¶2** [Those who] call it by this name is the Father, the l[life, the one who is before all] this unnameable, whom no one [knows] his name.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence is defining what ‘they’ call the Father and why the name is used; the substrate voice is impersonal and descriptive, so a neutral collective subject (‘Those who…’) fits the correspondential intent: minds directed toward the inmost Divine without preaching.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The Father is identified as Life itself and as prior to all derivations. The spiritual translation stresses the unoriginated source; ‘before all’ preserves the oldest-layer cosmological register (priority of principle) without later devotional coloring.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The point is the incomprehensibility of the inmost Divine: no created understanding can comprehend or define it. ‘Whom no one knows his name’ expresses that ungraspability as to quality.

**¶3** ⟨p.45⟩
The second [word is the Third]d Ambassador [the living envoy] of life, whom they also call the father of [all that is derived from him] [yet he is after] the first Father.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* Keeps the given reconstruction, which matches the structural listing of ‘first… second…’ names and preserves the technical title ‘Third Ambassador’ as the mediating proceeding described in the spiritual translation.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially this figure is the proceeding mediation by which life is made present and communicated. ‘Living’ ties directly to ‘of life’ and avoids later institutional framing; ‘envoy’ matches ‘Ambassador’ while keeping the older impersonal cosmological tone.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The text calls him ‘father of …’ in the sense of a paternal principle relative to what proceeds. This fill makes explicit the correspondential relation of source-to-derived without importing later moral or ecclesial terms.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The clause must preserve subordination: the mediator bears the quality of the source but is not the first. ‘Yet he is after the first Father’ states discrete order of origin, matching the spiritual translation (‘subordinate… still recognize the inmost Divine as the first’).

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of cosmological sense because the chapter is a definition-chain: (1) the inmost Father as unnameable Life beyond comprehension, then (2) a proceeding mediator called the Third Ambassador, paternal toward what derives yet subordinate to the First. Exact connective particles are less constrained, but the required correspondential relations (unoriginated source; proceeding mediation; derived order) tightly limit the viable restorations.

---

## Chapter 13: Concerning the Five Saviours, the Resurrectors of Those Who Are Dead, Together with the Five Resurrections

**¶2** ... i]t therein [the lives are planted in their forms].

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* The surviving “… i]t therein …” implies an impersonal placement or implantation. In the oldest substrate register, restoration begins with lives being set within their proper vessels or forms (inside → outside).

**¶3** The second resurrector is [the Living Spirit].

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* In the Iranian-Manichaean cosmological sequence, the Living Spirit functions as an early restoring power after the first saving summons, effecting extraction and re-ordering of the mixed lives. With the defining clause lost, the safest substrate-consistent title for a ‘second resurrector’ is the Living Spirit.

**¶4** And the third resurrector is the Father, the First [Established One], because by his word shall all lives [that are scattered] gather in and go to him, and they receive [their own form] and rest.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* The line already reads “the Father, the First […]”; the substrate title paired with ‘First’ here is the primal stability or permanence of the Divine. “Established One” keeps the impersonal cosmological register and matches ¶6’s repeated epithet.
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* Correspondentially required: the Divine Word gathers lives out of dispersion. This supplies the needed opposite of ‘gather in and go to him’.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* After regathering, lives ‘receive’ what is proper to their renewed state and then ‘rest’. In the substrate this is expressed as receiving their proper form or measure (order in ultimates).

**¶5** The fourth resurrector is the Light Mind, because [he enlightens the lives and turns them toward their likeness]. ⟨p.46⟩

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The Light Mind restores by illumination in the intellect: giving light to understanding and turning what is being restored toward its archetypal pattern. This matches the chapter’s stated mechanism for the fourth operation.

**¶6** The fifth resurrector to the good [lives] is the Father, the First established one [in his firmness]. [He binds] all the powers, and he stands firm [in the root of his stability]. He shall unveil the [veil] over them [that was spread], and he draws them to him [by the attraction of his light], his will [is their measure], and he [establishes] them, and he stands them up [in their rank] there, and they remain until [the completion].

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The phrase “to the good […]” in this register most naturally takes ‘lives’ as the subject-class being restored (good lives as recipients).
> *¶6 #2 [tentative]:* The Father is repeated as “First established one”; the clause after it should state the mode: acting from steadfast permanence (stability as the ground of re-ordering).
> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* The following clause is “[He ...] all the powers,” which correspondentially demands an action of holding-together in unity; “binds” is the substrate verb for unification under a higher principle.
> *¶6 #4 [tentative]:* “and he stands firm […]” calls for a ground or basis. In correspondences, firmness is from first principles (root), not from outward constraint.
> *¶6 #5 [strong]:* “He shall unveil the […] over them” requires the object removed: a covering that conceals and restrains. The most economical noun is ‘veil’.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* The veil is typically described as laid or spread over. This supplies a natural action consistent with the image and keeps the impersonal tone.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* “and he draws them to him […]” needs an instrument or means; in this substrate the drawing is by light (intellectual-spiritual attraction), not by moral exhortation.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* After drawing, “his will […]” should state what the will functions as for them: their rule, measure, or order. ‘Measure’ fits correspondentially (order determining form).
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* “and he […] them” in this context is the act of re-forming into stability after conjunction: establishing them in ordered state.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* “and he stands them up […] there” calls for their ordered placement. ‘Rank’ expresses discrete degrees and ordered function.
> *¶6 #11 [strong]:* They remain until an appointed end-state. In the oldest layer this is not judgment-language but process-language: the completion/consummation of the restoration.

**Assessment:** Most restorations are constrained by the chapter’s clear sequence of successive restoring operations and by the repeated epithet ‘First established one’. However, ¶2 and the explanatory clauses for the second and fourth resurrectors are genuinely underdetermined in the surviving natural text; those fills are therefore guided primarily by the correspondential requirements (implantation into forms; restoration by illumination) and remain tentative to moderate in exact wording.

---

## Chapter 14: The Interpretation [of] the S[i]lence, the Fast, [the Peace], the Day, [and] the Rest; [what] they are.

**¶2** [And when the gatherer has come,] the gatherer in [the great wheel] then he shall cau[se] the body [to be gathered up,] all the lusts of the world [and he shall subdue] of the body in righteousness [and he shall set the members] is the Pill[a]r of Gl[or]y [which is established above,] because [it bears up the lower powers] ⟨p.47⟩ [by the descent of the Living Breath,] up to the [height.]

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate voice is impersonal and procedural: first the function (the ‘gatherer’ as a cosmic mechanism of collection and ordering), then its operation. This matches the spiritual sense: a power that gathers scattered faculties before re-ordering the whole person.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* The correspondential context requires an ordered cycle or turning that lifts and arranges: a ‘wheel’ is a standard cosmological image for the ordered revolution by which mixtures are sifted and raised. It also fits the clause ‘the gatherer in … then he shall cause…’.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Needed after ‘cause the body …’ to express the effect of the gathering power: collection and consolidation before subordination of lusts.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: cravings are subordinated. The oldest-layer register prefers structural verbs (subdue, bind, restrain) over moral exhortation.
> *¶2 #6 [tentative]:* Bridges from ‘lusts of the world’ to ‘of the body in righteousness’: the body becomes an instrument arranged into order (members set in place).
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* The Pillar of Glory in the substrate functions as the fixed support from higher influx; spiritual sense says the mind is ‘raised and fixed in a central support’.
> *¶2 #10 [moderate]:* Directly follows ‘because’: the reason for the Pillar is sustentation. This matches ‘the lower powers are held up and sustained by what flows down’.
> *¶2 #11 [tentative]:* Oldest-layer cosmology explains support by a descending vital influx. ‘Living Breath’ is a substrate-appropriate impersonal influx-term (pneuma), avoiding later devotional phrasing.
> *¶2 #12 [moderate]:* Completes ‘up to the …’ with the spiritual sense ‘carried upward toward fuller reception’. ‘Height’ keeps the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶3** The one they call [the Honoured One,] it is the [Fi]rst [M]an who dwel[ls i]n the ship of living waters [and he is in] li]ght [the light of the first beginning;] he is c[alled Initiation,] that they have [those who know him] glorious [a portion of his light,]

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘the one they name with honor’ is the initiating good that engages evil. ‘Honoured One’ preserves that function without importing later institutional titles.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Required to connect dwelling ‘in the ship of living waters’ to the following lacuna about light/illumination: he abides within that outermost in light.
> *¶3 #6 [tentative]:* Spiritual sense: within the outermost knowledges made alive, he ‘abides in illumination’. ‘first beginning’ preserves the initiating-quality of this good in oldest-layer idiom.
> *¶3 #7 [tentative]:* The name must signify ‘initiating good’. Restoring a name-title as an abstract (‘Initiation’) preserves the substrate tendency to name by function rather than biography.
> *¶3 #8 [moderate]:* Sets up the causal clause: recognition leads to participation (‘they have received a share of that radiance’).
> *¶3 #9 [strong]:* Matches the spiritual sense precisely: reception of a share of radiance is the ground for perceiving what is holy/glorious.

**¶4** [And the enlightener of the Mind,] the enlighten[er of discernment, they cal]l it 'day' [the separation of truth from falsehood;] uni]verse as they shall [and by it they order the whole] in the great aeon [of the soul’s universe] are spread in the universe, it is [to be,] established in the [the truths] and the so[u]ls [inward parts and in the living forms in which they are received.]

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense explicitly identifies ‘the one who gives illumination to the understanding’. Restoring ‘Mind’ keeps the impersonal cosmological-anthropological register of the oldest layer.
> *¶4 #2 [tentative]:* The function is to distinguish states (truth from falsity). ‘enlightener of discernment’ captures this as an operative power; the phrase also yields a smooth lead-in to “l it ‘day’”.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: illumination is the clarity by which falsity is separated from truth. Oldest layer prefers ontic terms (truth, falsehood) rather than moralized language.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Needed to connect ‘day’ (clarity) to arranging the universe/domain of life as it shall be.
> *¶4 #5 [tentative]:* Correspondentially, ‘universe’ here is the organized system of thought and affection. ‘soul’s universe’ keeps the cosmic idiom while applying it to interiors.
> *¶4 #6 [strong]:* Supplies the verbal hinge: ‘as they shall …’ → ‘as it shall be’.
> *¶4 #7 [strong]:* Spiritual sense: ‘ordered truths of that higher state are extended’. Thus the subject of ‘are spread in the universe’ is truths.
> *¶4 #9 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: arrangement is made firm ‘within the interiors of the mind and within the living principles in which persons are received and held’. This restoration keeps the substrate register (inward parts, living forms) without later ecclesial language.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of function because the spiritual translation tightly constrains the sequence: gathering and subduing, sustentation by a higher support, an initiating combatant good dwelling in enlivened outermosts, and an enlightener that separates truth from falsehood and fixes ordered truths in interiors. Exact mythic technical terms (especially ¶2 #2, ¶3 #6-7) remain less constrained without the Coptic context, so those restorations are more tentative though correspondentially consistent.

---

## Chapter 15: [Concerning the ... ...] Five [Parts ...] Worlds of [...]

**¶2** ⟨p.48⟩ [The third time, when the thought came forth from the land of darkness,] it moved it and [it moved it and gathered it together;] they that are in the [depths] of the earth of darkness [the] first part.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence of “parts” and “times” in ¶3–¶6 requires ¶2 to introduce the third completed state. The substrate speaks in impersonal cosmogonic cycles: an emanated motion from Darkness that stirs what is lowest.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense says an inflowing power acted on what was lowest and inert; in the natural register this is Darkness’ thought moving and collecting its lower mass into an active state.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* Matches “final, obscure degree” in the spiritual translation: the lowest region of the dark earth is regularly called its depth.
> *¶2 #4 [strong]:* The phrase is the fixed formula “the earth of darkness.”

**¶3** Yet, after a long time, as there was not [any rest or quietness], it gushed and streamed up [from its inner parts,] the five elements of death that gushed [from the first root of death,] which are smoke, fire, wind, water and darkness [up from it;] the second time [and it came to pass] they were sculpted [that] those in which death was established [into bodies and forms of their own;].

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual context: “no reception of living good and truth,” i.e., no settling into order; the oldest layer casts this as the absence of repose, so the dark substance cannot remain contained.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the hidden denial forces itself outward; the natural expression is eruption from within.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The five elements are said to gush from the root or source of Death in the Iranian-cosmological register, fitting the spiritual ‘intention of spiritual death’.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* Completes the gushing imagery: eruption upward into manifestation.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Standard narrative hinge between eruption and the next ordering step (“second time”).
> *¶3 #6 [strong]:* Grammar-link before “the second time.”
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: “brought into fixed forms of life.” In the oldest natural register this is sculpting into bodies and forms where Death can ‘dwell’.

**¶4** The fourth part is the evil fruits [that came about] in the five trees that are in the five worlds of the land of darkness.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Connects “evil fruits” to their generation from the prior forming; keeps the impersonal cosmogonic tone.

**¶5** The fifth time it spread itself out to its creatures that exist in the five worlds; the ones that were formed and generated from the five fruits of death. They are the five worlds of flesh, the five creations of darkness in which death has been established. It spread itself out to ten parts, five male parts and five female [female parts.].

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The sentence demands completion of the dual division: five male and five female parts.

**¶6** Now, like the land of darkness, in that its spirit and its [thought,] they nourished the five elements [that are in them]; this is also [how] the ten parts that are in male and female, the fire and lust of the flesh [nourished] them, as they yoke them with one another [nourish] they were established against the First Man. This is [the end] of the darkness, it was condemned for ever.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ruling affection and dominant persuasion; in the substrate these are typically ‘spirit’ and ‘thought’ as the two interior principals of the dark land.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The nourishing is performed by the inner principals upon the elements; this supplies the object for “they nourished…”.
> *¶6 #4 [strong]:* Parallels the clause just before: as spirit and thought nourished the five elements, so the ten parts nourish lust and fire of flesh.
> *¶6 #5 [strong]:* Required verb for “the fire and lust of the flesh … them”; the spiritual sense uses “fed”.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation: “In this consists the final state of that falsity.” The natural register calls it “the end of the darkness,” i.e., its last completed state.

**¶7** Alongside the mystery of these twelve parts that came about in the darkness [that were established] against the First Man. The twelve [parts are] the twelve spirits of error that came about from the twelve signs of the zodiac [and from the twelve powers that are in them.].

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Matches the repeated pattern “established against the First Man” and the spiritual “all set in opposition”.
> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* Balances the structure: “The twelve parts: the twelve spirits of error…”.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: a complete framework of outward knowledges turned into a governing system. In the natural astrological register, the ‘signs’ bear ‘powers’ that generate the spirits of error.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are recoverable because the chapter is structured as a numbered sequence of completed ‘times’ and ‘parts’ (third through fifth; then tenfold duality; then twelvefold system). The correspondential constraints strongly fix the needed items: eruption from within, five elements of Death, ten male and female parts, nourishment by the dark land’s interior principles, final state and condemnation, and a twelvefold error-system generated from the zodiacal framework. A few phrases (especially ¶2–¶3 transitions and the exact source-term for the five elements) remain moderately constrained rather than exact-word certain.

---

## Chapter 16: [Concerning the Five] Greatnesses who [went forth] against the Darkness.

**¶1** “[When the battle was raised against] the darkness, it was defeated by five greatnesses.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening formula must introduce the primal contest and set the subject for “the darkness … defeated by five greatnesses.” The substrate voice is impersonal and cosmogonic, not hagiographic; hence a neutral battle-setting clause.

**¶2** The first greatness is the Father of Greatness who exists in calm and hiddenness; as he is established in his light-earth, in his own essence.

**¶3** Four greatnesses were manifested and came forth of him. They were appointed to ten parts, and established in total twelve.

**¶4** The second greatness poured out and was manifested. It was revealed from the first greatness. It is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, together with the First Man and his five sons; these are the second greatness.

**¶5** ⟨p.50⟩
The third greatness is the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder, the Living Spirit, and the five sons of the Living Spirit. These are the third greatness.

**¶6** The fourth greatness is the Third Ambassador, and the Pillar of Glory, and all the light powers who were revealed from him. These are the fourth greatness.

**¶7** The fifth greatness who came from the Father is Jesus the Splendour, and all his powers whom he summoned, his emanations who were [revealed in the elements] They poured out and were manifested from him.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The fifth greatness (Splendour) reaches the outermost; its emanations are typically said to be ‘revealed in the elements’ in this register, matching the spiritual sense ‘as it reaches the outermost’.

**¶8** This is, so that they were spread out and [It happened,] fives: these five [sons] of the First Man [who are spread out to five places, supporting] the entire building; another five are the five sons of the Living Spirit who are spread out to five places, supporting [the whole]

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* Bridges from the list of emanations to the explanation of their functional spreading-out; a simple narrative hinge fits the substrate style.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Editor’s fill fits: the fivefold is explicitly the five sons of the First Man in this teaching layer.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Parallelism demands the same distribution language used a few words later for the Living Spirit’s five sons; both fives are ‘spread out’ and ‘support’ the ordered construction.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts ‘the entire building’ with what is ‘supporting …’; ‘the whole’ is the natural completion and matches the spiritual sense ‘sustaining the ordered whole’.

**¶9** [Thus they are] total in twelve parts that [And they are] another two added to them who are the summons and the obedience. They are the father and the mother whom Jesus the Splendour by his advent has appointed as successors to his place [were appointed] the great earth and the [and they rule over] in them.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Needs a summative transition from the two fives to the twelvefold total.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* The bracket begins the clause that asserts the total and then appends the additional two; ‘And they are’ cleanly introduces the arithmetic statement.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* The phrase ‘total in twelve parts that …’ calls for a verb of establishment or appointment consistent with earlier ‘appointed to ten parts’ and ‘established’.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* The successors (summons and obedience) are appointed ‘to his place’ and therefore exercise governance over the ‘great earth’ and what is in it; this matches the spiritual sense ‘to govern the outermost plane’.

**¶10** [The advent] of the First Man who came [from the heights to the depths] belonging to [his own household] existing in a kingdom [and is found] esteem before they who belong to his household [and honour] his enemies [but] and suffering by their wickedness; and they take from him [come upon him] and a richness; and they imprison [him in] captivity and take from him their companies (?). Yet, he [his servants] by his humility; and he takes heart and restrains his enemy [endures] in silence.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Removes the later editorial ellipsis and restores the simple substrate heading for the simile that follows.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The simile requires a descent from a higher degree into a lower contested one, corresponding to ‘a noble truth from a higher state is present… yet is assailed’.
> *¶10 #3 [strong]:* The spiritual translation explicitly has ‘those of its own spiritual household’; the natural image must name that household.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* Smooths the clause ‘existing in a kingdom … esteem’ into a coherent description of standing and regard.
> *¶10 #5 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with ‘esteem’ in this register and suits the noble-son simile.
> *¶10 #6 [strong]:* Introduces the adversative turn: despite honour, enemies assault him.
> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* Gives the required action of enemies preceding ‘and suffering by their wickedness’.
> *¶10 #8 [strong]:* Editor’s fill is required by the immediately following ‘captivity’.
> *¶10 #9 [moderate]:* The enemies try to take away his ‘companies’; in the substrate this is typically his attendants or servants, corresponding to subordinate operative powers.
> *¶10 #10 [strong]:* Matches the spiritual sense ‘yet it endures by humility’ and fits the syntax ‘Yet, he … by his humility’.

**¶11** Just as with this man, this noble son whose enemies surround him, this also is how the advent of the First Man happened, [among the] rulers, the enemies of the light.

> *¶11 #1 [strong]:* Connects the simile to the cosmogonic referent: it happened ‘among’ the rulers.

**¶12** In contrast, the advent of the Living Spirit and the commandment that he received, whereby he came upon all the rulers, it differs from that of the First Man. His advent [is not like] the advent of the First Man.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence explicitly contrasts the two advents; ‘it differs from’ is already present, but the lacuna must supply the direct negation that sets up the comparison.

**¶13** ⟨p.51⟩
The advent of the Living Spirit is like this, when he was sent from the greatness that he might bring up the First Man. He resembles a judge the king would send to overturn the violence to that man, the noble son against whom his enemies sinned. And he, this judge, [is firm] hard and cruel [against those who are] and he gives the means [means of salvation] by his righteousness and his strength; and he also judges all who are condemned according to their rebellious heart, and repays each according to his deserts. They who are worthy of blows he strikes with hard leather whips. Again, these who are worthy of cutting off their limbs, or indeed their [their ears] and he repays them accordingly. [Again, those who are worthy of imprisonment] and he binds and fetters them, and hangs by [their feet] and thus he does to them with a righteous judgement. And he kills them to flay their skin [and flay their flesh] so that they will be killed by a death [that is strange and bitter] until they die. Again, [they for] whom it is fitting [to cast out] to banish from their dwellings he may remove according to a righteous judgement; just as they had faulted and sinned against the noble son.

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* The judge is characterized by unwavering firmness in executing judgement, matching the spiritual sense.
> *¶13 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the objects of judgement, leading into ‘hard and cruel’.
> *¶13 #3 [moderate]:* In the oldest register, the judge ‘gives the means’ for restoring the assaulted noble one; ‘means of salvation’ is functional, not devotional, and matches ‘means of restoration’.
> *¶13 #4 [moderate]:* List of punishments: blows, cutting off limbs, or their ears—an idiom of disabling powers of reception and obedience in the opposite sense.
> *¶13 #5 [moderate]:* The next action is binding and fettering; thus the missing clause must introduce imprisonment as the fitting sentence.
> *¶13 #6 [moderate]:* ‘hangs by …’ is naturally ‘by their feet’, corresponding to inversion of state (head down elsewhere).
> *¶13 #7 [moderate]:* Completes ‘flay their skin …’ with the common paired object ‘flesh’, emphasizing total removal of power in the opposite sense.
> *¶13 #8 [tentative]:* The death described is qualitative, not merely physical: a ‘strange and bitter’ death corresponds to extinguishing the power of acting from evil.
> *¶13 #9 [strong]:* Editor’s fill is mechanically required by the syntax.
> *¶13 #10 [strong]:* Gives the infinitive governed by ‘it is fitting … to banish’; aligns with the spiritual sense ‘casts out from the spheres’.

**¶14** Also, the associates in the mystery and the assistants of this judge, these who belong to him in great strength; in an instant and haste they shall do the will of their master.

**¶15** Now, just as this genuine judge, who was sent from the king to overturn the violence to this man, the noble son: this is also the case with the Living Spirit. He also was sent from the King of the lights to overturn the vengeance and violence to the First Man, against whom the King of the realms of Darkness had sinned. He faulted and sinned against him, and also his rebellious powers. Therefore, when (the Living Spirit) came, he bore up the First Man.

**¶16** Also, all the rulers, the powers of sin who had faulted and sinned against the sons of the First Man, he has judged them according to a righteous judgement. He has bound them in heaven and earth. He put each one to the place fitting for him; he weighed each of them according to his cruelty and oppression. While some of them he enclosed in the prison, others he hung head down. Some [among them] are crucified, others are sat down all the time. Some among them are fixed beneath their associates, restricted by a strong chain; others he made the holders of authority over their companions beneath, so that they may do their pleasure amongst these below them.

> *¶16 #1 [strong]:* Introduces the enumeration of differing sentences (‘Some … are crucified’).

**¶17** ⟨p.52⟩
Happen you know this [thing,] that when the Father of Life established the judgements [and established] He was revealed to all the powers of darkness. He judged them as a true giver of judgement [in righteousness.] This is the first judgement that the Living Spirit gave the rulers [in respect of the] First Man. Indeed, he gave victory to the First Man corresponding to his victory; but he condemned the King of the realms of Darkness corresponding to that by which he had erred.

> *¶17 #1 [moderate]:* The idiom ‘Happen you know this …’ requires an object like ‘this thing’ before ‘that when…’.
> *¶17 #2 [moderate]:* The Father of Life ‘established the judgements’; adding ‘and established’ reinforces the setting of order by judgement, matching the spiritual sense ‘set order’.
> *¶17 #3 [moderate]:* Completes ‘as a true giver of judgement …’ with the quality of that judgement; righteousness is the correspondential measure.
> *¶17 #4 [strong]:* Editor’s fill fits the required relational clause.

**¶18** [Thus] this chain, of this sort, became [fixed] the earth till the end time when the universe will be dissolved; and it perishes in the great fire and is separated. While the light goes to its country, the darkness remains in the chain and fetter for ever.

> *¶18 #1 [strong]:* Introduces the consequence from the first judgement to the enduring chain.
> *¶18 #2 [moderate]:* The chain ‘became … the earth’ needs the idea of fixed constraint in the outermost; ‘fixed’ matches the spiritual sense.

**¶19** The advent also of the Third Ambassador, when he came to inspect they who are imprisoned in the zone: he is like a great king who comes and inspects craftsmen [and soldiers] and [fortifications] and [guards]

> *¶19 #1 [tentative]:* A king inspecting craftsmen commonly also inspects soldiers guarding works; corresponds to providence inspecting functions maintaining order.
> *¶19 #2 [strong]:* Echoed explicitly in ¶21 (‘works and fortifications’), so it should already appear in the initial simile list.
> *¶19 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the triad with a role aligned to maintaining order; also prepares for ¶21’s ‘guarding’.

**¶20** Also, they who are good, of the craftsmen and architects and artisans whom he has there in each place: he shall see that they have worked well, and have fallen to his works in their fashion. He shall rejoice with great joy [in their presence] He shall thank the artisans who have constructed his works [and completed] his orders.

> *¶20 #1 [moderate]:* The king rejoices ‘with great joy …’ and then thanks them; ‘in their presence’ is a natural bridge.
> *¶20 #2 [strong]:* Editor’s fill matches the sense ‘fulfilled what was commanded’.

**¶21** Now, just as this great king who comes and inspects his works and fortifications, so also is the advent of the Third Ambassador. For, when he too came, he saw [his works] established and prepared and [arranged] series of the rulers prepared and established there, above and below, outside and within, in the great pattern that his brothers had made. They had come before him to the contest, they who had fixed and created the universe before he came. He saw the gods and the angels established and controlling the fortifications [in a] guarding the entire ruling-power [of the universe] which his first powers had done. They are the First Man and the Living Spirit and the Great Spirit, the Mother of Life.

> *¶21 #1 [strong]:* Editor’s fill fits exactly.
> *¶21 #2 [moderate]:* After ‘established and prepared’ a third participle like ‘arranged’ completes the triad of orderly disposition.
> *¶21 #3 [moderate]:* Needed to join ‘guarding the entire ruling-power … which…’; simplest syntactic connector.
> *¶21 #4 [moderate]:* The ‘ruling-power’ being guarded is over the formed system; ‘of the universe’ matches the teaching layer’s cosmological scope.

**¶22** Therefore, when he saw them, that they had created everything with great wisdom, [he revealed the hidden things] as they looked he plundered (?) [to them] in his advent, by which he came, he was unveiled to them. He says to the Living Spirit: “The judgement that you have [done is] good, what you uttered [to each one,] you gave to them correspondingly; so that they were moved and raised up to that of which they were not worthy”.

> *¶22 #1 [strong]:* The spiritual sense says ‘brought hidden things to disclosure’; this is the natural counterpart.
> *¶22 #2 [strong]:* Completes ‘unveiled to them’; required dative object.
> *¶22 #3 [strong]:* ‘The judgement that you have … good’ needs ‘done is good’ as the predicate in this register.
> *¶22 #4 [strong]:* Matches the spiritual sense ‘apportioned to each according to order’ and fits the immediate ‘you gave to them correspondingly’.

**¶23** ⟨p.53⟩
The advent also of Jesus the Splendour, the time when he came to all [the powers below,] being just like a man if he is sent to pull out a [root] burn a difficult fig-garden with fire. Therefore, again, when he has first cut the bad trees with his axe and has pulled out [their roots,] and their body with his fire, so that they should not return to sprout from this time, nor bear fruit bad to eat [from them.]

> *¶23 #1 [moderate]:* Jesus the Splendour comes ‘to all …’; in this layer that is ‘all the powers below’, matching the spiritual sense ‘all states below’.
> *¶23 #2 [strong]:* The tool is for pulling out a ‘root’, matching the later ‘pulled out …’ and the spiritual sense ‘root-intentions’.
> *¶23 #3 [strong]:* Completes ‘has pulled out … and their body’; the natural counterpart of extirpating root-intentions.
> *¶23 #4 [moderate]:* Closes the clause ‘nor bear fruit bad to eat …’ with a firm cessation, matching the spiritual sense ‘do not return’.

**¶24** Afterwards, he also planted good plants: the tree of life that will make good fruit.

**¶25** Again, this is like this simile of the advent of Jesus the Splendour. It happened like this; because he too set out [to the zone,] his power and his wisdom, in his [compassion]-ness [as] a king and a saviour [and as a healer;] in his glorious wisdom he destroyed them. He bound and crushed them like the fire that shall burn and destroy trees. This is also how he performed his will in the zone, among the many powers, until he reached the form of the flesh of Adam and Eve, the first humans. [He perfected] his will in Eve, he [purified her.] He also gave hope to Adam, and the good news [that he might live;] glorious [that he would be saved,] He went up to the heights [in the glory] of light.

> *¶25 #1 [moderate]:* The action begins in the intermediate region; this matches the repeated ‘in the zone’ later in the paragraph.
> *¶25 #2 [moderate]:* The phrase ‘in his …-ness … a king and a saviour’ calls for a quality of merciful governance; ‘compassion’ fits the substrate without Christianizing.
> *¶25 #3 [strong]:* Introduces the appositional role ‘as a king and a saviour’.
> *¶25 #4 [tentative]:* Completes the triadic royal function in this layer: king, saviour, healer—functional restoration rather than pastoral piety.
> *¶25 #5 [strong]:* Editor’s fill fits the subsequent ‘his will in Eve’.
> *¶25 #6 [moderate]:* Corresponds to completing the work in the receptive affection (Eve) by purification, matching the spiritual sense.
> *¶25 #7 [moderate]:* Pairs with ‘He also gave hope to Adam’—hope is toward life restored.
> *¶25 #8 [moderate]:* The ‘good news’ is of restoration; ‘saved’ here is structural deliverance from mixture, consistent with the substrate.
> *¶25 #9 [moderate]:* Completes ‘He went up to the heights … of light’ with a genitive phrase; ‘glory of light’ is standard for ascent to the luminous degree.

**¶26** ⟨p.54⟩
Then the summons and the obedience, the great counsel that came to the elements, which are set in conjunction: it mixed with them, it was established in silence. It bears up [it] until the end time when it can arise and stand firm in the great fire. It will gather to it its own soul, and sculpt it in the Last Statue. You will also find it sweeps out and casts from it the pollution that is foreign to it. However, the life and the light that are in all things it gathers in to it, and builds upon its body.

> *¶26 #1 [strong]:* ‘It bears up … until the end time’ requires the pronoun for the counsel (summons and obedience).

**¶27** The time when this Last Statue will be perfect in all its limbs, then it can become free and ascend from that great struggle through the Living Spirit its father, the one who comes and brings a [each] limb. He brings it up from within this gathering (?), the melting down and destruction of all things.

> *¶27 #1 [strong]:* The Living Spirit ‘comes and brings a … limb’; the spiritual sense says ‘brings each power into its place’. Thus ‘each’ is demanded.

**¶28** Now, this counsel, that summons and obedience, resembles thus cooled butter. If a person will take and set it in hot milk, it melts down and soaks in with this hot milk. It melts in another time in silence and quiet to the midst [of it,] yet also, of the power and the taste of this milk [that] is drawn to (the butter), and (the milk) sets and becomes cold [and] in [again,] and the [whey] this milk it shall clarify and discard. However, the oil and the grease and the ‘beauty’ of the milk it shall gather in to it.

> *¶28 #1 [moderate]:* After ‘to the midst … yet also,’ the clause needs ‘of it’ referring to the milk’s power and taste present within the mixture.
> *¶28 #2 [strong]:* ‘of the power and the taste of this milk … is drawn’ needs ‘that’ to introduce the relative clause.
> *¶28 #3 [strong]:* Joins the two results: the drawn-in goodness and the cooling/setting of what remains.
> *¶28 #4 [moderate]:* Signals the second phase: after drawing in, clarification and casting away occurs.
> *¶28 #5 [moderate]:* The discarded part of milk in this simile is ‘whey’, corresponding to what is cold and unsuitable being clarified and cast away.

**¶29** ⟨p.55⟩
Again, this too is what the advent of that thought is like, the counsel of life, which is the summons and obedience that came to the elements. This is the one that at the end can arise and stand firm, and will gather in to it all the lives, and the light that has remained behind in everything. It can build them upon its body; it will gather them in to it [in calm] in its image for ever and ever [in its form] from this time.

> *¶29 #1 [moderate]:* ‘gather them in to it … in its image’ is followed by an eternal stabilization; ‘in calm’ matches the substrate’s emphasis on rest and fixed order.
> *¶29 #2 [moderate]:* Completes the closing: gathered ‘in its image … from this time’ to remain ‘in its form’, i.e., the final organized human form (Last Statue).

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of function and correspondential structure because the chapter is a tightly patterned series: five operations, two added principles, and four advent-similes each with characteristic actions (descent, judgement, inspection, purification, final gathering). Where the lacuna touches stock cosmological phrasing (distribution to five places, judgement-sentences, disclosure of hidden things, uprooting roots, ascent to glory), restorations are moderately to strongly constrained. A few items remain tentative where the natural simile could take multiple equivalent forms (e.g., the king’s inspection list; the exact epithet in ¶25; the qualitative description of ‘death’ in ¶13), but even there the spiritual logic fixes the required function (inversion, disabling, extinction, separation) and keeps the wording within the oldest impersonal cosmological register.

---

## Chapter 17: The Chapter of the Three Seasons

**¶1** From the time when the light came forth and was crucified in the darkness, to the season when the Statue will ascend and the entire light, which was conjoined with the darkness, will be purified and cleansed. From that time on, from that time to the season of the end totals three seasons.

**¶2** Three seasons: from the time when the First Man went down to the abyss, and waged the war and the struggle against the King of the realms of Darkness and his other powers. He was victorious, while his sons remain behind with them in the worlds [and in the heavens] in the [midst] until he shall catch all of them [worlds of darkness] he bound them all up, so that not one of them escaped his hand. He won a great victory [in his net] The First Man spent human lifetimes and generations in the land of darkness, until he prevailed [afterwards] ⟨p.56⟩ he fulfilled the will of the greatness that was commanded of him.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The detained ‘sons’ must be held across the lower domains (worlds) and the upper boundary (heavens). This matches the substrate’s threefold cosmos (depths, worlds, heavens) and the spiritual sense: operative powers held within opposing domains until gathered back.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The phrase needs a locative that expresses being held among hostile powers. ‘In the midst’ fits the correspondential constraint: goods and truths detained among evils and falsities in their own domains.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The object of ‘in the …’ must be the domain of detention: the dark worlds. This is the fixed cosmological register of the oldest layer.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* What follows is ‘until he shall catch all of them’, so a capturing-instrument correspondence is demanded. ‘Net’ is a standard natural image for the Divine ordering constraint gathering dispersed powers; it also anticipates ‘bound them all up’.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* A simple connective is required between ‘until he shall catch all of them’ and ‘he bound them all up’. ‘Afterwards’ preserves the impersonal chronological sequencing of the substrate.

**¶3** Then they sent the Living Spirit, the Father of Life. He brought the First Man up from the struggle, he brought him up to the land of light. This is the first period, from the descent of the First Man to his ascent.

**¶4** The second season is from the time when the First Man ascended. The Living Spirit came, and the Mother of Life. They set in order the things of the universe, they set up [the firmaments] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living set in order [all] the universe, up to the moment when they go out [from it, until it was completed] it was reckoned to the second season.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* In the second season the Living Spirit and Mother of Life ‘set in order’ cosmic structures. The primary ordered vessels between heights and depths are the firmaments, corresponding to arranged degrees receiving and containing influx.
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* ‘set in order … the universe’ demands a totalizing determiner to mark the comprehensive ordering of degrees and uses.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* The clause must express the span ‘up to the moment when they go out … it was reckoned’. In the substrate, ‘go out from it’ marks the completion of their shaping activity; ‘until it was completed’ gives the required terminus without later pastoral coloring.

**¶5** [Afterwards] the universe [was established,] the Third Ambassador spread [his light] that is in the [universe of the] living; he [gathered it;] he separated beauty from all the powers and scooped it up to the heights, he swept the waste to the abyss, and he [set a boundary between them] Now, from the moment when the Ambassador revealed his image he purified the light. Sin spurted up towards him. He hid his image. Sin came [up] from the darkness. It sculpted the trees and the abortions fell down. Afterwards they sculpted Adam and Eve in the flesh. Until the moment when Jesus was sent and he [again] as he performed there his will. He gave the hope to Adam, and ascended to the heights.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* Begins the third season narrative, following the completion of cosmic ordering. A neutral temporal marker suits the oldest layer.
> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* The line resumes after a break: ‘… the universe … the Third Ambassador…’. A completion/settling verb is required to bridge from the second season’s construction to the third season’s emissive action.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* What the Third Ambassador ‘spread’ in the cosmos is the manifested light-image by which separation occurs. This matches the spiritual sense: a further Divine manifestation made perceptible for reception.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The phrase ‘that is in the … living’ needs the locative frame: the manifested light is ‘in the universe of the living’, i.e., the ordered system receptive of life.
> *¶5 #5 [tentative]:* After ‘spread … he … he separated beauty … and scooped it up’, a verb of collecting into one is demanded to keep the sequence: spread presence, then gather the separable light upward.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* Following ‘he swept the waste to the abyss’, the remaining action is to establish a fixed separation so mixture does not recur. This corresponds to discrete degrees: a boundary that holds the separated states apart.
> *¶5 #7 [strong]:* ‘Sin came … from the darkness’ requires the directional adverb marking surge from below toward the revealed image.
> *¶5 #8 [strong]:* After the hiding of the image, the narrative repeats the irruption: ‘Sin came up again from the darkness.’ This matches the spiritual sense: renewed forming of perverted perceptions after withdrawal.

**¶6** Now, from the time when the Ambassador displayed his image until the season when the Statue will ascend at the end, shall be reckoned to the third season.

**¶8** [The first season] lasts long in the time that the First Man spent in the world of darkness. It is a very great count of time, more than the (other) two seasons!

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* The subject of ‘… lasts long in the time that the First Man spent…’ must be explicitly the first season/process, since the comparison is with ‘the other two seasons’.

**¶10** [He replies to them]: If you should say that [the intermediate season] it is small, if you should [consult someone] to him [again] and you consult [someone else] you will tell him: Twenty times! It is greater again in this other one that you will consult: Thirty times! Less also is this other one [in another reckoning,] people will receive the {} of the trees [fruits] the years and the generations [they will carry up from the] earth to the heights. This is [...

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* Retained as a light editorial seam; no overt exhortation is added. It can stand as a minimal dialogic marker without shifting into later devotional register.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The comparison that follows (‘it is small… twenty times… thirty times…’) requires a named baseline. The spiritual sense contrasts measures across comparisons; the ‘intermediate season’ is the natural referent used elsewhere in the chapter.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* The syntax requires an action of inquiry leading to a reported multiplier (‘you will tell him: Twenty times!’). The substrate frame is measurement by consultation, not moral admonition.
> *¶10 #4 [strong]:* ‘to him … and you consult … you will tell him’ needs a connective adverb to mark the second inquiry. ‘Again’ preserves the iterative measuring sequence.
> *¶10 #5 [strong]:* The next clause explicitly contrasts ‘this other one that you will consult: Thirty times!’ so the consulted party must be ‘someone else’.
> *¶10 #6 [tentative]:* After ‘Less also is this other one …’ the text turns to factors that lengthen states (‘people will receive… years and generations…’). A phrase indicating a different measure fits the spiritual sense of variable comparisons.
> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* ‘people will receive the {} of the trees’ demands the natural product that corresponds to appropriation and reception. ‘Fruits’ fits the correspondence: received works and perceptions proceeding from what was formed.
> *¶10 #8 [moderate]:* The close of the sentence is about what is raised ‘… earth to the heights’. A verb phrase of elevation/transport is demanded, aligning with the spiritual sense: elevation of what can be raised from the lowest degree.

**¶11** This is the third period, from the time when the Ambassador revealed his image until the Statue will ascend from the lump. It again, that time, is greater than the intermediate; but it does not come equal to the time of the First Man. For this intermediate season, in which the Father of Life and the Mother of Life constructed the universe with its orders, is shorter than that of the First Man.

**¶12** Great is the period of the First Man! It lasts longer than the season in which the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the world and the Statue ascends. From the time too when the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the universe [it is greater than the season] when the Father of Life and the Mother of Life shaped the universe.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* The comparison at ¶12 requires an explicit comparative link: the duration from the Ambassador’s manifestation to dissolution exceeds the intermediate ordering season. This fill supplies the demanded comparative statement without importing later doctrinal jargon.

**¶13** These are the [only] three periods of time that occur. ⟨p.58⟩

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* The closing sums the doctrine: ‘These are the … three periods of time that occur.’ ‘Only’ marks their discreteness and completeness, matching the spiritual sense of three distinct processes.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s three-season structure and by the correspondential operations (detention, gathering, ordering of degrees, manifestation, separation, boundary-setting, renewed irruption). Where the lacunae require specific technical cosmological nouns (e.g., ‘firmaments’, ‘worlds of darkness’) restorations are fairly tight. Where the gaps sit in connective narrative tissue (especially ¶5 #2, #5 and ¶10 #6–#8), the direction of meaning is clear but exact wording remains less constrained; these are restored in the oldest impersonal register, avoiding later pastoral or explicitly Christian phrasing.

---

## Chapter 18: [Concerning the Five] War[s that the] Sons of [Li]ght waged with the Sons] of Darkness.

**¶2** The first war is that of the First living Man, which he waged against the King of the realms of Darkness, and all the rulers that had come forth from the five worlds of [Darkness]. He hunted them with his net, which is the living soul. He [bound them] in the snare and [sealed them] the face of the [Virgin of Light] his virgin, the living fire [shone forth, so that] they did not find how to escape his net [because] he caught them like fish [in the sea of smoke] the five counsels of the five elements [and seized]. He cut and dug out the root of the five trees, [and (struck) their first beginning] root of the evil tree, like an axe.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The five worlds are the canonical five realms of Darkness in the archaic cosmogony; the spiritual context speaks of “fundamental falsities” arising from a full sufficiency, i.e., the fivefold dark domains.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* After “He hunted them with his net,” the next required action is enclosure: the net as living soul is the inward faculty that binds and limits hellish reasonings.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Parallel to binding: the snare is made effective by sealing, i.e., closing their outlets so they cannot break out into freedom (spiritual sense: fixed limitation).
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* “the face of the … his virgin” in the oldest layer is the pure witness of uncorrupted love against Darkness: the Virgin of Light, not a later devotional figure.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The living fire is the immediate manifestation of pure love; it “shines forth” as witness, and the consequence is their inability to find escape.
> *¶2 #6 [tentative]:* Causal connector: their failure to escape follows from the manifestation and constraint of the living net.
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* Fish-imagery regularly pairs with a dark ‘sea’ in this substrate; spiritually it is the undiscerned mass of falsities (“reasonings” as a sea).
> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* The sentence turns from simile (“like fish”) to the capturing of the five counsels; “seized” matches the spiritual sense: “the whole sufficiency of their contrivances… was seized and held.”
> *¶2 #9 [tentative]:* After capturing the counsels, the text moves to uprooting: “cut and dug out the root…”; the demanded bridge is an explicit turn to origin or beginning (the source-principle).

**¶3** The second war is that of the Living Spirit [which he waged against the rulers and the firmaments]. He has constructed the things and the worlds of light; he spread out the rulers and chained them in the middle [of the firmament] the stars in [all] the earths [and he set] them in the three vessels. For the rulers rebelled and were set against the Living Spirit. They wished to [flee] since they knew and realised that he might bind and fetter them with a strong chain. Yet he, the Living Spirit, (appeared to them) with many aspects, with numerous [powers], with many hands and great cruelties. He was revealed to them, he imprisoned them with much skill. Not one of them was able to save (himself), and he {...} [did not prevail]. Now, since he [all of them] he trapped them [had foreseen (their) rebellion] ⟨p.59⟩ [by their own devices].

> *¶3 #1 [tentative]:* The Living Spirit’s war concerns ordering and binding the rebel powers in an intermediate arrangement (firmaments, structures). This keeps the register cosmological rather than personal.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* “chained them in the middle … the stars” requires the intermediate region: the firmament as middle placement between higher light and lower mixture.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* “the stars in … the earths” calls for totality: the ordering fixes all influences across the arranged knowledges and ultimates.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Governance verb demanded before “them in the three vessels”: he “set” or “placed” them within the complete three-degree containment.
> *¶3 #5 [strong]:* The rebels “wished to … since they knew… he might bind”; the required action is attempted escape from restraint.
> *¶3 #6 [strong]:* “with numerous …” in this register is a fullness of ordered forces or powers, matching the spiritual sense “fullness of ordered powers.”
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* After “Not one… was able to save (himself),” the next clause must negate their evasions; “did not prevail” fits the spiritual sense that attempts were brought to nothing.
> *¶3 #8 [moderate]:* Collective subject resumes: not one saved itself, and “all of them” were nullified and imprisoned.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation: “because their resistance was foreseen and met”; thus the substrate clause is foreseeing their rebellion.
> *¶3 #10 [moderate]:* Correspondentially they are taken in their own contrivances; this is the fitting closure after “he trapped them …”.

**¶4** The third war is that of the Third Ambassador who has displayed his image [in the height] what is below [and he gathered up] scooped [and he] all light and the property [of the rulers] the ones that are consumed [from] the entire ruling-power [and he uncovered] pride of the light [and the] extending it [over the whole]. A person comes, who is powerful [against it]; he takes his riches [and he stripped him], silver [gold], his pride and wantonness.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador “displayed his image” in the height so it can work downward (“what is below”); spiritual sense: visible operative mediation.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The motion is reclamation: drawing up scattered light; “gathered up” matches scooping and collecting in the next phrases.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* Small connective restoring continuous agency of the Ambassador through the sequence of actions.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* “the property … the ones that are consumed” is property stolen by the rulers and consumed in mixture; spiritual sense: carried-off spiritual qualities.
> *¶4 #5 [strong]:* Required preposition: the light and property are reclaimed “from” the devouring ones.
> *¶4 #6 [strong]:* Spiritual sense: “laid bare the whole pretended dominion”; thus “uncovered” is demanded.
> *¶4 #7 [strong]:* Connects “entire ruling-power” with “pride…”; simple syntactic restoration.
> *¶4 #8 [moderate]:* “extending it …” demands a scope phrase; spiritual sense: stretched its influence outward.
> *¶4 #9 [moderate]:* The powerful person comes “against” the ruling-power to strip it; matches spiritual influx opposing dominion.
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* Corresponds to taking away boastful possessions; “takes his riches…” is the stripping action.
> *¶4 #11 [strong]:* Triad “gold, silver” is conventional for apparent goods and truths in ultimates; the text already has “silver …”, so “gold” is the missing first item.

**¶5** This is what the ruling-power is like [: it is like a thief], all riches [that it takes] as they travel [through the paths] light [by borrowed]. He cleansed and purified it.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The ruling-power is described by analogy to one who claims others’ goods while passing through; “thief” corresponds to appropriating appearances of truth and good.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Explains its mode: it claims riches as its own by taking; matches spiritual sense “claims every abundance as if it were its own.”
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* “as they travel … light …” needs a mental topography; “paths” corresponds to ways of thought through which appearances move.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: it “borrows appearances of truth and good as it passes”; thus “by borrowed” introduces those appearances.

**¶6** The fourth war is this that Jesus the glorious one waged against all the rebels who had rebelled above and below. When the light was purified from them by the image of the Third Ambassador, their chains loosened [and they rose up]. The glorious one in the fourth war; he came and brought [and he restrained] a rebellion of the entire ruling-power. He girded himself [with the armour of truth] all of it another time. He constructed the whole structure and he made [and he bound] a great contest; he humiliated therein. [it firm] he fulfilled the will of the greatness, [he gave the] hope to Adam [the remnant] to his light ship.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* After purification, the loosened chains lead to renewed uprising; spiritual sense: disorder sought to return.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Jesus the glorious meets rebellion and restrains it by re-binding order; “restrained” matches binding without importing later pastoral tone.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* “He girded himself … all of it” in the oldest register is arming with ordered truth; correspondentially armour = defensive truths.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* He girds himself and then binds the ruling-power again; required action to match “all of it another time.”
> *¶6 #5 [tentative]:* Completes “made … a great contest”: he made it firm, i.e., established the structure securely before the contest.
> *¶6 #6 [strong]:* Accepted as fitting: the act is not devotional but structural—granting a state of expectation and preservation to the first human form.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* “to his light ship” requires the object preserved and carried: the remnant of light (remaining good and truth capable of elevation).

**¶7** The fifth war is this that the counsel of life [waged against the persuasion] which is the summons and the obedience [and the great counsel] great [its power] and it placed [its seal] upon the [earth] and the [depth] ⟨p.60⟩ [the bonds] of death, which [clung] with it. And it was victorious over [the persuasion] for ever, and it gave the kingdom [of life and of light].

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The fifth war targets a specific opposite: the persuasive power of death-falsity; fits spiritual sense “persuasion of spiritual death.”
> *¶7 #2 [tentative]:* Balances “counsel of life … which is the summons and the obedience”; “great counsel” is the inflowing directive of order.
