> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* “great … and it placed” needs the noun: great power or force entering ultimates.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondentially placing a seal is setting fixed opposition and boundary in ultimates; matches “set firm opposition.”
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* The counsel enters the ultimate degree; “earth” is the natural ultimate in this register.
> *¶7 #6 [tentative]:* Pairs with earth as its lowest extension; “depth” corresponds to the lowest natural level where death-persuasion clings.
> *¶7 #7 [moderate]:* “of death, which … with it” needs what death has: bonds or fetters that cling; corresponds to persuasion fastening itself to lower life.
> *¶7 #8 [strong]:* Verb required: death’s bonds “clung with it” to the lower degree; matches spiritual translation.
> *¶7 #9 [moderate]:* Object of victory: not merely persons but the persuasive potency of death-falsity.
> *¶7 #10 [moderate]:* The conclusion “gave the kingdom …” in the oldest substrate is the reign of ordered life and light, not ecclesial language.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored to a coherent cosmological sequence because the chapter is a fivefold correspondential schema of restraint, ordering, reclamation, re-binding, and final fixation in ultimates. Exact wording at several points (especially ¶3–¶5 connectors and some locatives like “sea of smoke,” “paths,” “depth”) is less tight because the natural text is highly formulaic and could admit multiple equivalent Coptic renderings; however the demanded functions—binding, placing in the middle firmament, uncovering the pretended dominion, stripping its boast, sealing opposition in the ultimate—constrain the restorations strongly in sense and register.

---

## Chapter 19: Concerning the Five Releases: what they are

**¶2** [The first loo]sening and saving is that of [the] First Man, since when the First M[a]n was freed fro[m the prison of] the rulers by the Living Spirit, who had come [to the rescue] of the First Man, much light and [much] power was saved from the entire ruling-power. They ascended with the First Man to the aeons [of] greatn[ess]. This [is the first] release.

> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The Living Spirit’s descent is not devotional but functional: an ordering power ‘comes’ to draw out and raise light. In the oldest register this is consistently described as coming ‘to the rescue’ or ‘for the release’ of the First Man, i.e., for the extraction of light from the rulers’ prison.

**¶3** The second saving is that of the Liv[ing] Spirit, since whe[n] the Father of Life and [the] Mother of [Li]fe constructed [the aeons and the firmaments] and [w]orlds [and]rlds, the earths and their [rulers; im]measurable light was saved f[r]om them [with the First] Man, who w[a]s devoured [and his light was gathered up] lig[ht ...

> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* What the Father of Life and Mother of Life ‘constructed’ in the substrate is the graded framework: aeons (inner degrees) and firmaments (outer fixed boundaries). This matches the spiritual sense: ordered framework of degrees and outward extensions.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* The sequence demands a connective: ‘and worlds … worlds, the earths…’. The cosmological list is being resumed, not introduced anew.
> *¶3 #10 [moderate]:* The saving ‘from them’ is the recovery of light that had been seized in connection with the First Man’s mixture. The sentence needs a phrase linking the rescued light to him, as in the spiritual translation.
> *¶3 #12 [tentative]:* After ‘devoured’ the expected oldest-layer movement is not moral lament but extraction: the devoured light is separated, collected, and withheld from further mixture. This supplies the missing predicate for the broken clause ‘lig[ht …]’.

**¶4** ⟨p.61⟩
... ... th]ree wars [and he completed] to the time when he [after] above [set himself] them upon the [the whole] universe.

> *¶4 #1 [tentative]:* The fragment speaks of a series brought to completion. In the substrate, wars are ‘completed’ when the ordering power has fixed the bounds above the rebels.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Bridges from ‘three wars’ to a terminal time clause: ‘after three wars … to the time when…’.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* The spiritual sense requires the victorious power being ‘established’ above them; ‘set himself’ (i.e., took his station) matches the cosmological idiom.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The close reads ‘upon the … universe’; ‘the whole universe’ is the required object in this register, marking totality of the ordered system.

**¶5** [The third red]emption is that of the Thi[rd Ambassador, since when] displayed [his image in the zones,] he [before the r]uling-power; he [and he drew out the light] from them [and he established it above] So this is [how] the [he displayed] his image that [is established] in the Th[ir]d Ambassador.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The paragraph is an explanatory ‘since when’ clause, paralleling ¶2-3 (‘since when …’). The Third Ambassador’s action is not exhortation but manifestation and separation.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador ‘displays his image’ as an accommodated manifestation in intermediate regions (‘zones’) to confront the rulers, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘form accommodated to those in lower states’.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Standard cosmogonic formula: the image is displayed ‘before’ the ruling power for judgment and separation.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador’s function in the oldest layer is extraction by manifested form: drawing out what can be rescued from the rulers’ grasp.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* What is drawn out is not merely removed but set in a higher placement, i.e., re-established in a new reception above the rulers.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* The summary sentence points back to the operative act: the displayed image is the means of the rescue.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* The image is said to ‘be’ or ‘stand’ in the Third Ambassador; ‘is established’ keeps the impersonal, structural tone.

**¶6** [Th]e fourth s[av]ing is that of Jesus the Splendour, since whe[n he was] re[ve]aled in the zone he displayed [his im]age in front of the firmaments and purified [the light] that is above. He established the first righteous o[ne in the midst] . He took the likeness [of the powers,] he made himself like the angels in [the firmaments] un[til] he travelled and descended to the form of flesh. He set in order [t]he earths and all the fastenings. He also loosen[ed the bonds, and he released] light without measure in the entire structure. He gave [the] summons and the obedience to the elements, he formed [J]esus the Youth. He as[ce]n[d]ed and rested himself in the light [land].

> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* Jesus the Splendour ‘establishes the first righteous one’ as a stable form of right order in the intermediate domain (‘in the midst’), matching the spiritual sense: first form of genuine justice instituted there.
> *¶6 #8 [tentative]:* He takes a likeness ‘of the powers’—the celestial beings/forces—before becoming like angels and then descending to flesh; this preserves the graded descent logic.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* He ‘made himself like the angels in …’ naturally takes ‘in the firmaments’ (the fixed outer structures) already mentioned earlier in the paragraph.
> *¶6 #12 [moderate]:* After ‘fastenings’ the oldest-layer move is loosening bonds and releasing light. This matches the spiritual translation’s ‘restored what had been bound, and released measureless wisdom’.

**¶7** The fifth release and saving is that of the great counsel, w[hich is the s]umm[ons an]d the obedience, since it also [draws up] the soul from the [land] of greatness.

> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* The great counsel (summons and obedience) functions as the call-and-response by which what is lower is raised into order; hence it ‘draws up’ the soul in a non-devotional, mechanical sense.

**¶8** ⟨p.62⟩
[Again] the Living Spirit [by] as he gave [also] the release [to each of the powers]
The first [stage, since when] ascended and he [was strengthened] as he [received] wisdom [and he set it in order]
The fourth [in the structures of the firmaments.] its wisdom [stage, it established]
And also, the fifth [and it sealed] into it, and it sets it in order [until it bears] its light image in this Last Statue.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* A resumptive marker fits: after the five savings are listed, the text returns to invoke the Living Spirit’s giving of release in stages.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* Links ‘the Living Spirit’ to the manner clause ‘as he gave…’.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Keeps the additive logic: the Living Spirit is invoked again ‘also’ as giver of release beyond earlier mention.
> *¶8 #4 [tentative]:* The release is distributed by degree: each power receives its proper operation. This matches the spiritual translation’s ‘successive stages’ gathered into ultimate formation.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* The numbered sequence begins (‘The first…’). A causal/temporal hinge ‘since when’ matches earlier style and introduces the ascent action.
> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ascent and strengthening through enlightened understanding; thus the first stage includes being ‘strengthened’.
> *¶8 #7 [moderate]:* He ‘received … wisdom’ is the needed verb for the fragment ‘as he … wisdom …’.
> *¶8 #8 [tentative]:* The sequence moves from reception of wisdom to ordering forms, matching the translation’s ‘further dispositions of wisdom in ordered forms’.
> *¶8 #9 [tentative]:* Wisdom is disposed into ‘structures’ at the level of firmaments (fixed ultimates of a heaven). This aligns with the text’s focus on firmaments, fastenings, and ordered framework.
> *¶8 #10 [tentative]:* Bridges to ‘The fourth … its wisdom …’: a fourth stage that ‘establishes’ wisdom in a fixed form.
> *¶8 #11 [tentative]:* The fourth stage commonly involves fixing and sealing boundaries (a correspondential ‘firming’). ‘Sealed’ suits the impersonal cosmic ordering.
> *¶8 #12 [moderate]:* The final clause: entering the ultimate recipient so that it ‘bears’ the light-image in the Last Statue. This gives the needed teleology.

**¶9** These five grea[t] powe[rs ...] were revealed. They came to a profit [in the five releases] they were established upon [and in the five saviours] First Man [and] when he came toward [their foundations;] from the struggle. He set in order and constructed [so that] universe [the whole] the First M[an, at] his advent.

> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Defines the ‘five great powers’ by their operation: they are manifested ‘in the five releases’, matching the chapter’s structure.
> *¶9 #4 [tentative]:* In the substrate these are operative agents (not moral teachers). ‘Saviours’ here means deliverers of light by function; it pairs naturally with ‘releases’.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* Connective needed: ‘They came to a profit … they were established…’.
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* They are ‘established upon’ something; ‘their foundations’ keeps the architectural-cosmic register.
> *¶9 #7 [strong]:* Introduces the purpose clause: establishment enables the First Man’s ordering of the universe.
> *¶9 #8 [strong]:* Before ‘universe’ the expected quantifier is ‘the whole’, matching earlier totalizing idiom.
> *¶9 #9 [moderate]:* Completes ‘First M[an ...] his advent’: ‘First Man, at his advent’ is the natural phrase for the moment of return and ordering after struggle.

**¶10** In [t]his way [all the powers,] they st[an]d with [their places.]

> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The clause ‘they stand with …’ requires the companions: the established powers standing together in the ordered system.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* The point is stability-by-order: each power fixed ‘in their places’, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘fixed in their functions’.

**¶11** ⟨p.63⟩
... because of the light [th]at had rushed from [Jesus] the Amba[s]sa[d]or.

> *¶11 #2 [tentative]:* The light ‘rushed from … the Ambassador’ points to the mediating descent. In this chapter’s sequence the mediating figure tied to the Ambassador’s work is Jesus the Splendour as revealed in the zone; naming him here keeps the causal chain of released light pressing onward.

**Assessment:** Most mid-word lacunae are mechanically secure. Larger gaps in ¶3–¶5 and ¶8 are only moderately constrained: the correspondential narrative requires a fixed sequence of (1) descent for rescue, (2) construction of aeons firmaments worlds and extraction of devoured light, (3) successive wars ending in establishment above the rebels, (4) Third Ambassador’s accommodated image drawing out light, (5) Jesus the Splendour’s descent to ultimates and release of measureless light, and (6) a staged ordering culminating in the Last Statue. Exact phrasing for the broken stage-list in ¶8 and the war-summary in ¶4 remains tentative, but the restorations preserve the oldest-layer, impersonal cosmological register and the inside-to-outside logic of ordering and fixation.

---

## Chapter 20: The Chapter of the Name of the Fathers

**¶3** Now, the Father, who is the first established thing, shall be called ‘the Father of Greatness’. His greatness is the great earth where he lives, he being established in it [as the great earth, and his root is in it; and] the essence of the light that swathes all the rich gods and the angels and the dwellings [of this greatness; and the boundary of the light] is set over it.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The Father’s ‘greatness’ is the ultimate plane of establishment (earth). Correspondentially, ‘root’ expresses first fixation and stability in ultimates (Love made present and operative). The clause prepares the shift from the plane (earth) to what proceeds within it (the light-essence that swathes and governs).
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The text moves from the Father’s established plane (great earth) to the proceeding wisdom-light that surrounds (‘swathes’) goods and truths (‘rich gods’), angelic states, and ordered abodes (‘dwellings’). Calling this light the ‘boundary’ matches the spiritual sense: the irradiating wisdom that encompasses and defines the whole abundance, and therefore can be said to be ‘set over it’ in governance.

**¶4** Furthermore, they shall call the Third Ambassador ‘father’. His greatness is the light ship of living fire wherein he lives, he being established in it.

**¶5** Once again, Jesus the Splendour shall himself also be called ‘father’. His greatness is the ship of living waters where he lives, he being established in it.

**Assessment:** Both lacunae are restorably constrained by the correspondential structure: (1) a clause must complete the idea of stability of the Father in the ‘earth’ by a fixation term (‘root’), and (2) a genitive bridge must connect ‘dwellings’ to ‘is set over it’ by naming the encompassing wisdom-light as the delimiting boundary of the Father’s greatness. Exact wording is not fully determinable, but the demanded concepts are tight.

---

## Chapter 21: [C]oncerning the Father of Gr[eat]ness: [ho]w he is established and determined.

**¶2** Very great is [the land of] light in the presence of they who will hear and know it, due to the first Father of Greatness. How he ex[ists and is] established in his five light limbs, which are [the great store]houses that have neither limit nor measure to the[them;] mind, thought, insight, counsel and [consideration; and also] his twelve light limbs that are his twelve wisdoms.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The natural phrase must name the spiritual state described: an immeasurable domain of Light proceeding from the First Father. “Land of Light” is the substrate’s cosmological locus, not a metaphor.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The five light-limbs are immediately defined as limitless interior “storehouses” containing the five inner capacities; thus the gap must introduce “the (great) storehouses” as the containing interiors within the limbs.
> *¶2 #4 [strong]:* The syntax requires a close: “that have neither limit nor measure to them; mind, thought…” matching the spiritual sense “without boundary or measure.”
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The five inner faculties culminate with an attentive/considering term; then the discourse turns to the twelve limbs as twelve wisdoms. The bridge “and also” matches the correspondential shift from five interiors to twelve forms of wisdom.

**¶3** Now, there are five great light lim[bs] in each one [of his five light limbs,] five grea[t] springs of praise gush for[th] five great [powers, and] lig[ight]

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense demands recursion: “within each… there is again a sufficient fullness.” Thus the text repeats the five within each one, not new entities.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* “gush forth” is fixed by the spring-correspondence of proceeding acknowledgment; only the final letters are missing.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* What follows is a listing of proceedings (springs, light). The missing noun should be the interior “powers” of the limbs, coordinating the praise-springs with the outflow of Light.
> *¶3 #6 [strong]:* Completes “light” in the description of the five great outflowings.

**¶4** The first is his light that enlig[htens all] it gushed forth from him and pour[ed into] a[l]] aeons.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The first proceeding in the spiritual sense is illumination that gives sight and distinction; thus “enlightens all” (aeons, regions) is required.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The flow is from the Father into the totality of aeons; “poured into” expresses influx into recipients as in the spiritual translation.

**¶5** The second is the perfume of the [sweet perfume] and pervades all the aeons of greatness.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The second proceeding is “delight of love” that pervades; in the substrate register this is rendered as “perfume” characterized as sweet/pleasant, matching penetration through all aeons.

**¶6** The third is his living voice of [praise, which is heard in] all his aeons [together.]

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The third proceeding is the living voice as communication that makes interior truth “audible” across the divine domain; “voice of praise… heard in” preserves the cosmological, non-moral register.
> *¶6 #2 [tentative]:* The spiritual sense ends with cohesion: “held together in one understanding.” The natural layer often closes such clauses with a unitive verb; “together” matches that function.

**¶7** The fourth is his [steadfast]-ness [power] gushes forth from him [out]ut [to the outer aeons.]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The fourth proceeding is firmness and steadiness in good going forth as strength; “steadfast-ness” matches the required quality.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* What gushes forth here is strength-for-use that orders lower degrees; “power” is the natural correspondent of firmness proceeding outward.
> *¶7 #3 [strong]:* The described motion is extension outward into lower recipients; the preserved “o” points to “out.”
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the fourth proceeding extends to lower degrees to hold them in order; “outer aeons” keeps the impersonal cosmological stratification.

**¶8** ⟨p.65⟩ The fif[th] is his great glorio[us glory] that [revealed, which]hich [manifested]nifested all the tim[e], whi[ch continues]ues [without end and holds] all of them. It was sculpted in them [together] sealed in the [minds,] sealed [again] as they send [thereafter] in [forth to the lower ones,] they too receive nourishment [from it,] immediately [from his] living voi[ce;] joy [they receive] set up anew. It shall gush forth from it [great] they shall [and their aeons are] and become rich in the [set up anew] too is beautiful [always;] portray[al portrayal] adornment [as an] and they live [in the living order] for ev[r]r.

> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The fifth is explicitly the Divine glory in full manifestation; the surviving letters require “glorious,” and the spiritual sense anchors it as “great glorious glory.”
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* The clause is about eternal manifestation; “revealed” fits the spiritual “in revelation from eternity,” and “which” carries on the relative description.
> *¶8 #7 [moderate]:* Matches “continues without end, holding all things together.” The natural layer typically states continuance then the binding function.
> *¶8 #8 [strong]:* Direct correspondent to the spiritual “holding all things together.”
> *¶8 #9 [moderate]:* What is “sealed in them” in the substrate is the inmost acknowledgement and fixed form within recipients; “minds” is the closest natural organ for that interior fixation in this register.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* The sequence is fixation and permanence; “sealed… again sealed…” expresses repeated confirmation of form in the recipients.
> *¶8 #11 [tentative]:* Introduces the consequence: as they transmit downward, they are sustained. A temporal connective (“thereafter”) is required.
> *¶8 #12 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense: “as they transmit what they receive into lower degrees.” The natural layer needs an explicit direction-of-influx phrase.
> *¶8 #13 [moderate]:* The nourishment and renewal come from the glory that is sealed in them; “from it” ties the sustaining back to the fifth proceeding.
> *¶8 #15 [strong]:* Completes “voice,” then pauses before the effects (joy, renewal).
> *¶8 #16 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense emphasizes immediate reception leading to gladness; thus the natural text must state reception before joy.
> *¶8 #17 [moderate]:* The joy is not moral exhortation but a state-change; “great joy” fits the fixed collocation and spiritual “immediately made glad.”
> *¶8 #18 [moderate]:* The renewal is of state-order in the heavens; “aeons” are the recipients whose condition is restored.
> *¶8 #20 [moderate]:* Corresponds to repeated restoration “again and again” and the unceasing character of the outflow.
> *¶8 #21 [moderate]:* The passage turns to beauty and form: “beautiful… portrayal… adornment.” “Portrayal” names the formed presentation of wisdom in ultimates.
> *¶8 #22 [moderate]:* Introduces the appositive: portrayal as adornment, matching the spiritual “presentation becomes an adornment of life.”
> *¶8 #23 [moderate]:* The closing must express enduring orderly life, not moral perseverance; “living order” corresponds to heaven’s fixed correspondence-structure.
> *¶8 #24 [strong]:* Completes “ever.”

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the chapter’s fixed fivefold proceeding (illumination, sweetness, living voice, firmness, glory) and by the repeated correspondence of “springs gushing forth” as the mode of procession. A few short connectors (¶6 #2, ¶8 #10–11) remain less tightly bound because multiple Coptic-English connectives could serve the same spiritual function; these were restored to preserve the substrate’s impersonal cosmological register and the inside-to-outside order of influx.

---

## Chapter 22: [On] the Land of Light

**¶1** ⟨p.65⟩
[The Father established all things in] the Land of Light.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Opening needs a cosmological subject that can be implied in ¶6 (“came from the Father”) and leads naturally into “the Land of Light.” Old substrate prefers impersonal cosmogonic statement of establishment rather than exhortation.

**¶2** [I was] in the great land of greatness [manner]s I will reveal to you [the things that are set] above.

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* Gives a simple subject for the self-report style that survives (“as I will reveal to you”), without importing later dialogic frame.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* Completes “great land of greatness … as I will reveal to you” with a disclosure-term. The following “above” suggests a promised exposition of the mode or order.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* Balances “reveal to you” and anticipates “above” (interior, prior). Keeps structural register: things set or established in the higher degree.

**¶3** [sur]ace is [the] living [oceeding]om [the Father] upon him.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Produces “surface” as the natural term that corresponds to outward perception. The fragment “[ ... f]ace” strongly constrains to “face,” but “surface” better fits correspondential move to what shines forth.
> *¶3 #2 [minimal]:* Needed to construe the clause: “surface is … living …”.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Restores “proceeding” to match the spiritual sense “presence that proceeds from the Divine.”
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The agent “upon him” requires a source; ¶6 explicitly names “the Father.” Oldest layer uses “Father” as principle of Light rather than Christian person-title.

**¶4** [From the] light and [his] face [goes] out; he has [sur]ace of the [greatness] in the [aeons] now fi[rays] of prais[se] in the [place] living [am]bros[i]a [they are in the] five [powers] compound [together] they become an image [so that] the li[i]ing air [and the breath within it is] also living.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the outflow described: “From … light and … face … out”.
> *¶4 #2 [minimal]:* Links “light” and “face” to the same subject implied in ¶3–4.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* Fits “face … out; he has …” with an emanation verb consistent with influx-outflow logic.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Again yields “surface/face,” keeping the same term-pattern as ¶3. The repetition suggests a technical term in the substrate.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* “face of the … in the …” is best as “of the greatness,” echoing “land of greatness” and “aeons of greatness.”
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* Provides the receptacle/location: “in the aeons,” matching ¶8 explicitly (“aeons of greatness dwell”).
> *¶4 #7 [tentative]:* “now five … of praise” suggests fivefold powers as rays or lights; “rays” coheres with ‘light’ and later ‘image’.
> *¶4 #8 [strong]:* Completes “praise” to “praise” with visible letters; minimal restoration.
> *¶4 #9 [tentative]:* “in the … living ambrosia” needs a container-term: place/region. Keeps cosmological geography register.
> *¶4 #12 [tentative]:* Bridges “living ambrosia … five … compound …”: locates the five within the delight-substance; maintains impersonal descriptive tone.
> *¶4 #13 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation identifies “a sufficient series of consecrated powers.” “Five powers” is the tightest old-layer term without later ecclesial language.
> *¶4 #14 [moderate]:* Makes “five … compound … they become an image” coherent: the five are compounded together.
> *¶4 #15 [moderate]:* Connects compounding to result: “so that they become an image …”.
> *¶4 #16 [strong]:* Produces “living” with the fixed l + i + v + ing letters around it.
> *¶4 #17 [moderate]:* Prepares “the living air … also living” by specifying the inner breath as the living air. Correspondentially: breath as life proceeding within the formed image.

**¶5** [As] those five shall [be purified] from all the contests of the greatness [and are delivered] and a beautiful [unity is formed] to the limbs of their body [h]hich exists [as an image of the great Man] the land of light; their [in]]ife.

> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* The spiritual sense speaks of being brought through opposition/contests; the old-layer natural expression is purification/refinement through struggle.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* Matches “from all the contests,” giving the completion of the process before unity/beauty is mentioned.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Supplies what “a beautiful …” points to in the spiritual rendering (“beautiful unity”).
> *¶5 #5 [strong]:* Completes “which” in “[ ... w]hich”.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* “to the limbs of their body … which exists … land of light” strongly suggests the Primal Man archetype. This is substrate-cosmic anthropology, not moral exhortation.
> *¶5 #7 [minimal]:* Makes “exists in the land of light;” coherent.
> *¶5 #8 [tentative]:* Completes “their … life” with a dependence statement consistent with ¶6 (“nothing … from itself”).

**¶6** Just as all the gods and the rich ones and the [angels w]ho were summoned came from the Father, so also all [moun]tains [and all] springs and trees [that are set in their places] gush forth [from him] great is a [wonder] every thing that you [see] neither water nor [fire]

> *¶6 #3 [tentative]:* Balances the series “mountains … springs and trees …” with an inclusive coordinator.
> *¶6 #4 [tentative]:* Makes the cosmological list structural rather than scenic: elevations, springs, trees as ordered states/uses.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Matches the repeated doctrine of procession: all gush forth from the Father/source.
> *¶6 #6 [tentative]:* “great is a … every thing” needs a noun like wonder/marvel; keeps impersonal admiration of order.
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* Completes “everything that you … neither water nor …” as an epistemic claim: “that you see”.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with “water” as complementary elements; correspondentially understanding and love, or truth and good in ultimates.

**¶7** These ar[e] which [are established in their measures]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Completes “These ar[e … ]” to “are”.
> *¶7 #2 [tentative]:* Supplies the missing predicate: ordered realities fixed by measure, matching the spiritual translation’s “ordered realities… series”.

**¶8** The [third]-th [degree of the] great light, the living air [is the] in which the aeons of greatness dwell, being established therein. [ministering]el[] that [stand] and the [treasures of] the gi[gi] of the great Father [which are given] in the [aeons of light]

> *¶8 #1 [tentative]:* A “-th” degree is referenced; “third” is a plausible sequence marker without forcing later systematics. Confidence limited because numbering is not preserved.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* Completes “The …-th … great light,” with “degree” matching spiritual rendering (“further degree”).
> *¶8 #3 [strong]:* Grammatical bridge to “great light, the living air …”.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Restores the function of angels in this layer: ministering powers carrying out ordered uses, not devotional guardians.
> *¶8 #5 [minimal]:* The plural is already supplied by the surrounding “angel[s]”; no extra letter required beyond the editor’s bracket, but the listed lacuna requires an entry. Best is to supply nothing substantive.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* Fits “angel[s] that … and the …”: they stand or are stationed in that atmosphere as established forms.
> *¶8 #7 [tentative]:* Prepares “the gifts of the great Father” with a repository-term used in cosmogonic descriptions: treasures, storehouses.
> *¶8 #8 [strong]:* Completes “gifts” with fixed letters.
> *¶8 #9 [tentative]:* Connects gifts to reception/distribution implied by the spiritual translation.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Closes “in the …” with the realm-name appropriate to this degree; maintains parallel with “aeons of greatness” and “Land of Light.”

**¶9** Blessed is whoever will k[w these things]

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Completes “k[now ...” as “know these things,” aligning with the concluding beatitude in the spiritual translation.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of cosmological logic: procession from the Father, fivefold powers forming a single image, life as breath within the formed image, and the doctrine that nothing lives from itself. Exact phrasing and any original ordinal numbering in ¶8 remain the loosest points; several small connectors are necessarily tentative because the Coptic-English surface syntax is too broken to constrain them more tightly.

## Chapter 23: [ ... ] which [ ... ]

**¶2** What the essence of [the Darkness]—no person could recount or unveil [or say how it] exists [which exists from ever and remains for ever]—all [its powers] and [its rulers] unveil [its measure] [in] eternity [in] great [Darkness] again and the [limits] tend [upon] the beginning of eternity and the end [of its permission] set that one down.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The opening defines the contrary, unorderly principle whose essence cannot be recounted; in the oldest cosmogonic register this is ‘the Darkness’ (not a moral abstraction).
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires an ‘enduring as a state’ rather than historical beginning; the oldest layer typically states ‘from ever’ and ‘for ever’.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* ‘All … and … unveil …’ calls for paired collectives; the substrate regularly speaks of the ‘powers’ belonging to each kingdom.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* In the darkness-world, ‘powers’ are often coupled with ‘rulers’ or ‘archons’ as governing forms of evil.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* What is unveiled is the limit or permission-set boundary of its duration; ‘measure’ fits the spiritual explanation of bounded permission.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* Required to link to ‘eternity’ as a locative phrase in the Coptic-English idiom: ‘in eternity’.
> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* Second locative linking of the clause chain (‘… in eternity …’).
> *¶2 #9 [strong]:* ‘… eternity … great …’ points to the ‘great Darkness’ as a fixed technical phrase in the oldest mythic register.
> *¶2 #10 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation speaks of ‘fixed limits of its permission’; ‘limits’ best matches ‘the beginning … and the end … set that one down’.
> *¶2 #11 [tentative]:* Needed to govern ‘tend … the beginning … and the end …’.
> *¶2 #12 [moderate]:* Completes the thought: ‘the beginning of eternity and the end … set that one down’ = not a true beginning, but a permitted boundary.

**¶3** Now, my [brother] the land of death [is called] nature of the essence [is the] which exists [e]ver and ever [an accord] in [enmity] with one another.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Vocative address in the stripped layer remains; ‘brother’ is the neutral, non-institutional term that fits the impersonal teaching voice better than ‘disciple’ or ecclesial titles.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The sentence explains why ‘the land of death’ is named; ‘is called’ is demanded by the spiritual paraphrase (‘This is why … is named’).
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Bridges to ‘nature of the essence …’ describing what that land is in itself.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: conjunction/accord of evil with falsity; ‘exists … in … with one another’ demands an ‘accord’ term.
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* That ‘accord’ is hostile; the substrate often marks infernal conjunction as ‘in enmity’ even while ‘with one another’.

**¶4** As for the eternity of [the two natures] which exists from ever, no person is able to [understand] how they exist. For no other power [is manifes]ted to them, in that it might speak or recount about their origin, how [e]xist. However, from the time when it stirred itself and rose up against the light [desiring that] it might come and rule over the land of the living.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The clause ‘eternity of … which exists from ever’ in this mythology commonly treats the two opposed roots as ‘two natures’.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: it wanted not only to exist, but ‘to come and rule’; ‘desiring that’ fits the transition from uprising to intent.

**¶5** ⟨p.70⟩
[The First]rst Man came forth against it and prevented it, [he kept] it apart from the house of the living. He prevented it. From that time, when the light was mixed with [the] darkness, and life [wi]th death, there was a measure of approval and an ability for [the] first-born [and the a]postle[s] to teach about it. They speak and unveil how the reproach and the fight did hap[pen], [how] the light [was] entwined with the darkness, which they [bound] life in death.

> *¶5 #11 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation: ‘living good was held as if imprisoned within what is dead’; ‘bound’ is the oldest-layer way to state that life is confined in death.

**¶6** [And after it] stirred itself in its essence, its th[ought and desire] in its own land. It [turned back upon itself] the entire land of darkness in its [gather up] of light in the abyss. For ⟨p.68⟩ [depth] essence in [i]t [was no light] because a [reason] they reckoned [as strength] on its side [firmness] this first nature [confirmed] it, because its body is made strong [harder] than the Adamant [stone] every [resistance] as it exists [se]se.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Restores the narrative continuation from the uprising in ¶4 to the self-turning described in the spiritual sense.
> *¶6 #2 [tentative]:* The text speaks of its inner ‘thought … in its own land’; the spiritual sense gives ‘turned its intention wholly within its own domain’. ‘Thought and desire’ matches that duality in the oldest register.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* Corresponds to ‘turned its intention wholly within its own domain’.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: to swallow the whole darkness-domain into one; ‘gather up’ is the structural verb for consolidation of evil into a single will.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Pairs with ‘abyss’ and ‘cast … light in the abyss’; ‘depth’ keeps the cosmological register.
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* The line ‘For … essence in it …’ requires that within that essence there is absence of light as an explanatory premise.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* Introduces the causal clause ‘because a … they reckoned …’; ‘reason’ fits the didactic, impersonal exposition.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘evil was taken for strength’; the reckoning inside the darkness counts evil as power.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘falsity for firmness’; complements ‘as strength’.
> *¶6 #11 [moderate]:* ‘this first nature … it’ calls for a verb of establishing/confirming its first formed nature by persuasion.
> *¶6 #12 [strong]:* The comparison ‘more than the Adamant’ requires an adjective of hardness/firmness; matches ‘as though it were harder than any resistance’.
> *¶6 #13 [tentative]:* ‘the Adamant …’ is treated as a superlative stone/metal of resistance; adding ‘stone’ keeps the cosmological-natural comparison.
> *¶6 #14 [moderate]:* ‘… every … as it exists in the universe’ calls for a universal comparator; ‘resistance’ matches the spiritual sense ‘any resistance found in created order’.
> *¶6 #15 [strong]:* Completes ‘universe’.

**¶7** [The will] of death [there arose] envy, which [a delight of] the land of [darkness and its consideration]deration in [its] wickedness [justification of] for as it [rushed] the wounds [to inflict] against this land [of light]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Spiritual sense explicitly: ‘From the will of spiritual death there arose…’.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Required verbal link between ‘will of death’ and the birth of envy/delight.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Envy in this register is not mere feeling but a delight at another’s good turned opposite; matches the spiritual sense ‘resentful delight’.
> *¶7 #4 [tentative]:* Restores the phrase into a coherent structural unit: the infernal domain (‘darkness’) along with its calculating ‘consideration’ (rationalizing falsity).
> *¶7 #5 [tentative]:* Links ‘consideration in … wickedness’ to the domain’s own interior reasoning.
> *¶7 #6 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘plotting and justification of wickedness’.
> *¶7 #7 [moderate]:* Matches ‘for as it … the wounds … against’; spiritual: ‘until it rushed to inflict injuries’.
> *¶7 #8 [moderate]:* Completes the motion-to-action sequence: rushing in order ‘to inflict’ wounds.
> *¶7 #9 [strong]:* The opposed land is the light-domain; the oldest layer contrasts ‘land of darkness’ with ‘land of light’.

**¶8** Then he will run [quickly]rom the land [of] darkness. It drew [toward the borders of the] his members [living] is deep [the more firmly it was bound] in them.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* ‘Then he will run … from the land …’ needs an adverb intensifying haste; spiritual sense: ‘hastened’.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Required to complete ‘from the land of … darkness’.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘toward the boundaries of life’.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Completes ‘borders of the living’.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: deeper entry yields tighter conjunction of delights and persuasions; ‘bound’ expresses infernal coherence.

**¶9** [It gathered together] every [of its own essence.] It spread itself out. It generated itself [of the five dark elements]; which [are] smoke, fire, wind, water and darkness. [From them] sprouted trees. It came up [there also] it formed its own self in the [and in them] it generated its own self with its birth [in] numerous [emana]tions, not resembling each other; [in] the storehouses of its abyss, many [dark] creatures [it brought forth] destroyers and dragons and evil beasts. It was established and [demons] and grew strong [it multiplied] in the ugly forms of those in the darkness [in its world] dark, and by the [until they became] impetuosity, by their wickedness and hatred they slaughter [fierce craving] each other as they war with one another.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the organizing/structuring of the darkness into elements; spiritual sense: ‘organized itself’.
> *¶9 #2 [tentative]:* Completes ‘every … It spread itself out.’ as an unfolding from its own quality, not from light.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* Natural sequence: from elements come growths and structures; spiritual: ‘From these falsities there sprang…’.
> *¶9 #6 [tentative]:* Links the emergence of ‘trees’ as a further product of the elements.
> *¶9 #7 [moderate]:* Prepares for ‘it being set in the trees’ later; establishes the indwelling of its life within its productions.
> *¶9 #12 [moderate]:* Introduces the catalog of infernal breeds (destroyers, dragons, beasts) as generative products.
> *¶9 #13 [moderate]:* Fits ‘storehouses of its abyss, many dark creatures…’: in this register they are ‘demons’ as affections and persuasions embodied.
> *¶9 #14 [moderate]:* Balances ‘was established and … and grew strong …’; indicates increase in forms and powers.
> *¶9 #15 [tentative]:* The strengthening occurs ‘in its world’ i.e., within the infernal order it constitutes.
> *¶9 #16 [moderate]:* Leads into ‘in the ugly forms…’: the process culminates in fixed hideous forms.
> *¶9 #17 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘from fierce craving … they attack and tear’; this is the driving lust behind mutual slaughter.

**¶10** Then the counsel of their ruler humbled its world and its powers. It sensed the essence of the [light and life above it.] It conspired to wage a war [against it] with the exalted kingdom and the [ordered earth of light]

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* The ruler ‘sensed the essence’ of what is above: wisdom and living good. ‘Light and life’ is the substrate pair matching that perception.
> *¶10 #2 [strong]:* Completes ‘to wage a war … with the exalted kingdom’ as direct opposition.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘heavenly state in the natural degree’; the natural-heavenly boundary is often called ‘earth of light’.

**¶11** However, he, the Father of Great[ness, also knew at once] it also knew [the impending] war, to disturb the [land of] light [into disorder] in the wisdom of his [mercy]-ness [great] he transformed himself against it [and set a form] against it.

> *¶11 #1 [tentative]:* Restores the sense: the Father of Greatness knew immediately of the coming disturbance; avoids later ecclesial expansion.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* Qualifies ‘war’ as about to be permitted and met.
> *¶11 #4 [moderate]:* Matches spiritual paraphrase ‘disturb the land of light’.
> *¶11 #5 [moderate]:* Pairs with wisdom as the Divine motive for providing a suited resistance; oldest layer often couples ‘wisdom’ and ‘mercy’ as governing qualities.
> *¶11 #6 [tentative]:* Completes ‘in the wisdom of his …-ness’: ‘great wisdom’ is a standard epithet without Christian overlay.
> *¶11 #7 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘provided a transformation… setting in order a mode of resistance’; ‘set a form’ matches the correspondential idea of accommodated presence.

**¶12** Indeed, at that time [he commanded his power to] go against [it] God [by order]

> *¶12 #1 [tentative]:* Spiritual sense indicates a ‘commanded engagement’ and ordered action, not passion; this fill supplies that structure.
> *¶12 #2 [tentative]:* Object of the command: the uprising power to be met.
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* Ensures the mode is orderly, corresponding to Divine government rather than wrath.

**¶13** [And fro]om its [root] poured it out [and] in the elements: in the smoke, the fire, the wind, the water and the darkness. It came up from the [great] depth, it being set in the trees [and fixed itself] against it.

> *¶13 #1 [strong]:* Completes ‘from’ at the paragraph start (‘… from its … poured it out …’).
> *¶13 #2 [moderate]:* The outpouring comes ‘from its root’ i.e., its originating essence as contrary principle.
> *¶13 #3 [strong]:* Connects the pouring out to the distribution ‘in the elements’.
> *¶13 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: it ‘established itself within perverted perceptions’ as bases; ‘fixed itself’ matches the setting-in-trees motif.

**¶14** Also, at the time when it generated itself [from the trees] it formed the fruits, it still being set in them. They summoned the five garments, the sons of the Fir[st] Man.

> *¶14 #1 [strong]:* Natural sequence: fruits are formed from trees; spiritual: works from perverted perceptions. This keeps the organic correspondence chain.

**¶15** And at the time when it generated its own self [f]rom the fruits through its offspring, they fell upon the earth and [drew] strength from the elements and were nourished by them. [Thus there gathered] wickedness, together with cruelty [all] in the ruler, the leader of all the demons. He hum[bled] his worlds and his powers; he gathered them all into him. He made himself ready for the battle; he set himself to the war with that light earth, and the great King who dwells in it.

> *¶15 #2 [strong]:* ‘fell upon the earth and … strength from the elements’: they ‘drew’ strength.
> *¶15 #3 [moderate]:* Transitions from nourishment by elements to consolidation of wickedness into the ruler; matches spiritual ‘Thus wickedness… was gathered’.
> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* ‘together with cruelty … in the ruler’: ‘all wickedness’ is gathered in him as head.

**¶16** At that very moment the First Man approached his garments, the gods [who shine]ine forth. He spoke with them, that he might entrust to them a [command] he [reve]aled to them and taught them about everything that [was needful for] them. He assumed them. He arranged them [the struggle before] He draped them over one another upon his limbs [in a single form] danger, it [so that, when there was] the heights of the earth [was coming upon] with all its powers [begin]inning, it being immeasurable [without measure]

> *¶16 #2 [moderate]:* He entrusts to them what must be done; ‘command’ keeps an impersonal operational register rather than moral exhortation.
> *¶16 #4 [moderate]:* Matches ‘taught them about everything that … them’: what is necessary for their action.
> *¶16 #5 [moderate]:* Gives the object of what is needful: the impending combat; corresponds to ‘impending danger’.
> *¶16 #6 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: he conjoined them as one defensive and offensive form; fits ‘He arranged them …’.
> *¶16 #7 [tentative]:* Links the clothing/ordering of garments to the purpose clause about meeting danger.
> *¶16 #8 [moderate]:* Completes ‘danger, it … the heights of the earth’: the danger is ‘coming upon’ the heights/border of light’s earth.
> *¶16 #10 [moderate]:* Matches ‘being immeasurable’ and the spiritual note that the full measure cannot be unfolded; keeps the cosmological, quantitative idiom.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae can be restored at the level of required cosmological functions (Darkness as contrary root; permission-bounded “eternity”; consolidation into elements; envy as delight; war-counsel; accommodated resistance; garments as operative powers). Where the surviving natural text is highly fragmented (notably ¶6 and parts of ¶9–¶12, ¶16), restorations are correspondentially constrained but philologically underdetermined; these are supplied in the oldest, impersonal cosmological register, avoiding later institutional and devotional phrasing.

## Chapter 24: [... / ...]

**¶1** [There occurred] a great ju[dge] now also a ti[me] that they sculpt[ed] he was revealed in the Father, the first established thing.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense opens with an advent of a vast judgment and new state. The substrate voice states the event impassively: an occurrence, not an exhortation.

**¶2** W[he]n [he called forth] the two summonses that he called [these] the Mother of Life [and he established] a long duration in the first Father [herself]

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Two ‘summonses’ in the natural layer correspond to inward impulsions of the Divine to bring forth the next hypostasis. The idiom ‘called forth’ matches the impersonal cosmogonic register.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Links to ‘the two summonses’ as determinate acts.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation stresses being ‘set’ within the Father for an immeasurable state; ‘established’ preserves substrate technicality of fixing a hypostasis in its place.
> *¶2 #5 [tentative]:* The Mother of Life as hypostasis: ‘he established her…’ then ‘a long duration… in the first Father’. Reflexive not needed; simplest is pronoun reference.

**¶3** For someone, if he might set a measure and rule [of the air and the winds] the earth {particles} and the {pebbles} of the mountains and [out upon one another,] of the sea and the body of the firmaments. Should they be poured [out also upon] the body of the four mountains and the three vessels that surround the universe; if they shall all be poured out [out as well upon] these fine {granules}.

> *¶3 #1 [tentative]:* The series in ¶3 enumerates ultimates and containers: earth particles, mountain pebbles, then a third natural totality before ‘of the sea’ and ‘body of the firmaments’. ‘Air and winds’ supplies the missing intermediate natural domain in the substrate’s cosmological catalog.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense compares pouring out all measurable particulars for comparison. ‘Poured out upon one another’ gives the correspondential idea of exhaustive comparison.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Keeps the repeated ‘poured’ cadence used to express exhaustive measurement across degrees.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Third repetition completes the rhetorical triad before ‘these fine granules’.

**¶4** Happen you know that the grains of the earth {particles} are measurable, [and those of] the [entire] universe. It is possible to count the {particles} of the earth each year [and to reckon] the sand grains of the entire universe; but the duration of the time that the Great Spirit spent in the Father, [the] first established thing, one will not be able to make a count of it!

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* Matches given editorial fill and the spiritual sense (‘even the least particulars… of the whole created order’).
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: could be ‘numbered and surveyed’; ‘reckon’ fits the counting motif and keeps impersonal tone.

**¶5** He first sculpted her like this. He established her in his inner storehouses in quiet and silence. When [they had] need of her she was called and came forth of the Father [of Greatness]. She looked at all her aeons of light!

**¶6** Once again, from the time when she was called to [the border, and] go to [the earth;] she gave [power] upon his [bod]y [and he received] all the aeons [within her]; she also [gathered] all of them. This [is also the case] with the First Man.

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* She is called toward lower operation; ‘border’ expresses turning from inmost to ultimate, matching the later ‘border of the earth’.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The downward destination in the natural layer corresponds to ultimate degree in the spiritual translation.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* She ‘gave’ something upon his body; spiritual sense: imparted life and power to the instrument.
> *¶6 #5 [tentative]:* Balances ‘she gave… upon his body’ with reception by the instrument; keeps structural, not devotional.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘she held within her the totality’; restores the containment correspondence.
> *¶6 #7 [tentative]:* Completes ‘all the aeons …; she also … all of them’: she ‘gathered’ them in ordered wholeness.

**¶7** This is how the Man spent a long time being anointed in the Mother, set fast in her inner storehouses [in silence;]; the extent of time that I have recounted to you—from when the First Man went down to the contest, till the time when the [Statue]mes i[n the end,] this time [belongs to him;] he appeared [in that interval.]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* Matches the repeated motif of hidden storehouses, quiet, silence—consecration before manifestation.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Given reading fits the chapter’s five-stage schema ending in the Last Statue; substrate term retained.
> *¶7 #3 [tentative]:* Produces sense: ‘till the time when the Statue comes in the end’.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: the whole stretch ‘belongs to the manner of his being made manifest’.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* Carries the ‘between’ correspondence used elsewhere (‘between the borders’).

**¶8** Again, from when he was called and came forth, till the time when he [himse]lf called the five shining [gods] who are his sons; they themselves also spent a long time existing in their father! It is the time that occurred from the coming down of the First Man till the going up of the Last Statue. The five gods spent this much duration being anointed in the First Man. Yet, [at the] time when they were awaited, they were called and came forth.

**¶9** Listen also to this, how I would teach you it. At the time when the Mother of Life was called from the Father of Greatness, when she expelled herself and came down by her own wish, from the heights of the Father to the earth, [she would spend thousands of] years and many ten thousands until she reached the earth. [Fo]r there is no measure nor rule to the heights of the Father. Rather, [just as] he had called her in his Great Spirit, so he set her upon [the] land of greatness through one of these three that are counted to the twelve hidden ones within the light veil, the ones [esta]blished after the [ordered] region of the south.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Accepted: corresponds to apparent immeasurable traversal in natural imagery, though spiritually it is states without number.
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* ‘Established after the … region of the south’ needs a verb-like qualifier: the hidden ones are ‘established after the ordered region’, matching spiritual translation’s ‘stabilized after the ordering’.

**¶10** [The] matter of the Mother [L]ife is like this. When [the Father called her he established her in the place he pleased,] to establish her there as the instruction of the people that [might instruct] many [souls,]; and she examined everywhere that [she saw] a blink of an eye, or like a [in] bird [swift]

> *¶10 #3 [strong]:* Accepted: exactly the spiritual sense of immediate fixing according to Divine pleasure.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘doctrine and guidance for receptive minds’; ‘might instruct’ keeps impersonal pedagogy, not pastoral exhortation.
> *¶10 #5 [tentative]:* The substrate idiom often uses ‘souls’ for receptive minds without Christian overlay; fits ‘instruction of the people’.
> *¶10 #6 [moderate]:* Her ‘examined everywhere’ in an instant; ‘saw’ is the perception correspondence.
> *¶10 #7 [tentative]:* Prepares the simile phrase.
> *¶10 #8 [moderate]:* Completes ‘like a blink of an eye’ and anticipates ‘swift … bird’.

**¶11** This also is lik[e as the] Mother of Life in [case with] whenever [the First Man] cast her out [in the Father;] upon her to the place [he had] him. He perfected her in his Great Spirit and set her in [the place he pl]eased, according to [his will].

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Restores comparative formula: ‘This also is like the Mother of Life…’
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* Links the analogy to the general law of emanation-by-call.
> *¶11 #3 [moderate]:* The chapter repeatedly parallels Mother of Life and First Man; here it generalizes the rule.
> *¶11 #4 [tentative]:* Spiritual sense: whenever wisdom is sent, she is perfected in Great Spirit and set where pleased; ‘in the Father’ keeps cosmogonic locus.
> *¶11 #5 [tentative]:* Begins the clause ‘whenever he had cast her out…’

**¶12** [Th]is [also is how] they called the [five g]ods from the [Fi]rst Man. They were set before him, as he wished that [rst]-ness [the power] down, that they would rule in them [of order might come] one another. He ordered and distributed them [to those below,] the son. He stripped himself of them [and they would not] and he [overstep] he girt and bound himself to [nor contend with] in a [and he fixed] of silence upon the border of the earth [them in their measures;]

> *¶12 #5 [strong]:* Completes ‘First’.
> *¶12 #6 [moderate]:* ‘…as he wished that …-ness … down’: what descends is ordered force; ‘the power’ is the operative influx.
> *¶12 #7 [moderate]:* Restores the correspondential idea: order descends into ultimates to govern.
> *¶12 #8 [moderate]:* Supplies the lower degree governed by the five powers.
> *¶12 #9 [tentative]:* Sets up ‘rule in them … one another’: mutual subordination requires non-contradiction.
> *¶12 #10 [tentative]:* Expresses ordered bounds; correspondentially, powers stay within their function.
> *¶12 #11 [tentative]:* Opposes disorder; fits ‘one another’.
> *¶12 #12 [moderate]:* Before distributing them, he fixes their stations ‘before him’.
> *¶12 #13 [moderate]:* Matches the chapter’s theme of measure and rule, now applied to the five powers.

**¶13** Again, he too, the First Man, spent a long period and [time] until he gave power to his sons; until he assu[m]ed them. After he came to wear them he stood fast in the heights between the borders. Again, he spent a duration and time like this, corresponding to such amount and measure as the raindrops and dew and falls of mist that come down in the universe. This is how he spent this duration also, standing between the borders, until the hour when he cast himself down to the contest!

**¶14** Whenever the Father of Greatn[e]ss called the Great Spirit and the First Man and the gods of the glorious one, who are provided to enable them to reveal him; at that very time [he] sculpted the Great Builder in the [land of] light, [and] the Living Spirit with the mighty and active gods who came [from] him. Also, at that time, he sculpted the [Amb]assador, with Jesus the Splendour and the Virgin of Light and the Pillar of Glory, and the gods who came from them. [All these the Father of Greatness sculpted at] a single time. They came forth of one another, one after one; the time when [the appearing] and the work.

> *¶14 #7 [strong]:* Accepted; it anchors the teaching in a single act of formation, with successive appearing due to reception.
> *¶14 #8 [moderate]:* ‘the time when … and the work’: spiritual sense says duration lies in reception and work; ‘appearing’ matches that.

**¶15** [It is also taught] from the time when the darkness raised itself up to [that it occurred] essence of the light [to fight the] these three emanations [very] from the will of the Father against the enemy [and then] darkness [were sent forth] according to the will [came] the universe [and went out] separate out [to establish] and he rejoices [and to separate] in his kingdom; until [the time when the face] of the Father will be unveiled over [all] the gods of glory, and all the rich ones of [light,] the em[anations] who came forth against the enemy.

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* Begins the long temporal span from uprising of Darkness to unveiling of Father; impersonal didactic opener fits substrate.
> *¶15 #2 [moderate]:* Links to the historical-cosmic sequence without personal address.
> *¶15 #3 [moderate]:* Darkness ‘raised itself up’ to assault; spiritual sense: denial rose against essence of light.
> *¶15 #4 [strong]:* Intensifies ‘essence of the light’ per spiritual translation (‘very essence’).
> *¶15 #5 [moderate]:* Introduces the three emanations set in motion.
> *¶15 #6 [moderate]:* Technical substrate term for ordered outgoings emanating by will.
> *¶15 #7 [moderate]:* Connects ‘these three emanations … came … from the will’.
> *¶15 #8 [moderate]:* Reinforces procession against the enemy.
> *¶15 #9 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: to establish the created order, effect separation, secure rule.
> *¶15 #10 [moderate]:* Second purpose in the triad: separation of light from darkness.
> *¶15 #13 [moderate]:* ‘rich ones of …’ in this register are ‘rich ones of light’ (plenitudes of light) rather than moral ‘rich’.

**¶16** [From] that time when he sculpted these three [emanations] till the time when the Father would unveil his face, [and they] go in to his hidden storehouses, and he calls them [and he] establishes each one of them to his place. There is no measure nor rule to this period until that time.

> *¶16 #2 [strong]:* ‘these three …’ must be ‘emanations’, matching ¶15 and the spiritual translation’s ‘three ordered outgoings’.
> *¶16 #4 [strong]:* ‘he calls them … establishes each one’: requires ‘and he’ before ‘calls’.

**¶20** ... he gives [Again,] about this Matter, this thought of death [an account] which I have told you; for [this] is the way that [of] came up to its storehouses. It has [come to be] in its five elements.

> *¶20 #1 [moderate]:* Natural text resumes after omitted ¶17-19 with a fresh didactic turn to the contrary process.
> *¶20 #2 [strong]:* Spiritual translation: ‘the teaching also gives an account’.
> *¶20 #4 [strong]:* ‘the way that … came up’.
> *¶20 #5 [moderate]:* Matter’s arising into its storehouses corresponds to the opposing substance rising into its interiors.

**¶21** Again, it also [says] the period and the time that it spent, until it revealed itself to its elements, before it came to the trees. It spent a great period and a long time established [recounts] before it came up to the trees. It was [in its storehouses] in the trees. It spent a long time and duration [there,] existing in them, before it sculpted the fru[its works] and sculpted the fruits.

> *¶21 #1 [moderate]:* ‘Again, it also … the period’: needs a reporting verb; ‘says’ is minimal and impersonal.
> *¶21 #2 [moderate]:* Strengthens didactic continuity: it recounts the time spent before manifesting to elements.
> *¶21 #3 [moderate]:* Matches ¶20 and spiritual translation (‘its own interiors’).
> *¶21 #4 [strong]:* Closes ‘established … before it came up’.
> *¶21 #5 [moderate]:* Completes ‘before it sculpted the fru[its ...]’: in the contrary sequence, ‘trees’ → ‘works’ → ‘fruits’ as effects; ‘its works’ corresponds to produced acts.

**¶22** Again, it spent a long [time] in the fruits. Not little is the period and the time that it spent existing in the fruits, before it sculpted [its worlds;] after a long time [the hells,] the d[emon]s of its five worlds in various forms [cannot] be measured [their number] in p[erfec]tion of the fruits in the demons and fiends. They fell upon their earth and [tion] were nourished. Not little is the period that they [spent in the wo]rlds, until they were nourished amongst [rlds] to make [their like,] that land which shines, and the great [King] who dwells there.

> *¶22 #2 [moderate]:* Prepares ‘the demons of its five worlds’.
> *¶22 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation explicitly: ‘the hells of its full organized domains’. Substrate can still say ‘hells’ without later pastoral tone.
> *¶22 #5 [moderate]:* Removes editorial hesitation and matches spiritual sense: measure is unclear.
> *¶22 #6 [moderate]:* ‘cannot be measured …’ most naturally takes ‘their number’.
> *¶22 #8 [strong]:* Completes ‘perfection’.
> *¶22 #10 [strong]:* Completes ‘worlds’.
> *¶22 #11 [strong]:* Spiritual translation: ‘nourished among their like’.

**¶23** [You] know that [have learned] that in [the Enemy,] at the time when he [seized] its essence. It assumed [upon] Matter [living] to the war [and took] the Perfect Man. [From the time] when the war occurred [when it began.]

> *¶23 #2 [moderate]:* Keeps didactic tone without exhortation; ties to ‘You know that…’.
> *¶23 #3 [strong]:* The agent in spiritual translation is ‘the adversary’; in substrate register ‘the Enemy’ is standard.
> *¶23 #4 [strong]:* Matches spiritual translation ‘seized upon what is living’.
> *¶23 #6 [moderate]:* ‘what is living from its essence’.
> *¶23 #7 [moderate]:* Introduces assumption of Matter for war.
> *¶23 #9 [moderate]:* Closes the transition into the five stages that follow.

**¶24** [The first is this:] he came to be in some [that] when they ensnared a [snare]

> *¶24 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation says first state unclear but an initial entanglement; minimal formula introduces it.
> *¶24 #2 [strong]:* Begins explanatory clause.
> *¶24 #3 [moderate]:* Preserves the fragment’s ‘ensnared’ as the key correspondential idea: deceptive constraint.

**¶25** [The second is this:] that he built [a building,] the universe was established. The zone [in which] down until the time when it will be dissolved.

> *¶25 #1 [strong]:* Standard enumerative formula for the five states.
> *¶25 #2 [moderate]:* Corresponds to ‘ordered structure … built and fixed’.
> *¶25 #3 [strong]:* Links ‘he built … the universe was established’.

**¶26** The third is the [duration,] the things and the universe will [when] from the time when they were established there, till the time when their end will be complete and their existence ceases [be dissolved.]

> *¶26 #1 [moderate]:* The third is the continuing duration of existing things.
> *¶26 #2 [strong]:* Introduces temporal clause.
> *¶26 #3 [tentative]:* The third runs until end; the phrase anticipates dissolution stage.

**¶27** [The fourth is the dissolution when they will dissolve the universe] there, and all things are destroyed. Obliteration reaches them in that great fire, [which will] burn them for fourteen hundred and sixty-eight years.

> *¶27 #1 [strong]:* Accepted: matches spiritual translation exactly.

**¶28** [The fift]h is the way that the Last Statue will be sculpted [from] the remnant of all things. The light separates out to its place, and goes up and rules in its kingdom. However, [da]rkness [rkness is gathered up,] and they take it in to the tomb [into its own place;] and they are bound with it in an eternal chain.

> *¶28 #4 [moderate]:* Opposite process to ‘light separates out’: darkness is ‘gathered’ into confinement, matching burial and restraint.
> *¶28 #5 [moderate]:* Correspondential symmetry: each returns to its proper ‘place’.

**¶29** Now, from [the tim]e when this first war occurred, till the time of this chain [e of] these five things [are the whole course of the] light.

> *¶29 #2 [strong]:* Completes ‘time of this chain’.
> *¶29 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: these five are the whole course through time. Restores the concluding summary in substrate register.

**Assessment:** Most restorations are constrained by the chapter’s correspondential structure: (1) immeasurable abiding within the Father, (2) immediate establishment by call rather than traversal, (3) ordered descent and mutual subordination of powers, (4) a five-stage temporal schema from first conflict to final binding. Many gaps, however, are too short in the surviving natural text to recover exact Coptic phrasing; in those places I supplied minimal, substrate-appropriate connective wording that preserves the spiritual logic (measure versus immeasurable duration, fixing in place, procession by call, separation and confinement). Where the apparatus already provided clear letter-fills, I retained them unchanged.
