## Chapter 25: Concerning the Advent of Five Fathers from the Five Limbs of the Father

**¶2** The [Ambassador]sador [came] from his mind; the Beloved of the Lights [from his thought]; the Mother of Life from his insight; [Jesus] the Beloved from his counsel; the Virgin of Lig[ht f]rom his consideration.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* Agent of outwardly operative truth proceeding from the region of intention; the title must be the emissary-function (truth going forth), not a personal authority-name.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* Maintains the discrete-degree mapping in the substrate: mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration as distinct interior regions from which the five goings-forth proceed in order.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* In the oldest register this denotes the saving presence of the Divine united with love (not ecclesial confession). The series demands the specific named hypostasis paired with “the Beloved” and sourced in “counsel,” i.e., governing purpose.

**¶3** So in this way were summone[d these five] fathers. These five advents came from the five limbs [of the Father]. One came from another and was manifested in its season.

> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The correspondential sense requires these five to issue from the fullness of the primal Divine powers; “limbs of the Father” expresses operative powers in ultimates (effects), consistent with the substrate’s impersonal cosmology.

**Assessment:** All lacunae are tightly constrained by the fivefold procession schema: each hypostasis must proceed from a distinct interior faculty in ordered degrees. Only ¶2 #4 has mild looseness because later layers can color the name, but the function and pairing in the series constrain it to the established substrate title.

---

## Chapter 26: Concerning the First Man and the Ambassador and the Ship; what they resemble.

**¶1** The First [Man] is like a great distinguished man whose treasure has been taken away from him. He came to pursue his treasure. This is how the [First] Man too came after his sons from the heights, so that he might redeem them and retrieve his treasure, which had been scattered amongst his enemies.

**¶2** Once again, [he is like a man] whose two sons were taken away from him by [his enemies. He] came out toward them to redeem them. Again, this is how the First Man [also] shines in the heights because of his two sons, who were separated from him. They are his living soul that exist in two powers, which makes four.

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The second similitude requires an agent in the opposite camp who seizes the two sons; in this substrate register the simplest, non-institutional term is “enemies,” matching ¶1 and keeping the impersonal cosmological tone. The immediate return “He came out toward them” then resumes the redeemer’s action.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The sentence needs only the correspondential hinge: the First Man “also shines” in the heights because of the separated paired principles. Adding names or titles here would shift toward later framing; the oldest layer keeps it structural.

**¶3** Once again, the ship [of the day] is like a great spear [of truth that is set in battle array. Thus]man who might beautify and set aright [all] the armour for war.

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The ship of day corresponds to ordered progression of enlightened discernment in ultimates; its likeness to a spear demands the determinative quality: piercing truth in combat. The transition “Thus” cleanly carries into the next comparison about the Great Spirit within it arranging the armour.

**¶4** Once again, the Living Spirit is like a combatant who might go towards a king's son who has been taken prisoner of war, and he redeems him from the hands of his enemies.

**¶5** [The Amba]ssador too is like a great man [who speaks for the heights] because of the treasure [which was bound and seized] hand of his prisoners [from the] these light powers [and all] sons and their soul, so that they would [come out, and that] be brought forth in the land wherein it was imprisoned.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The Ambassador in the oldest layer is the mediating, representative influx, not an ecclesial office. “Speaks for the heights” preserves the impersonal cosmological function and matches the spiritual sense of communication and conjunction from above.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The action is ‘because of the treasure’ that has been taken and held fast in the lower contrary domain; “bound and seized” states the condition that necessitates mediation and release, without importing later juridical or devotional phrasing.
> *¶5 #4 [strong]:* The phrase “hand of his prisoners” requires the preposition to complete the idiom of extraction from captivity; this is the minimal restoration that preserves sense.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* What is ordered is not only discrete ‘light powers’ but also what proceeds from them; “and all the sons and their soul” matches the spiritual sense (liberated powers together with their life) and keeps the old cosmological grouping.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* The goal is emergence from imprisonment into the land where use can be effected. “Come out” is the required liberation-movement; “and that” links to “might be brought forth,” avoiding later salvation-language while keeping the causal-final structure.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are tightly constrained by the chain of correspondences: each similitude needs a specific functional predicate (pursuit of treasure, redemption of paired principles, spear as piercing truth, armour as ordered defending truths, ambassador as mediating speech). The larger gaps in ¶3 and ¶5 allow some variation in wording, but the oldest-layer register restricts additions to structural, impersonal cosmological functions rather than titles, exhortations, or institutional vocabulary.

---

## Chapter 27: Concerning the Five Forms that exist in the Rulers of Darkness.

**¶1** Consider the ruler, the leader of all the powers of Darkness. Now, five forms exist in his body, corresponding to (the archetype) of the five creations that exist in the five worlds of Darkness.

**¶2** Indeed, his head is lion-faced, which came about from the world of fire. His wings and his shoulders are eagle-faced, after the likeness of the children of the wind. His hands and his feet are daemons, after the likeness of the children of the world of smoke. His belly is dragon-faced, after the likeness of the world of Darkness. His tail is the form of the fish, which belongs to the world of the children of water. These five shapes are in him, and they came from the five creations of the five worlds of Darkness.

**¶3** ⟨p.78⟩
Should he wish, he shall walk on his two feet [like the children of the] world of smoke. Also, when he wants, he shall [crawl on his] four [feet, and walk] on his hands and feet [and burn] like the children of fire. Should he wish, he shall rise up on his wings, like the children of the wind. Also, should he wish, he shall plunge down to the waters like the children of water. Again, should he wish, he shall crawl on his belly like the children of Darkness. These five shapes are in him.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts walking on two feet with the world of smoke; the oldest-layer pattern is: manner “like the children of X” then the named world. This keeps the fivefold correspondence series intact (smoke as ultimates).
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The next clause begins “four … on his hands and feet … like the children of fire”; the missing verb should introduce the shift from two-foot walking to fourfold motion using hands and feet together. “crawl on his” preserves the natural-image logic of quadrupedal motion as a mode he can assume at will.
> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Completes the quadruped description: “four feet” as hands and feet together. The following phrase “on his hands and feet” then specifies the fourfold ultimates.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The close of the clause must match “like the children of fire”; the governing correspondence in this section is that each mode not only moves but takes on the proper activity of its world. “burn” fits the fire-world activity in the oldest register better than moralized terms.

**¶4** Also, there are three other (qualities) in him.

**¶5** The first: [He shall marshal] his powers [according to his will.]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation indicates this damaged quality concerns ordering or deployment of subordinate powers. An impersonal, technical verb (“marshal”) matches the substrate’s cosmological mechanics rather than exhortation.
> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* Completes the first quality as a statement of governance: the ruler can arrange his powers in ordered ranks. The clause is kept brief to avoid importing later institutional phrasing.

**¶6** The second: Shall a [man conceal by] his magic arts. When he wishes, he shall make an invocation over himself, and hide from his powers. When he wants, he shall show himself to them and strike a death-blow by his magic arts. Any word he might utter at that moment, he shall [work magic] by it.

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* The line requires a capability statement introducing the arts of deception: “Shall a … his magic arts.” The spiritual sense is concealment and disclosure. “man conceal by” yields a coherent oldest-layer instruction-like description without Christian overlay.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Replaces the editor’s bracketed phrase with a more substrate-neutral verbal form. The function is effective utterance as operative enchantment; “work magic” expresses that directly.

**¶7** The third: His body is strong, a[l]] [so that the] nails and claws of his powers are not able to penetrate it. No iron nor copper body will equal him [in strength]; they will not be able to destroy him, because he was formed and fashioned from the hard-hearted counsel of Matter, the mother of demons and fiends.

> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Bridges “His body is strong, al … nails and claws … are not able” into a purpose-result clause. This matches the correspondential point: invulnerability to subordinate assaults.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Completes “No iron nor copper body will equal him … ; they will not be able to destroy him,” by specifying the comparison domain (strength). Keeps the natural-image register (metals, bodies) rather than moral terms.

**¶8** Again, there are three others in him.

**¶9** When he wants, his fire shall {blaze), and his entire body be made like the [substance of] fire.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* The fire-quality is total assimilation: “entire body be made like the substance of fire.” This matches the spiritual translation: the whole state becomes as though only ardor were present.

**¶10** Also, when he wishes, he shall send out frost, and his entire body freezes like a [body of] snow.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Parallelism with ¶9: “freezes like a body of snow.” The image is not meteorological but total-state correspondence (coldness as privation).

**¶11** The third: Whenever his powers are stood before him, he shall look at them and understand; for [whatever exists]ists in their heart he shall understand, what is in their heart and their face so long as they are stood before him. When they retreat before him and distance themselves from him, he shall not understand what is in their heart. There is no life in him whatsoever, but his 'life' is the bile of anger that is apparent on his face and in his fear. Shall a [man set before him the] destruction that is establ[fore him]fore him.

> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The closing sentence must express fixed orientation toward destruction as a continually-presented end. “set before him” matches the spiritual translation (“sets before itself as its goal”) while keeping the impersonal, mechanical style.
> *¶11 #3 [strong]:* Completes “established before him” in the final clause; the sense is the destruction fixed as an objective in his sight.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae fall into tightly patterned fivefold and threefold series (modes of motion, elemental states, concealment-disclosure, invulnerability), which constrains the restorations. The first quality in ¶5 and the broken capability statement in ¶6 remain the least determinate; the fills there are correspondentially fitting (ordering powers; concealment by arts) but textually more uncertain than the parallel elemental clauses.

---

## Chapter 28: [Concerning the T]welve Judges [of] the Father

**¶2** Twelve judges exist; the great mighty givers of judgement! They were sent and came from the exalted greatness to this place. They were assigned to different works according to the command that the Judge, who belongs to the greatness, entrusted to all these judges. Now he, the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges, who exists and is established in the aeons of greatness; he is the Father of Greatness.

**¶3** The first judge is the First Man, he who humiliated the ruler of Darkness in the beginning. He judged him according to a judgement of righteousness; for he had exalted himself against a kingdom that was not his own.

**¶4** The second judge is the Living Spirit, he who came and bore up the Man. He too made a judgement on all the powers of darkness; which had wounded the garments of the First Man.

**¶5** The third judge is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, she who beautified and set up the heavenly part of the universe, according to her pleasure; which [was entrusted to] her. She judged the rulers above; she fettered and set them firmly in the place that is fitting for them.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The Mother of Life ‘beautifies and sets up’ the heavenly part; in the substrate this ordering is not self-originating but proceeds by commission from the higher Greatness. The verb must express delegated appointment: her formative delight operates within what has been given to her.

**¶6** The fourth judge is the Great Builder, he who built the new aeon of joy. He judged, according to a judgement of righteousness, the storehouses of the enemy; that death may not well up from this time on. He has constructed a prison for the enemy. Also, upon the crown of the building he has constructed a throne for the First Man and all the fathers of light; they who engaged the struggle with the evil one and were victorious over him.

**¶7** ⟨p.80⟩
The fifth judge is the great King of Honour, he who dwells and is established in the seventh firmament. He is the judge of all the firmaments who gives a true judgement, according to a judgement of righteousness, upon all the powers and all the kingdoms of the firmaments.

**¶8** The sixth judge is [the Th]ird Ambassador, he who came and displayed his image. He purified the light [that was mingled] which his own [image had seized] also of the enemy, he has [separated it]

> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The Ambassador’s work here is discrimination and cleansing: the light is described as not simply defiled but mixed, i.e., good bound together with what is alien. This matches the correspondential sense: truth-wisdom obscured by admixture, requiring separation.
> *¶8 #3 [tentative]:* The clause must distinguish what belongs to him from what belongs to the enemy. In the oldest cosmological register this is expressed as seizure or capture: the enemy ‘took hold’ of light. Thus: the purified light is that which his own image had seized (recovered) as against what remained with the enemy.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* The line closes the purification by an act of division: separating what is his own from what is the enemy’s. This is the required correspondential operation (discernment by discrete degrees), and it fits the fragment’s sense that ‘he has …’ completed a cleansing by separation.

**¶9** The seventh judge is ... ... the worlds [as he gives the victory to] they who are steadfast; but he condemns the ones who are convicted ... ... he shall become for them the steps of the way (?) [in their works; for the steadfast]; but the convicted he sweeps and casts out to the depths.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Retained: it exactly matches the substrate function of this judge as the law of combat that confirms the steadfast and grants victory.
> *¶9 #2 [tentative]:* The surviving structure demands a balancing: he condemns those convicted (in their deeds), but for the steadfast he becomes ‘steps of the way’. Filling with an impersonal criterion (‘works’) keeps the oldest register (cosmic order by effects) rather than moral exhortation.

**¶10** The eighth judge is Jesus the Splendour, the father of all the apostles; because after the Ambassador had displayed his image and the light was taken [up;] a mass of rulers rose up from above and below. Then they sent Jesus the Splendour. He came ... ... he descended and made a judgement in the firmaments. He separated the steadfast from the convicted.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* The narrative needs the transition: after the light was taken up, a mass of rulers rose up. This is the natural-sequence marker demanded by the spiritual sense (purified good received, then resistance surges).

**¶11** The ninth judge is the Virgin of Light, [she who took the heart] of the powers by her image, gathering her own ones into her. She makes a judgement on the ruler of the moist, and the ruler of the dry.

> *¶11 #1 [strong]:* Retained: this is a technical correspondential expression for drawing in the inmost affection or will of opposing powers by the manifest image, enabling the gathering of what is her own into her.

**¶12** The tenth judge is the Judge who dwells in the atmosphere, judging all mankind. He makes a separation between they who are good and they who are evil, setting apart the righteous from the sinners.

**¶13** ⟨p.81⟩
The eleventh judge is the Light Mind, he who shall come and appear in the world. And he chooses the holy church, and he unveils [the hidden things] and separates light from darkness, and sets the truth apart from lawlessness.

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* The Light Mind ‘unveils’ what corresponds to interiors previously covered. The oldest layer prefers a plain cosmological term (‘hidden things’) over later ecclesial or revelatory phrasing; it matches the subsequent separations (light from darkness, truth from lawlessness).

**¶14** The twelfth judge is the great counsel, which is the summons and the obedience, which is appointed in the elements. It is the one that makes a separation between the good and the evil ones. At the end also it can gather itself together and sculpt its own self in the Last Statue. And it separates light from darkness.

**¶15** These are the twelve great and mighty judges who were sent. They came from the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges. He is the Father that is hidden and immeasurable, the one who exists; who is established in the aeons of greatness upon the great throne of honour, which is the King of Honour [who reigns] in his kingdom.

> *¶15 #1 [tentative]:* The closing identifies the throne of honour as ‘the King of Honour … in his kingdom’. The missing predicate must express rulership rather than naming another hypostasis: the Father is established upon that throne, which is the King of Honour reigning in his kingdom.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae can be restored at the level of function: the missing pieces consistently describe delegated ordering (¶5), purification by discrimination and separation (¶8), judgement by confirmation or expulsion according to works (¶9), the sequence ‘light taken up → resistance’ (¶10), unveiling of interiors (¶13), and final enthroned rule (¶15). Exact verbal forms remain uncertain in ¶8–9 and ¶15 because the fragment preserves only the action-type, not fixed wording.

---

## Chapter 29: Concerning the Eighteen Great Thrones of all the Fathers

**¶1** Eighteen [great thrones for all the fathers] exist and are appointed, which are established in eighteen places! While there are nine among them [appointed in] the outer aeons, they exist [i]n all the worlds of the damage.

> *¶1 #1 [strong]:* Matches the substrate register: ‘thrones’ as fixed governing determinations; ‘fathers’ as enduring heavenly principles.

**¶2** The first throne is the throne of the Father, the God of [trut]h, the King of the aeons of greatness, the one who exists; who is established [in the aeon of the Father] he alone, in his own essen[c]e, [existing alone] apart. This is the first throne [of the Father] the one that surpasses all the thrones.

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The first throne must be located in the inmost, unconditioned degree. ‘Aeon of the Father’ is the oldest-layer cosmological placement for the self-subsistent Father, without introducing later ecclesial framing.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondentially required: self-subsistent, unconditioned being. Fits the sequence ‘he alone, in his own essence, … apart’.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Needed to close the identification: ‘This is the first throne …’ must restate whose throne it is, grounding the superordination ‘surpasses all the thrones’.

**¶3** ⟨p.82⟩ The second throne is that of the [gl]orious Mother of Life. She is the origin of all the emanations that have come to this world.

**¶4** The third throne is constructed for the father, the First Man. He is the one who humiliated the entire first enmity, which existed in the worlds of darkness.

**¶5** The fourth throne is that of the Beloved of the Lights, the great honoured Beloved of the aeons of greatness.

**¶6** The fifth throne is that of the glorious Great Builder; the great architect who built the new aeon for a newness, for [a new aeon] for [the] fathers of light; also for a place of binding and a prison for the enemy and his powers.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The Builder’s function is formative intelligence establishing a renewed heaven-state; ‘built the new aeon for a newness’ naturally completes with an apposition of the same governing structure.

**¶7** [The sixth thro]n[n] is that of the Living Spirit, the [glorious] mighty one, he who bore up the First Man out from the [land of darkness]; who also set in order and constructed the world [so that he might set in order and construct the worlds] both for a cleansing of the light, [and] for a binding [of the] powers of the enmity.

> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* Required to complete ‘throne’.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* The Living Spirit’s correspondential work is ordering lower degrees and constructing the cosmos as a mechanism for separation and restraint; the gap must introduce that purposive clause before ‘both for a cleansing…’.

**¶8** The seventh th[rone is] that of the Third Ambassador, the king of the zone, also the master of all advices.

**¶9** The eighth throne is that of Jesus the Splendour, who [is] the releaser and redeemer [of] all souls.

**¶10** The ninth throne is the throne of the great M[ind, he to whom] all the churches shall gather; and all the [life] that will be purified from the world returns to him.

**¶11** These are the nine thrones of ho[no]u[r] appointed in the outer aeo[ns], <which are established> for the fathers of the glorious realm; [in the kingdom of the King of] Honour, for ever and ever, amen.

**¶12** In contrast, these are the other nine thrones that are appointed in this world of the zone.

**¶13** Three thrones in the ship of [the d]ay: one is that of the Ambassador, the second i[s that of] the Great Spirit, the third is that of the Living Spirit.

**¶14** ⟨p.83⟩ Once again, there are another three in the ship that [belongs to the night]: the first is the throne of Jesus the Splendour, [the second is that of] the First Man, the third is tha[t of the Virgin of] Light. These six thrones are established in the two ships.

**¶15** Also, the seventh throne is established in the seventh firmament. The great King of Honour sits upon it.

**¶16** The eighth throne is established in the atmosphere. The Judge of truth sits upon it, he who judges all mankind. Three paths shall be distinguished before him: one to death, one to life, on[e] to the mingling.

**¶18** Th[ese] are the [nin]e great [th]ro[nes of honour] that are established in this world of [the zone] of the glorious realm, the one that the [king of honour and his powers] honour them [in their order] and the [remainder of the discourse is broken].

> *¶18 #4 [moderate]:* Restores the fixed phrase naming these determinations; keeps the impersonal, structural register (not devotional).
> *¶18 #5 [moderate]:* The paragraph is summing the ‘other nine’ established in the intermediate realm; ‘world of the zone’ is the natural-layer expression for that mixed intermediate region.
> *¶18 #6 [tentative]:* Correspondentially this clause must relate the zone-governance to the sovereignty that rules in ultimates (King of Honour) and to the operative forces by which it is administered (‘powers’).
> *¶18 #7 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual translation: ‘set so that they may be acknowledged in their order’. This is the oldest-layer purpose statement (recognition of order).
> *¶18 #8 [tentative]:* The spiritual translation indicates the close is irrecoverable; this supplies a minimal oldest-layer notice of textual discontinuity without importing later editorial voice.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are short and tightly constrained by repeated formulae (‘thrones of honour’, ‘world of the zone’) and by the fixed ninefold order. The main uncertainties are in ¶2 (locative and resumptive phrases around the Father’s throne) and especially ¶18 where the surviving text only signals a concluding summary; those restorations are guided primarily by the correspondential structure (intermediate governance under the heavenly realm) rather than by recoverable surface syntax.

---

## Chapter 30: Concerning the Three Garments

**¶1** Happen you know this, my beloved ones, that when the Living Spirit came he brought the three garments; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. He assumed them from [the first formation] he has [his power was operating] in them in those three garments [and] he established [them] for all the things of [this universe]e, both above and below. He arranged them in the [boundary] he was displayed. Afterwards [and there] his works [when he had completed] the [which]e stripped them off before [the righteous]ghteous ones.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The three garments are outer operative forms. In the oldest cosmogonic register they are taken from the already-established first boundary or ‘first formation’ of the cosmos, not from a devotional or personal source.
> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The Spirit ‘acts through’ the garments; the spiritual sense demands operative influx, not mere wearing. So the gap calls for an expression of activity within them.
> *¶1 #3 [strong]:* Connects the operative clause to the establishing clause; minimal connective required by the flow of the teaching.
> *¶1 #6 [moderate]:* The garments are disposed in the ultimate plane ‘where manifestation occurs’; in this tradition that is the limit, border, or boundary which contains the arranged cosmos.
> *¶1 #7 [strong]:* Supplies the localization required by ‘he was displayed’: manifestation occurs ‘there’ in the outmost.
> *¶1 #8 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation: ‘Afterwards, when the works were completed in that plane’. This gap must carry the completion condition for the stripping off.
> *¶1 #9 [moderate]:* The preserved tail ‘... h’ fits a relative connector leading into the stripping action; ‘which he stripped them off’ matches the sense.

**¶2** ⟨p.84⟩
Now, when he was about to loose [oose them from them, he] divested himself of them from their (clasp) and he [left them arranged]n them, and thus these three g[ments]nts are arranged [in their order] from below the edges of the zone as they clothe i[it all]l [from below] up [to the middle] above [and from the middle] up [to above] above them strips them of the zone from that place at its topmost point; and it is placed below them and ra[n]p to the heights above, from which it had been sent o[t]t at their beginning.

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* The spiritual sense is ‘release what had been bound in those outer forms’. The natural phrasing in this layer commonly repeats ‘from them’ to mark separation from the garments as vessels.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* After divesting, the garments remain disposed in order. The gap precedes ‘in them’ and should state that they were left set in place, not destroyed.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Corresponds to the spiritual sense ‘disposed in their proper order within the ultimate boundary’.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* Marks the lower edge of the zone as the starting point of their encompassing, matching ‘from below the edges of the zone’.
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* A standard tripartite spatial articulation (below, middle, above) matching the threefold order implied by the three garments and the complete encircling of the zone.
> *¶2 #8 [tentative]:* Balances the prior segment so the ascent is continuous and ordered rather than a single leap.
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* Completes the encompassing movement up to the upper boundary before the action ‘above them strips them of the zone’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the correspondential structure: three garments as outer operative forms arranged in the ultimate boundary (‘zone’) and later removed once their instrumental work is complete. The tightest restorations are those anchored by the spiritual sequence (completion of works → stripping in presence of the righteous; divesting → garments left arranged in order; zone removed from its topmost point and raised). The weakest points are the fine-grained spatial connectors in ¶2 (below–middle–above), where the teaching’s tripartite logic guides the fill but exact wording remains uncertain.

---

## Chapter 31: Concerning the Summons, in which Limb of the Soul it descended to the First Man.

**¶2** Indeed, the First Man dispersed himself and he [poured himself out and scattered] limb of the living soul as I have written for you [each] of the living soul; he has [the members] he did not know [what he had become] but the blessed, glorious Man came [in] secret; in his image; in his shape; in his love; in his holy virgin, she who is the virgin of light, the soul of the father.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate describes the First Man’s self-diffusion: the ruling good going outward into ultimates, causing dispersion of the soul’s powers. This matches the spiritual sense: “poured itself outward and became scattered.”
> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* Requires a distributive term before “limb of the living soul,” since the sense is that every derivative power was dispersed.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Balances the parallelism: “limb … of the living soul … of the living soul.” The teaching layer regularly alternates “limb” and “member” for derivative powers.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: the scattered state “no longer recognized what it had been given to know.” Old layer frames this as loss of self-recognition in dispersion.

**¶3** After the fashion of this fleshly body: as the root of all the limbs hang upon the head, so that should one of the person's limbs be cut off, while the head exists he has hope for [healing] but if his head should be cut off, the entire body will die and he is lost.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* In the body analogy: if a limb is cut off while the head remains, there is hope of restoration. “Healing” best fits the impersonal, structural register (not moral exhortation).

**¶4** This is also the case for the Firs[t] Ma[n]. He is the head, while his sons attach to it the limbs [of] his soul. Or, conversely, like the living air, on which all flesh entirely lives as it breathes and [is sustained] therein. His head is placed on the body of the Pillar of Glory in the heights of the universe.

> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Living air correspondence = general influx giving vitality as one breathes and is sustained in it. The missing verb must express continuance-in-being by influx.

**¶5** [This i]s also the case [for] the father, the blessed Man; as he is like the head that is upon the body and the air that upon the Pillar is set i[in the height] being made strong by his light virgin, [she] who is his soul that he clothes [with her]

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* He is “set … upon the Pillar” in the highest degree. The lacuna after “set i[n ...]” demands a locative expressing the supreme placement above the ultimate foundation.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The Father “clothes” himself with his Light-Virgin (pure wisdom as living consciousness) for operation. The instrumental phrase is required by the correspondential sense of investiture.

**¶6** [He brought forth] t[h]ose [powers]: the mind, thought, insight, counsel and c[on]sideration that he produced and sent forth from hi[m to] do his will. He sprang and travelled behind them. [for the sake] of his living soul, which is entwined among the rebels; as they are like the limbs of its body, and [bound in the body of the] universe.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Introduces the list of sent powers as an emanation from the Father. This matches the spiritual sense: “From Himself He brought forth and sent out the sufficient powers.”
> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* The demonstrative “those …” needs its noun: the produced faculties/powers listed next.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* He follows behind them by continual influx “for the sake of his living soul” (i.e., because it is entangled). This is the causal connector required by the spiritual logic.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* The entangled powers are “like the limbs of its body” and are bound within the ultimate domain. The phrase supplies the required binding-into-ultimates relation before “universe.”

**¶7** And when they were sent, at [that ti]m[e] he was found with the Virgin of [Lig]ht and [he] stood up, asking and entreating for a p[o]wer. [and the Father] gave him peace and a ki[ss of peace] he [gave] him goo[d] tidings [concerning the restoration of the] Man. The Man him[s]elf gave [power to gather] his limbs and gathered his soul i[in its place] he built it in its place like this tower [of the heights]

> *¶7 #6 [moderate]:* The giver of peace and kiss is the transcendent source (the Father). In the oldest layer this remains impersonal-cosmological: the supreme grants conjunction and calm to the operative Divine-Man.
> *¶7 #7 [moderate]:* The kiss corresponds to conjunction/union; specifying “of peace” matches the immediately preceding “he gave him peace,” keeping the old layer’s structural pairing.
> *¶7 #10 [moderate]:* What is announced is not moral ‘gospel’ but certainty of deliverance: good tidings about restoration. This aligns with the spiritual translation’s “certainty of deliverance and restoration.”
> *¶7 #12 [moderate]:* After receiving peace and assurance, the Man receives the operative power needed: to restore limbs and gather the dispersed soul. This bridges to “his limbs and gathered his soul …”.
> *¶7 #13 [strong]:* The gathering results in re-establishment in the proper degree/location, matching “he built it in its place like this tower.”
> *¶7 #14 [moderate]:* The “tower” image corresponds to a fixed structure of conjunction in higher order; specifying “of the heights” keeps the cosmological register and links to placement on the Pillar.

**¶8** [Then he] shaped it and beautified it skillfully [he] for[m according to] the voices that he sent [so that] he might sink in and quench [the fire of wrath] so[u]l that was crushed by the enemy. They were gathered in. They came, set firm once more, in the image of their father.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* Resumes the acting subject (the restored Divine-Man) continuing the forming and beautifying work.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Maintains verbal sequence: “Then he … shaped it and beautified it …”.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* After “form” the text requires an ordering principle: arranging the restored soul “according to” the heavenly pattern, matching the spiritual sense “according to the order of heaven.”
> *¶8 #4 [strong]:* Introduces purpose clause for the sent “voices” (living utterances) to descend and effect quenching and raising.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* The inflamed opposition to be quenched corresponds, in the old cosmological register, to a ‘fire’ state in the rebels. “Wrath” preserves impersonal passion-state rather than moral sermonizing.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae here are tightly constrained by the chapter’s correspondential anatomy: head and limbs (governing good and derivative powers), living air (general influx), Pillar of Glory (ultimate foundation supporting higher degrees), and the Light-Virgin (pure wisdom as living consciousness/investiture). Single-letter brackets are certain. Larger gaps are recoverable in direction and function (dispersion, loss of recognition, emanated powers, conjunction as peace and kiss, restoration as building a tower, utterances quenching the rebels’ fire), though exact phrasing remains occasionally approximate where the Coptic base could allow several near-synonyms.

---

## Chapter 32: Concerning the Seven Works of the Living Spirit

**¶1** The Living Spirit has done seven [wo]rks with his strength.

**¶2** [The fi]rst: He brought the First Man up from the contest, the way a pearl is [brought] up from the sea.

**¶3** [The second: He spread about the one who had rebelled, he crucified h]im in every body.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The seventh-works schema is formulaic in this substrate: each work begins with an ordinal tag, then a cosmic action. The ‘rebelled one’ is the bound Archon of the mixture; ‘crucified’ here is the fixing of that ruling defection into extension and ultimates (the cross as ultimate boundary) so it cannot rule inwardly.

**¶4** The third: He [tr]ampled, piled up, and pierced the essences of death.

**¶5** [The f]ourth: He constructed the ships of light.

**¶6** The fi[f]th: He evoked his five sons, and distributed them about. They [took] possession of the zone, they bore up under all the universe's weight. Also, he evoked three living words. He set [them] upon the th[re]e vessels, another one upon the giant. [Again, he evoked the] summons, that it might be mixed with the five shining men.

> *¶6 #5 [strong]:* This clause marks a second evocation within the fifth work: after sons and living words, a further ‘summons’ is called forth so it can be conjoined with the five luminous agencies in the zone. The oldest-layer logic is functional and serial, not hortatory.

**¶7** ⟨p.86⟩ The sixth: When he had constructed the zone he aro[se] and s[e]nt forth a multitude of powers and many [angel]s; so that they would bound the circuit of the z[o]ne on every si[de], un[t]il he finished [co]nstructing the works. And at the end [of] all the [wor]ks he to[ok s]ome of them into his storehouses, and he appointed others at the watch-posts.

**¶8** The [sev]enth: A[t the] time when the Ambass[ador dis]pl[aye]d his glorious imag[e], the L[iving] Spirit [established] many [go]ds and angels. They [were set over the zone and over its circuit] Th[ey to]ok hold of it, that the entire building [might stand firm, and] not [that it would] one another against [the] light, which he had [set in the beginning]

> *¶8 #9 [moderate]:* The seventh work continues the boundary-and-stability theme of the sixth: once revelation is shown, additional goods and angels are installed as superintending powers, specifically ‘over the zone’ (ultimate boundary) and its circuit, to keep the ordering from collapsing into counter-action.
> *¶8 #11 [moderate]:* This supplies the required purposive hinge before ‘They took hold of it’: the powers are made to stand in stability, then they ‘take hold’ (fasten, maintain) of the established structure. The spiritual sense demands a stabilization function rather than moral exhortation.
> *¶8 #12 [moderate]:* The following clause reads ‘not […] one another against the light’; correspondentially this is the prevention of inversion, i.e., the ordered parts turning into mutual hostility. A short governing connector like ‘that it would’ is demanded here.
> *¶8 #14 [moderate]:* The close ‘which he had …’ requires a prior act of establishment. In the oldest-layer register the light is not ‘given’ devotionally but ‘set’ or ‘appointed’ as the ruling order. This matches the spiritual constraint: subordination under proceeding light.

**¶9** Also another gr[ea]t and glorious work he will enact at the e[nd] is [the] Last [Statue], which he will bring up to the aeons of [light]; and he [will enter] in, and [he ta]kes hold, and he smites [the opposing powers], and [he] builds, and he [sets in order], and he [binds t]hem.

> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* After ‘which he will bring up to the aeons of light; and he … in,’ the spiritual sequence requires ‘enter’ as the act of coming into the final formed state, before ‘takes hold’ and ‘smites’.
> *¶9 #8 [moderate]:* The verbs ‘takes hold’ and ‘smites’ need an object in the opposite sense: resistive forces within the mixed order that must be struck down for the final formed state to be stable.
> *¶9 #10 [moderate]:* Following ‘he builds’ the next required operation is re-ordering, not moral ‘saving’: the substrate describes structural rearrangement leading to a perfected form.
> *¶9 #11 [moderate]:* Final verb before ‘them’ must denote conjunction into unity. ‘binds them’ fits the correspondential endpoint: powers remaining are held together in the last formed state.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are minor letter-restorations and are secure. The only substantial gaps are in ¶8 and ¶9; these are constrained by the chapter’s correspondential structure (boundary, stabilization under light; then final-state grasping, striking, rebuilding, and binding). Exact wording remains somewhat open, but the restored phrases are aligned to the oldest-layer impersonal cosmological register and the serial logic of the seven works and the final operation.

---

## Chapter 33: Concerning the Five Things that he constructed with the hard Bodies of the Rulers

**¶1** The Living Spirit constructed five great things from veins and skull[s in the] hard [bodies].

> *¶1 #2 [strong]:* The oldest-layer register here is anatomical-cosmic: the Living Spirit builds from the hardest ultimates (bones, skulls) of the dark mixture. “Bodies” keeps the impersonal structural sense (ultimates as resistant forms) required by the spiritual translation.

**¶2** The first is the disc that is placed upon [the sh]oulder of the Porter.

**¶3** The se[con]d are the arches of [the] pillar that is before him.

**¶4** [The th]ird is the [mou]ld of the fo[r]m for the gehennas.

**¶5** The fourth are the [seven] pillars that he has erected in the great sea, in [the] seven parts of the universe.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The spiritual sense demands a complete distribution through the full lower expanse; “seven” is the fixed cosmological number for a full progression in ultimates. The editor’s uncertainty is unnecessary correspondentially here.

**¶6** The fifth are the four walls that surround the vessels.

**Assessment:** All lacunae are tightly constrained by the given natural text and the correspondential mapping. The only substantive decision point was ¶5 #1, where the structure of completeness in ultimates requires the unqualified number seven.

---

## Chapter 34: Concerning the Ten Things that the Ambassador began by his Advent

**¶2** ⟨p.87⟩
The first: The Great Builder came forth to build the new aeon.

**¶3** The second: The ships moved through the heights in the heavens.

**¶4** The third: The wheels begin to send the life to earth and the upper worlds.

**¶5** The fourth: [The measures were set in their places in the zone.]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The series lists ten ordered operations in the establishing of the new aeon. After the ‘wheels’ transmit life downward (ordered operations in ultimates), the next correspondential step is the fixing of limits, times, and bounds in the intermediate region (the zone) so that subsequent openings of the ship-doors can function as regulated admissions and expulsions. Thus ‘measures’ (bounds, determinations) being set in the zone fits the cosmological register of the oldest substrate and prepares for ¶6–7 (openings for influx and for the removal of waste).

**¶6** The fifth is the doors of the ships, that they were opened up [ ...

**¶7** The sixth is the doors too [ ... ... that they were opened to receive the waste.

**¶8** The seventh is [the Sin], which he had separated from the rulers by cause of the Third Ambassador; it fell upon the earth.

**¶9** The eighth: Night and day were separated [from each other in the zone]

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Night and day correspond to obscurity and illumination; their ‘separation’ requires an ordered alternation within the intermediate region where states are successively arranged. Adding ‘from each other’ expresses discrete distinction (not mixture), and ‘in the zone’ anchors the operation in the same middle region that governs the subsequent state-changes (¶11).

**¶10** The ninth: The abortions [fell] to the ground.

**¶11** The tenth: Transmigrations came about in the zone.

**Assessment:** Two lacunae are trivial (¶8, ¶10). The missing fourth item is only moderately recoverable: the correspondential sequence strongly suggests the establishment of measures or bounds in the zone between transmission (¶4) and the opening of doors for influx and expulsion (¶6–7), but the exact noun could vary (limits, measures, ordinances). The night-day clause is fairly constrained: it must express discrete distinction and likely locate it in the zone, though alternative short completions remain possible.

---

## Chapter 35: Concerning the Four Works of the Ambassador

**¶2** The first is his image that he displayed in a glorious image.

**¶3** The second is all the doors of the firmaments that were opened at that time. They open [before him] and shut behind him.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The “doors of the firmaments” correspond to passages of influx between discrete degrees. In the described motion they must open in advance for admission, then close afterward for retention and order; thus the demanded complement is that they “open before him and shut behind him.”

**¶4** The third is the light and the releasing that was purified by his image. That light is more than the light that he shall [give forth]

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The purified light is said to exceed the prior light he would otherwise emit. The required verb is one of proceeding or emanating, matching the spiritual sense “there proceeded an illumination”; “give forth” keeps the impersonal, efflux-register of the oldest layer.

**¶5** The fourth: He has [made apparent], by the perfection of people, his light ship. He has [brought forth] the living soul that shone forth in perfection and fullness over the worlds of enmity.

> *¶5 #2 [strong]:* After making the light-ship evident through a completed human receptacle, the next act must be the production of “living soul” as an effect of that conveyance. The spiritual translation specifies “it brought forth living consciousness”; therefore the oldest-layer verb is generative, not exhortative or institutional.

**Assessment:** All four lacunae are tightly constrained by the correspondential sequence: opening and closing of influx-passages (¶3), proceeding of purified illumination (¶4), and the manifestation of a conveyance followed by the generative production of living consciousness (¶5). Only ¶4 allows minor lexical variation; the rest are structurally determined.

---

## Chapter 36: Concerning the Wheel that exists in front of the King of Honour

**¶1** ⟨p.88⟩
[Agai]n the [teaching] speaks about the wheel that exists in front [of the King of Honour who dwells in] the seventh firmament [there], the root of the rulers above and below in a great and cruel fetter; as the entire will of the great King of Honour is placed in [it]. Also, their entire enmity, which is in the firmaments, is [gathered] and ensnared in it.

> *¶1 #2 [strong]:* The oldest layer introduces sections with the impersonal didactic voice: ‘Again the teaching speaks…’, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘Again the teaching speaks…’.
> *¶1 #3 [strong]:* Fits the substrate cosmology where the King of Honour stands at the summit (seventh firmament) and the ‘wheel’ is positioned before him as an ultimate restraint and disclosure.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* The phrase needs a local adverbial completion: the wheel exists ‘in front … in the seventh firmament there’, i.e., fixed in the highest outermost boundary.
> *¶1 #6 [moderate]:* Correspondentially, hostile impulses are not merely ‘in’ the restraint but collected into it, then held fast (‘ensnared’ follows). ‘Gathered’ matches the spiritual translation’s ‘gathered, confined’.

**¶2** It exists in a blaze of splendour [with] ornamentation and great beauty. Now, there are twelve seals in it. The seal between i[s] every firmament and the rulers who are there [which bind them] are in it; for should one of them wish to escape, he will be bound [there]. If he should wish to cause a trick in his bond [it will be] apparent and known in [that] wheel [itself]. The spheres and stars and [the] leader of all the powers exist there, and can be known by it, in front of the King of Honour.

> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The seals function as discriminations and constraints between each firmament and its rulers. The text immediately explains: they are ‘in it; for should one… wish to escape, he will be bound…’.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Completes the conditional: if a ruler wishes to escape, he will be bound ‘there’, i.e., by the wheel’s present constraint before the King of Honour.
> *¶2 #5 [strong]:* Old-layer register prefers direct manifestation: any attempted trick ‘will be apparent’, not moralized. This matches the spiritual translation’s ‘becomes at once evident’.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* The disclosure is immediate and internal to the ordering: the trick is known ‘in that wheel itself’, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘known within that ordering’.

**¶3** The twelf[t]h seal that is in it is its five shining men that are conjoined and enmeshed through the entire zone; because every place where they are cramped, and every place where they have space, is displayed by that wheel. For the root of all the firmaments above and below, and that of their rulers, is ensnared and fixed in it.

**¶4** The observation [of the wheel] is set right at all times opposite the great King o[f Honour in his place] so that fear, wh[ich is from him] will assume all the powers of mixture [and will hold] their root affixed there, so that they cannot make an esc[a]pe from the fetter of their bond.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence concerns the perpetual ‘observation’ stationed opposite the King; specifying ‘of the wheel’ keeps the focus on the same ultimate instrument of disclosure and restraint.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Restores the fixed throne-setting of the King of Honour without devotional overlay; ‘in his place’ expresses the stable Divine presence before which the observation stands.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the ‘fear’ is not terror but awe proceeding from the Divine presence; thus it ‘is from him’, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘rising from the Divine presence’.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The fear seizes the mixed powers and also maintains their fastening; ‘and will hold’ supplies the operative continuation leading to ‘their root affixed there’.

**¶5** That wheel rese[m]bles, in this respect, a chain in front of the chief guard, for when [anything moves] in th[ose who are guarded] in the [chain]in.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The simile needs the trigger-condition: whenever any motion toward disorder occurs among those restrained, it is immediately detected—matching the spiritual translation’s ‘whenever anything stirs toward disorder’.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The referent is the restrained rulers and powers under vigilance. ‘Guarded’ keeps the impersonal custodial register (chief guard) rather than moralized ‘saved’.
> *¶5 #4 [strong]:* Completes the repeated image: wheel resembles a chain; later clause refers to ‘in the … in the chain’.

**¶6** This is also the case for all the rulers who are in the firmaments, and every power of the zone: If they should wish to escape, they shall be recognised and revealed by that wheel. [As] the wheel is like a great mirror, for [the di]scrimination of all things [set] in it.

> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* Restores the clause ‘the discrimination of all things is set in it’, i.e., the distinguishing faculty is established within the wheel, aligning with the spiritual translation’s ‘power to distinguish… is present within it’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are recoverable because the chapter is structurally repetitive: the ‘wheel’ is the ultimate restraint and disclosure before the King of Honour; the ‘seals’ are discriminative constraints; attempted escape or hidden perversion becomes immediately manifest. Where only a locative or brief connective was missing (e.g., ‘there’, ‘itself’), restorations are tight. A few fills remain moderate because the exact adverbial wording is not uniquely forced, but the correspondential function (immediate manifestation within the ultimate ordering) strongly constrains the sense.

---

## Chapter 37: Concerning the Three Zones.

**¶2** When he built and constructed the worlds, he suspended the zones on their zones, and within another zone also. The first zone is the five powers of darkness, males and females.

**¶3** This is why he hung one zone on a zone! He hung the five powers of Darkness on the five shining men, the sons of the Man; because all the enmity is entwined and bound in them.

**¶4** Also, within the zone are the five sons of the [Living] Spirit; because the entire edifice is suspended from them, it being [as they heard]ard (?) him too, the oth[others who came] after them.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The substrate sense here is an impersonal ordering of supports: first the primary five are established, and then it is said that they were “heard” or “acknowledged.” The phrase needs to yield “it being … heard him too,” i.e., the edifice depends on them, “as they heard” him. This keeps the cosmological-register verb of perception without introducing exhortation.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* The passage contrasts the first supports with what follows: “the others … after them.” The spiritual context requires a subsequent order of subordinate supporters established beneath the first. “others who came” provides that sequential, structural sense (procession and ordering) without later community language.

**¶5** And this is the one who came to [the heights, to]rmaments above [that] which is in one zone, is the great [King of Honour] who e[exists and dwells] within the [inmost depth] of the universe.

> *¶5 #1 [tentative]:* The One “came to” an interior height governing the firmaments. In the substrate this is descent or approach into the higher structural degrees. “the heights, to” fits the required motion into the upper interiors while remaining cosmological and non-devotional.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* A minimal connective is demanded to bind “firmaments above” to the following relative clause “which is in one zone.”
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense identifies the supreme authority present within the inmost. The clause requires a verb of being with an inward abiding. “exists and dwells” preserves impersonal cosmological presence (immanence) rather than a salvific visitation.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The final gap must name the interior-most region “of the universe” where this One is present. The correspondential requirement is the inmost plane that holds all from within; “inmost depth” states that function in oldest-layer register (structural interiority).

**Assessment:** Most lacunae can be restored at the level of structural correspondential requirements: ordering degree upon degree, primary supports, subsequent supports, and an inmost governing presence. Exact surface wording in ¶4–¶5 remains only moderately constrained, but the demanded functions (acknowledgement of the first supports; later supports following; descent to higher interiors; inmost indwelling) allow coherent restorations in the oldest cosmological register.

## Chapter 38: Concerning the Light Mind and the Apostles and the Saints

**¶1** You have told us that the Light Mi[nd] is this one who shall come and assume the saints. [You sai]d it to us like this: He too is one among the gods [and] many gods are with him. You have also told us: [W]hen he enters withi[n the body of] the flesh and binds the ol[d ma]n with his five counsels, he [set]s his five [couns]els upon him in the five limbs [of his] body.

> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* The questioner is troubled that the Light Mind is said to be "one among the gods"; the oldest substrate typically adds the simple coordinating link: he is among them, and yet many accompany him.

**¶2** [So], no[w], where is he? In that the old man is chained in the body! For I see how rebellions arise there despite his bondage, from time to time.

**¶3** Also, secondly, I a[s]k you: If then he is a great God, unchanging and immeasurable, how could he come and appear in the smallness of the bo[dy]?

**¶4** The third thing I want you to recount to [me the third thing] a holy one is the Mind, and he is pure; [how could he enter and not be defiled by] the defilement of this body?

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The syntax requires the direct object of "recount" and the enumerated sequence. The substrate voice is procedural: “tell me the third thing”.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially this is the purity problem: how the holy Mind can be conjoined to what is impure without taking on its state.

**¶5** Fourthly: If truly the Light Mind exists in [the s]aints, why is his likeness not displayed to us, the way that he is?

**¶6** The fifth thing I want, for you to tell me and explain about your apostola[te]. Look, it is not clear to me! For they oppress and persecute you in the world.

**¶9** All that you have unveiled you have [declared] in our presence. Yet, I wanted to kno[w also about the] L[igh]t Mind, the way that he is.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Matches the recap: “All that you have unveiled you have declared in our presence.” This is the impersonal report style of the substrate.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* He asks to know about the Light Mind “the way that he is”; the connective “also about the” is required after “wanted to know”.

**¶10** If I shall repeat [again] to you about these things that you are so set upon, and they become true f[o]r you after you are so set upon them, will you understand what you [seek]? On your account I will give vision to they who see! I will make the living fountain overflow for the thirsty, that they may drink and liv[e].

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* Sets up the conditional: “If I shall repeat again to you…” typical instructional turn.
> *¶10 #3 [strong]:* “will you understand what you seek?” aligns with the spiritual sense: insist until it becomes living, then understand what you seek.

**¶11** All the error, when the en[e]my of the lights constructed it, he constructed after the likeness of a man. The head of the universe is the beginning of the garments. [H]is neck is the nape of the garm[ents]. His stomach is the five unfolded ones (?), which a[the foundation] of the garm[e]nts. His ribs are all the firmament[s]. His navel is the sphere of the stars and the signs of the zodiac. And, also, [the par]ts [that] come from his navel to his hip are [the extensions] that come from the sphere t[o the corne]rs of the four worlds. His loins are the th[ree eart]hs that are below [the abysses; they are placed] upon the head of the Porter. His [thighs are the roads from the loins] from the [hip] to the earth upon which the Porter [is stood] firm. His shins and his feet a[are the seas] and the entire zone that belongs [to it]

> *¶11 #4 [moderate]:* The “five unfolded ones” are being identified as a structural support of the garments. “Foundation of the garments” fits the body-as-cosmic-structure mapping.
> *¶11 #9 [moderate]:* From navel to hip = what extends from the middle ordering to the extremes; “extensions” fits the outspreading influence in the spiritual translation.
> *¶11 #12 [moderate]:* Loins correspond to the lower discrete degrees; they rest upon the Porter. “Below … upon the head of the Porter” demands mention of the deep lower region and their placement.
> *¶11 #13 [tentative]:* Keeps the anatomical progression: loins → thighs → shins/feet, corresponding to descending extensions into ultimates.
> *¶11 #14 [moderate]:* Needed for the phrase “from the … to the earth upon which the Porter…”.
> *¶11 #16 [tentative]:* Shins and feet correspond to the ultimate spread in externals; in this cosmography seas are the lowest expanses alongside the final zone.
> *¶11 #17 [strong]:* Completes: “and the entire zone that belongs to it.”

**¶12** ⟨p.91⟩
His heart is mankind. His liver is the four-footed animals. His lung is the race of birds that [fly in] the air. His spleen is the race of fish that swim in the waters. His kidneys are the world of reptiles that creep [up]on the earth. His outer skin is the wall that [which] surrounds the piercing and the great fire. His [the inner skin is the firmament] the vessels of the great fire. His [entrails are] of darkness. His gall is the [His] great intestine is the breadth of the great [bitterness is the ruler] of the worlds. His veins [firmament] all [the spri]ngs and wells. His e[eyes are the two luminaries] His feet are his [soles]

> *¶12 #5 [tentative]:* Introduces the next identifications (vessels, darkness, gall, intestines).
> *¶12 #7 [tentative]:* Gall corresponds to bitter ruling motive; “ruler” keeps the cosmological register rather than moralizing.

**¶13** This is how each of the worlds h[ave been ha]rmonised (?). Five gods are fastened in him. [And] they are his soul and his life [In them] man is a sinner [also] the Living Spirit and the Ambassador [yet] the five sleepless guardians.

> *¶13 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the inference from the cosmic body description to the equilibrium statement.

He has humbled [n the third firmament] also of the Adamant of Lig[h]t, who is the insight [in the fifth firmament] from {} he has given [to him] because of lust [He has] also appointed the King of Glory, who is the coun[sel and] patience over the three images; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. > *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* Opens the list of overseers placed over the cosmic structure: Keeper of Splendour etc.
> *¶14 #2 [moderate]:* Matches the fixed-in-camp motif; these powers are stationed and fixed.

> *¶14 #5 [strong]:* Required article before “whole … of the powers…”.

> *¶14 #19 [moderate]:* Porter corresponds to consideration and wisdom in the lowest region; restores the doctrinal mapping.
> *¶14 #20 [moderate]:* Foundation below corresponds to the lowest plane under the three earths, consistent with ¶23 and ¶28.

Furthermore: The Ambassador refined from them five intellectuals of life. Also, the summons and the obedience were situated there. Now, they made six sons of the Living Spirit, together with the six sons of the First Man!

**¶16** Further: The Ambassador placed in them the great Mind; who is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man.

**¶17** Furthermore: Jesus the Youth was set there; who is the image of the living word, of the utterance and the obedience.

**¶18** He has made strong these camps, those above and those below [by their ordinances], (so that) each one of them will be secure in the circuit of his watch-district, so that neither uprising nor treachery are made in his watch-district.

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* The strengthening is by fixed ordinances so each province remains secure—impersonal substrate term.

> *¶19 #4 [moderate]:* Keeps the sequence: treachery, uprising, Sin aborts, tangles, mixes, then moves and insinuates; “and it was” bridges to the next action.

They have revealed crafts in the world and have unveiled to people the mysteries of heaven. An uprising came about, and a destruction, on the earth [and great ruin was added] to it.

> *¶20 #2 [tentative]:* “He is made … with wrath” needs the infinitive idea: thought turned toward nothingness or negation; but here the phrase is idiomatic: “made full of wrath.” Next lacuna provides “full”.

> *¶21 #1 [moderate]:* Identifies Adamant’s function as insight, matching the fivefold mapping later (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration).
> *¶21 #2 [moderate]:* Restores “set firm upon …” as his camp or station.

> *¶23 #2 [moderate]:* Corresponds to “uprising of the abysses below”.
> *¶23 #3 [moderate]:* Sets up: “bent, the fastenings underneath were loosened”.

Certainly he is cruel, with great cunning; until the Light Mind finds how to humble this body, and drive it [according to] his pleasure.

So, also, is the watch-district of the Mind, which is the body of the flesh. Sin raises [up], from time to time, its agitations in the body.

Then they seize [it at once, and bind it, since] they have acted first with a righteous judgement.

As it is set fast, wors[hipping] the things that [are in the world] will not remain; making obeisance [to] idols of wood and gold and silver [and stone] worshipping beasts that are unclean and polluted! They are ugly in their appearances and their forms.

> *¶36 #4 [strong]:* These powers “pronouncing… continually leading”; the missing verb describes their constant insinuation.

It became hostile to its father [and it became hostile to] itself, being wicked [and to its mother within] its own light [and it extinguished]
