> *¶38 #2 [moderate]:* He assumes it “in the body” as the locus of bondage.
> *¶38 #3 [strong]:* Introduces “by his wisdom”.
> *¶38 #4 [moderate]:* Introduces the result: he becomes for it…
> *¶38 #5 [strong]:* He becomes “power over the bonds” and members.

**¶39** ⟨p.97⟩
This is how he shall release the members of the soul, and make them free from the five members of sin. Conversely, these five members of sin, which were loose, he shall bind them.

**¶40** He shall set right the members of the soul; form and purify them, and construct a new man of them, a child of righteousness. [And] when [he] fashions and const[r]ucts and purifies the new man; then he shall bring forth five great living members out from the five great memb[ers]. And he places them in the members of the new man. He shall place his m[ind], which is love, in the mind of the new man. Also, the th[oug]ht, which is faith, he shall pl[ace in] the thought of the new man; whom he shall purify. His insight, which [is perfection, he shall place] in the insight of the new man. His counsel, which [is] patience, he shall place in his counsel. Also, [w]isdom, which is his consideration, in the consideration of the new m[an]. He shall make the image of the word pure from the word of sin; and he adds to it his word, so that his [utterance] becomes nourisher and strengthener [of the new man]

> *¶40 #12 [moderate]:* After adding his word, what is received becomes “nourisher and strengthener”; the substrate term paired with “word” here is “utterance”.
> *¶40 #13 [moderate]:* Completes: “so that his utterance of the new man becomes nourisher…”

**¶41** Now, when he shall perf[ect also the tw]elve members. This [is how he shall perfect] and his wisdom. His [mind] becomes righteous, as he perf[ects the twelve members] While formerly he was running [in the way of error;] but instead of this, now he runs [in righteousness,] his road and his path and his [ascent;] also [he mounts up] to the heights, to the great aeons [and he is joined] thus the old man is bound in the [in the body] and his lust, his [craft,] his foolishness in these five members of the bo[body in his five members] the dark spirit is imprisoned with them in a bond and in severe misery.

> *¶41 #1 [moderate]:* The process is brought to fullness “and perfect also the twelve members” (twelve as fullness of organized truths).
> *¶41 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the description of the righteous state as he perfects the whole.
> *¶41 #3 [moderate]:* “and his wisdom. His … becomes righteous” needs “mind”: mind and wisdom as the interior pair being rectified.
> *¶41 #4 [moderate]:* Repeats the completion statement to close the clause.
> *¶41 #5 [moderate]:* Formerly running in disorder; spiritual translation: ran in track of disorder.
> *¶41 #6 [moderate]:* Now runs in ordered way.
> *¶41 #7 [strong]:* “his road and his path and his ascent”.
> *¶41 #9 [moderate]:* Ascent results in conjunction with higher aeons or communities.
> *¶41 #10 [strong]:* Old man bound “in the body” as ultimate restraint.
> *¶41 #11 [strong]:* Triad: lust, craft, foolishness.
> *¶41 #12 [moderate]:* Completes the sentence: “in these five members of the body …”

**¶42** Also, the new man reigns by his love, by hi[s] faith, by his perfection, by his patience, and by his wisdom. Yet, his king [is] the Light Mind, he w[ho is] king over the totality. He re[igns] o[ver it according to] his pleasure. While the members [are set fast], thus sin is imprisoned. Still, the Light Mind is king, and an affliction may arise in the body from t[im]e to time.

> *¶42 #2 [strong]:* “Yet, his king is the Light Mind”
> *¶42 #6 [strong]:* “While the members …, thus sin is imprisoned” requires the fixing of the members in order.

**¶43** There are occasions when sin shall mount up with its foolishness; and it disturbs the consideration, and it clouds the wisdom and the understanding of the person. It causes truth to be split into doubt in him, and he utters some foo[li]sh words and some [empty deeds]

> *¶43 #2 [moderate]:* Parallel to “foolish words”: foolish deeds arising from clouded understanding.

**¶51** [T]hen, have I taught you, and opened your (sg.) [eyes], as to how confusion shall arise in the zo[ne], in the camps of these great mig[hty] gods? Again, this is how disturbance shall come about, from ti[me] to time, in the [camps] of the [Li]ght [M]ind.

> *¶51 #6 [strong]:* “confusion… in the zone… Again… disturbance… in the camps of the Light Mind” matches the structural mapping of provinces.

**¶52** So al[so] these watch-keepers, for they are set [as] fi[rm in] the zone, (but) they are not visible. Again, this too is the way of the Light Mind, [for he is] not visible in the body.

> *¶52 #2 [strong]:* “for they are set as firm…”

**¶53** Also, [corr]esponding to th[ese] outer [watch-keepers]: in that while they are great, [y]et have they contorted themselves. They have [bent themselves down], they have become small, to the measure of the task that is apportioned to each o[n]e [of t]hem. Again, this too is like the Mind, himse[lf] a great one, and exalted; but he is bent over and [has] become small, to this little worthless [bo]dy.

> *¶53 #5 [strong]:* Core point: great powers “contorted themselves” by accommodation; “bent themselves down” is the substrate phrasing.

**¶54** [Even so] the gods in the outer [z]one: they are transcendent and purified, they are set [f]a[st in] the mingling of the totality, (but) are not defiled. [This too is like the] Light [Mind]. While he is placed in hi[s body of flesh,]

> *¶54 #7 [moderate]:* Completes the comparison: as pure gods are not defiled in mingling, so the Light Mind is not defiled while placed in the fleshly body.

**¶55** ⟨p.100⟩
Again, behold the mightiness and the activity of the Light Mind: how vast he is over all the watch-districts of the body! He stays fast at his camp. He shuts off all deliberations of the body from the beguilements of Sin. He limits them and distributes them out. He sets them down at his pleasure.

**¶57** I have taught you] the deeds of the [Light] Mind! Whoever has an open and percept[ive] eye, [he can] appear to him. Whoever has not that eye, he can not appear to him.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations and are secure. The substantial gaps cluster in the cosmic-body catalogue (¶11–12) and the appointment list (¶14); there the correspondential structure constrains functions (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration; discrete degrees; stations in firmaments), but exact cosmographic nouns are sometimes only tentatively recoverable. Where the spiritual translation fixed a required relation (ascent-paths, confinement in camps, seizing and binding after permission), restorations are strong to moderate; where the manuscript likely had detailed technical terms for zones, seas, skins, vessels, and firmaments, confidence drops to tentative.

---

## Chapter 39: Concerning the Three Days and the Two Deaths

**¶4** The first day is our father, the Man. From the time when the First Man went down to the abyss of darkness and drowned in it; and the Living Spirit was sent, who went and bore him up. After the Man had ascended, the Father of Life and the Great Spirit set things in order, and the worlds above and below.

**¶5** Furthermore, the glorious Ambassador came and dwelt in the ship of the day. He displayed his glorious image, and he purified the light out of all things. He walked in the heights of the heavens. Till the time when the world will be dissolved, all things destroyed, the great fire released, and the Last Statue ascends. These three deeds were begun on this first day, when they sent the First Man with his five sons.

**¶6** ⟨p.103⟩ In contrast, [the first death] is the darkness that mixed with the five sons of the First Man.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Matches the chapter’s antithetical structure: three ‘days’ (states) versus two ‘deaths’ (perishing states). Here it names the first perishing, i.e., Darkness mixing into the First Man’s powers.

**¶7** The second day is the time when the fathers of light, who were victorious in the struggle, will sit on their thrones in the new aeon and dwell on the new earth. And they reign in the new aeon till the Father unveils his image above them.

**¶8** The {length of the sojourn} in the new aeon equals the measure of the first day spent in affliction. They will reign like this and rejoice in the new aeon.

**¶9** The great third day [is the time] when the Father will unveil over them his image ... ... and he raises them up [above them his image] and he receives them into his hidden treasury ... and he gives [to the life] his soul. He can pour out upon them ... his ambrosia and his sweet perfume, which takes away all affliction that they have seen with their eyes. [to them of] fullness and a joy eternal! When his grace would sate them, he gives to them his [of his own] and they are made anew in them.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Sets the third ‘day’ as a distinct state, parallel to ¶7 ‘The second day is the time…’.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* The third state is defined by the Father’s unveiling. The correspondential translation requires a repeated clause of ‘unveiling his image over them’, not a new topic.
> *¶9 #3 [tentative]:* After unveiling, the next act is elevation into higher reception. In the substrate register this is commonly rendered as being raised ‘to the life’ (life as the higher degree), before being received into the treasury.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* The line ‘and he gives … his soul’ demands a prepositional turn: the Father imparts something ‘to them of his soul’, i.e., of his own life-substance in correspondential terms.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* Completes the idiom ‘of his own soul’, emphasizing that what is communicated is from the Father’s own life, not an external gift.
> *¶9 #6 [tentative]:* Before ‘fullness and a joy eternal!’ the sense requires an introductory particle so the phrase reads as a resultant state: ‘a fullness…’.

**¶10** Also, he can summon them once more in peace and silence, and perfect their stature in same measure as his first aeons. And he sets each power in its aeon, in its dwelling-place. They will dwell in the new aeon in their aeons!

**¶11** Now, when they dwell in their aeons, they will become rich beyond measure, for ever, beyond time. From then on they will not count that season amongst them; nor the number of the days, nor the hours, generation after generation, for ever and ever. The Father will not be hidden from them, from this time on. Rather, he has accomplished (his) unveiling to them [once;] he being revealed to them for ever.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* The unveiling is described as completed ‘once’ (decisively), after which revelation is continuous. This preserves the impersonal, process-register: unveiling as an accomplished act with lasting effect.

**¶12** These are the three great days, [which the Father of Greatness has given number to in the reckoning].

> *¶12 #1 [strong]:* Accepted as fitting the oldest-layer register: impersonal ordering, numbering, and measure. It summarizes the three states as a complete ordered process.

**¶13** In contrast, the two deaths that I have proclaimed to you are these.

**¶14** ⟨p.104⟩ The first death is from the time when the light fell to the darkness, and was mixed in with the rulers of darkness; until the time when the light will become pure, and be separated from the darkness in that great fire. The remainder left behind there can build and add to that Last Statue.

**¶15** Now, the second death is the death in which the souls of sinful men shall die; when [they will] be stripped of the shining light that illuminates the world. And also they are separated from the living air, from which they receive [living] breath; and they are deprived of this living soul, which [had been placed] in the world; and they are separated and swept away from this Last Statue [and they are seized] and bound on [their own bonds] the female will become [female of lust] their torture and their affliction; for their deeds [have set them in] those three places;

> *¶15 #3 [tentative]:* The ‘living soul’ here is the life-faculty present for reception in the world. ‘Had been placed in the world’ preserves the substrate’s impersonal cosmology: life is stationed as a principle, not morally ‘given’.
> *¶15 #4 [moderate]:* After being swept away from the Last Statue, the opposite process is arrest and detention. ‘Seized’ matches the correspondential sense of being held fast in their own bonds.
> *¶15 #5 [moderate]:* Completes the detention image demanded by the spiritual translation: cast away from the final order and held fast in self-made constraints.
> *¶15 #6 [tentative]:* In the oldest cosmological register, the ‘female’ functions as the corrupted will and concupiscence in ultimates. ‘Female of lust’ makes the correspondence explicit without later pastoral moralizing.
> *¶15 #7 [moderate]:* Their deeds fix them into the infernal series. ‘Have set them in those three places’ expresses determination by one’s own works into a completed order of states.

**¶16** These are the two deaths. The first death is temporal; [but] the second death is eternal. It is the sec[ond deat]h!

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the chapter’s antithetical architecture (three days versus two deaths) and by the correspondential sequence: unveiling, elevation, reception into treasury, communication of the Father’s own life, satisfaction and renewal. The most uncertain restorations are in ¶9’s compact gift-formulas and in ¶15’s punitive imagery, where the substrate likely used fixed technical phrases now only recoverable in approximate form.

---

## Chapter 40: Concerning the Three Things that were established by the Light

**¶2** Three things would be fashioned by the Light through necessity. For there can be no other way nor other result, to allow them to stay without coming about. For the darkness, the enemy, must be bound by them; he who stood up against the Light from the beginning. Due to this, these things have no (other) result, to allow them to stay without coming about.

**¶3** Now, the first thing is the new luminous earth, which was set and built on top of this earth [and no one will be able to build upon] it, namely that alien body [which will not be able to be] built upon it.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The first outcome is the new ultimate plane set above the former. The gap must express that what is foreign to the order cannot again take footing on the old ultimate once the new one is established above it. The immediate continuation “namely that alien body … built upon it” demands a verb of building with a negation: the new luminous earth prevents further construction upon the old earth.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* This completes the clause: “namely that alien body [ ... ] built upon it.” The spiritual sense requires incapacity and exclusion (the foreign cannot support itself on the new order). A short auxiliary phrase of inability fits the correspondential demand and keeps the register impersonal and structural.

**¶4** The second thing is this: They will [shut him up] and they [will bind him in] the new earth, the coffin [and the prison] ⟨p.105⟩ of the dark fire, the companion of the enemy.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The second outcome is confinement of the destructive lust with its companion falsity. The verb required is an act of final containment applied to the enemy: not exhortation, but cosmic restraint. “Shut him up” matches the necessity-language of binding and the later “coffin” and “prison” imagery.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Parallelism in the line calls for a second constraining verb after “They will … and they … the new earth”. The spiritual sense is twofold: enclosure and fastening. “Bind him in” strengthens the notion that the new earth itself becomes the containing form.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* The apposition “the new earth, the coffin … of the dark fire” wants an additional identification term for the containing form. In the oldest substrate the new formation is both ‘coffin’ (closure) and ‘prison’ (incarceration). This anticipates ¶6’s explicit “which is the prison of the enemy.”

**¶5** The third thing is the souls of all the sinners who have been condemned by their deeds. They were [judged, and there is] over them woe; they have been condemned according to the wicked deeds they had committed, and due to this they will be crucified on the enemy, he who had raised himself up against the greatness.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence requires a judicial fixation of state before “woe”. The spiritual sense: they are determined by their own deeds, and grief is over them because of confirmed opposition. “Judged, and there is over them woe” keeps the impersonal, correspondential tone (state according to works) without later moralizing additions.

**¶6** They will seal up this final lump when all the likenesses and images of every shape will be nailed in it. Also, those will be bound by this last fetter for eternity, and be placed as a footstool and a mat and a rag for this coffin [and prison] which is the prison of the enemy. It will be placed, being a boundary-marker and a great void between the two, between the darkness and the enemy, so that they cannot reach one another, nor can they hear their voices; rather they will become sterile as they are separated from each other for ever and ever.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* The phrase “for this coffin [ ... ] which is the prison of the enemy” needs a linking noun so that the coffin is explicitly also the incarcerating vessel. “and prison” produces the classic substrate pairing of ‘coffin’ and ‘prison’ as one containing boundary-form, matching the final separation logic.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of function demanded by the correspondential sequence: (1) a higher ultimate plane is set; (2) the enemy and his dark fire are enclosed and fastened within it; (3) the condemned are judged and fixed to the opposing power; (4) the containing form is explicitly a coffin-prison boundary. Exact verb choices in ¶3 #2 are less tightly constrained because the surviving syntax is minimal, but the required meaning is clear: incapacity of the alien element to establish itself on the new order.

---

## Chapter 41: Concerning the Three Blows that befell the Enemy because of the Light.

**¶1** The Darkness itself, the Enemy, has received three bad blows with three wars; and worse dangers from the Light in those three wars.

**¶2** The first: (The enemy) was brought out to the middle and separated from his dark earth, whence he had departed. He was vanquished in that first war, caught and [bound] by the living soul.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The first war ends in restraint by the Living Soul: the enemy is seized, restrained, held fast. “Bound” matches the substrate register of fettering and capture.

**¶3** The second blow is the time when he will be dissolved and melted, and he perishes in this great fire. He is destroyed and overthrown, from the images that are all by which he exists. And he will be gathered in [to the] fetter corresponding to his archetype, and be made like he was from the first beginning.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The second blow culminates in being “gathered” into a fetter that corresponds to his own archetypal pattern. The directional movement is into confinement; “to the fetter” is the demanded construction.

**¶4** The third blow that will befall the Enemy is the 'rolling back' [at the] end when everything will be separated, and male will be divided [from] female. Now, the male will be bound in the lump, but the female [will be thrown] in to the tomb. For it will be divided [and be divided apart, and be set apart] stone in their midst, for generation upon generation, for ever and ever.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* The ‘rolling back’ is specified as occurring at the completion of the cycle; “at the end” is the fixed eschatological marker in this substrate’s impersonal cosmology.
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* The separation is a discrimination of active from receptive within evil (male from female). The required preposition is the one of severance: “divided from”.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Opposition of outcomes: the male is “bound” (constrained in a lump); the female is not merely placed but cast into a tomb-like shutness. “Thrown into the tomb” preserves the harsh, impersonal finality of the substrate image.
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* The sense demands an absolute, irreversible separation established by a barrier: not a mere physical stone, but a fixed partition ‘in their midst’ across successive generations. The restored verbs make explicit the decisive severing that the great stone effects.

**¶5** ⟨p.106⟩ This is the way that the binding of the enemy will come about, in a fetter that is burdensome and strong, one from which there is never escape; because [they have achieved] his binding and bound him for ever. And they have achieved his being set apart, and he has been set apart for ever.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The close is a confirmation formula: the powers of Light have fully effected the binding and separation ‘for ever’. “They have achieved” preserves the accomplished, juridical-cosmic tone rather than devotional language.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are tightly constrained by the chapter’s fixed three-stage defeat pattern (exposure and capture, dissolution and consolidation into a fitting fetter, final separation and irreversible partition). Only ¶4 #4 is genuinely open-ended because the surviving syntax allows several equivalent ways to express the same correspondential requirement (an unremovable separation established by the great stone).

---

## Chapter 42: Concerning the Three Vessels

**¶2** Happen you know, my beloved ones, that when these vessels were discharged, they were discharged in [great] slaughter, but those [took out] these vessels from them.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The opening of the corrupted receptacles is said to occur in violent conflict in the lowest degree; the idiom in this layer is ‘discharged in great slaughter’.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the separation is effected ‘from within them’; the verb needed is an extracting action upon the vessels’ contents, not a moral agent. ‘took out’ matches the impersonal substrate register.

**¶3** ⟨p.107⟩
They are like the form of a man who has fallen into a purulent sickness. Other wounds and diseases are in his body, and even bile and other poisons are in his inner limbs. He achieves good health by a wise doctor, who gives him one remedy to drink that causes his purulent sickness to break out above, and gives him another medicine to drink that causes his sickness to discharge below, and he gives him yet another medicine to drink that cures his wounds for him [and makes him rest] calmly and silently.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* After the three medicines (upward manifestation, downward discharge, then healing), the close is quiet restoration ‘calmly and silently’; thus a clause of settling into rest/order fits.

**¶4** Through the skill of a wise doctor, with his effective medicines to consume that take away all sicknesses in this manner and dissolve all magic, the doctors shall [discover] they work cures through the aroma of the medicine, other wounds [remove] what is hidden, they at last tear them out from [the inner limbs] manifested above and below [and manifested above and below, by the] the aroma of edible medicines that were placed upon them.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts hidden and manifest; the doctor’s art ‘discovers’ what is concealed so it can be removed.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Parallel to the prior clause: once perceived by the ‘aroma’, the remaining wounds are removed.
> *¶4 #3 [tentative]:* What is ‘hidden’ corresponds to interior faculties; the natural image is the patient’s inner parts.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation requires a twofold manifestation (outer and lower) accomplished ‘by’ the gentle assimilated influx (aroma) of the medicine.

**¶5** This is also what these vessels of water, of darkness and of fire are like, which are hidden in the body of all the rulers above; while the Living Spirit is like the wise doctor. These three healing drugs are the three garments that the Living Spirit assumes upon his body: of wind, of water, and of fire. Through them he has set in order the things below. For by those three garments he has been displayed to the rulers who are above, with hard-heartedness, with shaking and fear, with derangement; from them he discharged the three vessels, those of water, of darkness and of fire.

**¶6** The case of these vessels is just like a child who is in his mother's womb and is swathed by seven garments. The first is the marrow, the second is the bones, the third is the tendons, the fourth is the flesh, the fifth the veins, the sixth the blood, the seventh the skin of the body that covers him. The other garment also [that is upon him] this child. The marrow and the bones, the sinews and flesh, the veins and blood and skin, together with the garment that she wears and the linen that swathes her, total nine. Together with the nine of the child, these and the others, they shall complete and make eighteen garments. Now, you shall find the soul of this child is entwined within all these.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The missing phrase must introduce the mother’s additional covering as an outer garment upon the child within the womb, before shifting to ‘His mother also is just like this child’.

**¶7** ⟨p.108⟩
And look see: When the smell of the action of the demon, which brings about the stench of birth, will reveal the child; immediately [the seven garments] shall [be loosened] all [these things] and it emerges from a crack that has become visible, while no tear nor wound occurs in the body of the child. In the same way as this child opens up the womb of his mother, so is it also with these vessels of water, of darkness and of fire. The actions that open up his mother to the child, and she shakes him out and reveals him; these are the three living garments that the Living Spirit assumed on his body.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The immediate yielding of ‘encompassing restraints’ in the spiritual sense corresponds to the seven fetal coverings just enumerated.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* The restraints ‘yield in ordered fashion’; ‘be loosened’ gives the mechanical, non-devotional substrate action.

**¶8** For by the aspect of the garment of water [he drew out the vessel of water] from the rulers; he moved [from them,] they are the poison and death with which their veins and entrails are filled.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* This paragraph treats the water-garment’s operation and explicitly says ‘from the rulers’; thus it must name the expulsion of the water-vessel here.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* Completes the required movement ‘drew out … from the rulers’ before describing what it contains as poison and death.

**¶9** By the aspect of the garment of wind, in which he was revealed [to the rulers;] he has swept away and erased the entire destructive shadow and dirt, discharged it down to the earth. Afterwards he swept it out and confined it in the vessel of darkness that surrounds the universe.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The wind-garment is the mode of revelation ‘to’ the rulers; the spiritual sense is manifestation of clarifying discernment confronting dominant powers.

**¶10** Also, by the garment of living fire, through its splendour and vigour, he discharged the fire of the darkness from all the rulers and threw it down upon this earth. He swept it another time from the earth and confined it in the vessel that surrounds all the worlds, and they call it the wall of great fire.

**¶11** Now, those three garments, through which he established these three things, he did not peel them off all at once; but he stripped them off one by one at the appropriate time [when he had cast them] down to the earth [and set each one in its vessel.]

> *¶11 #1 [tentative]:* The successive stripping is tied to the securing of the expelled materials; thus a temporal clause referring to the casting-down is required.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense: removal ‘as each lower arrangement was secured in its place’; in the natural narrative this is ‘set each in its vessel/boundary’.

**¶12** In this way the universe of the [lower world] his vessels [was guarded through] these vessels like [fetters and measures.]

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* The lacuna names the organized totality preserved by these operations; ‘universe of the lower world’ matches the substrate cosmological register.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* Needs a governing verb for preservation from being overwhelmed; ‘was guarded through’ fits impersonal exposition.
> *¶12 #3 [tentative]:* The spiritual translation emphasizes fixed limits and restraints; ‘fetters’ is already used later and belongs to this layer’s vocabulary.

**¶13** The use of the matter, and the management of these vessels that were swept out and discharged from above to earth, have brought an advantage and benefit of twelve great deeds. Afterwards, they were swept from the earth and pushed outside to the edges of the worlds and to the {extremity}.

**¶14** The first deed is this: So that evil-doing does not increase in the universe, neither in the creations nor in the trees [because]tself up in three things.

> *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* The clause explains why evil would not increase: ‘because it has set itself up in three things’.

**¶15** The [The first is lust.]

> *¶15 #1 [tentative]:* Within the triad of corrupt forces, the first distinguished member in this context is lust (root of increase in ‘trees’ and ‘flesh’ in the following argument).

**¶16** The second is error [in the rulers above.] Indeed, about this I have spoken to you as I [saying,] For, if it were so, if [the vessel of water] had not been swept down from above, but rather the waters existing up till now in the rulers above [the] torrents that would flow [from above;] and the droplets that would [drip] down, so lust would have sprouted [up]n the trees and in the fruits as well as all flesh [in] down [till]oday [and] you could not find a single person [holy,] in the [whole world;] a saint does [if] is master of [them.]

> *¶16 #1 [moderate]:* The broken opening of the ‘second deed’ requires specifying the location of the retained waters/falsities: ‘in the rulers above’.
> *¶16 #2 [tentative]:* Introduces the explanatory discourse ‘Indeed, about this I have spoken…’.
> *¶16 #5 [moderate]:* Completes ‘the torrents that would flow …’ as descending influx from the higher region.
> *¶16 #6 [strong]:* Pairs with ‘torrents’ as the smaller, continual descent: ‘droplets that would drip down’.
> *¶16 #7 [strong]:* Completes ‘would have sprouted up’ (growth of lust in ultimates).
> *¶16 #8 [strong]:* Restores the preposition in ‘all flesh in …’.
> *¶16 #9 [moderate]:* Temporal continuation: ‘down till today’.
> *¶16 #10 [tentative]:* Connects the consequence clauses in the rhetorical chain.
> *¶16 #11 [moderate]:* Matches spiritual sense: scarcely anyone ‘in whom anything of holiness could be preserved’.
> *¶16 #12 [tentative]:* The scope is universal in the spiritual translation (‘present state’ broadly); ‘whole world’ fits the natural idiom here.
> *¶16 #13 [tentative]:* Introduces the conditional continuation: ‘a saint does …’.
> *¶16 #14 [moderate]:* Completes ‘is master of them’—the assaults/streams just described.

**¶17** Not only this, but if the vessel of darkness had not been swept out and thrown down to the earth, nor swept from the earth and cast outside the region of the worlds into this pit; again the drops of darkness, which will drip down from it, and the torrents that will continue to flow down from above to the earth and assume the trees and all flesh, would have multiplied, and the forgetfulness that is in a thousand people today you would have found in just one person. There is not one among mankind [who can keep] him [that in] the thought and the knowledge of the [God.]

> *¶17 #1 [tentative]:* The clause requires the possibility of retaining Divine thought/knowledge; ‘who can keep’ expresses that minimal remnant function.
> *¶17 #2 [moderate]:* Connects ‘among mankind … him …’ as ‘that in him’.
> *¶17 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation specifies ‘what is Divine’; the substrate register here uses ‘God’ without Christian overlay as the Divine principle.

**¶18** Furthermore, if the fire were in the rulers of the worlds of the heavens until today, and had not been swept out and cast down to the earth, and swept from the earth and shut up inside the vessel of fire that surrounds the worlds; [the fury of] this fire [would pour down] above through the torrent [and would surge up] below;

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘its fury would have poured down’; thus ‘the fury of this fire…’.
> *¶18 #2 [moderate]:* Matches the two-direction movement demanded: from above downward and from below upward.
> *¶18 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the antiphonal motion: downward torrent above, upward torrent below.

**¶19** ⟨p.110⟩
and the dark fire would have multiplied in the tree and in fruit and in all flesh. The power of the fire that is now in a thousand people, you would have found it occurring in a single person today.

**¶20** Behold, I have taught you about this single evil that was purified by three archetypes [three seals.]

> *¶20 #1 [tentative]:* The ‘triad of governing patterns’ in this oldest register is expressed as ‘seals’ (fixed archetypal forms that constrain and purify).

**¶21** The second deed: Al[l] the heavens [were purified] of the sediment and the pollution [that remained in them,] so that [the Light] surpasses in them [and not the darkness.]

> *¶21 #2 [moderate]:* Second deed: cleansing of the heavens from dregs; thus ‘All the heavens were purified’.
> *¶21 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the notion of residual sediment/pollution still clinging to the higher region.
> *¶21 #4 [moderate]:* What ‘surpasses’ in the cleansed heavens is the pure principle; in this layer ‘Light’ is the impersonal celestial good.
> *¶21 #5 [moderate]:* Required opposition: purification so that Light prevails, not Darkness.

**¶22** [The third]: The heavens and the firmaments [b]urden [and the firmaments were] relieved from the weight-carriers [and the yokes,] and it ceased being heavy. This rich one [that were set over them from] above.

> *¶22 #3 [moderate]:* The subject pair is ‘heavens and firmaments’; the broken clause must restore the copular/verb phrase leading into burden/relief.
> *¶22 #4 [tentative]:* The ‘weight-carriers’ correspond to coercive burdens; ‘yokes’ fits the load-bearing imagery of this layer.
> *¶22 #5 [tentative]:* The ‘rich one … above’ suggests an imposed burden from above; thus ‘set over them from above’.

**¶23** The fourth: That they might purify and cleanse their dwelling places [The fourth:]

> *¶23 #1 [strong]:* Heading marker required by the enumerated deeds.

**¶24** The fifth: Because the greatness and the richness that dwells in these ships, as it [is gathered there,] you might find them living in these ships, purified and holy.

> *¶24 #1 [moderate]:* Fifth deed: the greatness/richness dwelling in the ships as something collected and resident there; ‘is gathered there’ matches the container-vessel logic.

**¶25** The sixth: That you might find the mixed, incomplete, and unconfined temples above at rest and purified; since rest will reach them and pass through them and purify them.

**¶26** The seventh: [The seventh: That the] nature alone of the vessels, which will advance and extend to their places and sites [shall not pass beyond] in [their boundaries.]

> *¶26 #1 [moderate]:* Restores the deed-heading and introduces the unclear clause about the vessels’ own tendency.
> *¶26 #2 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: advance and extend only to fixed limits; thus a restriction formula.
> *¶26 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the sense: their places and sites are their appointed bounds.

**¶27** The eighth: That great enclosures and walls would be made, and confine everything.

**¶29** The tenth deed: That they would become releasers and purifiers of everything at the end of the universe like the fire [that] burns [all filth.]

> *¶29 #1 [strong]:* Introduces the simile ‘like the fire… burns…’.
> *¶29 #2 [moderate]:* The tenth deed is final purification by consuming removal; ‘burns all filth’ matches the correspondence.

**¶30** The eleventh deed: [The eleventh: That they] will make a crossing [for the souls,] and the entire ruling-power [of the darkness be cast]

> *¶30 #1 [moderate]:* Restores the deed-heading and subject.
> *¶30 #2 [tentative]:* A ‘crossing’ in this register is for the transferable living principle; ‘souls’ is the natural term for what passes outward.
> *¶30 #3 [moderate]:* Matches spiritual sense: dominion of falsity driven outward into the boundary.

**¶31** ⟨p.111⟩
to the outer land, for they were not envious of this [as] a man who is richer than them; because the aeons of light are outside [the boundary of the worlds,] the place [is] of peace, the place of honour and grace, the place where the joy of the householders who dwell in it shall never pass away nor recede.

> *¶31 #1 [strong]:* Simile connector: ‘as a man… is not envious…’.
> *¶31 #2 [moderate]:* The light-aeons are ‘outside’ the ultimate limit; thus ‘outside the boundary of the worlds’.
> *¶31 #3 [strong]:* Links ‘there is the place…’.

**¶32** The twelfth: That you might find all the deeds of loss hidden from the aeons of light, because of the wall of fire that surrounds the entire building; and these unclean things are displayed (only) in its [outermost border.]

> *¶32 #1 [moderate]:* Twelfth deed: unclean things appear only within their allotted external domain; ‘outermost border’ renders that limit in substrate cosmology.

**¶33** There was a need for these twelve great deeds because the vessels that were discharged and swept out from the firmaments to the earth, and were also swept from the earth to the {extr[extremity] the three words of life watch over them that [ar]e set over them in great power [were appointed.] that they might watch over them at the last time; because of this, they are firmly set thus in these fetters, in the way that I have told you.

> *¶33 #1 [strong]:* Restores the broken word ‘extremity’.
> *¶33 #3 [moderate]:* The ‘three words of life’ are set over the vessels; thus a verb of appointment/establishment in power.

**Assessment:** Most gaps admit only moderate reconstruction because the surviving English base is already smoothed and several sections (¶12, ¶14-16, ¶22, ¶26, ¶30-33) are conceptually clear but lexically underdetermined. Where the spiritual translation fixes the functional sequence (manifest upward, discharge downward, then quiet healing; three garments; two-direction torrent of fire; concealment by an ultimate boundary), restorations are correspondentially tight. A minority of fills remain tentative where exact nouns (e.g., ‘inner limbs’, ‘seals’, ‘yokes’, ‘souls’) could vary without changing the spiritual mechanics.

## Chapter 43: Concerning the Vessels.

**¶2** As for what you have asked me about the vessels, you must understand this, that when the Father of Life was about to discharge these three vessels he poured the three upon this earth, one by one at its (appointed) hour and season. He discharged them and cast them down to the earth.

**¶3** Then he bailed and scooped them from this earth in these three garments, they of wind and of water and of fire, which swathed his body. He poured and made them flow into the three pits [which]hich he had [es]tablished during the construc[tio]n of the worlds [and when he had shut them in,] they [yet] something remained behind on the face of the earth from these three vessels, so that you can find their mark and type displayed in the universe.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The sentence requires a relative clause identifying the three pits as the ultimate repositories. The visible tail “w” constrains to “which” in this register (“the three pits which he had established”).
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the three vessels must be directed into prepared repositories and then confined there as fixed ultimates; the narrative then turns to what remained on the earth’s surface. This bridge phrase supplies the required act of enclosure before the ‘remnant remained behind’ statement, without importing later institutional language.
> *¶3 #5 [strong]:* The logic needs an adversative pivot: despite the pouring into the pits, something remained on the face of the earth as a signature. “Yet” gives the demanded turn in the oldest, impersonal register.

**¶4** The element that is left in water is the pungency and the salinity of this salty inner sea that surrounds the worlds.

**¶5** And the remnant that is left from the darkness is this black mountain that exists in the surroundings of the sunrise. For when the sun would shine over the world no sunlight is visible there, because the darkness that gushes from it manifests above it.

**¶6** And the remnant that remains behind from the fire, upon the earth, are all the mountains and islands from where fumes of fire gush up and are visible. For these things are the remnant of the great vessel, the vestige that was conjoined with this fire that comes up from the earth; together with the mountains and hills [and valleys, and barren] places where no fruit sprouts on the trees [and stony] greater than that place from the limb and [the root of] the trees shall not bear fruit in that place.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The passage enumerates external ultimates expressing the vestige of disordered fire: mountains, islands, then further landforms leading into ‘places where no fruit sprouts’. “Valleys” pairs naturally with mountains and hills; ‘barren’ prepares the ensuing correspondence of unfruitfulness.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The correspondence is of sterility from a consuming heat: the ground is ‘stony’ where influx cannot root, matching ‘no fruit sprouts on the trees’. This keeps to cosmological description rather than moral exhortation.
> *¶6 #3 [tentative]:* The clause needs a natural term linking ‘greater than that place from the limb’ to the later ‘the trees shall not bear fruit’. ‘Root’ is the correspondential ultimate of reception; barrenness from root upward matches the described state of destruction of productive capacity.

**¶8** Happen you know this! When the Father of Life discharged these vessels from above upon the earth, he poured them out for this reason: so that also the water and the darkness and the fire that exist in these three earths would be drawn and ascend towards they that had come down from above, and the earths would be purified and cleansed. Due to this he poured these vessels down to the earth, of water and of darkness and of fire, so that also the water and darkness and fire that exist in the earths would be drawn up towards they that had descended from above.

**¶9** Now, first of all, he discharged the water, the waste, the poison of the rulers who are above. He cleansed the firmaments of the [water of darkness] he poured down the dark waters. [Just as the] waters fell from heaven to earth, so also the waters below in the earths were drawn and came up from below towards the waters that had come from above to the earths. The waters [were] purified of the dark poison that exists in them. Then he swept them outside and made them a great vessel and a wall of sea that surrounds the universe, and on which the ships sail.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The firmaments are cleansed of the corrupt ‘waters’ characterized as dark poison; the phrase must mark the opposite-sense quality of truth falsified. “Water of darkness” fits the substrate idiom used elsewhere in this chapter (‘dark waters’).

**¶10** After the water, he discharged the darkness. He purified the firmaments and dried from them the darkness with which they are weighed down. He discharged it down to the earth. Just as he had purified the firmaments, so also he purified the earths. The darkness that is in the earths was drawn up towards the darkness that had been discharged from above. He swept them both out and shut them in that [vessel] of darkness [beneath] the water.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* After sweeping out both kinds of darkness, they are confined ‘in that vessel of darkness’ as a fixed repository, parallel to the ‘great vessel and wall of sea’ made from the waters in ¶9. This keeps the impersonal cosmological container-language.
> *¶10 #2 [strong]:* The spatial correspondence requires darkness to be shut into a lower, subordered region relative to the sea-boundary of waters: ‘beneath the water’. This matches the spiritual sense: denial set beneath external knowing.

**¶11** And he discharged the fire from heaven to earth, [so that] that time again the fire that was shut [in] the earth below was drawn and came up towards its kind. Fire was added to fire. Afterwards, he swept it out and cast it into the outer underworld, which [he had constructed for] them outside the universe.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* The clause expresses purpose and simultaneity: the fire is discharged, so that at that time again the fire in the earth is drawn up toward its kind. The visible tail “at” in the bracket is satisfied by the following fixed words ‘that time’.

**¶12** In the same way that he purified the firmaments above as a place for the light-givers to journey [and for the luminaries to go and come,] so also he purified the earths below and swept from them the fire and the darkness and the water [that were mixed in them.] He has purified them for the ascent of these three powers, they of the wind and the water and the fire, which ascend before the Porter and traverse the earths.

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* The purified firmaments are described as a prepared way for the light-givers’ orderly motion. This fill preserves the oldest-layer cosmological function (a cleared path for luminary traversal) without importing later ecclesial terms.
> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* The sweeping out of fire, darkness, and water requires specifying their prior admixture in the lower earths. ‘Mixed in them’ matches the correspondential idea of impurity as commingling in ultimates, and completes the clause without adding overlay language.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the chapter’s container-and-separation structure: three descents, three purifications, three confinements into prepared repositories, then ascent of the three powers. Several restorations (¶3 #4–5; ¶6 #1–3; ¶12 #1–2) are guided primarily by the correspondential sequence rather than surviving letters, so their wording is moderate to tentative, but their functions in the cosmological logic are tight.

---

## Chapter 44: Concerning the Sea Giant

**¶2** After the Living Spirit constructed things and the worlds, he came up and set himself upon this earth. He gave a sign to the King of Glory, who turned the three wheels and caused the wheels that are found [in their place] up above all the mountains; he spread forth the wind to them, he breathed on them, he stirred them up, he washed the earth with the waters. And the waste and the accretion of the three vessels, they of water and of Darkness and of fire, which had been discharged upon the earth, he gathered them and deposited them in the sea that is within the walls and the vessels; because of this sea waters are salty. For they have received salt and bitterness from the washing out and cleansing of the three vessels.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The King of Glory “turned the three wheels” and thereby caused the wheels to be set and elevated. The correspondential sense requires establishment in a fixed ordered station (the higher arrangement “up above all the mountains”). The gap fits an adverbial phrase marking their being set in their proper position before the wind and washing act on them.

**¶3** When the Father of Life came, rising in his ascent to the heights, he discharged the wheel of the stars; he washed it out and loosened all the sediment that was in it. He cast it down to this great inner sea like [a stone] of the mountain. And that waste, the sediment of the washing out of the wheel, he cast it down and mixed it in with the sea waters. Its own thought collected it together; and the thought gathered the waste together in this way. It made it fast and fixed it [in the depths]

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The waste from washing the star-wheel is cast into the inner sea “like … of the mountain.” The spiritual sense is a descent from an elevated state into an external one; the natural correspondence is a heavy piece falling from a height. “A stone of the mountain” is the tightest image for that downward casting and for the fixed, hardened remainder that will be formed.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* After the repository’s ‘thought’ gathers the residue, it must be bound and stationed at the lowest, most external level (fixed in ultimates). “In the depths” matches the correspondential requirement: a fixing in the lowest general containant (the inner sea as external knowledges).

**¶4** The sea giant had stamped upon him the seal of the seconds and the hours, the seal of the days and months and years, the impression of the stars and the signs of the zodiac [and the courses of the seven planets.] He came into existence from them and was discharged from their power and cast into the sea. The images and the seals and aspects and doctrines and counsels of [the wheel of the stars] were sealed upon the body of that giant, because he is the residue of them all. Consequently, each star that will shine, and each sign of the zodiac that will turn: of one he shall be its inducement, and of another its confirmation.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* The seals named are measures of time and the ‘impression of the stars and the signs of the zodiac’; the oldest cosmological substrate regularly completes this set with the planetary courses as the immediate governors of sublunary changes. This also matches the spiritual translation’s ‘determinations of cognitions with their ordered varieties.’
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* The giant bears ‘doctrines and counsels of …’ and in ¶3 the residue explicitly comes from the washed-out star-wheel. The substrate logic is that the composite is the condensed remainder of that very wheel; thus the source must be named here as the wheel of the stars, not a later institutional or moral referent.

**¶5** He was discharged into the sea like this, the way a cloud might discharge rainwater into the midst of the sea. They compelled him totally through the action of the power and the helmsmanship of the angels. He was swept down to the mill, the place where he was constructed in the setting up of the universe; and the sea also conceived him [and took hold of him] for its part [in the work of his shaping;] it being his chief sculptor and chief craftsman. It sculpted him in a likeness, he being changed more than the other residue of the sea bodies.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The sea ‘conceived him … for its part … it being his chief sculptor.’ The correspondential sense requires that the general repository not only receives but actively seizes and engages the residue to give it determinate form. “Conceived him and took hold of him” expresses reception plus formative appropriation in the oldest register.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The following clause explains why: ‘it being his chief sculptor and chief craftsman.’ The lacuna should specify the mode of participation—its share in the shaping process—before naming it as the primary artisan. This keeps the impersonal cosmological tone and matches the spiritual translation’s “took part in its formation.”

**¶6** This matter is comparable with a craftsman who might take the stamped impressions of many coins and cast them on the fire, and mould them [to a single] image. These thousands of impressions and thousands of shapes [are perfected] and make a single body [and become one form.]

> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The example concludes the correspondential point: many stamped determinations dissolved and recast into one coherent organized whole. The oldest-layer wording should finish the analogy with unification of form rather than moralizing. This also matches the spiritual translation’s ‘brought into a single coherent form.’

**¶7** This is also the case with the sea giant, who too was moulded and sculpted by the power of the lust inside him from many doctrines and counsels that belong to the residue of the wheel of the sphere.

**¶8** The archetype of the giant was constructed for three things.

**¶9** The first: That the sphere would be relieved and purified of the burden present in it. In the same way that the ten firmaments were purified and relieved of the burden of the three vessels; so was it also done to the sphere, that it too might be relieved and purified of the burden and the waste inside it.

**¶10** The second: That he might add a {fetter} and a prison to the wheel and the signs of the zodiac that are bound to it, so that when they wish to go up above, you can find the sea giant dragging them back as by an iron {fetter}, and they are unable to go up; and when the powers of the wheel would take a decision on a guileful thing, and it enters their mind to come down to the earth, they are not able to descend because of this stake that is nailed to the wheel upon the {} of the firmaments. This is one. However, this other one, because of its sleepless watchers that are set over it, it is turned all the time.

**¶11** The third thing: That the wind and the water and the fire that gush from the circuit of the sea, and the rivers that flow upon the face of the earth discharging into the sea, and the rainwaters that come to ground, and the waste and rebirth that come down to it; that sea will be a hostel and gathering place for all of these.

**¶12** As for all the power and the life that comes down to the sea, and in the breath that the sea giant will breathe and draw it into him that he might disturb the sea [there is] fullness and a [interaction] becomes between his movement [that will] purify; but the sea [does not cease.]

> *¶12 #1 [strong]:* The syntax “that he might disturb the sea [ ... a] fullness” demands a verbal bridge leading into “fullness.” The spiritual sense indicates ‘an interaction and fullness arises’; thus “there is … a fullness” is required. The surviving ‘a’ at the end aligns naturally with “a fullness.”
> *¶12 #2 [strong]:* The spiritual translation preserves the required concept: an interaction arising from the composite’s movement and inbreathing. The natural-layer term that best fits the oldest substrate here is the straightforward “interaction,” naming the reciprocal agitation between sea and giant before mentioning “fullness.”
> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* The clause “becomes between his movement [ ... ] purify” needs a connective introducing the telos of the arising interaction: it tends toward cleansing. “That will purify” matches both the natural sequence and the spiritual sense ‘tending toward purification.’
> *¶12 #4 [tentative]:* The final broken clause “but the sea …” in context contrasts the purifying tendency with the continued general agitation or ongoing receptivity of the repository. The oldest-layer closure most consistent with the preserved sense is that the sea’s motion or surging continues—hence “does not cease.” Exact object is lost, but the required opposition is persistence of the sea’s general state despite the purifying interaction.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the correspondential structure: residue from the star-wheel cast into the inner sea, gathered and fixed in the depths, then sealed with time-measures and astral determinations. The most uncertain restorations are ¶2 #1 (idiomatic placement of the wheels) and especially the final broken contrast in ¶12 #4, where only the general oppositional logic (purifying interaction versus continued sea-condition) can be recovered.

---

## Chapter 45: Concerning the Vessels

**¶1** Understand also this other (matter): Before these vessels were discharged and swept down from heaven, first three ditches were fashioned and constructed for them, in which they would be bound and fettered.

**¶2** Before the sea giant was discharged from the sphere, the Father of Life constructed a place for [him in]n this great sea that is within the walls, whereon the ships sail, and where the fullness shall [be kept apart]

> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense says the ‘fullness’ of outward knowledge is made to serve separation, not confusion; hence a restraint-function (kept apart, held in distinction) rather than an ecclesial or moralizing phrase.

**¶3** Also, before the [ruler of the darkness] was displayed to the worlds [and sank to] the earth [and its thought]ell to the sea and its thought shaped it, the Father of Life constructed a place for it in [the great ditch] below the four mountains, above the mountains of the heavens above [and he dug] that [opened] a great plain it digs in Matter in the midst of the worlds. When the Adamant of Light was sent to it, he hastened after it to that place, smote it and threw it between mountains in the place made ready for it [and its body] is set fast upon it till the end of the universe.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The next subject is a power that ‘fell to the sea and its thought shaped it’ and later is pursued and struck by the Adamant of Light; in the oldest cosmological register this is a demonic ruler, not a moral abstraction.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The correspondential sequence is: made manifest, then a descent into the ‘sea’ (external knowledge) before being confined below; a simple descent-link is demanded here.
> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* The spiritual translation explicitly: ‘after it had sunk into external knowledge and its own fantasy formed its condition’. The remnant ‘f’ at the end fits ‘thought shaped it’ as the next clause begins.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The chapter opens with ‘three ditches’ prepared as bindings. This later ‘place’ for the fallen power is correspondentially one such prepared containment: a ditch or pit made ready beneath the four mountains.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* The passage needs an action that produces the ‘great plain’ as a hollowing-out in Matter; the spiritual sense: ‘an empty expanse was opened… so that this power might be isolated’.
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* Carries the correspondential necessity of making a voided expanse (‘plain’) within Matter as a state of isolation in the lowest degree.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* What is ‘set fast upon it till the end’ is the bound power’s own fixed form. In the substrate register, the binding fastens the entity together with its formed body, not a moral ‘sin’.

**¶4** Before the waste and dirt of the darkness were swept out from creation, gehennas were constructed in which the waste might be covered till the dissolution of the universe.

**¶5** Before the rebels rose up, they whose corps rebelled in the ruling-power above and below, they [were gathered] outside, and also the tyrants in whose heart it lay [to ty]rannise amongst the murk and the clouds and winds and spirits and storms; for them were constructed in the great outer sea seven hostels, places whence they are thrown into the depths and the darkness [according to their measure]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence lists classes of rebels and then turns to their assignment outside; ‘they were gathered outside’ matches the spiritual sense: a regulated lodging in the most external general knowledge before being cast down further.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation adds ‘according to order, so that their outbreaks might be regulated’. The oldest-layer phrasing tends toward impersonal measure and allotment rather than exhortation.

**¶6** Also, before the abortions [fell down] to the earth, there was set apart [a pen]: they are gathered in and dwell therein.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The line requires only the descent into the natural degree (earth). ‘Fell down’ fits the correspondential motion without importing later rhetorical colouring.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* A ‘separated enclosure’ in the oldest register is a penfold-like confinement where such malformed products are ‘gathered in and dwell’.

**¶7** Again, before the watchers rebelled and came down from heaven, a prison was fashioned and constructed for them in the depths of the earth, below the mountains.

**¶8** Before the children of the giants were born, they who had [no] knowledge of righteousness in them nor divinity, thirty-six cities were assigned and constructed for them wherein the children of [the giants would] live; they who would come to beget from each other, they who shall spend ten hundred years alive.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are structurally constrained by the chapter’s repeated motif: prepared containments (ditches, prisons, hostels, pens) established before each class of fallen power descends. Where the spiritual translation specifies function (separation, isolation, regulation), restorations follow that impersonal, cosmological register; a few places remain only moderately constrained because the exact Coptic lexeme (ditch, pit, place) cannot be fixed with certainty from correspondence alone.

---

## Chapter 46: Concerning the Ambassador.

**¶2** Know that before night and day were differentiated in the universe, the Third [Am]bassador was summoned. And afterwards, he was sent to this void and outer waste that he might purify the light, and separate night and day from each other.

**¶3** ⟨p.117⟩ Before the light was released and freed and ascended through this zone, the Father of Life made beautiful for it the ships of light. He constructed them so that it might be purified in them, and be sculpted and fashioned in good order.

**¶4** [Bef]ore Sin had multiplied and had made a kingdom in the flesh, before the acquitted and the condemn[ed] were set apart from each other, the Judge [w]as sent forth and revealed. His throne was created and placed [i]n the living atmosphere, so that he would be a just Judge, so that there the righteous and the sinners would be set apart and tested and separated from each other.

**¶6** Again, [befo]re mankind had been engendered, some were named ['rich' and] others 'poor' on earth [and the fate was set] upon them. ⟨p.118⟩ Before they die, they who will die are marked out by the stars and the signs of the zodiac in the sphere. They are appointed for them; in them are their births. And their root is bound up with their zodiacal signs; and they are compelled by them and brought to an equal judgement in accordance with their deeds and their sins.

> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires an antecedent allotment of states before actual human engendering: an impersonal determining or casting that rests upon them. In the oldest substrate register this is expressed as ‘fate’ being set, matching the immediately following astral constraint language (stars, zodiac, sphere) as the outer form of that prior allotment.

**¶7** Again, before the universe is dissolved, [and the] entire earth, the destroyers were appointed and instructed for its dissolution.

**¶8** And before the ne[w] aeon was [built and] made be[auti]ful and marked out, [the] Great Builder [w]as summoned, the first architect and the angels who are with him, who would build and make beautiful the new aeon.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations tightly fixed by the immediate word-forms. The only substantial gap (¶6 #3) is constrained by the correspondential structure: predetermination and allotment preceding astral marking; the restoration supplies the required impersonal ‘allotment’ concept in the cosmological register without importing later devotional language.

---

## Chapter 47: Concerning the Four great Things

**¶1** The wheel of the stars, which is displayed to you, is a great mighty thing! Also, it is one among the four great things; since the whole ruling-power is assigned to four parts.

**¶2** The first part is the powers who occur in the ten firmaments that exist above the wheel, and the ruling-power that is bound in them, as it is imprisoned within them in the heavens that are above.

**¶3** The second part is the eight earths that are below; the four that are composite, and the four places of darkness. The second part extends from this earth, upon which mankind walks, towards the underside.

**¶4** The third part is the walls and the vessels that surround the worlds. They are the four mountains and the three vessels; with what is in them, and the ruling-power that is trapped within them, and the powers who are masters over them.

**¶5** The fourth part is the sphere of the stars that is appointed with great diligence, as it is ordered in a [fore]any [orderings]; that of the Father of Life, [and that of the Mother of Life] the living.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The phrase must read “in a foremany …” i.e., in a very great plurality and patterned multiplicity. This matches the correspondential sense: the sphere is an ordered complex, not a single thing, and it is arranged in a ‘patterned whole’. The surviving final m- fits the common Coptic-English rendering “foremany”.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Completes the thought: the star-sphere is ‘ordered in a foremany orderings’, i.e., structured in numerous distinct arrangements. This suits the oldest substrate’s impersonal cosmological register (structural arrangement), and matches the spiritual sense of a meticulous patterned whole.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Restores the standard dyad in the oldest cosmological layer: Father of Life paired with Mother of Life (not ‘Mother of the living’, which leans later and more devotional). The spiritual translation already treats them as living principles of love and wisdom; ‘Mother of Life’ is the more archaic, structural title.

**¶6** ⟨p.119⟩ Its [ordinance] very [ordered] with diligence, because of the great and cruel powers, and the titanic figures, [who are] in it; they being suspended from it. So, all the first-born and the leaders of all the rulers, they who were existing in the three parts, these were garnered. They were collected together, seized, bound and assigned on this wheel. Each was apportioned by these three parts. They were distributed, entwined, and weighted against their companions of the same size.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* “Its ordinance … with diligence” gives the required impersonal, structural statement: the wheel’s arrangement is kept in strict order. This aligns with the spiritual sense: ‘held in exact order’.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Yields: “Its ordinance … [is] very ordered with diligence,” directly matching the spiritual sense ‘held in exact order … with diligence’.

**¶7** Still, for its part, the wheel is equal to them in its strength; the powers of the enmity who are bound on it. Indeed, they were suspended on the circling wheel because of their cruelty; so that, due to the wind and the motion in which they go round, they comprehend not the place wherein they are set. For if they had been bound in the heavens, the heavens would not have had the strength to support them. Again, if they had been assigned on the earths, on the fixtures that are below, they also would not have had the strength to support them. Rather, they were suspended from the wheel of the circling stars; so that the power and the light that should come up from below can be received, and given to the heavenly ones above them. Conversely, whatever will fall from above, whether cold or hot, the wheel of the sphere will also take it and cast it out upon the entire earth, upon all the wood.

**¶8** Behold, the ordinance of the wheel is in this form. For the powers who are entwined on the wheel are like this: a king mastering his kingdom by this mighty legionary force of all the leaders of the kingdom; and with all the armour of the soldiery that is collected together for him; and also with the mass of the property of the kingdom; and the beautiful lustful aspect of the women who are collected together for him; together with [all the] property [besides] and those cattle [also] that have been distributed about among his retinue. Yet, if you ask about that king, he is matched with all his retinue. His retinue, for its part, is matched against his entire kingdom. Judgement shall come forth from him, vindication and condemnation, to his entire kingdom; by his wisdom, skill and strength.

> *¶8 #1 [tentative]:* The list is a catalogue of a king’s gathered supports; after ‘together with’ the text needs a general totalizing determiner before ‘property’, matching the spiritual sense ‘along with other acquisitions’.
> *¶8 #2 [tentative]:* Balances the catalogue: “together with all the property besides …” i.e., further acquisitions beyond the already-mentioned mass of property. This preserves the impersonal, enumerative style of the substrate and matches the spiritual sense of ‘other acquisitions’.
> *¶8 #3 [tentative]:* Smooths the final item: “and those cattle also that have been distributed …” marking an additional, dispersed class of possessions (appetites/attendants in the spiritual sense).

**¶9** Now, just as for this king, who is matched against the measure of his entire kingdom by his wisdom, skill and strength; so also is it for the powers who are bound on the sphere as compared to the ruling-power, and the powers who exist in the entire zone. Like the king towards his retinue; so too is it that this totality is gathered to the sphere, to seven leaders, to twelve signs of the zodiac. As all the cities are gathered to the king, again they gather to the leaders of the universe. All that is done in the universe above and below: the battles and the disorders and the captivity and the hunger and the lust and the property; so shall they increase and diminish through these leaders. They are the motivaters of the whole created order; the totality being gathered in to them the way that I have told you. I have opened your eyes!

**Assessment:** Most restorations are structurally constrained by the chapter’s fourfold scheme and by the repeated emphasis on meticulous ordering. The dyad ‘Father of Life’ and ‘Mother of Life’ is strongly demanded by the oldest cosmological register, so that restoration is relatively tight. The three small lacunae in ¶8 sit inside an expansive simile-catalogue; correspondentially their function is clear (additional acquisitions and dispersed attendants), but the exact connective particles are less constrained, so those remain tentative.

---

## Chapter 48: Concerning the Conduits.

**¶1** There are three conduits that exist through the entire zone, from the abyss to its heights.

**¶2** The first conduit is the root of all the powers above, they that exist in all the heavens. They are cast off and fettered on the earths below; because the earth below is the ‘stripping off’, the garment and the body of the powers that are above the heavens.

**¶3** For when the Father of Life constructed the heavenly worlds, he divested them of their bodies, [he] threw them to the earths below.

**¶4** So, the upper worlds are of the soul and the spirit; but the worlds below are of the body and the carcass [of death] ⟨p.121⟩ Now as for this, he [fastened] the powers of heaven; he sealed it on their bodies and their carcasses that are on the earths, so that when life comes up from the fullness of these earths, it can be entirely drawn up to the heights in the roots of what is heavenly, that are fastened in their bodies. And also, all life can be purified in that place.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The lower worlds are defined as the plane of mere effect severed from interior life; in the substrate register this is regularly named as the realm of Death (not moral ‘evil’ but privation of life). The sequence ‘body and carcass …’ calls for the governing opposite term to ‘soul and spirit’.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The action described is the fixing of higher powers to their ultimates so ascent can occur. ‘Fastened’ matches the repeated root-binding language in ¶6–¶8 and keeps the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶5** However, the waste that will be swept from the purified one who ascends to the heights will be let fall to the ground through the conduits of these conduits, and will be poured down [to the earths] and thrown to the underside.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Waste descending is consistently directed to ‘the ground’ and ‘the underside’. The missing phrase should identify the receiving plane before ‘and thrown to the underside’.

**¶6** This is the first conduit, which comes from the powers of the heavens to their bodies and their carcasses that are upon the earth; also, from their bodies and [their] carcasses that are on the earths to the powers of the heavens.

**¶7** The second conduit is the one that comes from the temples and dwellings and cities that exist in the heavens down to the ground, to the five shapes of tree that grow upon the earth. The life comes up from the trees to the temples and cities. Life too, and the residue of the heavenly ones, comes down to the trees in a conduit.

**¶8** And the third conduit is all the powers and householders who dwell in all the firmaments, whose roots come down from them to the five worlds of flesh that creep upon the earth, being fixed to one another. Now, the power and the life that shall be gathered in from the worlds of flesh are dispersed among them in various shapes. The powers above shall draw them to the upper side through conduits.

**¶9** [The waste]te too, and the lust and the evil-doing and the anger that will be greater in the powers of [heaven]n, shall be poured to the ground through their various conduits. They shall be discharged upon mankind and the other remaining animals. When what is heavenly will wash the waste and the stench and the poison down on the creations of the flesh below, in their turn the creations shall be greater in lust and anger and evil-doing against each other through the action of their fathers who are on high.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* Context requires the subject ‘waste’ to parallel ‘lust … evil-doing … anger’. Restores the obvious broken word.
> *¶9 #2 [strong]:* Restores the broken word and keeps the cosmological agent ‘powers of heaven’.

**¶10** Still, look at the wheel of the stars! It has no [root in this earth], but its roots are bound in the totality. The wheel of stars, then, receives life through the power and the life that ascend from the earths through conduits and the firmaments above. It (also) takes life from those conduits that receive up to the firmaments and the heavenly worlds above it, it takes life out from that place.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Matches the doctrinal point: the star-wheel lacks a terrestrial root yet remains bound to the totality by another bond explained in ¶12–¶14.

**¶11** Look, in that the wheel has no root in this earth, from where did the stars and signs of the zodiac find this authority? They became masters over these five fleshes and five trees. Even though you have told us that the wheel has no root there! For you said to us that every thing, if it can be reduced from the earth, the heavenly powers above draw it through a conduit. Also, we see that every thing that a person will attain, whether it be wealth that comes into his possession or poverty that will accrue to him, or his sickness and health; he attains it through the signs of the zodiac and the star under which he shall be born.

**¶12** Nevertheless, what I proclaimed to you, namely that the wheel has no root therein, I have recounted to you properly. It is set aright in its position. For when the Living Spirit, the Father of Life, constructed the heavens and the firmaments above, he bound their roots into the earths below; in their carcasses that he had stripped from them. Yet, when he constructed the wheel, [he] did not bind its root into the earths, but he bound the root of the wheel by the sea giant.

**¶13** And when [the] Ambassador came, he displayed his image and purified and brought the light out from all [the] heavens. Matter, however, which is present in all the rulers, spurted out against the image of the Ambassador. It went up in order to reach it, but reached it not. It dropped back and fell to the [under]side. Now, when it had fallen from above, it separated out into three parts.

**¶14** The first part fell to the wheel; another part fell to the earth; an[other part fell to the sea]a. ⟨p.123⟩ [By means] of the part that fell to the sphere, [the root]oot was bound in the trees and flesh. Therefore, because of this, they received authority and became masters in the five worlds of the flesh and the five worlds of the tree.

> *¶14 #1 [strong]:* Tripartition in ¶13 demands the third portion go to the sea (sea-giant, outermost receptacle), aligning with ¶12 and the spiritual translation’s ‘most external generality’.
> *¶14 #3 [strong]:* The clause explains how authority was gained: a ‘root’ being bound. So the broken initial must yield ‘the root’.

**¶15** Through the nature that came down [upon]n the wheel the root was bound in the trees. Also, by means of the abortions that came to the ground, another root was bound in the fleshes.

**¶16** Therefore, because of this, the stars and signs of the zodiac received authority over the trees and the fleshes: through the root of Sin that fell down; and [the root]ot of the abortions that were aborted there, they fell to the ground.

> *¶16 #1 [strong]:* Parallelism: ‘root of Sin’ and ‘root of the abortions’. The lacuna must restore ‘root’.

**¶17** However, the[se] other conduits that [the] Living Spirit has bound in the e[a]rths, they that the soul of the elements comes up through to the heavens; those (conduits) are not bound to the wheel. Rather, they are plundered from them by the powers on the wheel, that hang from it.

**¶18** Just as you have instructed us and put together this lesson on [the three conduits] you have told us about the prototype of the conduits. The first is the one that comes from the firmaments to the earths, and the one that comes from the wheel to the sea giant. That is only one. So, the other root is the one that comes [from]om [the temples] to the trees. The third is bound by means of the abortions in the f[fleshes]; also in the fleshes the fathers of the abortions who are in the heavens [above] are bound and twined, and the [y] that [are bound to them]

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* The speaker now summarizes the lesson ‘on …’ and immediately lists first, second, third. The missing referent is the triad itself.
> *¶18 #3 [strong]:* Required by the stated second conduit in ¶7 and repeated in ¶21–¶22.
> *¶18 #4 [strong]:* Restores the technical term for the five bodily worlds; aligns with ¶8, ¶14–¶16.
> *¶18 #5 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts bindings in flesh below with the fathers ‘who are in the heavens’. The missing locator should be ‘above’.
> *¶18 #7 [moderate]:* The clause ‘and the y that …’ needs a predicate describing the intertwined heavenly fathers’ operative connection into flesh; ‘are bound to them’ maintains the root-binding motif and fits the spiritual sense of conjoined origins acting into ultimates.

**¶20** You have inquired about a [root] and you have asked about this lesson in superiority, great [and might] the earth from which much life ascends [from all] the Father of Life has drawn them. ⟨p.124⟩ He has fastened them from the powers [that] he has bound them in the earths.
