(These conduits) are the roots of the ent[ire] ruling-power that the life of the living soul is drawn up through, it coming up from the whole earth, from all the mountains, fields and islands.

> *¶20 #1 [strong]:* The inquiry is about ‘a …’ that grounds superiority and from which much life ascends; this is the ‘first great root’ later named in ¶21. So the missing noun is ‘root’.
> *¶20 #2 [moderate]:* ‘superiority, great … the earth’ needs an attributive pair for the preeminent root; ‘great and might’ (power) matches the chapter’s concern with ‘ruling-power’ and avoids devotional phrasing.
> *¶20 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the sense: life ascends ‘from all … the earth’, before ‘the Father of Life has drawn them’.
> *¶20 #4 [moderate]:* Introduces the relative clause: the conduits as roots ‘that the life … is drawn up through’.

**¶21** After that first great root is the root of the trees, which is bound to the temples and the buildings above. For there also is much great life coming up from the tree and the entire Cross, being drawn up from them to the upper side like a great host.

**¶22** What, however, is lesser [in comparison] of their conduit [than this] he takes up [in the places] the roots [also] the earths, they [of the roots] the whole earth in all the worlds. Again, these of the trees are present in the whole earth, whether small or great; but the root of mankind [in] is not present in the whole earth, other than only this world of the south. Therefore, even now these fleshes do not make [being in] in the entire world of the south [and filling] there is a small part of them in [of mankind] of the upper side of the world of the south in [and beasts] in number than the [others]

> *¶22 #1 [moderate]:* The paragraph contrasts the widespread presence of tree-roots with the limited presence of mankind’s root; it opens ‘What, however, is lesser …’ requiring a comparative marker.
> *¶22 #2 [moderate]:* Carries the comparison forward before speaking of ‘their conduit’.
> *¶22 #3 [tentative]:* The fragment discusses distribution through regions of earth and worlds; ‘in the places’ supplies a neutral cosmological locator.
> *¶22 #4 [tentative]:* Links the ‘lesser’ matter back into the ongoing account of roots and conduits.
> *¶22 #5 [moderate]:* The clause ‘he takes up … the roots … the earths’ demands the object ‘roots’ explicitly.
> *¶22 #6 [moderate]:* Gives the expected preposition before ‘the earths’.
> *¶22 #7 [tentative]:* The broken syntax is about presence and spread; ‘being in’ sustains the distributive argument without importing later moral vocabulary.
> *¶22 #8 [tentative]:* The passage seems to say the tree-roots are throughout the whole earth; ‘and filling’ matches that distributive sense.
> *¶22 #9 [strong]:* Directly required by the contrast that follows: ‘but the root of mankind … is not present…’.
> *¶22 #10 [tentative]:* Given the chapter’s repeated pairing ‘mankind and the other remaining animals’ (¶9), the limited distribution likely concerns fleshly life broadly; ‘and beasts’ keeps the substrate’s impersonal zoological register.
> *¶22 #11 [minimal]:* The concluding comparison ‘in number than the …’ requires a plural counterpart; ‘others’ supplies the contrast class without over-specifying what the damage does not allow.

**¶23** I have instructed you about the roots [and about the binding of] them, [I] have taught you that they are bound by their [roots in their orders] with one another. They stir one another, they draw life from one another through the action of the powers above, which stirs the nature that dwells in them all.

> *¶23 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence resumes: ‘I have instructed you about the roots … them,’ then ‘they are bound…’. The missing phrase should introduce the topic of their conjunction; ‘binding’ is the governing concept throughout.
> *¶23 #3 [tentative]:* The teaching is that the roots are ‘bound by their roots … with one another’ across discrete degrees. ‘in their orders’ expresses that they are arranged by degree without introducing later ecclesial terms.

**¶24** Now, when the Matter that is in them will be crushed and despoiled, then that Matter too shall [war against] its members and overturn them and [tear apart] each other [make them strike] stir [and the members] ⟨p.125⟩

> *¶24 #1 [moderate]:* In the opposite sense, Matter stripped away turns violently upon the forms it had inhabited; ‘war against’ captures the destructive reaction described (‘overturn them’ etc.) in the substrate’s cosmic-conflict idiom.
> *¶24 #2 [moderate]:* Corresponds to ‘crushed and despoiled’ leading to internal dismemberment; keeps the bodily correspondential imagery of members.
> *¶24 #3 [moderate]:* Prepares the following ‘each other’; expresses induced mutual aggression among interconnected members.
> *¶24 #4 [tentative]:* The closing fragment points to increasing disorder among linked parts; restoring ‘members’ keeps continuity with ‘its members’ earlier in the verse.

**Assessment:** Most mid-word and short lacunae are recoverable with high confidence because the chapter is structurally tripartite (three conduits, three-part fall) and repeatedly governed by the fixed motif of roots bound into lower planes. The largest uncertainties occur in ¶22 and the end of ¶24 where the source is highly fragmentary; there the restorations preserve the oldest-layer cosmological register (roots, binding, distribution, conflict) but remain tentative where the correspondential constraints do not uniquely determine the exact wording.

---

## Chapter 49: Concerning the Wheel and the Conduits

**¶2** The reason that the conduits shall not be cut, for they are not cut, is because they are spiritual. Thus the conduits are like the waters through which ships sail. You shall find the prow that is in position in the front part of the ship divides the waters, casting them back and forth. So, when it parts the waters and that ship opens up the water with its cleaving prow, immediately and without delay the waters mingle with each other again behind the ship; and the path of the ship shall not be discernible in the midst of the waters.

**¶3** Just as when a bird flies up in the air, for its path is not visible in the air, and in the way of mankind, in the sun within the conduits. They also shall not be tangled up in them, and neither shall you find their path through the air known.

**¶4** This is also the case with the wheel. It turns through the conduits and the air. The wheel shall not cut the conduits, nor are the conduits themselves entangled in the wheel.

---

## Chapter 50: Concerning these Names: God, Rich One, and Angel; who they are.

**¶3** There are these three archetypes [in the land of light, and there is no measure to] them!

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* Fits the oldest-layer cosmological register: the triad of primordial forms appears within the Light-realm, whose quality is unbounded and therefore not subject to mensuration by lower degrees. The phrase also balances the later refrain in ¶6.

**¶4** So, the ones who are called 'god', are the gods whom the Father has summoned from himself. He has established them after the likeness of his greatness.

**¶5** Conversely, these who are named 'rich [ones]', are the evocations of the first rich gods of the Father; because, when the Father had summoned (the first), they themselves summoned evocations. They called them 'rich'.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The passage is establishing a threefold naming: gods (first summonings), rich ones (second-order evocations), angels (third-order evocations). Here the contrast requires a plural designation for the second archetype: “these who are named ‘rich ones’”. This also maintains the impersonal, structural tone of the substrate (classes of beings, not titles).

**¶6** They too, the rich ones, have summoned their evocations. They call them 'angel'. These are the three archetypes who occur in the land of light. There is no measure to apply to them!

**¶7** Once again, listen! Other persons who are named 'god' are the emanations who have come from the Father; the evocations of the Father whom he summoned forth. They came out to the contest and humiliated the enmity.

**¶8** Conversely, the 'rich ones' are the evocations of these first living words. They too, the rich ones, have come and performed and fulfilled the pleasure of the greatness, in the worlds that are above and below.

**¶10** Again, they are called 'god', all the gods who belong to the household of the Great Spirit.

**¶11** Conversely, the ones who are called 'angel', are all the angels who belong to the household of the Beloved of the Lights.

**¶12** The ones who are called 'rich', are all the rich ones who belong to the household of the glorious Ambassador.

**Assessment:** Both lacunae are tightly constrained by the chapter’s triadic correspondential structure (first determinations, derived fullness, ministering powers) and by the recurring ‘no measure’ formula; restorations are certain.

---

## Chapter 51: Concerning the First Man.

**¶2** When the [First] Man came forth against the ene[my] ⟨p.127⟩ garment [and he clothed] the darkness with the garment of [of] living fire. With that garment he withered the tumescence (?) of the enemy; he brought down i[t]s foolery and its vanity.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* Standard oldest-layer cosmological title: the primal championing hypostasis that goes out to meet Darkness.
> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* Completes ene[my ...] → enemy; the ellipsis belongs to the following lacuna.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires an act of investiture: the First Man ‘invests’ himself with the garment that is living fire (outer truth bearing love’s force). The clause must introduce the direct object ‘the darkness with the garment…’ as the opponent being met and constrained in that invested state.
> *¶2 #4 [strong]:* Matches the fixed phrase ‘garment of living fire’.

**¶3** Now, this one alone was sufficient for them. [Y]et, si[n]ce the living fire was weakened, because of this he added [to it another fou]r garments: that of the wind, that of the water, that of the light, and that of the a[i]r. And this, (why) has he given the four? He gave them s[o] th[a]t they would be helpe[r]s to t[h]at god, wh[ich i]s the living fire; so that [the enemy might come] not with [in] the living f[ire], and penetrate it; and [po]ison and the [enmi]ty cut it off.

> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Restores the support-by-fullness motif: four additional ‘garments’ as exterior operative forms surrounding the weakened living fire.
> *¶3 #9 [strong]:* Completes ‘which is’ for identifying the governing element as living fire.
> *¶3 #10 [moderate]:* The correspondential logic demands a prevention-of-influx clause: hostile falsity must be kept from entering the living fire in its outward operation.

**¶4** While the fire received a bl[ow and] a wound in its body; yet still, [it constrained] all the powers of da[rk]ness because of it [were constrained] It [was also a] necessity [for the five to be companions; it was a] brothers, to cause them to receive a blow and a woun[d to]o. The power of the enemy mixed in with them. Rath[er, ex]cept [the air]. The darkness did not mix in with it, nor was the burning of the enemy able to penetrate it.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: although wounded, the governing love in ultimates still ‘pressed and constrained’ the array of falsities. This supplies the needed adversative continuation before ‘all the powers of darkness’.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Completes the effect: ‘all the powers of darkness’ are held in check ‘because of it’ (because of the fire’s invested combat).
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Bridges to the necessity statement: the conflict required conjunction and thus ‘it was also a necessity…’.
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* The oldest-layer structure is the fivefold set (fire plus four garments). The spiritual translation explicitly speaks of companions in cooperation, leading into ‘necessity… brothers…’.
> *¶4 #9 [strong]:* The exempt element is the ultimate plane that does not admit mixture; matches the spiritual sense ‘except for the ultimate plane of action itself’.

**¶5** Never-theless, even the one that remained behind without the wound, which is the air, it stripped off garment and power. It gave it to its [fo]ur brothers in its place; so that by that [ga]rment of the living air they would become strong, and [they might increase in] profit, and endure, namely its other four brothers. And it [be]came for them [a remaining] life.

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense requires an efficacy-for-use clause: strengthened by the living air, the four injured powers become effective (profit) and enduring. This fill supplies the needed verbal idea before ‘profit, and endure’.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Matches the closing idea: the living air becomes for them a continuing vitality (remaining life) in their labor.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are single-letter or fixed formulae and restore strongly. The larger gaps are constrained primarily by the correspondential sequence: (1) investiture for combat, (2) fourfold supportive garments as exterior operations around living fire, (3) prevention of hostile influx, (4) necessity of shared wounding among the five except the ultimate plane (air), and (5) air’s transfer of its garment as enduring support and continuing life. Exact idiom in the larger gaps remains moderately uncertain, but the required spiritual functions are tight.

---

## Chapter 52: Concerning the [ ... ] of the Light

**¶2** [Consider] how far [to the Light]ht that came forth from [the first Light] in that place in a [measure] alone [they were] ⟨p.128⟩ the greatness of the light with one another for ever, and nor did one of them transgress the command and order that his brother had entrusted to him forever.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Opening imperative fits the impersonal, expository substrate: the teacher directs attention to a measurable proceeding of Light (wisdom) before describing unanimity of its operations.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Restores the standard cosmological term matching the spiritual sense “wisdom proceeded”: the passage measures the extension of the Light that comes forth.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The chapter later names “that first light”; the emanation here must proceed from the same principial source.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense begins “a certain measure of wisdom proceeded”; the natural-layer teaching commonly expresses extension of Light by ‘measure’ or ‘limit’.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Required to yield the sense: in that measure each stood in its own place, in unity with the others, before the statement about mutual greatness and non-transgression of order.

**¶3** However, the darkness, the enemy, was doomed and overcome; because of the arising of disharmony it was divided within itself. Even the kingdom was divided within itself; [and] ev[en] the [powers, who had] left their abyss behind and come up fo[llow-ing] it, were doomed because of the disunity that was present in all of them. The darkness was destroyed, and its other powers were humiliated, because the p[ower] of that first light functions in the same way as the kingdom of this world.

> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Fits the correspondential logic: derived faculties (powers) rising from the abyss (lowest persuasions) to follow the Darkness.

**¶4** As long as its [associates in the m]ystery are in agreement with it, th[at] kingdom [stands firm] and they marvel at it and it receives glory, the one that [is honored] and it surpasses and is even more exalted than this other kingdom, which is about [to burst out] against [it].

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* The phrase points to the interior consorts of the kingdom (its indwelling affections and knowledges) as in the spiritual translation; no later institutional term is introduced.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual sense “that opposed arrangement stands firm for a time” and prepares for the following admiration and self-exaltation.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Natural-layer equivalent of “receives glory” and “is credited with eminence”; keeps the impersonal cosmological register (not devotional).
> *¶4 #5 [strong]:* The image of an impending eruption corresponds to truth rising into manifest resistance; retained as the natural sign for emergence into act.

**¶5** Still, the kingdom, [the one that] its associates in the mystery [are in agreement] upon, is compelled to condemn that (other) kingdom; and the[y] are humiliated and come under the hand of this kingdom that is set up against it.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* Contrasts the kingdom held together by unanimity with the other it must judge; preserves the tight parallelism of the exposition.
> *¶5 #2 [strong]:* Core condition of temporary stability: interior consorts unanimous with the kingdom; aligns with the spiritual rendering.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the chapter’s correspondential structure: a measured proceeding of the first Light, perpetual unanimity of its distinct operations, and the self-division of Darkness. The restorations in ¶2 and ¶4 (#1–#4) required choosing the oldest-substrate register (measure, first Light, stands firm, is honored) over later devotional or institutional phrasing; exact connective particles remain the least certain.

---

## Chapter 53: Concerning the First Man.

**¶1** At the time when the First Man was sent to the war in the first garment of [living fire], which he gave to the first nature that exists [first] in that single garment. [And] he hunted Matter, the thought of death, [the rulers of darkness] and all its other powers.

> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The substrate regularly distinguishes the ‘first nature’ as the earliest formed receptive level; ‘first’ tightens the correspondential sequence: First garment → first nature → first combat.
> *¶1 #3 [tentative]:* The lacuna marks the transition from invested protection to the act of combat; a simple connective best fits the impersonal oldest layer.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* What is hunted in this stratum are the archontic powers that administer the death-intent; naming them keeps the register cosmological (agents), not moral.

**¶2** ⟨p.130⟩ When the First Man came up from the abyss, [then] the Living Spirit brought the rulers whom he had hunted [together with the] element, that [the element of denial] of [the land of] darkness, that he might construct from them this universe and fashion everything appropriate for the greatness. From that time on, the place of the storehouses of the elements [he took out of the storehouses] he destroyed and laid waste the [storehouses of death] all that were in it.

> *¶2 #1 [tentative]:* Temporal-step marker is required: descent and return of First Man, then the Living Spirit’s ordering work.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The phrase must coordinate two brought-up sets: rulers plus the general principle-element that follows.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Correspondentially this is the general falsified principle belonging to the ‘domain of denial’ (darkness). ‘Element’ here is not a physical element but a general principle out of which effects are formed.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The Living Spirit brings up what the First Man has hunted; the next clause speaks of the storehouses of the elements, so the extraction-from-storehouses is the demanded connective action.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The chapter’s controlling term for the interiors devoted to spiritual death; it must recur here where their ‘place’ is then devastated.

**¶3** Just like a marsh, of which the fish and birds are snared in a net, and only the waters are left behind and the reeds and the plants that grow in it, this is also the case with the storehouses of death. The [First] Man snared their counsels and [their kindred]in that were [in them], and the storehouses were left behind in their [dwelling]ng place.

> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The text needs a plural collective allied to ‘counsels’: not ‘king’ but ‘kin’, i.e., associated powers bound to the deliberations.

**¶4** So then, the case of the land of darkness is like this, as is that of the storehouses that were in it.

**¶5** Just as the body of man has many limbs in it, [the land of darkness] has [storehouses from] its beginning [in it, and] all [the limbs] that hang from the great head of the [body]. A single root is the root of them all, because they all hang from the great head that is on top of the body. Should a limb be cut from the tip of [the]se small [h]eads, the great head that is on top of them [al]l stays in its place the way that it is [and] you find this person alive. Al[l] these sm[all h]eads are firmly set in it [the great head]

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The syntax requires a bridging phrase: the land has storehouses in it from its beginning, and then the comparison to limbs continues.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* Needed to complete the conditional comparison: even if a limb is cut, the great head remains, and you still find the person alive.
> *¶5 #12 [moderate]:* The following clause ‘are firmly set in it’ requires an antecedent ‘it’ = the great head; correspondentially the derivatives are fixed in the ruling falsity.

**¶6** Wh[en] they will cut from them [the little heads,] all of them, these little [heads] shall [become dead like] the corpses, because they might see [no power, because they no longer] be firmly set [to] in the body, which they joined wi[th it,]

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Direct continuation of ¶5’s ‘small heads’; the paragraph explains what happens when they are cut off in entirety.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* The comparison must make explicit the loss of life when severed: ‘dead like the corpses’ matches the spiritual explanation (void of life).
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* The logic demanded: they lose apparent power once severed from what fixed them; this matches the correspondential ‘no longer appear to possess power or stability’.
> *¶6 #6 [tentative]:* Needed to link ‘be firmly set’ to its ground: being firmly set in the body to which they were joined.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Completes the preposition+pronoun sequence (‘with it’) without importing later editorial matter; keeps the clause flowing into the lost continuation.

**¶7** In exactly this way, it is also the case that the storehouses of darkness are the same, the things from which the [darkness gushed] forth.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are short connectors or repeated cosmological terms constrained by the chapter’s governing correspondence (storehouses as interiors, heads as ruling falsities). A few larger lacunae in ¶1–2 and ¶6 admit more than one philological solution; restorations there were chosen to keep the impersonal, Iranian-cosmological register and to preserve the fixed correspondential sequence: investment → hunting/binding → extraction → ordering into universe → devastation of storehouses → persistence of the great head until all derivatives are cut.

---

## Chapter 54: [Concerning] the Quality of the Garments

**¶1** At the time when the F[ir]st [Man of the Greatness arose] he gave the leadership to his five [sons, the five powers: the] Living Spirit and the Mother of Life [in the bond of the lower region,] as the entire ruling-power is entwined and crucified [in the place of mixture;] they were poured out and mixed with each other, the light with the darkness and the darkness with the light.

> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The substrate opens with the first ordering principle coming into its appointed combat-state. The idiom in this tradition is the ‘First Man’ arising or standing to meet Darkness; adding ‘of the Greatness’ keeps the impersonal cosmological register while matching the function described in the spiritual translation (first good entering its state).
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* The ‘five sons’ are the pentad of operative powers. In the oldest layer they function as powers, not ecclesial persons; naming them as ‘powers’ matches the correspondential description of a ‘sufficient complement of powers’ entrusted with government of outward operations.
> *¶1 #4 [tentative]:* The gap follows ‘Living Spirit and the Mother of Life’ and precedes ‘as the entire ruling-power is entwined and crucified’. The substrate sense is the binding of rule in the outermost degree where mixture occurs; ‘bond of the lower region’ gives the state of constraint without importing later devotional phrasing.
> *¶1 #5 [moderate]:* Immediately before ‘they were poured out and mixed with each other’. The oldest layer regularly marks a specific boundary-plane where Light and Darkness commingle. This fill supplies that correspondential locus (outermost plane, ‘place of mixture’).

**¶2** At that time the Living Spirit called one of his sons, one rich with power. He gave to him the leadership of the garments. Now, he himself [after these things,] of [received the garments from him;] he hung them and set them up [the garments of the powers,] of the Father of Life [in the heights;] he set up all things [by the command] he opened [of the Father of Life;] ten firmaments [for the ordering of the All;] the wheel of the stars [the gates of the great firmament,] them and the [twelve signs,].

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Sets temporal sequence: the Living Spirit’s ordering act follows the initial entanglement described in ¶1.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* The ‘leadership of the garments’ implies stewardship over the external coverings, i.e., ordered ultimates. The son is constituted as governor by receiving them.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* Clarifies what ‘garments’ are in the oldest layer: external forms of the powers, i.e., ultimates for service, aligning with the spiritual translation (‘external truths for service’).
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The action ‘he hung them and set them up’ requires a spatial-cosmological placement. ‘In the heights’ maintains impersonal cosmography.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The fixing of the garments is ‘by a Divine establishment’ in the spiritual translation. In the substrate idiom this is ‘by the command’ (not exhortation).
> *¶2 #6 [strong]:* The next clause already begins ‘of the Father of Life ...’; the most economical restoration is to complete the governing phrase ‘by the command of the Father of Life’.
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* After ‘he set up all things’. The cosmological point is establishment of the total framework, matching ‘provided a stable framework for the lower heavens of the mind’.
> *¶2 #8 [tentative]:* ‘He opened ... ten firmaments’ suggests ‘gates’ or ‘openings’ as the means of access between degrees. ‘Gates of the great firmament’ suits the correspondential ‘distinct levels for reception’.
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* After ‘the wheel of the stars ...’. In the oldest astral-cosmographic register, the wheel is articulated by the zodiacal ‘twelve signs’. This matches ‘general patterns of knowledge and their revolutions’.

**¶3** ⟨p.132⟩ The Living Spirit is like this: Just as [a wise craftsman,] and an architect who might build a dwelling for the king, and divide it up into separate houses and [separate chambers] everything that is required for beauty [and he furnishes in it] and he cleans them. And also the stones [the stains, and scrapes away the defilement from] them for beauty and {loveliness}, and he arranges them and makes them so that should the king come and inhabit that palace, he might glorify him.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The simile requires a figure embodying formative wisdom before ‘and an architect’. ‘Wise craftsman’ keeps the impersonal didactic tone and prepares the parallel with the carpenter in ¶4.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The architect ‘divide it up into separate houses and [separate ...]’. The spiritual translation stresses distinguishing interior and exterior regions. ‘Separate chambers’ supplies the needed subdivision within the one dwelling.
> *¶3 #3 [tentative]:* After ‘everything that is required for beauty ...’. The substrate point is provision of all requisites of order, not mere ornament. ‘Furnishes in it’ keeps the building analogy concrete.
> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* The line ‘and he cleans them’ refers to removal of what defiles reception. Restoring as ‘stains…defilement’ matches the correspondential ‘removes what defiles reception’.

**¶4** Furthermore, the Father of Life resembles a wise and skilled carpenter [who has in his mind the whole form,] before it was worked [as a log,] before it was established and made beautiful and [unshaped,] carpenter [this] works in it, but [first exists] in him in his [thought,] in his understanding, and he brings the saw and the adze, and cuts it into separate pieces [and he separates] it and he pulls the [waste from] it with the {plane} and he smooths it. He shall make many [roughness from] wooden implements, arranging them shape by shape; he cuts and produces wooden implements from it, wooden implements of different kinds, (such as) dishes, bowls. This wood, that [useful] since previously it had not been worked before it was sealed by [ll]ll and wisdom of this dextrous carpenter. He cut it up into different sorts of implements useful to his master.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The Father of Life is likened to a carpenter whose design exists inwardly before work. This fill expresses the prior internal pattern (intention and understanding) from which tools and operations proceed.
> *¶4 #2 [tentative]:* The ‘wood’ is the unworked subject-matter before formation. ‘As a log’ gives the natural base for subsequent sawing and planing.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Pairs with ‘before it was established and made beautiful’. The substrate emphasizes unformed matter prior to wisdom’s determination.
> *¶4 #4 [minimal]:* Completes ‘and ... carpenter ... works in it’. A minimal demonstrative keeps the sentence coherent without importing later names.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Corresponds to the teaching that the whole pattern is present inwardly ‘before anything is brought forth into act’. ‘First exists in him’ fits the sequence.
> *¶4 #6 [strong]:* Pairs naturally with ‘understanding’ in the next phrase (‘in his ... in his understanding’).
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* After ‘cuts it into separate pieces ...’. The carpenter’s next act is discrimination: separating what must be distinguished.
> *¶4 #8 [moderate]:* Before ‘and he pulls the ... it with the plane’. The spiritual translation explicitly includes ‘removes what is superfluous’. ‘Waste from it’ is the natural analogue.
> *¶4 #9 [moderate]:* To prepare for planing and smoothing: ‘removes what is uneven’ and ‘reduces what is rough’. ‘Roughness from it’ fits the tool sequence.
> *¶4 #10 [strong]:* ‘He shall make many ... wooden implements’ requires the adjective ‘useful’, aligning with ‘each according to its own form…assigned for service’.

**¶5** So is it also with the Father of Life. In his wisdom he has [formed the whole likeness] in limbs [all the faculties] palace [of this great] in it or in a [or in a member] useful [for every service,] the ships of light [and all the lamps of light,] all the other works [and] in the [lowest region,] so that when the great king comes, he who is the Third Ambassador, who [is the Mediator of the Light,] he might dwell in this universe [having come in glory,]

> *¶5 #1 [tentative]:* The Father of Life ‘in his wisdom’ forms within the ‘universal human pattern’. ‘Whole likeness’ is the natural-layer phrasing for that archetypal form.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual translation (‘all faculties and functions’) and keeps the technical, impersonal tone.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Precedes ‘palace’; the ordered universe is the ‘great palace’ for indwelling of the kingly end.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The text contrasts ‘in it or in a ...’. Correspondentially, uses exist both in the whole and in each part. ‘Member’ fits ‘limbs’ earlier and the universal-human analogy.
> *¶5 #5 [strong]:* Supplies the teleology: each faculty exists ‘for its proper service’.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* Balances ‘the ships of light’. In the substrate, ‘lamps’ are fixed illuminants (ordered truths) while ‘ships’ are conveyances (operations of ascent). This matches ‘works of illumination’.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* After ‘in the ... so that’. The ordering is in the outermost plane where mixture and governance occur, matching ‘in the lowest degree’.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* The Third Ambassador functions cosmologically as mediation between higher and lower, enabling conjunction. ‘Mediator of the Light’ keeps the oldest functional naming rather than later christological overlay.
> *¶5 #10 [tentative]:* Completes ‘who ... he might dwell in this universe’. The ‘approach’ of the great king in the similes becomes the advent of the mediating end ‘in glory’, still within cosmological register (manifestation of order).

**¶6** ⟨p.133⟩ ... live. The light, however, that is in it [that is gathered out from it will] wise, and he takes it up to the land; but the waste and the darkness will be gathered and thrown down to the abyss.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* ¶6 resumes with an ellipsis then ‘... live. The light, however, that is in it ... wise’. The required sense is separation: what can live is extracted; the light is taken up; the waste is cast down. This fill supplies the extraction clause that governs the ensuing separation.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae can be restored at the level of function demanded by the correspondential structure: mixture in the outermost plane, delegation of five operative powers, ordering of ultimates as ‘garments’, establishment of firmaments and astral revolutions, and the twin craft-similitudes (architect, carpenter) expressing inward pattern preceding outward formation. A few micro-gaps in ¶4 are too underconstrained to recover exact diction beyond minimal connective sense, but the cosmological logic tightly constrains the main missing terms (place of mixture, separation, removal of waste, smoothing, usefulness, mediation by the Third Ambassador).

---

## Chapter 55: Concerning the Fashioning of Adam

**¶1** There are some amo]ng us telling us that we [understand] that the (Ambassador) consents about [that]t f[fig] of flesh; he who shall be fashi[oned] at another time. And next, he says: [The Ambassa]dor displayed his image in the un[iverse]. The rulers and the powers of the universe saw h[is image], and they formed their shapes after his likeness, who are Adam and Eve. Behold, you see that god con[sen]ts to its happening; because of this, he has displayed his image to the rulers; and they constructed [a figure of flesh] If he was not consenting to humankind, he would not have uncovered his image, nor they fashioned mankind after his likeness!

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation has “we understand…”, so the older substrate likely had the plain cognitive verb rather than a confessional ‘know’.
> *¶1 #3 [strong]:* Completes “figure” with the following letters outside the bracket.
> *¶1 #4 [strong]:* Completes “fashioned”.
> *¶1 #9 [moderate]:* What the rulers ‘constructed’ from the displayed image is the outward human pattern, i.e., the bodily form (the paired Adam and Eve follows immediately).

**¶2** Regarding the Ambassador, the occasion when he came displaying his image, he [d]id not come so th[a]t he might reveal his image to the ru[l]ers in the universe. Rather, he came and displayed to the universe be[cause of h]is soul and his son, who is [his soul] every [bond], that he might enable him to live [from death] and make for him a release; and to free (the soul) from every bond and all the fetters, in which it is entangled and bound.

> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The manifestation is for the sake of the living interior (soul) that is in captivity; this matches the release theme that follows.
> *¶2 #6 [tentative]:* Corresponds to “every bond and all the fetters” later in the sentence; the son is what loosens bonds in the lower domain.
> *¶2 #7 [tentative]:* The aim is to ‘enable him to live’ and ‘make for him a release’; the contrary state in the substrate idiom is death.

**¶3** Still, you, look and see! The rulers and the authorities, in that they had no form in [truth in] him, they lusted for his image. They reflected upon themselves, that [they] had no [one] like him in their creation. They sealed hi[s im]a[g]e in their heart, within their soul. Afterward[s, they] built it according to his likeness; they formed Adam and [Ev]e. They sealed the image of the exalted one in their [soul]. So, they [co]pied with an imitation; but they have not [copied i]n truth! Rather, just as [in a painted image] it is made as these, for [i]t came [from their own matter] but it is not l[ik]e him in truth. [Again, so too] is this figure of ma[mankind. They copi]ed it against the image of the exalted one [and they did not make it in truth]

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* They had ‘no form in truth in him’: correspondentially, no genuine participation in the higher human quality, hence lust and imitation.
> *¶3 #11 [moderate]:* An imitation that resembles but is not ‘in truth’; the substrate commonly contrasts living form with a mere painted likeness.
> *¶3 #13 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual sense: it ‘came from what could be taken and shaped in their realm’.
> *¶3 #17 [moderate]:* Reinforces the doctrinal pivot: likeness without truth, i.e., correspondence severed from its inner degree.

**¶4** Similarly the [t]ree, for while it is like the [image, yet] not [copi]ed in truth.

> *¶4 #2 [tentative]:* The ‘tree’ in the spiritual translation is a seeming living perception that resembles higher life; thus: like the image, yet not in truth.

**¶5** This matt[er is comparable to] this: As a great, fre[e] woman [dwells hidden] within her [treasures and ornaments] rich in wealth [many] po[ss]essions; she is also beautiful in her beauty, and is virtuous in her modesty. She walks in a free and reputable position. The fame of her beauty has spread in every c[i]ty. She sur[surpasses all women in] Behold h[er] chamber [and there is not] one who is mad, because [of] the nobles and the great ones. Al[so], they lust for her beautiful face. They did not [able to seize] her, because she is hidden in her palace [nor to approach] within in a [for she is] meet that woman [and no one can] his so[n]; or rather the head [upon her head; for]hat garment. This free woman shall [put on] leave the cha[mber] behind her and come to the street [and she will] her head [lift up] and she reveals her [face and her beauty and her splendor] because of her beloved broth[er]. This wom[an], on whom no man ever looked, nor did they ever see her fa[ce], as she neither desires nor rejoices [in wantonness or in pride] leave her chamber behind her, and come in the midst of mankind [but grief compelled her] and everyone view her. The hones[t] men and the nobles, even the servants too, and the [common people also] look at her.

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The free woman corresponds to interior wisdom held within its own order; she is ‘hidden in her palace’.
> *¶5 #4 [tentative]:* Fits ‘rich in wealth … possessions’ in a substrate register (goods and truths as treasures).
> *¶5 #5 [tentative]:* Quantifies possessions; matches the spreading fame motif.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* The simile’s point is excellence beyond rivals, matching the spiritual sense ‘surpasses’ as supereminent wisdom.
> *¶5 #10 [tentative]:* Leads into the chamber scene: no one can reach her while hidden.
> *¶5 #13 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: they cannot possess it while guarded within interior order.
> *¶5 #14 [tentative]:* Extends the impossibility of taking her while in the palace.
> *¶5 #15 [moderate]:* Prepares the causal clause ‘because she is hidden…’.
> *¶5 #16 [tentative]:* Keeps the impersonal, structural tone of the substrate.
> *¶5 #18 [tentative]:* The garment over the head corresponds to an outer covering of manifestation; the syntax anticipates “that garment”.
> *¶5 #19 [moderate]:* She ‘puts on that garment’ before going out: manifestation by assuming an outer form.
> *¶5 #21 [moderate]:* Introduces her going forth into the street.
> *¶5 #22 [tentative]:* She lifts what is on her head to reveal the face: disclosure of interior quality into externals.
> *¶5 #23 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense stresses visibility of excellence; ‘splendor’ fits the wisdom correspondence.
> *¶5 #27 [moderate]:* Directly constrained by the spiritual translation: not for display, not from self-exaltation.
> *¶5 #28 [moderate]:* The key causal hinge in the simile: constraint of grief for the beloved.
> *¶5 #30 [tentative]:* Balances ‘honest men and nobles… servants too’; the simile includes all lower degrees seeing the manifestation.

**¶6** ⟨p.135⟩ In accord with this simile: Again, [this] too [is] how [it is] the Ambassador becomes [in the same manner] as this free woman [not] according to wantonness, in pride, nor [nor that she co]nsented to the outer world, she [did not consent], she revealed her face and her [beau]ty [to those outside] but only due to the constraint of grief [about] her [bel]ov[oved one].

> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* Provides the copular link into the application of the simile.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Marks the formal correspondential alignment of simile and cosmological event.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Constrained by spiritual sense: ‘nor from consent to be possessed by the outer world’.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* She revealed face and beauty outwardly, i.e., to the outer world.

**¶7** Now, this too is the way of the Third Ambassador, when he came and display[ed his] image with a show towards all the powers. [He did] not [co]me so that all the rulers and the author[it]ies would sculpt a form [after h]is likeness. R[ather], he came because of his son being crucified in the totality; [fo]r he would release him, free him, and rescue him from oppres-sion. Yet, when the rulers looked o[n] him, [th]ey lusted for his image. They fashioned after his [like]ness Adam and Eve, without the consent of the gre[at]ness.

**¶9** [Understa]nd also this other (matter) that I will teach you. For, [a]s with Adam and Eve, whom the rulers fashioned and [se]aled after the image of the exalted one, so too this [i]s how a manifold divinity and many powers and angels [were stamped and received] the stamp of that image, which was displayed to them. For, in the majesty of the l[ig]ht and (the image's) love, they glowed with their desire and they were undone. Th[ey] were eager for their lust; like a m[man who looks] to the majesty of a lusted after face, and [is driven onward] and he is eager for the {} of the desire.

> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* The point is imprinting: many divinities and powers ‘received the stamp of that image’.
> *¶9 #9 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual sense: ‘driven onward by the force of desire’.

**¶10** ⟨p.136⟩ This is [als]o the case with those powers, when they looked on that majestic and beautiful [imag]e, they () [fashioned within themselves] a first image. They received the stamp of th[that image], which was displayed to them. They copied [outwardly, wh]ich th[ey co]pied out in a likeness, it being made [in the forms] the ones that they had put forth, which had received a [stamp of this im]age; but inward even to the rocks [were hardened] which occur from place to place. The {} of that image, which was displayed in the universe [likeness] they received a stamp, it being revealed there, and an impress.

> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* Constrained by spiritual sense: they ‘fashioned within themselves a primary copied appearance’.
> *¶10 #5 [moderate]:* Bridges to “which they copied out in a likeness”.
> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* They copied in likenesses of the forms they had already projected.
> *¶10 #9 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: inwardly hardened in lowest confirmations, ‘even to the rocks’.
> *¶10 #10 [moderate]:* “The likeness of that image… they received a stamp”: summarizes the correspondential result.

**¶11** Now, this new birth that they begot in a new [form] in the world, is Adam and Eve. [There is not a] form in their world since the beginning like it; because that [im]age [occ]urs not in the worlds b[el]ow.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Prepares “new birth… in a new form in the world”.

**¶12** Not all the figures that have been fashioned were fashioned by birth from displayed intercourse. Rather, there are other figures fashioned [without intercourse]

> *¶12 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense distinguishes figures not from ‘displayed intercourse’; thus other figures fashioned without such conjunction.

**¶13** One of them is the sea [gi]ant, the 'sweeping' from the sphere, which the [Father] of Life discharged into the sea. Its own fire and thought fashioned it [in] a figure.

**¶14** The second figure is the one [that] fell to the earth. It made the sea its womb. Its own l[us]t became its sculptor. It alone fashioned it to a nature, which is the root of death. Yet, when it came up from the sea, it [set itself to rise up] that it might destroy the works [that come from the works] of Life. They immediately sent against it the Adamant of Light, the great 'instruction of vigour'. He humbled it in the districts of the north. He has trampled underfoot and set h[him]self upon it, until the end of the world.

> *¶14 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘When it rose into clearer manifestation… it set itself to destroy’.
> *¶14 #4 [tentative]:* Completes “destroy the works … of Life” in an impersonal substrate idiom.

**¶15** ⟨p.137⟩ [The] third figure is the nature that fell on wh[at] is dr[y. It] fashioned the tree and set itself there. [and from] new figure that was fashioned [and it]isplays.

> *¶15 #4 [tentative]:* Links the dry ground with the consequent fashioning of a system (tree) from that dryness.
> *¶15 #5 [tentative]:* Carries forward: the new figure was fashioned and it displays.

**¶16** So, in accord with the essence they [inclined toward]ness; but yet th[ey] did not come with the King of Dark[ness to the wo]rld. Rather, their fashioning [came] about [through mixture; for] they are composite and have been displayed in the world.

> *¶16 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: ‘According to their essential quality they tended toward denial and obscurity’.
> *¶16 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: these appearances arose through mixed and composite states; ‘for they are composite…’.

**Assessment:** Most restorations are tightly constrained where the spiritual sense specifies function: imprinting, imitation without truth, manifestation compelled by grief, and the purpose of the Ambassador’s appearing. Mid-word lacunae are certain. A minority of gaps (notably ¶2 #6–7, parts of the simile in ¶5, and some connective tissue in ¶10–15) remain only moderately constrained because multiple substrate-idiom phrases can express the same correspondence; those have been completed with the most neutral, impersonal cosmological diction consistent with the oldest layer.

---

## Chapter 56: Concerning Saklas and his Powers

**¶2** The sin that spurted out from the rulers, which is Matter, shot up [tow]ards the image of the Ambassador. It was cut off from [tha]t place and came down to the earth, for they did not accept [it wi]thin the firmaments. When it came do[wn to] the earth, it formed the tree. It was established within the wood and formed the fruits. And when [the] abortions fell [to] the ground, to the earth, at the [second] time [it came up again in them and entered the rulers]

> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires a return of the same disordering principle through the malformed works (abortions) back into the governing powers. This supplies the transition into ¶3–¶4 where it ‘assumes’ the fruits and speaks in the chief ruler, and later ‘went into the rulers through the fruits’.

**¶3** ⟨p.138⟩
[And when it came up] fruits, it assumed the[m] and spoke in the ruler, their leader. He says to his companions: Come! Give me your light, and I will construct for you an image aft[er] the likeness of the exalted one. What he said, they did: they gave it to him, and he constructed [the form of Adam and Eve after the likeness of the exalted one].

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Restores the needed link from ¶2’s return of Matter in the abortions to its presence ‘in the fruits’. The teaching-layer voice is impersonal and sequential: the disorder rises again, then is present ‘in the fruits’.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The leader’s project in the oldest substrate is the fabrication of a human likeness modeled on the higher pattern; ¶4 explicitly says they ‘formed Adam and Eve after the likeness’. This gap completes the causal statement ‘and he constructed …’.

**¶4** The sin that s[purted out from the rulers,] which is the Matter that saw the im[age of the Ambassa]dor and that formed the tre[e and was established i]n [i]t; afterwards it came up in the [fruits and assumed them]. It went in to the rulers [and by its power] they formed Adam and Eve after the [likeness of the] exalt[ed one]. Through the energy of the sin that had s[een the image of the] Ambassador, it went into the (rulers) [through the fruits. It] assumed them and they [t]oo sculp[ted with it. And they] begat each of them according to his destiny [in their generations]; one he resembles, one he does not resemble.

> *¶4 #1 [strong]:* Matches ¶2 verbatim and keeps the substrate’s structural repetition: the same ‘sin’ as gross Matter bursting from the rulers.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* The spiritual sequence requires: lodged in the tree, then rises in the fruits, then takes on their quality (‘assumed them’) before entering and driving the rulers.
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* Provides the required causal hinge: it enters the rulers and, by its driving efficacy, they produce the human form (Adam and Eve).
> *¶4 #12 [moderate]:* Completes the parallelism: it ‘assumed them’ and they ‘too sculpted’ by the same borrowed force; ‘with it’ keeps the impersonal mechanism of influx.
> *¶4 #13 [moderate]:* The line describes successive begetting ‘each according to his destiny’; the oldest-layer register prefers impersonal succession of states, here rendered as ‘generations’.

**¶5** Behold, I have taught you this lesson, how the rulers shaped Adam and Eve by the action of the sin that went into the [m] through the fruits, and he was ordained after the image of the exalted one.

**¶6** The moulder placed in the form of Adam and Eve limbs, outside and within, for perception and activity. He was apportioned house by house. For everything that his perceptions and elements will receive externally there are internal storehouses and repositories and cavities; and what is received in to them is stored in them. Whenever they will be questioned about what is deposited in their internal storehouses, they bring out what they have received within and give it to the questioner who requested it of them.

**¶7** In this way his faculty [of the eyes has] outer [limbs] to look at [all sights,] every type with [in its storehouses.]

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* Paragraph 7 anticipates ¶8’s explicit treatment of the eyes. The missing phrase should introduce the specific outward faculty being exemplified.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Fits the correspondential point: the outward organ is for noticing ‘every type’ presented externally; ‘sights’ is the natural carrier for ‘images’ developed in ¶8.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Completes the thought: receiving ‘every type’ and placing it within the internal repositories, which the chapter repeatedly calls storehouses.

**¶8** ⟨p.139⟩
Also [the fa]culty of the eyes [has] houses and cavities and repositories and stores within, so that every image it might see, whether good or evil, whether loveable or detestable or lustful, it can receive into its storehouses and repositories. Also, when the faculty of the eyes is pleased to send out the image that it saw and took in, it can go in to its storehouses [at the] time and think and seek [every image that it stored within,] and it brings it out [and] gives it to the questioner who requested it and the one who wan[t]ed it—whether it be something from lu[stful thing,]n image of love [or something] hateful. And thus shall that faculty (of the eyes) produce and do what it does in each category.

> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* After ‘think and seek’ the faculty searches its inner memory for what was deposited; the phrase must point to the retrieved stored image before ‘and it brings it out’.
> *¶8 #7 [moderate]:* Balances the triad in the sentence: lustful, loveable, hateful; the spiritual sense lists craving, genuine love, aversion.
> *¶8 #8 [moderate]:* The remaining category is aversion. The natural text already ends with ‘hateful’; this gap supplies the generic ‘something’ leading into ‘hateful’.

**¶9** The faculty of the ears has its own storehouses also. Every sound it might receive, whether good or evil, shall be taken in and placed in its houses and inner repositories, and it is guarded in its [inner storehouses] for a thousand days. After a thousand days, if someone comes and asks that faculty about the sound that it heard [at] this time and took into its storehouses, immediately it [sh]all g[o] into its repositories and seek and review and search after this word, and send it out from where it was first put, the place in which it was kept.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The ears ‘guard’ what was received; the thing guarded is the deposit in inner repositories, consistently termed storehouses in this chapter.

**¶10** [In] like order, the faculty of scent [s]hall function just as that of the eyes and that of the auditory organs. Every [o]dour it shall smell it shall take in [to it] and [d]eposit in its inner storehouses. Every time it will be [a]sked by a questioner, it shall go in [to its inner repositories] and [seek in its] storehouse and remember [these odours] only these things.

> *¶10 #7 [moderate]:* Required by the repeated mechanism: questioned → goes into inner repositories → searches → brings out. Scent follows the same order as eyes and ears.
> *¶10 #8 [moderate]:* Pairs with the next phrase ‘storehouse and remember’: the faculty searches within its storehouse to recall the stored odour.
> *¶10 #9 [moderate]:* Specifies the object of recollection in the scent faculty; keeps the chapter’s repetitive, enumerative style.

**¶11** ⟨p.140⟩
However, even the mouth and the tongue within it, and the taste organ, have a faculty dwelling in them. Again, that faculty too, of taste, has thus cavities and repositories set apart for it. It too receives these tastes and gathers them in. A[nd] at any moment when someone will ask of a taste, if [it wishes,] it shall send it out and remember tha[t] taste. It shall snare and give even the mar[k of] th[a]t taste; give its memory to the qu[est]ioner who asks for it.

> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The mechanism is volitional recall: when asked, if it so chooses, it sends out the remembered taste. This matches the impersonal faculty-language of the chapter.

**¶12** Again, the fac[ul]ty of touch by the hands is also s[o]: When it might touch, touch shall [receive i]ts memory. And it [takes] it in to its inner repository [until] som[eo]ne will ask this faculty for the memory. [Immediately], it shall go in again and bring out the memory of this touch that it made, and give it to whoever asks for it.

**¶13** And the faculty of the heart that rules over them all is much the most like this. Every thing that these five faculties will receive and put in store for the faculty of the heart it shall receive and guard. Any time that they will ask for their deposit it shall send out and give every thing that they gave to it.

**¶14** For the counsel of the heart is like the king. All the ad[v]isers of his city and the leaders of his a[r]my are placed with him. Any time if his genera[ls] will entreat him for whatever they wish, he shall give them the ad[v]ice and opinion by which they move.

**¶15** Furthermore, it is like a cellarman: [Eve]ry thing that will be entrusted to it by them, and given to it, it shall receive and put in separate vessels and places. Any time [if they ask it for what was entrusted to it, it brings it out]

> *¶15 #2 [moderate]:* The steward simile demands the same retrieval formula used throughout: deposit entrusted → stored in distinct vessels → when requested → produced from the place it was set. This also leads smoothly into ¶16 ‘… in the place that it put it.’

**¶16** ⟨p.141⟩
... in the place that it put it.

**¶17** This also is the way of the faculty of the body. The vision that the eyes will see and give to it, and it receives it in, it shall bring out again from the storehouses wherein it had placed it; and give it to whoever asks for it.

**¶18** Again, [the] sound that the ears will hear and give to it, and it receives it from them. At any time when someone will ask it for that sound, it shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked it for that sound. It shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked.

**¶19** Again, [the] faculty of smell in this same pattern; and that of taste is of the same [ty]pe. The touch by the han[ds too,] they shall bring it out in to [its storehouses and repositories] And whenever they will as[k it] for it, it shall bring it out and gi[ve it].

> *¶19 #3 [moderate]:* Completes ‘hands’ and keeps the parallel list of faculties whose deposits can be produced on request.
> *¶19 #4 [moderate]:* Matches the chapter’s fixed pair for inner retention; touch is brought out ‘into’ these internal places before being given outwardly on request.

**¶20** Indeed, watchmen are a[t] these doors guarding them, and bolts are fastened on the doors at the hands of the guards that guard them!

**¶21** Just like watchmen guarding a camp with towers and watch-posts. As the holding rooms come out from there and the bolts are upon the gates, the guards control the bolts of the gates while they sit at the entrance [of] the watchposts. So, someone who belongs to their city as a fellow [cit]izen of them, belonging to their country, for him they shall open [the ga]te and receive him in by their will. However, the foreigner who comes they shall prevent, and leave him standing at the gate. They shall not yield to let him enter. Again, another one whom they will receive in, with great trouble before [they] take him in, you shall find many holding rooms [behind them,] that [and locks and bolts;] there are a mass [within] them. The number of d[oor] bolts [of the gates] are [many,] Whenever someone will wish [to bring out,] the bolts at his hand, opens the gate and brings out whoever is hidden within.

> *¶21 #5 [moderate]:* The analogy requires interior ‘holding rooms’ behind the watchposts and gates, corresponding to inner containments behind the senses.
> *¶21 #6 [moderate]:* Completes the enumeration of restraints upon admission points, aligning with the later ‘number of door bolts’.
> *¶21 #7 [moderate]:* Supports the phrase ‘there are a mass … them’: a mass ‘within them’ (inside the holding rooms and gates).
> *¶21 #9 [moderate]:* ‘Door bolts of the gates’ keeps the concrete image and matches the later ‘guards control the bolts of the gates’.
> *¶21 #10 [moderate]:* Required by ‘the number … are …’: the teaching stresses multiplicity of restraints and containments.
> *¶21 #11 [moderate]:* Leads into the closing clause: when someone wishes ‘to bring out’, he controls the bolts, opens, and brings out the hidden one.

**¶22** This body too is like the mighty camp. And the gates of the camp with their guards are like the orifices and organs of the body. Now, the orifices of the body are of sight, hearing and smell; and they that send out words. There are many sentinels and a mass of guards placed over the limbs of the body, guarding their orifices. And the faculty in it is the directive of the body, the queen of the entire camp; so that whenever it wishes it can open, or when it wishes it can shut.

**¶23** However, if and wh[en] the Light Mind comes, it shall tu[rn i]n to the orifices of the body. The rulers, who [a]re the door gua[rds], shall h[in]der and restrain it from en[ter]ing. They shall bind the circu[it] of the body so that the foreign[er] can not enter it.

**¶24** Nevertheless, the Light Mind by its wisdom and awe and diligence shall hum[ble] the guards who are set at the body's orifices. When it finishes humbling them it shall take fr[o]m them bolts to all the orifices of the body. Now, the orifices that had been opened before to the parades of lust, to receive in the sights of pleasure, to transfer the [wor]ds of the practice of li[f]e's concerns, to receive in the t[astes] of the different foods and unclean meals together with the sin therein; whoever stops receiving th[is] in, his heart and mind shall follow after.

**¶25** So, now, bec[ause] the b[olt]s to the body of the righteous person are in the hands of the Light M[in]d within, he is open [to receive] in all that is pleasing to God. He is op[en to take] in by his eyes the visions of lov[e and justice] and righteousness. Indeed, due to this, the si[ghts and] the [ad]ornme[nt of the gar]ments of kings, which are woven with gold; and the garments of silken wom[en], which are woven with gold and pearls, made beautiful for the shape of lust; first they were lovable before the eyes of the chosen person. Yet, now, by the power of the Light Mind that has suffused him and lived in the body he has humbled lust, the goddess of the body. The parades of lust have become loathsome in the presence of the righteous person. How-ever, the aspects of divinity and righteousness and humility and scorned clothes are pleasing to him.

> *¶25 #6 [moderate]:* The righteous person’s opened sight receives what corresponds to genuine love and right order; ‘love and righteousness’ is already present, so the gap likely completes ‘love … and righteousness’ as ‘love and justice’ in the older structural register.

**¶26** Again, [these] ears were first opening to empty sounds and to the melodies of lust, to the secrecies of [wic]kedness. All these were pleasing at first, at the time when he he[a]rd them. Yet, now, by the power of the Light Mind, the sounds of lust and the words of magic and evil mysteries have become loathsome in his presence; as he is not pleased to hear them from this time on. Rather, he likes all the time to listen to the sounds of the lessons of righteousness, the words of the psalms and of the prayers, the praise of the hymns and the lessons of truth, and the knowledge of charity.

**¶27** [S]imply, all orifices in similar fashion were opening at one time to wickedness, but now have opened to the good. Sin was ruling in the body this time, doing anything it liked, having none in command.

**¶28** Yet, [n]ow, authority has been taken from the Sin in the org[ans] of the body. The Living Spirit has become lord over all its limbs. It has bound them with a chain of peace, and sealed them with the seal of truth. It has opened the orifices of the body to the good, and because of this the good enters them through the ears and the eyes. It dwells in the heart. The H[o]ly Spirit reigns. It does all that it pleases. It annuls [the] will of [S]in, the begin-ning and the end. [In contrast], the will of the [Holy Spirit] comes about, and the purpose of the Light Mind.

> *¶28 #7 [moderate]:* Removes the scribal double-l in ‘Holly’ to keep the oldest-layer register consistent while preserving the intended cosmological agent opposed to Sin.

**¶29** With wisdom and skill make yourselves secure at the circuit of the body's orifices, so that the sin that dwells in the body has no power over you. Nor does it take from you your light, nor scatter it from place to place, nor (drive) it from site to site in various forms.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations and are secure. The substantial gaps occur at ¶2 #8, ¶3 #1, ¶3 #4, ¶4 #5–6, ¶4 #12–13, ¶7 #1–4, ¶8 #4 and ¶15 #2, plus several in ¶21. These were restored by enforcing the chapter’s correspondential mechanics: repeated cycles of influx (Matter or a faculty) entering, being stored in inner ‘storehouses and repositories’, and being retrieved on request; and, in the cosmogonic section, the cyclic return of the same disorder through the products (fruits, abortions) into the rulers to fabricate a human likeness after a higher pattern. Exact wording remains moderately constrained by this structural parallelism, though some phrases (especially ¶21’s camp analogy) could admit alternative but equivalent formulations.

---

## Chapter 57: Concerning the Generation of Adam.

**¶2** Now, no[te] that Adam is mighty in his stature, and great is his size. Even his years are many! He spent many years living in the world. And he is not the only one we have found to be mighty; but all the others who were begotten in his generation, many are their years too. Still, these people who are born today, their stature has been diminished in power; even their years are lessened. Also, why is the birth today, of [the]y who are born, altered compared with that of these first [ones, and they did not change from] place to place? See, even the stars and signs of the zodiac continue in their positions. Why now have the age and the years of these last ones diminished, and he has also become smaller, compared to these ancient ones who belong to these first generations?

> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires that the fixed cosmic order (stars, zodiacal signs) remains in place; what changes is the receiver’s state. So the question contrasts “these first ones” with later births, while affirming no displacement of the ordered framework. Filling with a clause that keeps the ‘not moved, not displaced’ idea before “place to place.”

**¶3** They increased, have turned, and diminished; because there are five types of authorities and leaders appointed in the sphere of zodiacal signs, and the heavens above it. They have names they are ca[lled] by: the first name is the year; the sec[on]d is the month; the third is the day; the fou[rth] is the hour; the fifth is the moment. Now, these five positions, and these five houses, exist in the sphere and the heavens. These places have five powers, and they are made masters over them. The master of the year exists; the master of the month exists; the master of the day exists; the master of the hour exists; the master of the moment exists. In turn one is above his companion; and in turn the one above is made the master over the one below him.

**¶4** So, mankind is begotten, and [the ani]mals. They are born in these powers. And, thus, these powers have received authority since the beginning of creation till the end of the world. Now, in the beginning of the generations the powers who were masters over the years reigned. Therefore, because of this, the offspring born in their generations and their lifetime: their years were found to be greater. They attain a long life span in the generation of Adam, and [tha]t of Sethel his son, and that of the ones who came after him.

**¶5** After they ceased to reign, namely the powers who are masters over the years; the powers who are masters over the months themselves reigned. Like the months, for they are less than the years; again this [is] also the case with the offspring w[ho were] born in them. Their life span is less [than the life span of the ones born] in the generation of the powers who master the years!

**¶6** When the authority of the powers who master the months was completed; the powers of the days received authority after them. Therefore, like the days, for they are less than the months, this also is the case with the offspring who were born in them. Their life span is less than the life span of the ones born in the powers who are masters over the months. Again, even so the offspring who were born in the hours, and of the moments. Their {conduit} is less than that of the months and the days.

**¶9** Now, can you recount to me this other (matter): By what sort of birth was the entire offspring of mankind made from one man and one woman, Adam and Eve?

**¶10** For the manner of these first generations is that they are richer in their light and purer in stature. Even their years are greater. Thus, again, even their offspring is much more than the offspring of this tim[e]. They were spending a longer time [forming] in [the w]omb of their mother. Afterwards, they [brought forth] a number in a single womb. There are times five were brought forth from a single womb, and times six were brought forth from [a] single womb! There are some lesser than this; again, some greater than this. Indeed, because of this the offspring of Eve multiplied, and of her children. The earth was filled by reason of them alone; because the offspring from the generation of the people of old, who they bore into the world, differ greatly from those of today. Their conception and their birth is not like latter births, brought forth nowadays from the woman's uterus.

> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The substrate point is extended interior gestation: a longer inward formation of new life before external manifestation. “Forming” matches this correspondential mechanics without introducing later devotional language.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* After the prolonged interior formation, the text shifts to outward production: they ‘brought forth’ multiple beginnings within one interior reception. This keeps the impersonal, cosmological register.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations and are certain. The main reconstruction points are ¶2 #3 and ¶10 #2 and #4; these are constrained by the chapter’s correspondential logic: the cosmic order is fixed while human receptivity diminishes, and the early ‘womb’ period signifies prolonged interior formation before outward birth. Exact wording is therefore moderately constrained rather than uniquely determinable.

---

## Chapter 58: The Four Powers that grieve

**¶2** ⟨p.147⟩ The first who grieves is the first Mother of Life. She is sad because of her children who are set in affliction, for they were conjoined with the Darkness and the [poison]son. They have been bound with the entire ruling-power. For what was she grieving, [watching]tching over them taking these afflictions? Whenever she might [see]ee them and how suffering is brought upon them, she shall grieve and be unhappy on account of them.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The pair “Darkness and poison” in this substrate names (1) denial of good and (2) the destructive falsity that corrupts what is living. The grief concerns the children’s conjunction with both: evil as state (Darkness) and falsity as corrupting agent (poison).
> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* The sense required is vigilant oversight by the Mother of Life over the afflicted offspring; “watching over them” matches the correspondential idea of wisdom’s continual perception and care.

**¶3** ⟨p.148⟩ [The]econd one who is troubled of heart is the First Man, whose [greatness]ness and kingdom is established in the li[light] ship [of his Father]. And he too is troubled of heart because of his five sons who are set in the midst of danger. For any time he may see that they are given trouble, always he might hear the sound of their weeping as they cry out because of the blows and wounds of the enemy who assaults them daily; and as he sees them like this constantly, he shall g[grie]eve and mourn on account of his [s]o[n]s.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The clause begins with the ordinal construction “The second…”. Restoring only “The” yields the expected substrate register without adding later framing.
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The First Man’s “greatness” here is his full power and ordered domain (primary good in its might). This is the fixed cosmic description, not honorific praise.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual context requires that his kingdom is established in wisdom from the Divine. In the oldest layer this is expressed as derivation from the Father of Greatness, without institutional or Christological overlay: the light-ship is “of his Father.”

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tight, mid-word restorations constrained by fixed surrounding letters. The only substantial lacuna is the light-ship clause; correspondentially it must indicate establishment from the Divine source (the Father), so “of his Father” is the most economical oldest-layer completion, though the exact epithet (e.g., ‘of Greatness’) cannot be proven from the surviving context.

---

## Chapter 59: The Chapter of the Elements that wept

**¶2** The garments, the sons of the First Man, wept bitterly three times. Then they were silent. There is a fourth time of weeping, for they will weep at the end. And then their crying can stop, and they will never again weep from this time on.

**¶3** Now, the first time of their weeping is when they had seen the darkness, the enemy being about to burst out against them. They understood everything that would happen to them. They were aware of the one that would rise and come upon them, but by necessity they hastened to him and fought with him. They mastered him, they detained him. They bound him with their body and their limbs, they overcame his death and his fire and his darkness.

**¶4** [The] second time that they wept is [when the]ir father, the First Man, [ascend]ed from the [abyss] of darkness and left them behind; that is the time when he stripped [them off] and left them below. Indeed, when th[ey] s[aw] how the First Man had risen out of the abyss and [l]eft them behind, and that they were abandoned in the enmit[y, th]ey wept and were sore of heart. Still, what could they do, wishing [to subject] their heart to every affliction. They [remained b]ehind and grew [s]ick under the pressure of the weighty burden of all the works, until the end time.

> *¶4 #3 [strong]:* Context requires the upward withdrawal of the First Man from the lower mixture; the verb must express elevation out of the deep.
> *¶4 #4 [strong]:* The correspondential ‘ultimate obscurity’ is expressed in this layer as the abyss of Darkness, i.e., the deep of mixture.
> *¶4 #5 [strong]:* Stripping here is the removal of the outward investment, leaving the operative powers below in the ultimate plane.
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: they could do nothing but endure; their willing was turned toward permitted suffering for the sake of the whole work. In the oldest register this is expressed as ‘to subject their heart’ to affliction (submission under necessity), not moral exhortation.

**¶5** [The] third time that they wept is when the Living Spirit stripped off the three garments of activity, placing them beneath all things. He gave them a sign to lift their heads and rise to the heights from that place; and to pass and journey through all the earths until they should reach the land of tranquilli[t]y and peace, the place of rest prepared for them. So, when they had seen him, that he had separated himself from them, while they themselves remained behind after him and had not ascended; at that moment when they stayed behind these three garments wept. After their crying, they turned their [hear]t to all the heights that were concealed from them, and were of one mind. For [they would] soar up and come to the heights; and pass over all things until the time that was ordained for them all [and be gathered up] at the end of the worlds.

> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* The trajectory demanded is: soar to the heights, pass beyond all things, until the appointed consummation when they are taken up (the completion of elevation, matching the later ‘Statue taken up’ in ¶6). This keeps the impersonal cosmological register.

**¶6** [The f]ourth time when they will weep is when the [S]tatue will be taken up on the last day, and they will weep [for] the souls of the liars and blasphemers; for they may give [no help to them] because their limbs have been severed [from the bonds] of the darkness. And also [th]ose souls, when the Statue will go up and th]ey are left alo[n]e, they will weep in that they will rema[in] behind in affliction for ever. For they wi[ll be cut off] and separated from the Last Statue. An[d it is] a necessity to take these souls who are ready for loss as retribution for the deeds that they have done. They go i[n to] this [darkne]ss and are bound with the darkness; just as they desired it and loved it, and placed their treasure with it. At that very moment, when the Last Statue rises up, the[y wi]ll weep. And then they will scream out loud because they will be severed from the company of this great Statue. And they remain behind for ever. This great weeping [before the rulers] is terrible, it occurs in front of the souls and [the powers] who are ready for loss in accordance with their deeds. And they [will] weep with that crying, they will never be silent; because they will not attain rest from this time on.

> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: they cannot receive restoration. In this layer it is stated as the impossibility of ‘help’ or ‘profit’ being given once separation is fixed.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* The severing of limbs corresponds to the tearing away of operative means of conjunction; what they are severed from is the binding-structures that held them in mixture. ‘Bonds’ preserves the cosmological-structural idiom.
> *¶6 #14 [tentative]:* The dreadful weeping is described as occurring ‘in front of’ those fixed for loss. In the oldest substrate this is regularly framed as occurring before the cosmic powers that administer separation, i.e., the rulers of the realm (not ecclesial spectators).
> *¶6 #15 [tentative]:* Pairs naturally with ‘rulers’ as the administering agencies of the lower realm; keeps the impersonal cosmological diction and matches the public, manifest character of the separation.

**¶7** So, they too are the garments of these elements. This fourth time of weeping is heart-rending for them. It is much worse for them than these other three times of weeping before. This fourth time the weeping is not for themselves. Rather they weep and are heartsore for these soul[s] who will be cut off from peace, because rest will not come about for them, to allow them to rest from torment for ever.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are trivial restorations of fixed letters or already-constrained phrases. The substantial restorations were ¶4 (the clause of endurance under necessity), ¶5 (the appointed completion as a taking-up), and ¶6 (the impossibility of restoration, the severing from binding-structures, and the public character of the final lamentation before the administering cosmic powers). These were constrained primarily by the correspondential sequence: withdrawal, remaining below under burden, promised elevation, and final separation at the taking-up of the collective form.

---

## Chapter 60: Concerning the Four Fathers; what they are like.

**¶1** Just as the corporeal body has limbs that are hidden, so it also has others that are visible. They can be seen upon it, they can be grasped and receive blows and wounds, whilst others can neither be grasped nor receive blows.

**¶2** So it is also with this entire greatness, as we resemble it. Some of it is hidden, whilst some of it is visible; some of it is far away from the blows and suffering, whilst some of it receives blows and wounds.

**¶3** Now, thus the directive of the body that lives within, hidden in the storehouses inside the body and unseen. So it is also how the Father, the God of truth, exists; as he too is hidden in his kingdom, not displayed before this outer desolation.

**¶4** Thus, too, the body has two eyes that are visible and seen, and are even the body's source of illumination; so there are also these two light ships that shine and are seen in the entire universe and the whole of creation. The light has never been hidden, just like the two eyes of the body.

**¶5** The living soul too is like this; it is established in apparent silence.
