In its apparent silence it is grasped and receives blows from these five fleshes, which destroy (the soul) and strike it. It can be likened to the mystery of the corporeal body, as it can be grasped and mastered, can receive blows and wounds.

**¶6** ⟨p.152⟩ Also, thus this tongue that speaks, proclaims, reprimands and reveals about everything happening in this body, inside and outside, also the considerations and bodily knowledges. The tongue, which is outside, makes it manifest. It also proclaims about the honour and shame of the body. And it speaks, unveiling the pain of the body that is sick from its sores.

**¶7** This is also what Jesus the Splendour is like, who shall be sent from the greatness. He manifests and reveals about everything, both the external and the internal, both what is above and what is below. Be it about the outer aeons that are hidden and not displayed, or about the ships of light that are seen: he is the one who unveils about them. He is also the one who reveals about their glory, and the greatness that dwells in them.

**¶8** And regarding this beating and wounding of the living soul, Jesus is the one who reveals it. He also preaches about (the soul) and its peace. He reveals about its cleansing and healing.

---

## Chapter 61: Concerning the Garment of the Waters: how great is its Measure.

**¶6** [At] the time when the First Man assumed the elements, and stood [fi]rm against the first enmity that had come about in the darkness, he defied these great and powerful rulers. They are immeasurable, greater than the extent or size of thousands and ten thousands of parasangs. At the start, when he spread the fight against them, he gave them fire and wind; and afterwards he gave the garment of the waters to the rulers of death. So they, the rulers, drank in and consumed those waters, [the way that I told] you [about it]. Many [waters] poured forth from the garment of waters [and from them] the rivers flow. The mouth of the rulers [received them] those [is not filled by], the warriors of destruction [and all] laboured and brought them forth.

> *¶6 #3 [strong]:* The clause points back to an earlier, already-given exposition; the oldest-layer register prefers simple anaphora rather than rhetorical embellishment.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* The sense requires a brief anaphoric closure: they consumed the waters “as I told you about it,” i.e., about this mode of devouring and perverting. Keeps the impersonal instructional tone.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Outflow sequence: from the garment of waters → many waters pour out → from these derive the rivers. The substrate logic is derivation by emanated effects.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Fits the correspondential role of “mouth” as receptacle: the rulers’ craving receives the poured-out waters before further production.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the craving of evil is insatiable; the mouth is “not filled” by these influxes. This also prepares ¶7 where they cannot be satisfied to a single stomach.
> *¶6 #9 [tentative]:* The list-like construction suggests an added collective term: “those …, the warriors …, and all … laboured.” Marks the totality of destructive agencies producing the outflows.

**¶7** There were seven (rivers), like the great river Tigris [that you] see, flowing in to the mouth of each one of them, as they shall not [be gathered] to a single stomach, to their fill; because in turn the descent to its stomach shall burn them with its fire and heat. Those rulers were like the [images] of [deception]; they were not emptied; rain and [mist] their existent days.

> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: the streams cannot be brought together into one satisfied reception. “Be gathered” supplies the needed process-term without importing later moralizing language.
> *¶7 #3 [tentative]:* They are compared to “images” of something—i.e., self-renewing persuasions/phantasms. “Images” fits the substrate’s cosmological descriptive register better than later ecclesial terms.
> *¶7 #4 [tentative]:* Opposite-sense correspondence: the rulers are like images of deception, i.e., persuasive falsities that replicate. Keeps the teaching impersonal and structural.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with “rain” as continual downward influence sustaining their “existent days,” matching the spiritual explanation of continual descent of falsified influx.

**¶9** [This] is how great a struggle, since it has [revealed] these creatures of destruction, the mighty giants. Behold, they are entwined, bound, and [fixed] in every place. They are hidden, not revealed before the eyes of every flesh that exists on the land of death [but they are in their place]. These rulers [also shall be] revealed. The entire universe of flesh will be destroyed and dissolved [in that day]; all pass away [in a moment]. They will be destroyed with their angry voice, which is mightier than the thunder.

> *¶9 #1 [strong]:* The paragraph begins with a demonstrative summarizing what precedes: “This is how great a struggle …”.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* The struggle “has revealed” the creatures/giants, matching the spiritual translation: “brought into view.”
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Triplet requires a third constraint-term after “entwined, bound,”; “fixed” matches the spiritual sense of being held in a set state in every region.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* Bridges the concealment from common perception with the fact that they are manifest in their own order: hidden from general sight, yet ‘in their place’ they appear.
> *¶9 #5 [moderate]:* Required to complete the predication: “These rulers … revealed.” The oldest layer states the alternation: concealed to some, revealed in their own domain.
> *¶9 #6 [moderate]:* Applies the consummation motif in cosmological (not pastoral) terms: the universe of flesh dissolves “in that day,” i.e., at the appointed state of dissolution.
> *¶9 #7 [tentative]:* Completes the cadence “all pass away …,” corresponding to the spiritual sense that such appearances vanish swiftly when their supporting state ends.

**¶10** Now, as this small stream is reckoned [as nothing] before the vastness of these waters of which I have recounted to you. For only one river from thousands of rivers of water, from the secrets that are not revealed, is the river Tigris upon the earth.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Directly matches the correspondential point: a small outward stream counts “as nothing” compared with the vastness of the hidden waters.

**¶11** See [at these waters] and be amazed at the measure of the elements of the waters. Marvel at the stature of the Man who bore them. And understand how great is that god, active in power, the one whom these waters [are greater] than the stature of the First Man who [assumed] this garment of such size; and how much was made.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Object of “See”: the didactic imperative points to the very subject under discussion—the measure of the waters as lowest truth covering.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* Syntax requires a comparative: “the one whom these waters are greater than …”. Also fits the spiritual sense: what the evils could not withstand was greater in measure.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the correspondential sequence: garment of waters (outer truth) → devoured by rulers (perverted) → outflow of many waters (derivative falsities) → rivers (streams of reasoning) → insatiable mouth and stomach (craving) → concealment from ‘every flesh’ yet manifestation in their own domain → dissolution of the universe of flesh. The least secure restorations are where the underlying manuscript likely carried a concrete simile (“like the images of …”) and a collective subject (“and all …”), which the spiritual logic narrows in function (self-renewing persuasions; totality of destructive agents) but not in exact wording.

---

## Chapter 62: Concerning the Three Rocks

**¶2** Three great rocks, which exist, were appointed by the greatness. They were set in these outer worlds.

**¶3** So, the first rock is the [Pillar] of Glory, the Perfect Man, which was summoned [by the] glorious Ambassador. He set it in this zone; [and] brought forth its base (?) from the earth to heaven. It bore up under the [en]tire uni[verse], and became first of all the weight-carriers in [it]s powers. In its firmness it has been set upright, and has made fast every [t]hing above and below.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The first ‘rock’ is explicitly the axial support in the outer worlds: the upright stabilizing truth that holds the whole in connection. “Pillar” matches the correspondential function (support, vertical conjunction of higher and lower) and the surrounding title “of Glory.”
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The commission is immediate: the Perfect Man is “summoned by the glorious Ambassador,” i.e., set in manifestation through a delegated proceeding. This keeps the impersonal cosmological register while preserving the agency-chain required by the narrative.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The sentence requires simple coordination: “He set it in this zone; and brought forth its base …” The action is one continuous establishing and extending downward-to-upward, matching the spiritual description of opening the natural upward to the spiritual.

**¶4** [The] second great and glorious rock is the new earth of light, which was set upon the earth of darkness. It has squashed the fastenings of the five storehouses of death. It has established firmness, with great firmness, over and beyond the earth of darkness. It has flattened, crushed and fixed the root of death.

**¶6** [These] are the three great rocks, they that have come forth in great confidence, in three great and honoured places. They were appointed and distributed in three great places of these [self]same worlds. They were made fast according to the will of the greatness.

> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* “These selfsame worlds” points back to the already-mentioned ‘outer worlds’ as the fixed ultimate plane in which the three supports are distributed. It preserves the structural, non-exhortatory substrate style.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations and are recoverable with high certainty. The only substantive gap (¶3 #3) is constrained by the correspondential sequence of establishing then extending the base from earth to heaven; a simple connective (“and”) best fits the oldest, impersonal cosmological register.

---

## Chapter 63: Concerning Love

**¶2** ⟨p.156⟩Love is the Father of Greatness, who dwells in his glorious land. The entirety of the divine has been revealed [therein]in! These two are a single living body, the Father and his love; because he has given himself for every thing, as he exists in his aeons. Indeed, due to this, they called the Father, the Lord of the totality, 'love'. For he gave the victory to his aeons and his limbs.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The passage identifies the Father and his Love as one living body in the Father’s own ‘glorious land’ (the inmost, proper heaven of Divine Love). The point is not location (‘there’) but manifestation within that land: the whole Divine is revealed in it, that is, ‘therein’. This matches the correspondential sense: the Divine is disclosed within its own proceeding Love, not merely at some place.

**¶4** Hate is the first death that welled up. It revealed the land of darkness. This one called 'hate', because it has ruined all its perdition's offspring; it sinned even against a power foreign to it, and ruined it. Due to this they shall call it 'hated'; for it has caused destruction, both wounding in flesh, and what is not its own.

**¶5** Today again it causes wounding and destruction in all flesh. It kills them all with diverse deaths; these that are displayed today in every thing, in every type. Even the Cross of Light, the living soul, likewise too it ruins and kills them; as it did at the start. Indeed, due to this have they called it 'hated', this one from the hated; the envy from the envy; the devil from the devil; the bad that came about from the bad.

**Assessment:** Only one lacuna, and the correspondential constraints make it tight: the sense requires an ‘in-it’ (manifestation within the glorious land) rather than a bare deictic ‘there’.

---

## Chapter 64: [Concerning Adam]

**¶2** Three great things that are distinct were revealed in Adam the first human. Indeed, due to this, he was found to be better and more outstanding than the conjoined powers that are in heaven and on earth.

**¶3** The first: The image of the exalted one was placed upon him. The creators and moulders of his body sealed him after the shining form of the image that had been displayed to them above. Indeed, due to this, the image of Adam was found to be surpassingly beautiful beyond any conjoined power above and below.

**¶4** The second thing: Adam was shaped in his structure through the light of the first-born of heaven and [earth, through] the five sons. Indeed, due to this, his structure was found to differ from the structure of even the other creations; [sin]ce the form of all creations is different. As for Adam, the formation of his soul fits over the correct distribution of the elements. Therefore, he has intelligence surpassing that of the other creations and beasts.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The constitution is said to be shaped “through the light of the first-born of heaven” and then immediately qualified by “the five sons.” The oldest-layer cosmological logic is a twofold derivation (heavenly and earthly) followed by the operative pentad. “Earth, through” supplies the missing second pole (heaven and earth as discrete degrees) and links it syntactically to “the five sons” as the mediating operative powers.

**¶5** The third are the teachings and counsels and the seal of all the powers above and below. The creators, who set him in order, gathered them and sealed them in him. Adam and his consort Eve became a dwelling and home for the signs of the zodiac and the stars; and the months, the days and the years. For the seal of the entire universe is stamped upon Adam. Indeed, due to this, heaven and earth moved because of him; trouble and disorder arose on his account between they that are good and they that are evil. So, the good induce him to life because of their image and shape placed upon him; while the [wi]cked were drawing him to death, so that they would have power through him and they would conceive the kingdom, and through him humiliate the entire universe.

**¶6** Furthermore, understand that the rulers determined and formed Adam so that they would be masters through him and conceive the kingdom because of seven things.

**¶7** ⟨p.158⟩
The first: He became the enlightener in their creation; instead of the light of the image of the Ambassador who had displayed himself to them above. For they all desired the likeness of the image of the Ambassador. Their heart was drawn after it.

**¶8** The second: So that they would scoop up the light through him and restrain and obstruct it from its ascent to the heights.

**¶9** The third: So that because of the image of the Ambassador that is placed upon him the entirety of the divine that is above and below would be humiliated; on account of the likeness of the splendour, of the beauty of his superiority. For he is superior in his appearance to all creatures.

**¶10** The fourth: He is a seal-ring and a new impression, so that all births who will be begotten might be brought forth after his seal.

**¶11** The fifth: That might be displayed through him all the skil[ls] of the powers, they above and below, secret and manifest; and that they might determine through him the totality, and every thing be revealed in him.

**¶12** The sixth: That there would come to be a great protection for them through the image of the exalted one that was placed upon him; for as the heavenly would [restrain] even rulers; and no evil thing would be done to them on account of Adam.

> *¶12 #1 [strong]:* In the sixth purpose the safeguarding arises “through the image of the exalted one,” because the heavenly order acts as a check upon the lower ruling powers. The correspondential requirement is a verb of limitation: the heavenly (higher order) curbs the rulers (lower evils). “Restrain” matches the spiritual translation’s sense (“would restrain even rulers”) and keeps the oldest substrate register (impersonal cosmic constraint, not moral exhortation).

**¶13** The seventh: That he might become chief and king and master over all creatures; and they might conceive the kingdom through him.

**Assessment:** All lacunae are recoverable. Three are trivial letter-restorations. The two substantive gaps are tightly constrained by the chapter’s discrete-degree structure: (1) the second pole to “heaven” plus a connective into the pentadic operative powers; (2) a verb of heavenly constraint upon rulers in the safeguarding purpose. Exact phrasing in ¶4 #1 remains the least certain, but the required cosmological relations (heaven–earth, then the five sons as operative mediation) fix the reconstruction within a narrow range.

---

## Chapter 65: Concerning the Sun.

**¶2** He says, it is the gate of life and the vessel of peace, to this great aeon of light [which comes daily to the world]. ⟨p.159⟩ However, since Satan knows that it is the gate of the souls' departure, he placed an exclusionary judgement in his law that no one worship it, saying: Whoever will worship it can die. He has otherwise called it: The light that will be nullified. He has hindered the souls from themselves turning their faces towards the light. He has caused them to deny the light of their being.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The passage identifies the great light-giver (the sun) as a cosmic ‘gate’ and ‘vessel’ for the aeon of Light, and immediately turns to Satan’s prohibition of worshipping it. The oldest-layer logic regularly defines the sun here as the daily appearing emissary of the Light-realm, so the missing clause should specify its recurrent descent into the world, not a devotional epithet.

**¶3** Now, people are blind about everything! They have not understood the greatness of this great light-giver. They have denied the grace of this great light that shines upon them. They have not perceived its greatness and its divinity. Nor, also, have they understood the good things that it does for them, even today, as they are entrenched in their body; when it comes to this world and shines. Behold: How many are the charities that it does for mankind! Yet they, for their part, deny its graces, which they know not.

**¶4** Every day, would this great light-giver shine its light, it shall do seven good things for them. Shall they not see them?

**¶5** The first good thing that it does for them is its light, which it shines upon them. It opens the eyes of all mankind. They see underfoot as they walk by it. It takes away the blindness of the night, which made them blind with its darkness.

**¶6** The second: When (the sun) is come to the world, with its coming it shall bring calm and peace to the entire world. With its coming it shall also take away from them all the fear and trembling, which their heart is filled with the whole night; for it shall take away the darkness present by its light, and sweep it out. Also, this is how it extinguishes fear by its calm.

**¶7** The third is its vigil; because when (the sun) comes to the world all sleeping mankind shall arise and awake. And the watchmen too shall cease from watching. The guards who sit at the gates of camps, they break vigil at the watchposts, because of its light that has shone upon them in the world.

**¶8** ⟨p.160⟩ The fourth: It shall nourish, and give strength, taste and scent, to the trees and fruits and vegetables, and all the herbs, and the flowers and grasses, which are upon the entire earth.

**¶9** The fifth: When (the sun) shines in all the world, the evil reptile and the sharp-toothed beasts that are filled of wickedness, they shall flee to hiding in their dens.

**¶10** The sixth: When it shines its light, the wound of any person is eased from pain. Its light shall melt away the evil arts of every doer of wickedness.

**¶11** The seventh: (The sun) shall display and manifest to the world the sign of the splendour of the aeons of light, from which it has come out; it here in this world being their sign. It brings light to the whole of creation.

**¶12** Behold: How great is the foolishness that has befallen the sects of error! Not only that they have not perceived the mystery of the divinity of this great light-giver; but also, even all the benefits that it does for them. They forget them. They do not perceive them.

**¶13** Now, just as I have shown you the sun, which does seven good things in the world, hear also what I teach you about the night, for it has all wickednesses therein. Every occasion, would the night fall, it shall do seven evils in the world.

**¶14** The first wickedness that the night shall do in the world is the darkness, with which it shall fill the entire world; because when the sun sinks from the world and draws to it its rays, at that instant the shadow of the night shall spread out over the whole world. The eyes of the people fill with blackness. They neither see nor wonder with their eyes, because of the darkness of the night which shall fill the whole world.

**¶15** The second evil thing that the night shall do is its fear; because when its shadow looms forth, its fear and its trembling shall possess all of mankind. The entire world shall fill of the night.

**¶16** The third: When the night falls, all creatures take up revolt. They rebel in their heart, to do wickedness and perdition.

**¶17** The fourth: The heaviness of sleep shall lay upon them, and they fall asleep; they slumber and doze, and they are like these corpses in the night.

**¶18** The fifth is its ugliness; again, when the night would fall, the form of the people shall be hidden in the darkness and the ugliness of the night. The beauty and the form of men and women shall not be displayed before one another, because of its darkness and its ugliness, which it shall spread out over all their forms.

**¶19** The sixth occasion is when the night would fall on the world. At that instant evil people shall come out to do wickedness; the adulterers, and the robbers and poisoners. The evil beasts shall come out, and all the reptiles that are filled with wickedness, from their dens. And they stalk in the night. Also, the demons and fiends find power to do wickedness in the night. Again, the blows and buffets and wounds and discharges shall torment people in the night; because in it the whole of evil shall find strength.

**¶20** The seventh: The night shall display the sign of the darkness, its father. It has arisen out of its essence; because this night that came out of this first darkness has been displayed in the world. Note this night, the shadow of this first darkness, the one that is entwined and bound in all things above and below.

**¶21** Behold, I have taught you about these seven wickednesses of the black night; the ones that it shall do daily in the world, in the night. The sects have not seen this black night: what it is; nor from whence is its essence. That all these evils find strength in it!

**¶22** There are five archetypes occur in the sun, it making them [five archetypes] as it finds the world.

> *¶22 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence is a heading-like transition: it first announces ‘There are five archetypes occur in the sun’, then must restate that the sun ‘makes them apparent’ or ‘manifests them’ in the world. Since the object is already ‘five archetypes’, the lacuna supplies that direct object again in idiomatic Coptic-English: ‘it making them five archetypes as it finds the world’ = ‘manifesting these five archetypes upon the world as it is’.

**¶23** ⟨p.162⟩ The first is its light; for it illumines by its light the world, and all the creatures who exist in it.

**¶24** The second is its beauty; because when it shines, it shall flood beauty and loveliness upon all creatures and everything.

**¶25** The third is its peace; for, again, when the sun would shine on the world, all people shall receive affection through it, and give peace to one another.

**¶26** The fourth is the life of the living soul, which it shall release from all the bonds and fetters of heaven and earth.

**¶27** The fifth: It gives a strength to the elements; and also it gives a scent and a taste to the entire Cross of the Light.

**¶28** So, you yourselves see that its light illumines more than all lights that occur in the world. Also, its beauty is more beautiful than all the beauties of mankind. Again, its peace is more surpassing than all the powers and guards who exist in the world. Its releasing action, by which it releases the living soul, is a full day ahead of all releasing actions! The strength that it gives to its limbs is a great strength, being mightier than all strengths!

**¶29** Once again, there are another three archetypes made apparent by the sun, in respect of the mystery of the first greatness.

**¶30** The first is the filling up of the disk of its ship; because its ship has filled up every season, and shall not wane at all, the way that the ship of the moon wanes. This continuous filling, by which it is filled, displays the mystery of the Father, the great greatness; from whom all the powers and the gods have come forth. He shall never wane, nor shall lack ever exist in him!

**¶31** The second: (The sun's) light surpasses the light of all the stars, and all the lights that occur in the universe; corresponding to the mystery of the light of the Father; for his light surpasses and is greater than the light of his aeons.

**¶32** ⟨p.163⟩ The third: This sun is very high above all else. It surpasses the height of all the mountains and hills that exist upon the entire earth, corresponding to the mystery of the Father of Greatness; for he is high above all the household of his light-earth. Also, because the Father himself is high above all the heights and the mountains of his light-earth. He is 'filled up' in his image [every] the time and every hour from eternity to eternity!

> *¶32 #1 [strong]:* The intended sense is continuous plenitude: ‘filled up in his image every time and every hour’. ‘All the time’ is an editor’s smoothing; the substrate idiom is ‘every time and every hour’, stressing unceasing renewal in discrete moments.

**¶33** [Behold]ld the two mysteries I have taught you, O my beloved ones! The mystery of the night and that of the day, which daily come forth in the world. Now, the mystery of the light and the mystery of the darkness are displayed daily in the created order. The sects have not seen them in their error!

**¶34** The mystery of the first great light is the sun, which is this second light-giver, the one that comes daily to the world. It displays all the marks of its graces, displaying and signifying on its own behalf that it is good, out of the essence of the good. It came from the good Father and appeared in this world.

**¶35** In contrast, this night that is made of fear exists after the mystery of the darkness. Behold too the mystery of its essence! It displays it in the world in all these wickednesses. It displays this mystery daily in the world, but the sects know it not in their error. They do not distinguish the mystery of the light from that of the darkness.

**¶37** You are the children of the day and the children of the light. However, you <[if you shall take up] the character of the darkness> are the children of the night and the children of the darkness.

> *¶37 #1 [tentative]:* The contrast requires a conditional uptake of quality: those who are ‘children of day and light’ versus those who assume the opposite quality and so belong to night and darkness. The oldest register speaks impersonally of ‘taking up’ or ‘putting on’ a nature, aligning with the correspondential point that one becomes of that state by reception, not by lineage or exhortation.

**¶38** ⟨p.164⟩ Yet, blessed are you, my beloved ones! Blessed are your souls, for you have known the mystery of the day and that of the night. You have understood, that the day exists after the mystery of the light, but the night after the mystery of the darkness. They have not arisen from one another. Blessed is he who can know this mystery and distinguish these two essences, that of the light and that of the darkness, the ones that have not arisen from one another; so that he can inherit life eternal!

**Assessment:** Most gaps are short and structurally constrained by the chapter’s parallelism (day seven goods, night seven evils; then five and three ‘archetypes’). ¶32 and ¶33 are tightly determined. ¶2 and ¶22 require brief formulaic clauses typical of this cosmological instruction and are recoverable with moderate confidence. ¶37 is least constrained because the surviving English already contains an interpolated gloss; the restoration supplies the minimal substrate-style conditional needed to complete the antithesis.

---

## Chapter 66: Concerning the Ambassador.

**¶2** Two great mysteries are apparent in this shining sun, at its rising and setting. Now, at the moment when it rises and is visible in the universe, all creatures of flesh shall lift up their heads fr[om their sleep] towards the splendour of the sun, and open their doors. While at evening time, when the sun sinks from the universe, all people from every place and even all beasts shall go in to their dark hiding places. The population of the entire universe shuts their doors and dark night overcomes them. They go inside and stay within the dark night in their hiding places.

**¶3** Very great is this mystery revealed by the sun! For when the sun rises and [be]come[s] visible at the beginning of the day all people open their doors, and all of humanity and every creature emerges onto the ear[th] to destroy this Light Cross that is established i[n] silence.

**¶4** This pertains to the mystery of the First Man, because when he came out from the grea[t]ness he shone forth over all the children of darkness. At that mome[nt] all the children of darkness came forth from their storehouses, its powers and its armies, abandoning their caves and their black abyss. They destroyed the [outer] body of his five sons, who were swathed over hi[s b]o[dy, in t]hat first struggle. ⟨p.165⟩

> *¶4 #3 [strong]:* Kept as ‘outer’ (not ‘body’ alone) because the substrate is mapping the assault on the ultimate, protective covering of the Five Sons: the ultimates of the First Man’s powers that were exposed to contact and could be ‘destroyed’ in the first mixture. This corresponds to the spiritual sense ‘outermost protective truths’.

**¶5** Again, when the sun sinks from the universe and sets, and all people go in to their hiding places and houses and conceal themselves; this also pertains to the mystery of the end, as it presages the consummation of the universe. For, when all the light will be purified and redeemed i[n] the universe at the last, the collector of all things, the Last Statue, will gather in and sculpt itself. It is the last hour of the day, the time when the Last Statue will go up to the aeo[n of] light. The enemy too, death, will go in to bondage; to the prison of the souls of the de[ni]iers and blasphemers who loved the darkness. They will go in with it to bondage and the dark night overcomes th[em]; and thenceforth this name 'light' will not shine upon them.

**¶6** Behold the two mysteries made visible daily in the sun, which is this splendrous light-giver.

**Assessment:** All lacunae in this excerpt are either trivial letter restorations or already tightly constrained by fixed surrounding words. The only semantically weighty bracket (‘outer’) is correspondentially determined by the doctrine of ultimates: what is attacked in the first combat is the exposed, ultimate covering of the Five Sons, matching the chapter’s inside-to-outside logic.

---

## Chapter 67: Concerning the Light-Giver.

---

## Chapter 68: Concerning Fire.

**¶1** This fire, which burns and is apparent today in the world, it does five works.

**¶2** The first work that it does is this: It incinerates and burns wood, and everything that shall be placed upon it.

**¶3** The second: It separates four aspects of the wood, which are these: fire and light, ash and smoke.

**¶4** The third: It gives a taste and an {aroma} to whatever will be cooked on it. It also gives heat and cheer to whoever will be close to it.

**¶5** The fourth work that [it] does: All craftsmen shall work the utensils that they will fabricate in it.

**¶6** The fifth: It shall display light, and the people see by [it].

**Assessment:** Both lacunae are trivial and syntactically fixed: the fourth and fifth works are predicated of the same subject introduced in ¶1 (“This fire”), so the pronoun must be restored as “it” in each case. No alternative reading better fits the correspondential structure, which maintains one agent (active love in the natural degree) performing five operations.

---

## Chapter 69: Concerning the Twelve Signs of the Zodiac and the Five Stars

**¶2** Whether these twelve signs of the zodiac that are fixed on the sphere, or these five stars that circle upon them; those and these are rulers, all of them, according to their essence. They are all enemies and rivals to each other ⟨one oppresses another, they threaten each other⟩. They plunder one another by the action of the great craftsman who has formed all the worlds. He has gathered [them] and bound them on the sphere above, which circles constantly. Now, while it circles and tumbles them, so they shall do everything that they desire, above and below, according to their pleasure. Nevertheless, a guardian is over them, a debt-collector above them; compelling, extorting, and taking what is theirs away from them!

**¶3** Now, this is how it can rightly be understood. They were [s]eized from the five worlds of Darkness, and bound on the sphere. Two zodiacal signs were taken per world. Gemini and Sagittarius belong to the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aries and Leo belong to the world of fire. Taurus, Aquarius and Libra belong to the world of wind. Cancer and Virgo and [P]isces belong to the world of water. Capricorn and Scorpio belong to the world of Darkness. These are the twelve rulers of depravity, the ones that wicked[ness] shall not [cease] For they cause all the evil and [disorder in the world]rld, whether in tree or in flesh.

> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The passage is defining the twelve as the fixed representatives of the five dark roots, i.e., stable governing perversions. Correspondentially they are what keeps disorder in motion and in form; thus “wickedness shall not cease” (not “be destroyed” or “be judged,” which would shift into later moral or soteriological register).
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* The clause needs the universal effect of these rulers in ultimates. In the substrate register this is not moral exhortation but structural consequence: they are causes of “evil and disorder in the world,” appearing in both vegetative and animate planes (tree and flesh).

**¶4** ⟨p.168⟩ And understand also about these five stars, the leaders: from where were they formed? The star of Zeus (Jupiter) was generated from the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aphrodite (Venus) came about from the world of fire. And then Ares (Mars) belongs to the world of wind. Hermes (Mercury) belongs to the world of water. Cronos (Saturn) belongs to the world [of] Darkness.

**¶5** And also, the two ascendants are counted to fire and desire, the dry and the moist. They are the father and the mother of all these.

**¶6** And also, the sun and the moon, which are counted in with them as a rule ⟨[counte]d to their kind, but why did they count them in with them?⟩ so that they would reign over them. You will discover they subjugate them at every moment.

**¶7** These seven we have named, the five stars and the two ascendants: They are the doers of wickedness that do every wicked and bad act in every land, the whole of it, above and below, in every creature, in the dry and the moist, in tree and flesh.

**¶8** Once more I reveal to you about these signs of the zodiac: [They] are distributed, appointed on four sides, three per angle in these four places. And they are fixed to this revolving sphere. Aries, Leo and Sagittarius, they three belong to a single side. In contrast, Taurus, Capricorn and Virgo, these other three belong to another side. And then, Gemini, Libra and Aquarius belong to another side. Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer belong to another corner. Now, they are placed like this, appointed to these four parts, and distributed on the sphere.

**¶9** So, when the side of Aries, Leo and Sagittarius will be plundered by the guardian who is over it, who extorts from it and the leaders who move upon it; at that instant shall affliction strike all the four-footed creatures below. However, when [the side] of Taurus, Virgo and Capricorn will be plundered; [affliction] shall ⟨p.169⟩ at once befall the herbs, together with the vegetables and all the fruits of the trees. Yet again, when the side of Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer will be plundered; scarcity shall befall the waters upon the earth, and drought be from place to place. Conversely, should the side of Gemini, Libra and Aquarius be plundered; deformity and stuntedness shall befall the form of mankind from place to place.

**¶10** Behold now, I have explained to you about these twelve signs of the zodiac: They were fashioned from the five worlds of Darkness, and bound on the sphere.

**¶11** Also, I have taught you about the other five, the stars: They too came about from the five worlds of the land of Darkness.

**¶12** I have recounted to you also about the [t]wo ascendants: They are set to the mystery of fire and desire. They are the dry and the moist, the father and the mother.

**¶13** Again, I have revealed to you about the sun and the moon: They are strangers to them. Still, because of the constraint of the arrangement, in that the thing receives light, and as they belong and plunder them; because of this they were counted in with them in relation to the calculation of the number. For, the sun and the moon are from out of the greatness, not belonging to the stars and the signs of the zodiac.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations fixed by the preserved surrounding wording. The two substantive gaps in ¶3 are tightly constrained by the correspondential structure: (a) the twelve rulers are stable ultimates taken from the five dark roots, so wickedness is said not to cease; (b) their effect is universal disorder manifested in both vegetative and animate realms, requiring a generic term like “disorder in the world” rather than later moralizing or salvific phrasing.

---

## Chapter 70: Concerning the Body: It was constructed after the Pattern of the Universe.

**¶2** This whole universe, above and below, reflects the pattern of the human body; as the formation of this body of flesh accords to the pattern of the universe.

**¶3** Its head is like the first-fruits of the five garments. And, from its neck down to the site of its heart, it resembles the pattern of the ten firmaments. And again, the heart accords to the wheel of the rotating sphere. And, from its heart down to its intestines (?), is like this atmosphere that extends from the sphere down to the earth. The [male] part of the body corresponds to this great earth. And also, from its intestines (?) down to its loins is like the three earths. And also, its shinbones to the pattern of the space in which the Porter stands. Its footsoles to the great earth upon which the Porter is stood; and the four fastenings that are under his feet. Its liver to the vessel of fire. Its flesh to the vessel of darkness. Its blood to the vessel of water.

> *¶3 #1 [strong]:* The substrate contrasts the active outmost plane with its receptive counterpart; the masculine here is the active ultimate corresponding to the great earth (the plane of effects).

**¶4** Now, this is how the small body corresponds to the macro-cosmos in its firmaments, in its orderings, in its mountains, its walls and its vessels, as I have made clear to you!

**¶5** Once more, I reveal to you: There exist five great camps in this great outer zone, of which the five sons of the Living Spirit are masters.

**¶6** So, in the first watch-station above, of which the Keeper of Splendour is master, on the battlement of all the works, is found his authority over the three heavens. Then the ones below him [are under his command, and they serve] the great King of Honour.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The first watch holds the highest rule; what follows must introduce subordinate powers beneath the Keeper of Splendour, aligned under the next administrator (King of Honour). The wording keeps the impersonal administrative register: subordinates ‘under command’ who ‘serve’ the next authority.

**¶7** The second watch, of which the great King of Honour is master, his own authority (extends) over the seven firmaments beneath him.

**¶8** The third watch, of which the Adamant [is master, his authority] reigns from the firmament down below to the [earth]. [And he] invigorates, according to his authority, the sphere and the worlds of the atmosphere; and the other four worlds that are appointed on this earth.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* Matches the administrative formula repeated in ¶7–10: each watch is named, then ‘of which X is master, his authority…’.
> *¶8 #2 [strong]:* The Adamant’s extension runs from the firmament down to the lowest plane; in the natural mapping of the chapter that terminus is ‘earth’.
> *¶8 #3 [strong]:* Required to resume the subject (the Adamant) and continue his proper function: strengthening and ordering the sphere, atmosphere, and the four worlds on earth.

**¶9** ⟨p.171⟩
The fourth watch, over which the King of Glory has power, is the three wheels. And his authority lies over the three earths upon the head of the Porter.

**¶10** The fifth watch is the watch over which the Porter is authoritative. Again, he is master, according to his authority, over this great earth he stands upon, and the four fastenings under his feet.

**¶11** Affliction has come about in these five watches from time to time.

**¶12** So, in the watch of the Keeper of Splendour, sin desired to spurt up towards the image of the Ambassador. Yet, they barred it from that place, and it fell back down in shame.

**¶13** Again, an earthquake and treachery occurred in the watch of the great King of Honour, which the watchers who had arisen at the time and had [stood firm, and restrained] they who were sent came down, until they were humbled.

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* The disturbance in the second watch demands a counter-action by the watchers: they ‘stood firm’ (held order in that province) and ‘restrained’ (checked the treachery), after which the sent ones come down until humbled. This keeps the substrate’s governance-and-restraint logic.

**¶14** Also, in the watch of the Adamant, the abortions came to the earth and fashioned [the] figure of flesh.

**¶15** Again, in the watch of the King of Glory, a commotion happened in these three earths above the Porter. The pathway was obstructed for the ascent of these three wheels; that of wind, that of water, and that of fire.

**¶16** Also, in the watch of the Porter, the fastenings beneath were laid bare and strained their chains. A great earthquake befell that place.

**¶23** Also, the teaching of wisdom, which circulates in that body: to the likeness of the Virgin of Light, who goes up and comes down in this zone, above and below.

**¶24** Also, love and joy, faith and truth, in which the person should live: they are to the likeness of these two light ships. For, the living soul should go up in them and become free through them; and it ascends from the abysses below and arrives at the heights above.

**¶25** Once more, understand this other (truth): There exist four worlds in this body of the flesh, and there exist countless times seven rulers in the body of the four worlds!

**¶26** So, the first world is from its neck up; and also the seven rulers who are in this upper world are these: the two organs of sight, and the two of hearing, and the two of smell. The other one is the mouth, which is the organ of taste.

**¶27** The second world is from its neck down to its entire upper torso. And also, the seven rulers who are in this second world are: its two arms that accord to the pattern of its two ears, which occur in its first world above; and its two breasts that are to the pattern of its organs of smell; and the two eyes of its heart; and the gullet of the stomach between the two breasts, which in the chest is the pattern of the mouth.

**¶28** ⟨p.173⟩
Once more, there exist seven rulers in the third world of the body, which are these: the fat, the lung, the spleen, the liver, the gall, and its two kidneys.

**¶29** Also, the fourth world below. There are another seven there of this same kind: its two buttocks (?), and its two testicles, and its two loins; and the member out of which the seed comes, that begets all the forms.

**¶31** Aries and Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius and Pisces; these are the twelve signs of the zodiac that occur in the worlds above, on the sphere. They are interlocked and bound on this turning wheel, the one in which they are [set], fixed, and planted. And, regarding all these twelve signs of the zodiac, this is how they were appointed and made leaders [set] on the sphere beneath the heaven that is the wheel of the stars. Now, they are appointed like this, they are thus, so that you can find the head of [upon the twelve stations] and you find the tail too [each sign, and its companion]

> *¶31 #1 [moderate]:* The zodiacal signs are ‘set, fixed, and planted’ on the turning wheel: a triad of stabilization terms suited to correspondential ‘planting’ of truths in their places.
> *¶31 #2 [moderate]:* Completes the stabilization sequence: ‘set, fixed, and planted’, expressing orderly emplacement in the system.
> *¶31 #3 [moderate]:* The signs are ‘appointed and made leaders’ according to a structural arrangement; ‘stations’ fits the oldest cosmological register (fixed places of rule) without later ecclesial language.
> *¶31 #4 [tentative]:* The sentence aims at knowing beginning and end together: finding ‘the head’ and ‘the tail’ within one bound system. ‘Each sign and its companion’ preserves the paired, interlocked structure that later is described as head-to-tail distribution.

**¶32** Again, they occur like this, these that we have recited, one after another in this body. They are counted by order and number from the head to the feet. Its head is Aries. Its neck and its shoulders are Taurus. Its two arms Gemini. Its upper torso is Cancer. Its stomach is Leo. Its belly is Virgo. The vertical spine and its intestines (?) are Libra. Its genitals Scorpio. Its loins are Sagittarius. Its knees are Capricorn. Its shinbones are Aquarius. The soles of its feet are Pisces.

**¶33** Behold, these are also distributed one after another. They exist in this body, as if turned to the side and bent to the pattern of the twelve signs of the zodiac. They too are thus, arranged one against another, head to tail, as they occur on the wheel [as it rises] up.

> *¶33 #1 [moderate]:* The wheel imagery requires a verb of cyclic ascent; ‘as it rises up’ matches the turning order in which the series is seen ‘head to tail’ on the wheel.

**¶34** Also, they are counted by order and number of the body and all its members. So, we have proclaimed that these are turned to the side, bent, and spread out; because from its head down to its hip shall count six to its left, and another six to its right.

**¶35** ⟨p.174⟩
The six that are down its right are these that I will recount: the right temple is Aries; its right shoulder is Taurus; its right arm is Gemini; its right rib-cage is Cancer; its stomach is Leo; the right side of its reproductive organs are Virgo.

**¶36** Conversely, the other six about which we have spoken, which are to its left, in contrast they come from its hip to its head: its left [hip] is Libra; its left rib-cage is Scorpio; its left breast and left kidney is Sagittarius; its [left] elbow is Capricorn; its left shoulder is Aquarius; its left temple is Pisces. This is how the creator of the body himself has appointed them. He has [set] them in order and arranged them, one against its companion, head to tail.

> *¶36 #1 [strong]:* The left-side series is counted in reverse ‘from its hip to its head’; therefore the first left member named must be at the hip region, corresponding to Libra in the bent, side-turned distribution.
> *¶36 #3 [strong]:* Keeps the technical ordering vocabulary of the substrate: appointed, set in order, arranged.

**¶38** Listen to this other lesson that I will proclaim to you. Understand that there exist many powers in this body. They are the house-dwellers who are made the leaders in it. There are eight hundred and forty times ten thousand rulers made chiefs in the human body! They are distributed and set firm, quartered according to house. The count and number of their houses is two hundred and ten times ten thousand.

**¶39** When all these rulers come creeping and moving within the body, they will meet one another; and they shall beset and destroy one another, and [work destruction] in them there. When [when the time is fulfilled]] they shall erupt from the body of the person who [will] die; and make putrid boils and sores and burning wounds in the body. Either they might have made him sick within; or else they might come forth upon his outer side. Those wounds shall first be squeezed and discharge their liquid, and empty out their pus, until they are squeezed [again, and again] and the suppuration forced out from the wound. Afterwards, their scars shall close over; and they are healed and obtain relief from the places that came out upon them.

> *¶39 #1 [moderate]:* The clause describes disordered powers opposing each other; ‘work destruction’ is demanded by the correspondential sense of mutual assault within one domain.
> *¶39 #2 [moderate]:* The break-out occurs at the end of the outward life; ‘time fulfilled’ expresses the termination point without later devotional framing and fits the following ‘they shall erupt from the body… who will die’.
> *¶39 #4 [tentative]:* The squeezing and discharge is iterative until the impurity is expelled; a brief repetition marker fits the process-description and the correspondential sense of successive purgings.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are formulaic or structurally constrained by the chapter’s repeated administrative pattern (five watches; rule-extent statements; head-to-tail ordering). The hardest points are ¶6 #1 (the exact wording for subordinates under the first watch), ¶31 #3–4 (the precise term for the appointed ‘leaders’ and the head-tail linkage), and ¶39 #2 and #4 (idiom for ‘end of life’ and iterative purgation). These were restored by correspondential necessity—subordination, fixed stations, paired interlock, and successive expulsion—while keeping the impersonal cosmological register of the oldest layer.

---

## Chapter 71: Concerning the Gathering in of the [E]lements.

**¶2** The light shall be gathered in to the fire, and the fire itself gathered in to the water, and the water gathered in to the wind, and the wind gathered to the air, and the air gathered to the answer, and the answer gathered to the summons. Also, the summons to the purified Mind, which is the intellectual. The intellectual to the Pillar of Glory, [the Pillar of Glory to the First Man, the Man to the Ambassador, the Ambassador to] the aeons of greatness.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The sequence here must continue the inward-to-outward reversion through the hypostases that mediate ascent: purified Mind (intellectual) → Pillar of Glory (fixed support and manifested column) → First Man (first formed heavenly human, archetypal form) → Ambassador (mediating office that conjoins higher and lower) → aeons of greatness (the whole expanse of the Divine realm). This matches the correspondential order given in the spiritual translation and preserves the impersonal cosmological register of the oldest layer without importing later devotional framing.

**¶3** They are gathered in like this; but, they will be gathered on one occasion only, and go up above to the place of rest for ever and ever.

**Assessment:** This chapter has a single lacuna, and the surrounding correspondential chain uniquely constrains its content: it is the standard ascent sequence from Mind through Pillar, First Man, and the mediating Ambassador into the aeons. Restoration is therefore tight and effectively certain.

---

## Chapter 72: Concerning the worn and torn apart Garments, or the tattered Clothes, or the Elements, the Cross, and the rest too.

**¶7** “It is proper for you to understand this. The garments that are named ‘great garment’ are the five intellectuals that make perfect the body of the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. They were purified by the coming of the Ambassador.

**¶8** “In contrast, the ones named ‘rag’: the rags are the devoured enlightening power. It is compounded and entangled in the body of the universe in the rulers who are above, who exist in all the firmaments. Also, its shine is that which lights up and is displayed in the stars of the chain and the powers of the course. And likewise, these rags that are entangled in these three earths below, above the head of the Porter [at the foundation of all things] on the earth that is under the Porter. These are named the ‘rags’, because [it has been torn away from its own order,] set [and has been fastened in an outward damaged form]

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* The Porter is the ultimate boundary that supports and restrains the lower domain; the rags are said to be entangled ‘below’ and thus appear at the base level where the threshold is. This anticipates ¶10’s explicit ‘foundation of all things’ before the Porter.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires the definition of ‘rags’ as light broken from proper order and fixed in externals. The cause is not moral exhortation but structural displacement: separation from its own order.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* Completes the correspondential reason for the name ‘rags’: the seized light is held in a ruined external state (outermost knowledges, external regions). This matches the description of scattered shine in stars and courses and of entanglement in the three earths below.

**¶9** “However, the ‘great worn and torn apart garments’, which we have proclaimed, is this entangled living power. It is reaped, cut, and devoured. It is overpowered by the five worlds of the flesh, which creep as they move from place to place.

**¶10** “On the other hand, [these] we have termed the ‘images and the great tattered clothes’ [which we have proclaimed,] are the three great garments of the wind, the fire, and the water. These the Living Spirit cloaked upon his body. With them he set in order things in the universe. With them also he emptied the vessels. He swept them out and laid them bare in front of the Porter at the foundation of all things.

> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* The passage is continuing a chain of identifications (‘which we have proclaimed… is…’). The oldest-layer register prefers the impersonal resumptive formula over later homiletic expansions.

**¶11** “Conversely, ‘[the elements]ments’, we have called them this name, are this power that remains in all things below. It rises up in the wombs of all the earths, gathering together, and pouring forth upon all things.

> *¶11 #1 [strong]:* The bracket clearly completes ‘elements’, matching the identified reality: the remaining power in all things below, rising and flowing out as a foundational principle.

**¶12** “And then, the ‘[Light] Cross’ [we have termed] this bound ‘light power’. It [is stretched out] all [things] upon the earth, in the dry and the moist.

> *¶12 #3 [moderate]:* The Cross, in correspondence, is extension under restraint: illumination bound yet spread through the lowest domain. ‘Stretched out’ expresses this extension without importing later devotional language.
> *¶12 #4 [strong]:* The spiritual sense requires the bound light to be ‘through the whole lowest domain’; the construction ‘all things upon the earth’ is completed by ‘things’.

**¶13** “Also, the one named the ‘counsel of life’ is the summons and the obedience. It moves in all these powers that we have named. It awakens them, gives them ease, and drives them to their movement.

**¶14** “The one named ‘the soul that is slain’, which is killed, oppressed and murdered; it is this power of fruit, vegetables and seeds. They shall be threshed, plucked and cut down; and they give nourishment to the worlds of the flesh. Further: the wood should it be dry, and the garment should it become ragged, will perish. Again, it is [a part]rt of this entire soul that is slain and killed; since also even this whole part of it they kill [as they also kill it]

> *¶14 #1 [strong]:* Completes the phrase ‘it is a part of this entire soul…’, required by the sense that the perishing of dry wood and ragged garment is still a portion of the same slain life.
> *¶14 #2 [moderate]:* The close requires a causal reinforcement: even that whole portion is subjected to destruction. An impersonal restatement (‘as they also kill it’) keeps the oldest-layer explanatory tone.

**¶15** “For all these names [that we have proclaimed] are a single [power,] since the beginning, it [was one power of light] as it enlightens; but they separated into all these parts in this first contest. They became set in all these altered forms, and these many names. Of course, if now all these varieties are laid bare, and stripped of all these appearances, and parted from all these names; they will gather together and make a single form, and a single name, unaltered and unchangeable for ever in the land of their first essence, from which they were sent forth against the enemy.”

> *¶15 #1 [moderate]:* The paragraph sums the foregoing names as a single reality; the resumptive formula maintains continuity with ¶9–10 (‘which we have proclaimed’).
> *¶15 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense demands ‘one origin… one reality’: a single living illumination. ‘Power’ is the substrate term used throughout for the unitary reality underlying the names.
> *¶15 #3 [moderate]:* This supplies the unitary beginning: ‘a single enlightenment proceeding as it enlightens’. The oldest layer speaks in cosmological terms (‘power of light’) rather than later personal or ecclesial idiom.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are constrained by the chapter’s internal correspondential mapping: ‘rags’ must be defined as light torn from order and fixed in damaged externals; the Porter must be located at the ultimate threshold and foundation; ‘elements’ and ‘Light Cross’ are fixed technical identifications. A few short gaps (¶10 #2; ¶14 #2; ¶15 #1–3) allow more than one wording, but the oldest-layer register and the requirement of structural, impersonal explanation narrow the restorations to a small set of viable options.

---

## Chapter 73: Concerning the Envy of Matter

**¶1** [The] entirety of envy is this first nature that occu[red] in the worlds of darkness.

**¶2** In this envy that [was] in it, the First Man [received] its poiso[n] its evil-doing. His garment[s] were [rent;] it cut them and drew them; [it drew them down.]

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The envy is treated as an active first nature. The substrate idiom regularly says a quality “is” or “exists” in something (state as container). The spiritual sense requires an indwelling active principle, not a mere description.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The envy “receives” the assaulting elements into itself: the intoxicating delight (poison) and evil-doing. This matches the correspondential idea that a perverted state takes in falsity as delight and then acts from it.
> *¶2 #5 [strong]:* The garments correspond to outer truths. The required action is their being torn away. “Rent” (torn) directly matches the spiritual translation: taken away and torn.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The last clause must express distortion and subjection: truths pulled from their guarding function into the service of evil. “Drew them down” fits the inside-to-outside logic: lower degree domination by the dark state.

**¶3** ⟨p.179⟩ It envied the first image of the Ambassador, which he displayed to it in the heights abov[e], i[n] his form that it saw. It established the seal and the image of flesh, and put the impression of t[h]at image on flesh, its very own birth.

**¶4** [The th]ird time it envied Adam, the fir[st hum]an. It seized a power from him, and bore it forth from a [rib of] of Adam's [flesh].

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter restorations. The only substantial uncertainties are in ¶2, where the correspondential constraints are clear: envy as an indwelling active state, an internal reception of poison and evil-doing, the rending of garments as the stripping and tearing of outer truths, and their being drawn downward into subservience. Exact verbs are less philologically constrained but are tightly bounded by the spiritual sequence (indwelling → assault/reception → tearing → downward drawing).

---

## Chapter 74: Concerning the living Fire: It is present in Eight Places.

**¶2** The first place wherein it is, is this [light] land [where it is present and] exists. It is established beneath [it, and is]terly beyond the totality. It is the foundation and ba]sis of that great earth, and the consideratio[n that]at is beneath them all.

> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The first ‘place’ is the outermost receptacle-state (‘light land’) standing as a base beneath what transcends the whole. The clause must assert stable being there: ‘is established beneath it, and is utterly beyond the totality’ preserves the discrete-degree contrast (beneath vs beyond) required by the spiritual translation.
> *¶2 #4 [strong]:* Completes ‘consideratio[n th]at is beneath them all’ as the abstract governing discernment underlying particulars (impersonal substrate register).

**¶3** The second place wherein it is, is the Father, the God of truth; because [the Fathe]r is established in his great, mighty consideration. So, [firs]t in the Father. The other powers that came from him were revealed through him, and (the fire) too came w[i]th them [and was revea]led by them.

**¶4** The third [place wherein it is], is the First Man, h[e] wh[who is clothed in] armour. Again, it is this fire [swathed and bound] on the body of the Perfect Man.

> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* The third degree is the First Man as primal combat-form. The text wants the idiom of investiture for battle: he ‘is clothed in armour’. This matches the correspondential idea that love is strength as a protective covering.
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* Parallels ¶7 where the Living Spirit ‘swathed’ the fire on his body. In the third place, the fire functions as the binding, strengthening investiture upon the Perfect Man’s body.

**¶5** [The fourth is this fir]e that was purified from all the [light] powers, [which is i]n the outer wall, in the ship of the day.

**¶6** [The fifth i]s this fire that is in the light-giver of the night [and is held] within the water.

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The fifth degree is the night luminary: a lesser illumination governing obscurity. Correspondentially it is love present in reflective truths (‘within the water’), so a verb of containment fits: it is ‘held within the water’.

**¶7** [The] sixth is this f[i]re that is purified from the totality. The Living [Spirit] swathed it upon his body, over the summons and the obedience. He has established many things by it. He went down and stripped himself of it before the Por[t]er.

**¶8** The seventh is this living fire that is of the new earth, and is the consideration. For it is the first foundation in the new earth, it they built anew [and set in order] the first course, that is a crown for the entire earth. T[hey] are below the totality, over the entire earth of da[rkne]ss [and they restrained] in it, in the [boundary] whole [of the]

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* After ‘they built anew’ the renewed order must be described as an ordered rebuilding beginning from the first encompassing formation. The phrase supplies the required governing-structure sense without importing later devotional diction.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Seventh degree stands over the whole earth of darkness to limit and enclose it. The verb must express boundary-setting and restraint, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘restraining it and enclosing it within fixed limits’.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* Needs a noun for the limiting structure within which darkness is contained; ‘boundary’ matches the wall-crown imagery (first course as crown) and the function of fixed limits.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* Minimal connective demanded by the remaining clause ‘in the [ ... ] whole [ ... ]’; preserves a genitival construction without over-specifying what the damaged exemplar no longer supports.

**¶9** [The] eigh[th] is the fire that shall be revealed in this [last formed] person, and it signifies fear and war. Furthermore it [is fearful, and] it displays and manifests its sign in [its ter]ror. It shall be r[ev]ealed in these two light ships.

> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* The eighth degree is to appear ‘in this … person’: correspondentially a final representative reception. ‘Last formed’ keeps the impersonal cosmological register (formation, not biography) and fits the eschatological disclosure described.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* The eighth is characterized by dread and conflict; the syntax ‘Furthermore it … it displays’ needs an appositive predicate introducing that quality before describing its sign-manifestation.

**¶10** This i[s the way] that the holy living fire exists in these [eight places].

**Assessment:** Most gaps are small and restore directly. The larger losses are in ¶4 and especially ¶8–¶9; there the correspondential structure tightly constrains function (investiture for combat; boundary-setting over darkness; final representative manifestation with dread and war), but exact phrasing remains only moderately recoverable. Where the exemplar is too indeterminate (¶8 #6), the restoration is kept minimal to avoid importing later overlay language.

---

## Chapter 75: [Concerning the Letter (?)]

**¶2** Consider the summons, which was sent from the Living Spirit in the beginning. He sent it to the First Man. It is a peace letter and greeting that he wrote and sent to his brother, in which all the tidings are written down, together with everything it will bring about and establish by that summons. That summons, the First Man [... is ordained to happen.

**¶3** ⟨p.183⟩
The answer that was sent from the First Man came to the heights, to the Living Spirit. That is also a letter, which was sent from the First Man to the Living Spirit, in which all the tidings are written down; and every war and the struggles that he had waged. It did [not only report] in the answer that the First Man [was recounting] everything that had happened and everything [that would happen], all that had been done and whatever is made ready to be done by him. The Living Spirit knew all the wars and the dangers and the contests that the rulers had waged against the First Man.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The answer-letter contains “tidings” and an ordered series of conflicts. The spiritual sense requires that it be comprehensive and sufficient, not a partial notice; thus a turn like “not only report” prepares for the following clause that it contained the whole account.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The clause needs a verbal act that gathers “everything that had happened” into the return-communication. In the substrate register, the answer is an impersonal conveyance of the full state; “was recounting” fits the correspondential requirement of a complete ordered disclosure of events and of what is prepared.
> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* The spiritual translation explicitly includes future continuation in the answer’s contents; this editor fill matches the required series: past in act and future in continuation.

**Assessment:** Most of the recovery is constrained by the correspondential structure: the return-letter must comprehensively disclose the whole series (past and future) of conflicts and preparations. Exact idiom for the two verbal gaps remains somewhat open, but the required function is tight: an explicit statement of completeness and an act of ordered recounting.

## Chapter 76: [Conce]rning Lord Manichaios: how he journeyed.

## Chapter 77: The Chapter of the Four Kingdoms

**¶2** ⟨p.189⟩ [The first is the kingdom of] the land of Ba[by]lon and of Persia.
The s[ec]ond is the kingdom of the Romans.
The thir[d is the k]ingdom of the Axumites.
The fourth is the kin[g]dom of Silis.
These four great kingdoms e[xi]st in the world; there is none that surpasses them.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The list that follows is a fourfold schema of the chief outward dominations in the world; the heading must introduce the first member as a ‘kingdom’, in the impersonal classificatory register of the oldest layer.

**Assessment:** All lacunae are mechanically constrained by the preserved surrounding letters and the fixed four-kingdom enumerative structure; no deeper alternative restorations are demanded by the correspondential sense in this short excerpt.

## Chapter 78: Concerning the Four Things over which People kill each other.

**¶2** The first thing is [the desire] of people; in that they would eat [for the increase] of their body [so that their thought is made heavy and their soul becomes forgetful]

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The oldest layer is describing the first ruling love: fixation of the will on bodily delight. “Obsession” is too modern-psychological; the substrate register typically names this as desire or lust (a ruling end) for eating and bodily life.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the body’s “swelling” is the drive to enlarge the outer self. “Increase” fits the impersonal, cosmological register better than a medical image, while preserving the meaning of self-enlargement.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* After “eat for the increase of their body” the teaching typically states the spiritual effect: the mind is dulled, interior remembrance is lost. This matches the spiritual translation’s point that the mind is driven and reduced to outer gratification.

**¶3** The second is [sexual inter]course; [the defilement of the body] fornication [and adultery] fornication [with men] men and women [with women] in it with each other.

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The second ruling love is the profanation of conjunction. In the oldest layer it is stated as impurity or defilement, not moral exhortation. This clause supplies the general heading before the repeated instances of fornication.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Pairs naturally with “fornication” in this register as the general forms of unchaste conjunction; also matches the spiritual sense of profaning the marriage of good and truth.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Completes the series “fornication … fornication … men and women …”, making explicit the disordering of relations in every direction, as demanded by the correspondential description of promiscuous union.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Balances the previous item and keeps the impersonal catalogue style of the oldest layer.

**¶4** The third thing is wealth; and [the gathering of possessions] the useless riches in [which] they shall pride themselves without [use] from it.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The third ruling love is external acquisition for its own sake. “Gathering” expresses the act of accumulation in the natural degree, corresponding to exalting means over ends.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* The contrast in the sentence is between “useless riches” and what they lack “from it”; correspondentially this is the absence of use (end-service) from possessions.

**¶5** The fourth is war; and the disputes, the [accusations] and the trials that they instigate against each other. Someone is victor over another, with each other in tyranny, so that they have received a name in the world.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The fourth ruling love (dominion) unfolds as contention, accusation, and judicial assault. “Disputes … accusations … trials” is the demanded sequence in the spiritual translation.

**¶6** All people, they who side with these four major things that [a]re on every tongue [in the] world [and are praised] they struggle so that they might generate them [in their assemblies] among them.

> *¶6 #3 [tentative]:* These ruling loves become “on every tongue in the world” not only as speech but as public approval. This keeps the external-community emphasis of the oldest layer (reputation as reward).
> *¶6 #4 [tentative]:* The striving to generate and multiply these loves occurs within social bodies; “assemblies” fits the impersonal societal register without importing later ecclesial language.

**¶7** [But the Judge of the worlds comes between them,] he separates the Light from the Darkness, the good from the wicked;

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation requires an agent that introduces discernment and separation. In the oldest substrate this is not a Christian title but a cosmic function: the ordering Judge who distinguishes and divides Light from Darkness.

**¶8** For they will not remain with them for ete[rni]ty; rather they shall leave them behind and die.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of the correspondential structure (the four ruling loves; their social multiplication; the cosmic separation of Light and Darkness; the non-endurance of corruptions). Several short lacunae in ¶2 and ¶6 admit more than one equally plausible Coptic phrasing; the restorations given aim to stay in the impersonal, cosmological register of the oldest substrate and avoid later moralizing or ecclesial terminology.

---

## Chapter 79: Concerning the Fasting of the Saints

**¶3** The first work: Shall the holy man punish his body by fasting, he subdues the entire ruling-power that exists in him.

**¶4** The second: This soul that comes into him in the administration of his food, day by day; it shall be made holy, cleansed, purified, and washed from the adulteration of the Darkness that is mixed in with it.

**¶5** The third: That person shall make every deed a holy one; the mystery of [the children] of light in whom there is neither corruption nor [defilement] the food, nor wound it. Rather, they are holy, [there is nothing] in them that defiles, as they live in peace.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* Accepted. In the oldest substrate this fixed phrase names the Light-stock itself, i.e., those constituted from unmixed Light, which matches the following description “neither corruption nor …” and the concern not to harm the Light-substance in food.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The series requires a second negated predicate parallel to “corruption”: not only decay (mixture leading to dissolution) but also stain (adulterating contact with Darkness). This also prepares the next clause about not defiling the food.
> *¶5 #3 [strong]:* Accepted. It states the interior condition of the Light-vessel: no foreign admixture that can pollute, corresponding to the spiritual sense of an undefiled inner life.

**¶6** The fourth: They make a [sign upon] the Cross, they restrain their hands from the hand [that he may] destroy the living soul.

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* The fourth work begins from “the Cross” as the fixed token of endured conflict and deliverance; the required verb is one of establishing a rule or mark from it. “sign upon the Cross” gives the needed sense: they take it as an instituted standard set over their conduct, leading into the restraint described next.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The clause needs a purpose link: they restrain their hands from the hand of harming, in order that they do not destroy the living soul. “that he may” supplies the functional connection demanded by the correspondential logic (restraint for preservation of life).

**Assessment:** Most restorations are structurally constrained by the correspondential sequence of the ‘four works’: (1) subduing the ruling power, (2) purifying what is received daily, (3) keeping the Light undefiled in deed and in food, (4) instituting restraint in outward action from the Cross so as not to destroy living soul. ¶5 #2 and ¶6 #1 remain the loosest because the Coptic idiom behind them is not recoverable from the excerpt alone; the fills supplied follow the substrate logic of mixture versus purity and of the Cross as an established rule of restraint.

---
