## Chapter 82: The Chapter of Righteous [Judgement]

**¶2** Through anger, then, I will [make him stand] before him. That person shall wither and be subdued instantly and come to the place [of judgement] with righteous judgement.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires an act of setting the resistant state in order under zeal for good: bringing the person into a compelled stance before what judges truly. Hence an ordering verb (make stand, set before) rather than harming.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The place described is where resistance must accept true ordering; in this register it is the place of assessment, not punishment for its own sake.

**¶4** "[Concerning] this anger with which you shall be angry, you are making in it a judgement of righteousness [As for] difficult people [so as to reprove, and] repentance. Their anger and wickedness and evil-doing [upon those] is only a scare and a rebuke; because [so that they may come to] For the anger alone is the scare, and when [in them] you would censure and upbraid that brother with words that scare, you are shaming him to good use, so that you might strip him of the obduracy [it is not to destroy them.] in this [the occasion is at hand,] his heart [that is in]."

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* Opens an impersonal exposition about the permitted kind of zeal, matching the didactic substrate style.
> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* Sets up a definition of the just kind of anger as an instrument of righteous ordering.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual context: zeal is allowed when it functions as correction and ordering, not injury; therefore it is ‘making in it a judgement of righteousness’ by reproving.
> *¶4 #4 [strong]:* The object is ‘difficult people’ (hard to lead); the phrase needs a preposition introducing them.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: severity aims at amendment, i.e., change of mind; thus it presses toward repentance.
> *¶4 #6 [strong]:* Refers back to ‘their anger and wickedness and evil-doing’: the hostile heat is within them, not in the corrector.
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* Correspondential constraint: the permitted severity is a restraint and warning, not a will to ruin; this explicit negation stabilizes the distinction.
> *¶4 #8 [moderate]:* The text contrasts constant function (anger as scare) with variable application (‘when … you would censure’).
> *¶4 #9 [strong]:* ‘Obduracy … in this … his heart’: the gap must carry the indwelling location of resistance, i.e., ‘that is in his heart’.

**¶5** "However, you have heard [also another kind of] the anger. For this is the anger that lets you find occasion to lust after an evil thing with sin in it. [But this one—] listen to him [Do not] and he is angry with an anger [And if he does not] look at him [what pleases him,] and he is violent [with heat from pride;] he does not utter constructive words [in order to overpower;] composed anger; he is angry in his pride and boastfulness. They speak ill in that they would [that make peace,] him. And they do not honour him as he is angry and abuses and curses, and wishes to humiliate the meek, in retaliation and violence against his ill-treated brothers. He engenders the things that [underestimate] or else if someone will falter and commit sin [stir up] and does not accept judgement. He is angry or he walks in his own [because of him,] not of the opinion of his brothers. They reprimand him about it, and he assumes anger and commits an evil deed in his anger. This is the evil anger [mind,] and zeal. [that is mixed with hatred] call it anger."

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Introduces the opposite-sense anger (destructive zeal) as a second species, matching the spiritual translation.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Marks the turn from the just zeal to the evil zeal and prepares the defining clause.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The line is a caution: you have heard, but are not to embrace this anger; imperative fits the substrate’s instructional tone.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: one listens only to what serves self; the gap introduces the condition of refusal to hear what is true.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* Completes the idea: selective listening that supports self-will, as in the spiritual translation.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* The anger described is from self, not zeal for good—arrogant heat.
> *¶5 #7 [moderate]:* ‘Look at him … and he is violent’: the gaze is for domination, not discernment.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* Opposes abusive speech; in the oldest layer the contrast is between building and tearing down—peace-making, constructive words.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* They speak ill so that others will ‘think little of’ the targeted brother; ‘underestimate’ matches the spiritual sense of making him worthless in others’ eyes.
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* From such rage come further evils: it either incites others into wrongdoing or uses another’s fall as fuel; ‘stir up’ fits the causative function.
> *¶5 #11 [moderate]:* The faltering of another is made an occasion ‘because of him’ (on his account) for further accusation.
> *¶5 #12 [strong]:* ‘He walks in his own … not of the opinion of his brothers’: ‘mind’ gives the interior self-rule contrasted with common understanding.
> *¶5 #13 [moderate]:* Summarizes the evil species: anger joined to zeal in appearance but actually hatred; corresponds to ‘evil zeal’ in the spiritual translation.

**¶6** "[But this is the distinction:] Whenever you may produce evil or [stubborn falsity,] two or one [matters,] becomes obdurate and not listen to you. You see him [of your words,] he humiliates himself, assuming up to now a [lowering himself,] You understand that they [hardness of heart.] harshness to you [attribute] them with hard words, in cruelty and harshness. And they will not be able to name it anger; rather its name is the strong reprimand that leads to repentance.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* Transitions from the description of evil anger to the rule for firm correction, matching the doctrinal, impersonal mode.
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Spiritual sense explicitly includes ‘stubborn falsity’ alongside evil; the oldest layer often pairs moral evil with falsity as its form.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The phrase ‘two or one … becomes obdurate’ needs a general noun for the issues produced—evil or false matters.
> *¶6 #4 [strong]:* He ‘becomes obdurate and not listen to you’: the object is the offered correction, i.e., your words.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation: he ‘lowers himself into obstinacy’; this fill matches that motion in the inner sense.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Names the state he assumes ‘up to now’: an interior hardening rather than a temporary mood.
> *¶6 #7 [strong]:* ‘You understand that they … harshness to you’: they ascribe it; ‘attribute’ is demanded by the spiritual sense.

**¶7** For you are like a wise doctor, whose hand is sensitive in [touching] the wounds that he shall heal are severe [and the] sometimes he shall place on the wound a remedy that is hot, sometimes a remedy that is cool [and this is] after the blow. Corresponding to the simile [according to the state] this too is how it happens [that has been said,] There are some, if he shall take the rebuke [in those who are being corrected.]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* A doctor’s hand is ‘sensitive in touching’: the correspondential point is delicate perception of states.
> *¶7 #2 [strong]:* Joins two clauses: severe wounds and varied applications; small connective required.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Introduces the alternation of hot and cool remedies as a rule of application.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondence: varied influx according to condition of the disorder; ‘state’ is the governing idea.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* Links the simile back to the preceding description; ‘corresponding to the simile …’ needs ‘that has been said’.
> *¶7 #6 [moderate]:* Completes the generalization: ‘this too is how it happens’—in the recipients of rebuke/correction.

**¶8** Through the hard word [is like] the blow that shall be cured by the remedy [So also there are two kinds] of people if he shall take the rebuke [and be healed;] with the humbling word, just as this [while another kind] healed by the cool remedy [and be harmed,] the wise person is like this understanding doctor [one is] speaks to him as he is subdued corresponding to error [so also] in humility. Also, this one who will speak to him [and he] he spoke [by words] so that [until he brings him] they of godly opinion. And [from what is pure in him,] so that he might [his intention remains] the brother in two aspects [be in] love, but the other one with fear [regard] obduracy in one of his brothers [one with] foolishness. He may speak with those ones with great fear [and the restraining] of evil-doing and hate. Fear, however, leads to repentance [If there is] Yet, this one [and from] as even he is [and restraining words,] he reckons words and what is [but not to destroy.] in the aspect [to be in order;] and he upbraids that brother until he brings him to repentance.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* Carries forward the medical correspondence: hard word corresponds to the painful remedy.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The paragraph turns to two recipient types; the fill must introduce the bifurcation.
> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* First type: accepts sharp rebuke and is restored—healing outcome.
> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* Sets the contrast: those harmed by sharpness need gentleness.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* Matches ‘driven further into resistance’ in spiritual translation; the opposite of being healed by sharpness.
> *¶8 #6 [strong]:* ‘just as this … healed’: needs subject marker for the comparison in the natural text.
> *¶8 #7 [moderate]:* Ties the correspondence: cool remedy parallels humbling gentleness.
> *¶8 #8 [strong]:* Connective to continue description of the wise corrector’s action.
> *¶8 #9 [moderate]:* He ‘speaks to him … corresponding to error’: the instrument is fitted words or truths.
> *¶8 #10 [moderate]:* Captures the end: pressing the proud down by truths until acknowledgment and humility occur.
> *¶8 #11 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation: ensure correction proceeds from what is godly so intention remains pure; ‘pure in him’ keeps oldest-layer register without devotional framing.
> *¶8 #12 [moderate]:* Completes the purpose clause: keeping the inner end in order.
> *¶8 #13 [moderate]:* ‘And … so that he might … the brother’: needs an infinitive construction ‘be in’ leading into ‘two aspects’.
> *¶8 #14 [strong]:* He is to ‘regard the brother in two aspects’: dual-mode correction (love, fear).
> *¶8 #15 [strong]:* Pairs with ‘but the other one’: grammatical necessity.
> *¶8 #16 [moderate]:* Fear here is not terror for its own sake but restraining confrontation; this term matches the two-aspect doctrine and ¶9’s ‘confrontation’.
> *¶8 #17 [strong]:* Introduces the condition: obduracy present in a brother.
> *¶8 #18 [moderate]:* Links ‘foolishness’ with its source (foolish confidence, delight).
> *¶8 #19 [moderate]:* What is spoken ‘with great fear’ is firm truth that arrests; this matches the correspondential role of fear as restraint.
> *¶8 #20 [moderate]:* Again enforces the substrate distinction: fear is instrumental for repentance, not annihilation.
> *¶8 #21 [moderate]:* ‘Yet, this one … as even he is … he reckons words’: even when using fear, the corrector must remain ordered within himself; this matches the spiritual translation’s weighing of words and effects.

**¶9** For the light-givers that are in the heavens shall be revealed with these two aspects, in the aspect of love and the aspect of fear. They display these two aspects of the powers above that are in the heavens, because of their soul that passes through the totality. For in these two appearances, the aspect of love that is beauty and sublimity, together with the other aspect of confrontation that is in them, they shall purify in them the powers above.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of semantic function because the chapter is built on a tight correspondence: two kinds of ‘anger’ (zeal as just correction vs rage from self), and the medical simile (hot and cool remedies) mapping to sharp and gentle speech. Exact Coptic idiom cannot be recovered from the English core alone, so several fills remain moderate, aimed at preserving the oldest, impersonal cosmological-instruction register and the discrete two-aspect structure (love and restraining fear).

---

## Chapter 83: Concerning the Man who is ugly in his Body, [but] beautiful [in his Soul].

**¶9** “Pearls shall arise not in every place in the sea, nor be formed in the sea as a whole. Rather, in various places that are in this sea, pearls are formed in them [by the power that is in] that sea, in which the [pearls] shall be formed [in] this [place of the sea] what the sea shall [receive in it] its fire {blazes} above and comes [down from above] and it makes foam like the drop of water that flows [from above] down in rainwater [and it] is the water [comes]own first [and it becomes] foam and comes down [toward] the sweet waters [above all] the waters.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* Pearls form only where a specific active cause is present; correspondentially, distinct truths arise only where an inflow has prepared a particular state, not from the sea in general.
> *¶9 #3 [moderate]:* Needed to link: “that sea, in which the pearls shall be formed in this …” i.e., in this particular place or region.
> *¶9 #4 [moderate]:* The logic contrasts the whole sea with particular loci; correspondentially, the ‘external field’ vs prepared ‘states’ within it.
> *¶9 #5 [tentative]:* What the sea ‘shall’ do here is to receive the descending fire and produce foam; the external mind receives influx in ultimates.
> *¶9 #6 [strong]:* The fire ‘blazes above and comes down’—the required direction of influx (higher to lower) in the substrate cosmology.
> *¶9 #7 [moderate]:* Balances the descent clause and keeps the inside-to-outside influx sequence.
> *¶9 #8 [tentative]:* Bridges: “drop of water that flows … down in rainwater … is the water …”
> *¶9 #9 [moderate]:* Fits: “is the water … comes down first … foam” matching the sequence: descent → first effect (foam).
> *¶9 #10 [moderate]:* Supports the process chain: descent first, then becoming foam, then coming down toward sweet waters.
> *¶9 #11 [moderate]:* Links foam’s movement to its destination (sweet waters), matching the spiritual sense: not to the depths but to accessible externals.
> *¶9 #12 [tentative]:* Construes “the sweet waters … the waters” as a distinction of purer vs general waters: sweet waters are ‘above’ the common waters in worth/order.

**¶10** “This drop of water shall [come] down to the sweet waters and [they came] and they absorbed them and were combined with the [sweet waters]. They did not [d]escend to the depths of the sea, but they [remained above it] it floated on the surface of the waters [and because of this] to it. The foam and the pearl-shell shall be formed [from] this wholesome drop [of sweet water in] it, and it becomes a pe[arl itself] that makes a drop of rainwater [upon the] waters [and the sweet] it not being whole. It breaks and separates out into many droplets, and it has time to become a drop of sweet water [because] and comes up in the sea of [again] rain and sweet water; and it is accomodated in the shell, which at first is foam. They shall be joined with each other at [this] time, and are shaped and become a great pea[r]l, a great and valued kind.

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Required verb: “shall come down to the sweet waters”.
> *¶10 #2 [tentative]:* Keeps plural subject for the droplets/foam elements: they come and are received/absorbed.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* They are absorbed and combined with what is already sweet/pure; correspondentially, higher truth conjoins with purer truths already present.
> *¶10 #5 [moderate]:* Opposes “depths of the sea”; spiritually, the inflow does not sink into mere generals but stays in the outer accessible plane.
> *¶10 #6 [tentative]:* Explains why it floated and why formation occurs at the surface: reception is possible only in that external plane.
> *¶10 #7 [strong]:* Links: “shall be formed from this wholesome drop”.
> *¶10 #8 [tentative]:* The wholesome drop is specified as sweet water; the pearl is fixed within the shell by that sweet reception.
> *¶10 #9 [moderate]:* Completes “pearl … itself” (pearl becomes a pearl itself), emphasizing completion of the formed truth.
> *¶10 #10 [moderate]:* Fits: “drop of rainwater upon the waters”.
> *¶10 #11 [tentative]:* Introduces the twofold water source in the narrative: rainwater and sweet water, matching the spiritual pairing of descending influx and prior purified truths.
> *¶10 #12 [strong]:* Introduces the causal: “it not being whole, because it breaks…”
> *¶10 #13 [moderate]:* Marks repetition: the dispersed droplets can ‘again’ become a sweet drop, i.e., be re-collected into unity.

**¶11** “When, however, a drop of rainwater falls, and that drop breaks into many droplets and various {particles of water}, they shall be formed into and be confined in [n]umerous pearls, in the shell and the pearl-shell. One might form two pearls, another may form three, others may form five; some mould more than these, some fewer.

**¶12** “Now, when you might [find a] whole drop, and the shell receives it, it shall become a great and valued [pe]arl as its worth is perfected. [However], if these two droplets will have time (to adhere) to one another before any (water particle} escapes, and they mix with e[ach o]ther, and the shell [receives them] before they break into [them] within [the shell] in a great kind [it and they are fixed] the drop of rain, which [of pearl] another one, that [has time to join] the [w]aters form them in [and they are perfected] in a great, valued commodity.

> *¶12 #5 [moderate]:* The shell’s function is containment and protection; it ‘receives’ the adhering droplets before dispersal.
> *¶12 #6 [tentative]:* Direct object of the breaking/scattering clause.
> *¶12 #7 [moderate]:* The containing form is the decisive agent in the paragraph’s logic.
> *¶12 #8 [tentative]:* Correspondentially, truths are held and fixed within a containing form; this is the condition for a ‘great kind’.
> *¶12 #9 [moderate]:* Completes “in a great kind …”: a great kind of pearl, i.e., a more valued formed truth.
> *¶12 #10 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual sense: given time to adhere before any part escapes into dispersal.
> *¶12 #12 [moderate]:* Carries the closing: waters form them and they are perfected “in a great, valued commodity.”

**¶13** “Behold, [I have] taught you how sea-pearls shall be formed. I have told you that as a pearl shall come into existence by means of rainwater that has [ti]me to become foam, the pearl-shell shall come into existence by means of the foam, and the foam itself comes into being by means of the transformation and the [agitation] of the sea.

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* Oldest-layer register avoids doubled emphatic self-reference; simple impersonal teacher-voice suffices here.
> *¶13 #3 [strong]:* Foam arises from transformation and agitation of the sea; spiritually, outer instability arises from change/agitation in the external mind as influx begins to order it.

**¶14** “Then immediately at the time when [the time of its rising] the pearl divers know it, they shall [set out] and they [go]own to those places and they bring pearls up from the depths of the sea, and each pearl diver finds according to the fortune that is [ordained]ned for him. The pearl divers shall [give]ve them to the traders, and the traders give them to the kings and the nobles.

> *¶14 #1 [tentative]:* Pearl divers recognize the state when pearls ‘rise’ or become available; correspondentially, workers recognize prepared states when truths are ready to be raised.
> *¶14 #2 [moderate]:* They begin the work once the state is known; corresponds to being moved by directed influx toward prepared states.
> *¶14 #3 [strong]:* Completes “they go down”.

**¶16** “[The] booty that shall be seized is like the dr[op of r]ainwater, while the apostles are like the divers. The traders are the light-givers of the heavens; the kings and no[b]les are the aeons of greatness.

**¶17** “Fo[Fo]ll the souls that ascend in the flesh of mankind and are freed shall be brought back to the great aeons of light. A place of rest comes about for them, at that place in the aeons of greatness.

> *¶17 #1 [strong]:* Completes “For all the souls …”

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored at the level of process-logic (influx from above, first foam, retention near the surface, conjunction within a containing shell, multiplication when reception is divided, perfection when held together). Where the surviving English is already far from the underlying Coptic word-order, several short connectors remain tentative; but the macro-sequence and key substrate terms (inflow from above, sweet waters vs general waters, agitation producing foam, containment/fixing in the shell, ordained portion) are correspondentially tight.

---

## Chapter 84: Concerning Wisdom; it is far superior when on the Tongue than in the Heart of the Person.

**¶1** Behold, when a person will be taught wise wisdom in his heart, and he seals it in his doctrine, so shall you find him rejoicing greatly about it. However, [sh]all his [joy be doubled] to him, more th[an] when he may pro-c[claim it] and utter it. ⟨p.205⟩ He shall be enlightened by it, and [t]hat [wisdom] shall shine forth the more in him. It is un-veil[ed be]fore him, and through it he assumes power and truth.

> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The contrast required is between inward sealing and outward utterance. The substrate idiom regularly speaks of joy increasing or being doubled when truth passes from interior containment into exterior use.
> *¶1 #4 [strong]:* The clause contrasts inward possession with outward proclamation; the minimal restoration that matches the sense is “proclaim it.”

**¶2** I understand [indeed] that this word I have uttered is correct. I know that the wis-dom I have been taught is spread through my heart and perfected in my soul. (However), it is not found like the splendour in me, so that I regard it [ad]vanced, except when I shall proclaim it by my mouth and utter it to others. Indeed, when I proclaim it, I am giving it to the ears [of] others to hear. Would I do these same things, even if I had never heard it in [my] days of being? Would I desire greatly and my heart be drawn to the wis-dom I now proclaim? [I entreat y]ou, my master, that you might instruct me as to [w hy] this wisdom becomes more advanced when I proclaim it, than when it is sealed in my heart.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* A discourse marker is demanded: the speaker affirms internal perception that the uttered word is right, preparing the request for instruction.

**¶3** Well [do] you ask! And great is this lesson for which you have sought, [that is] whence comes my great joy, on account of this wis-dom that I utter? [For this is why] it is superior in my mouth when I proclaim it than when it is set in m[y heart]. You yourself rejoice in it; and the other one who hears it from you, he shall rejoice in it, and be enlightened [by] it and receive thereby permanent strength.

> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The reply must introduce the causal ground of the increase: wisdom becomes more alive in utterance and in reception by another. A short causal lead-in fits the impersonal teaching register.

**¶4** For like this matter, just so a small b[o]y who is conceived in the belly of his mother. He [and as he lives,] turns in his mother's womb, filling her womb. The mother knows and understands that this child she conceives is alive within her. She rejoices over him until [the tim]e when she gives birth. And he comes from her alive with his [limbs whole] and perfect in beauty, without defect, ⟨p.206⟩ [in] the living open air that is more sp[ac]ious than the first air he was in. He fills his eyes with the light and speaks with his living voice in the way of they who are born.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The simile requires a sign of animation: the child’s turning is evidence of life within, corresponding to the stirring of a formed life within affection.

**¶5** Now, the time when this woman conceives the child in her belly, her joy at conceiving him in her womb is not so [ve]ry great as when she gives birth and sees him; and is full of his [be]auty and stature in the space of a single moment. The love and joy over him shall be a hundred [t]imes greater than it was, now that she has given birth and seen him.

**¶6** For the first time when she conceived him in her belly, his beauty and the sight of his eyes was hidden from his m[o th]er; but when she gave birth to him she saw his beauty. His s[t]ature and his loveliness came before the e[ye]s of his f[athe]r [a]nd his mother and all his relatives. They shall rejoic[e] over him more and more when they look upon him face to face and see his beauty and delightfulness.

**¶7** Just as in this simile, the wisdom that is present [in] the heart of the person is like the living child who is co[nceived] in the belly of his mother. And when he is taught and seals it in his heart, it becomes like the child who shall be born, and they see his beauty.

**¶8** So, in this way, the wisdom that the person proclaims, speak[ng] it from his heart, shall be advanced more and mor[e]. Its en-hancement and glory shall double from the time when the bea-uty and splendour of the saying will be displayed before the eyes [of t]hey who hear it, and it shall also advance for you [through the joy of] your hearing, and you are astonished at what you proclai[m].

> *¶8 #4 [moderate]:* The advance ‘for you’ is explained by the speaker’s renewed opening in the act of bringing-forth; in the natural wording this is best carried by joy and wonder as the immediate affectional correspondences of utterance.

**¶9** Once again, the wisdom is like this, while it is hidden in the heart of the person. [Bef]ore he has uttered it, it is just like [the blaze] of fire that is hidden in wood. An[d] that wood is [set aflame] by the blaze of the fire, but the garment of fir[e that exists in] the wood is not apparent. Indeed, you can see [many pieces of] ⟨p.207⟩ wood and they put them in a single house. It is impossible to [put li]ght to that house as long as the light within [the wood does not come forth] until the time when they are added to the fire, and the light [comes f]orth from them. It is possible for that entire house [to be] lit by the light of a single piece of wood.

> *¶9 #6 [strong]:* The example requires plurality: many woods gathered into one house correspond to many natural knowledges collected in one external life.
> *¶9 #8 [moderate]:* The constraint is that illumination cannot occur while the inner light remains unmanifest; thus the missing clause must deny emergence of light from the stored wood prior to joining it to fire.

**¶10** This is also the case with the wisdom that is in the heart of the person. [It] is like the fire that is hidden in the wood, as its light is not [d]isplayed. For its part, the wisdom is like this: its li[ght] is hidden and its glory is hidden in the heart; but when the person will proclaim it, its glory shall be displayed before the eyes and the ears of a multitude.

**¶13** For in this respect the wisdom is like this child about whom I have told you, the one who [was] born from the woman. Now, when he will be born, his father and mother and family circle shall rejoice over him. However, you find others grieving by reason of him, [s]ince they are strangers to him. These are not reckoned among his family. [They do n]ot rejoice over him, because he is not of their race.

**¶14** This is also [the case] with the wisdom. When it is proclaimed by the mouth of the teacher, these who are [a]kin to it shall receive it to them and rejoice in it; but [those] who are strangers to it neither rejoice in it [nor are they enlightened by it] nor receive it to them.

> *¶14 #4 [moderate]:* In the substrate logic, rejoicing and enlightenment go together as effects of kinship with wisdom; strangers neither rejoice nor receive, therefore the missing member parallels ‘rejoice’ with ‘be enlightened.’

**¶15** Just like the light of [the fire, which I] proclaim[ed] to you, that shall come from the wood [and be apparent o]utside before the eyes of every one. ⟨p.208⟩ So, [wh]oever looks shall see the light that has [come fro]m the wood; but whoever is blind does not see the [fir]e.

**¶16** This is also the case with the wisdom, when it will be pro-claimed. The person, in whom is the [Mi]nd, of him is the wisdom. Whenever he may hear it, he shall receive it in to him; but the one who has no Mind in him is a stranger to it. He neither receives it in to him, nor shall he listen to [it].

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter restorations. The few substantial gaps are tightly constrained by the correspondential structure: inward sealing versus outward manifestation, the childbirth simile of concealed formation versus open life, and the fire-in-wood image of hidden potency versus released illumination. Where exact phrasing is not recoverable, restorations were kept minimal and in the impersonal cosmological-teaching register of the oldest layer.

---

## Chapter 85: Concerning the Cross of Light: [...] trample upon it.

**¶3** Now, when you walk on [your] way about some godly matter, as your commanding superior sends you, you do no sin therein. For you are not walking there about some lustful matter, nor concerning [love of gain] of advantage, wherein is sin; but you make your walkway due to the work of God, as you hasten [not] because of your own desire.

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense specifies self-advantage as the spring of sin; the oldest-layer register expresses this as the acquisitive love (gain) rather than a moralizing term.
> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Required by the opposition in the sentence: haste from use, not from self-desire.

**¶4** Indeed, I, I tell you with a great voice: Every elect and righteous person who walks on a path due to the work of God, and though walking upon the earth and though treading with the soles of his feet on the Cross of Light, he does no sin therein. Rather, a crown and palm-branch is his entire path; because he walks not by his own desire, nor hastens about an acquisition and worthless things, as he tramples upon the earth and the Cross of Light.

**¶5** However, the other person who hastens on the path because of his own lust and [the lusts] of his body, as he [turns] his own about an acquisition and [worthless] things [everywhere]—a total sin is the walkway of that person, walking on the path of sin and error.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The other path is driven by bodily craving in the natural degree; the substrate contrasts holy use vs. lusts.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* He 'turns' or 'sets' his powers toward acquisition; this matches the spiritual sense: turning his powers toward possession.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Pairs with 'acquisition' as vanity and empty ends in the spiritual sense.
> *¶5 #4 [tentative]:* The clause needs a summative adverb before the conclusion 'a total sin is the walkway'; corresponds to the idea that the whole course is evil.

**¶6** [See,] behold, I have told you: Any one who, if his teacher will send him, goes on a path about some godly matter, a total victory is his path. Still, you also recounted this: “I trample upon the earth and the Cross of Light, I oppress with my [sole].” As you say: “Perhaps I have therein sin because of [the de]ed.”

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Introduces the recap and application ('behold, I have told you'), matching the spiritual 'See, then'.

**¶7** Again, do not be afraid even with this; because the living soul is like this. As a person, ⟨p.210⟩ if a disease will take hold of his limbs, and his heart [is sl]ow, his soul distressed, and he has pain in his disease. He seeks after a doctor to cure him, to make him strong of heart. And he cures, attends to, and gives him health [from] the disease that has risen up against him. So he thinks about this.

**¶8** [And] a wise person comes, knowing the formulas proper to a skilled doctor. If he shall achieve a healing of the one whom he [has entrusted] it, he recites over him the formulas that he knows, and he even tramples with his foot upon all his limbs! That person, who is ill, knows that this person is trampling upon him so that he might achieve a healing; rather, not doing this to him out of some enmity he had against him beforehand. Instead, all that he does, he does to him that he might make a healing of his body. And he expels [the] disease and the pain, trampling upon him to occasion this benefit. That person, who is ill, he can bear no anger nor hatred against this doctor, who tramples upon him. He can not hate him out of enmity, because he knows that he does this to him to his benefit.

> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The healer acts on 'the one entrusted to him' in the spiritual sense; in the natural image this is the patient committed to the doctor’s care.

**¶9** However, should someone come and trample upon him in enmity; he may hate, prevent and not permit him! For he knows that he tramples upon him in enmity; and as he strikes out of a wanton whim, he piles another illness upon his disease.

**¶12** Rather, the word that wounds is this: should a person utter a saying for the sake of the murder of a person, or the murder of animals, or for [the sake of] the murder of trees and the Cross of Light, the lying word and the wrathful and the bitter, or a word of envy and [hatred] only, or a word of accusation, which someone will spread against his brother; this is the [wor]d that wounds [the soul]. Should the person utter it, it befits [si]n; because [he has br]ought forth from it the demons [and the powers of Darkness] concerning this [word] the righteous person, or [and he binds himself] all of them, which I have proclaimed, in order that [he may bind] his word to the fetter; because anyone who would [bind] his word to the fetter. And again, he [binds] his coming forth, they will bind him in the place wherein he has bound his [soul].

> *¶12 #2 [moderate]:* In the spiritual sense the harmful utterance includes envy and malice; in this register 'envy and hatred' is the common pair.
> *¶12 #4 [tentative]:* What is wounded by such speech, in this oldest cosmological layer, is the living soul bound in created forms (persons, animals, trees, Cross of Light).
> *¶12 #7 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense says he opens a way for hellish influences; the substrate idiom is 'demons and powers of Darkness' acting through the utterance.
> *¶12 #8 [moderate]:* Refers back to the uttered saying as the channel by which demons are brought forth to act.
> *¶12 #9 [moderate]:* Directly corresponds to 'he fastens himself by it to the evil he has chosen' and anticipates 'bind him in the place'.
> *¶12 #10 [moderate]:* The logic requires an infinitival purpose: he binds his word to the fetter so that he may bind himself; the repeated binding motif governs the whole passage.
> *¶12 #11 [strong]:* Parallelism: 'anyone who would bind his word to the fetter'—the act itself is the binding.
> *¶12 #12 [moderate]:* Concludes the maxim: whoever binds his word (inner intention in speech) binds himself.
> *¶12 #13 [moderate]:* The final clause requires what is bound: when he comes forth (leaves the body), he is bound where he had bound his soul, i.e., his ruling love/state.

**¶14** [He is li]ke this, a wise doctor who might turn back [the evil within] his disease. As a wound is in his body, he cuts [open] this person who is ill, in his wisdom, [and heals] he [so that he may uncover] this wound, and he cuts it. After these things [may draw out] and he places the soluble drugs upon it, so that [they will be able to diss]olve. He might incise it with the iron, and he casts it out ⟨p.212⟩ [f]rom him, with the soluble drugs. And the wound is purified [fr]om the fever. After these events, he shall place the soothing drugs [upon it] and it heals up. That person, who is ill, he shall give [thanks] but he shall not [hate him] he [shall not] generate anger in his heart against him; because he knows that he is making the aspect of his benefit and his healing. After the cut that he may make, and the cauterizing that he may do there, he shall even give rewards to the doctor! He gives his thanks and is a friend to him his whole life through.

> *¶14 #2 [moderate]:* The doctor-image corresponds to removing embedded disorder; 'turn back the evil within' matches the spiritual 'harmful falsity embedded'.
> *¶14 #3 [moderate]:* He 'cuts open' to expose what is hidden, matching the spiritual: exposes what is hidden, separates it.
> *¶14 #4 [tentative]:* The doctor both cuts and heals; required to keep the sequence: incision, removal, purification, then healing.
> *¶14 #5 [moderate]:* Corresponds to exposing what is hidden before separating and removing it.
> *¶14 #6 [moderate]:* Matches the extraction motif: casts out what is poisonous.
> *¶14 #10 [strong]:* After purification, the soothing drugs are applied 'upon it' (the wound) before it heals up.
> *¶14 #11 [moderate]:* The restored one gives thanks (and then rewards) to the doctor; matches the spiritual 'gives gratitude'.
> *¶14 #12 [strong]:* Keeps the antithesis: he gives thanks and rewards, but does not hate or resent.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the correspondential structure: the chapter opposes motion from obedience and holy use vs. motion from lust and gain; and it develops the healer image to justify severe external pressure that serves internal restoration. The hardest restorations are places where the Coptic Vorlage likely had idiomatic connectors (¶5 #4; ¶14 #4) and the long binding sequence in ¶12, but even there the spiritual logic of intention becoming a fetter strongly constrains the required vocabulary (bind, fetter, soul, powers of Darkness).

---

## Chapter 86: The Chapter of the Man who asks: Why [am] I sometimes at peace, and troubled at other times?

**¶2** Sometimes [I] am peaceful in my heart, as my consideration [is constructive], and all my doctrines are ordered and in place. I conduct [straightway in] my works, as I utter my words to all people with sweetness. I am peaceful of heart in the gladness of the Mind. ⟨p.214⟩ Even my body is carefree, and my soul [rejo]ices in wisdom and true knowledge.

> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* The peaceful state describes orderly doctrine flowing into orderly use. The gap must express unimpeded execution of works from an ordered mind: ‘straightway in my works’ preserves the impersonal, causal register (order within → order in deeds).

**¶3** There are also times when I shall be troubled. My doctrines are confused. Gloom increases with them, and grief and anger and envy and lust. I am troubled, struggling with all my might that I would subdue them; not finding the strength of mind to subdue them at that hour nor day when they surged up in me. And all these evil teachings and wicked considerations come forth. They have tormented me. I do not comprehend, because there is no single shape in all these counsels that have entered me. Are they revealed to me, or indeed did they enter me from outside and have been shaken into me? I do not know if they have wakened within me alone, have rebelled inside me and set themselves up against me. I do not know about that. However, this only I understand: that I shall be very badly confused in all my doctrines. I shall do [error and disturbance] in my works, and you find my word is worthless in my mouth. I shall not find how [to be at peace in my heart], and I become a source of trouble and confusion for my brothers. Even my body rises up against me, and sickens for that entire day.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* In the opposite state, the spiritual sense demands uses done without right aim and under disorder. ‘Error and disturbance’ matches the described incoherence of counsels and the later contrast with ‘well constructed’ works.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The passage turns from worthless speech to inability to recover an interior state of order. The demanded complement is the loss of peace: not finding how ‘to be at peace in my heart’, which also echoes ¶2’s opening state and keeps the substrate’s interior-causality focus.

**¶6** Well do you ask about this lesson. Behold, I will explain it to you! ⟨p.215⟩ Understand this: The soul that assumes the body, when the Light Mind will come to it, shall be purified by the power of wisdom and obedience, and it is cleansed and made a new man. There is no trouble in (the soul), nor confusion nor disturbance.

**¶7** However, when a disturbance will arise for him and he will be troubled, this disturbance shall go into him in [many ways], first through his birth-signs and his difficult stars that [press upon him;] they turn over him and stir him and trouble him with lust and anger and depression and grief, as he wills. Also, as he wills, the powers of heaven shall trouble him through their roots, to which he is attached.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The disturbance is said to enter ‘first through’ natal and stellar determinations; correspondentially this is influx through fixed natural tendencies. ‘In many ways’ suits the layered avenues of influx without importing later institutional terms.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* The stars are agents that ‘turn over him and stir him’. The missing verb must express oppressive compulsion from above into the natural degree. ‘Press upon him’ fits the causal sequence (birth-signs and difficult stars that press upon him, then stir and trouble).

**¶8** Again, there are times you shall find the powers of heaven are peaceful and tranquil for him, and it comes to [another way; though] a difficult part comes into him by the nourishment that he has eaten, or else indeed in some [mixture of herbs] or in the water that they have drunk. Again, trouble and confusion and anger will increase in him, and lust multiplies upon him together with depression and grief; because of the nourishment of the bread he has eaten and the water he has drunk, which are full of bothersome parts, a vengeful counsel. They shall enter his body, [mixed in] with these foods, and they even become joined in with the wicked parts of the body and the sin that is in him; transferring the anger and the lust and the depression and the grief, these wicked thoughts of the body.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* The structure contrasts two causes: (a) heavenly powers tranquil, yet (b) trouble comes by nourishment. The gap needs a hinge introducing a different avenue while maintaining tranquillity above: ‘another way; though’.
> *¶8 #2 [tentative]:* The context lists vehicles of intake: nourishment eaten, then ‘or else indeed in some … or in the water’. A ‘mixture’ fits the correspondential idea of impure elements entering via ingestion, without moralizing. ‘Herbs’ keeps a natural, impersonal register as a common carrier of qualities.

**¶10** There are times, however, if you shall find that the nourishment that comes into you is pure [and well blended], so it is greater in light and life while the waste is less in it, and the evil-doing is diminished from it. Even the atmosphere above is still, being purified through the [pure air and clear wind;] also the image is calmed from [every disturbance; and peace] increases in you through ⟨p.216⟩ those good parts of the food that come into you. And they find you at ease and tranquil, as you are well governed in your abodes because of the living part, which is greater on account of the light of these justified souls that are in it. The ones that have perfected their deeds. Their dues have ceased, while their soul is carefree [are sweet in your mouth;] they have been allied with these living souls that [are p]resent in you. Due to this you shall find them in you, calm in tranquillity, and they come from you without trouble; and you are found flourishing of body. Even your deeds are determined, being well constructed after their manner, and your doctrines are in order, as your words [because] your soul is carefree within you, ascending like a bird.

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* Here the nourishment is ‘pure’ and therefore ordered: more light and life, less waste. ‘Well blended’ conveys right composition (order of parts) rather than later ethical language.
> *¶10 #2 [tentative]:* The atmosphere above becoming still is explained as purification through its finer elements. ‘Air’ and ‘wind’ keep the oldest cosmological register: surrounding mediums correspond to the natural degree being clarified.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* After ‘the image is calmed from …’ the next clause is ‘… increases in you through those good parts’. The missing sequence must remove agitation and introduce the resulting state. ‘Every disturbance; and peace’ matches the spiritual sense: quieted from disturbance, then peace increases.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* This closes the ordered-state description: doctrines in order ‘as your words …’. The spiritual sense demands teaching proceeding rightly and gently. ‘Sweet in your mouth’ echoes ¶2’s ‘sweetness’ and keeps the impersonal correspondence (ordered truth → sweet utterance).
> *¶10 #6 [moderate]:* ‘Their dues have ceased, while their soul is carefree … they have been allied…’ The causal link required by the spiritual sense is that carefreeness is due to conjunction. ‘Because’ supplies the needed relation without adding later doctrinal framing.

**¶11** Behold, I have explained to you the determination of this lesson for which you asked: Whence come these bad counsels and considerations into you, these vengeful thoughts that trouble; and also whence come into you these good counsels and sweet doctrines, when your Mind is calmed for you. I have told you that when [your bo]dy is troubled, you shall be troubled by the conduits of they below, the difficult parts that come into you by food and the act of eating. Again, when the conduits above are calmed from you, even your food that has come into you is clear and pure. Your heart shall be found ordered in its place, your counsels at peace from disturbance.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the chapter’s strict correspondence chain: inner order produces sweet teaching and determinate works; disorder produces confused counsels and sickening of the outer degree; disturbance enters by fixed natal inclinations, by heavenly attachments, and by impure intake; peace increases by pure, well-ordered intake and a stilled surrounding medium. A few noun-level details (notably the specific ‘mixture’ in ¶8 and the exact atmospheric phrase in ¶10) remain tentative because the spiritual sense fixes function more tightly than surface imagery.

---

## Chapter 87: Concerning the Alms, that [ ... ] life in the Church.

**¶5** What does the assembly of catechu[me]ns resemble? It is like good earth [made ready] in which the gardener shall plant a good tree. [Beho]ld, [th]at good tree transforms the power and the [quality] of all earth, and it produces good fruit. [Th]at [gardener] sets at ease the tree of life, and he r[ests himself].

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The assembly corresponds to a receptive natural state. Good earth is not merely ‘there’ but ‘prepared’ to receive influx, matching the spiritual sense: a receptacle that can be made fruitful before the planting of the living perception.
> *¶5 #5 [strong]:* The tree ‘transforms the power and the quality of all earth’: power = strength/effectual force; quality = the determined state or disposition of the recipient plane. This matches the spiritual sense: reorders the entire strength and quality of the outward mind.

**¶7** Likewise this [liv]ing sou[l] that today is set in mixture; for it wishes to [as]cend and go to the house of its people, but it kn[ow[s a]]nd understands that it has no open door in all the powers of heaven and earth. For they are its oppression that [binds] it everywhere. Indeed, it has no open door except the sun and the moon themselves, the light-givers of the heavens [whereby] they become the place of rest for it. And they become a door that opens for it [in] the coming forth, and (the soul) comes out through them to the country of the household of i[ts] people.

> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* The powers of heaven and earth, while disorder persists, act as constraint and oppression ‘binding’ the soul on every side. This fits the correspondential notion of mixture producing restraint rather than a free passage.
> *¶7 #6 [moderate]:* The light-givers (sun and moon) correspond to the paired essentials of discernment from which illumination proceeds; the clause needs a connector expressing instrumental cause: the light-givers ‘whereby’ they become rest and a door.

**¶8** Again, further in, also the light-givers of the heavens have no place of rest amongst all the powers of heaven; except for the lan[d] of light, that has indeed been theirs for ever.

**¶9** So, thus the living soul is purified of all afflictions; and they provide a rest for it, and open a door that opens to the house [of] its people.

**¶10** Also, it is the case that the land of lig[ht] will become the final receiver for the light-givers of [the] heavens, and they will rest themselves therein and rule in it [for] ever.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the correspondential structure already present in the natural text. The main restorations are ¶5 ‘made ready’ (receptive prepared ground), ¶5 ‘quality’ (state/disposition paired with power), ¶7 ‘binds’ (oppressive constraint of the mixed powers), and ¶7 ‘whereby’ (instrumental link from the light-givers to rest and exit). The remaining lacunae are trivial letter or obvious word completions.

---

## Chapter 88: Concerning the Catechumen who found fault with the Elect: why he is angry.

**¶5** This is also the case with the elect. They are gods as they stand firm in the image of the gods. The divinity that is planted in them came to them from the heights and [ab]elt in them.

> *¶5 #1 [strong]:* The divinity from the heights is said, in the substrate register, not merely to arrive but to remain as an indwelling presence. The spiritual context specifies “abides within their interiors,” which corresponds to Coptic ‘dw-’ completing ‘dwelt’.

**¶6** Further, when they will be troubled and angry and resentful, [they] curse each other. However, do not you be troubled in your heart. You know, once and for all, they are established in a body that is not their own, hating the Flesh of Sin that makes the other dwell in a foreign land. Due to this they shall be angry, and say and utter hard words to each others' faces.

**¶7** For you have known the mystery of the two essences, you have understood that what is good and what is evil dwell in every person. You have also understood that the saints bear a great burden on their shoulders. For you yourself have understood that they carry a great burden, they are established in the body that is not their own.

**¶8** Your hands are always free to beat the Cross of Light.

**¶11** For when quarrelling will arise among the saints [it is] because the old man too dwells in their body; and due to this they shall take offence [and fall into mutual opposition,] they {dispute} with one another.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence explains the cause of quarrelling as the continued presence of the ‘old man’ in the body. The spiritual sense is explicitly causal: “it is because the former self still lives…”. Thus the natural-layer connective must introduce reason, not a new topic.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* The sequence in the spiritual sense is: take offence → fall into mutual opposition → dispute. The surviving translation already has “they {dispute} with one another” after the lacuna, so the missing phrase should supply the intermediate oppositional state and also provide the needed conjunction into the final clause.

**Assessment:** All four lacunae are recoverable with fairly tight constraints from the correspondential structure. ¶5 and ¶6 are essentially certain (orthographic completion and a required subject pronoun). ¶11’s two gaps are constrained by the causal explanation and the progression of states (offence leading to opposition leading to dispute), though the exact connective wording is less mechanically fixed.

---

## Chapter 89: The Chapter of the Nazorean who questions the Teacher

**¶6** “The judge is not a man of wickedness, but rather the work counteracts wickedness, limiting the evil. Nevertheless, what you have recounted is that the judge shall go and punish, kill and slay. He acts like this that he might annul the evil and impede the wickedness in the person; and lay it upon the head of the sinner.

**¶11** “Nevertheless, God has not done anything that is ev[il to] them, rather they to themselves. It is their own deeds that shall con- demn them and cast them in the gehenna of burning.

**Assessment:** Only a trivial mid-word lacuna occurs in this excerpt (ev[il to] them), and the surviving context fixes it unambiguously. No deeper correspondential reconstruction was required.

---

## Chapter 90: Concerning the Fifteen Paths; and whether the Catechumen would have avoided causing his Wealth to go on the Three Paths to Gehenna

**¶2** For we have heard thus from you: ‘Fifteen paths are drawn out in the zone. Four paths are pure and belong [to the] light, leading up to life. Eight other paths are [mixed], leading above from that place. The light shall go [u]p and become free through them; be purified and go in [to the] ships. However, the waste is separated and thrown [dow]n to transmigr[a]tion and the other three paths of [waste are] discharged to the gehennas’.

> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* The structure requires: (1) eight mixed paths by which Light ascends and is purified into the ships, then (2) a separation of refuse downward, then (3) the remaining three paths characterized as wholly refuse and sent to the gehennas. The gap must supply a predicate for “the other three paths of waste … discharged to the gehennas”, i.e., that they too are waste in a complete sense. “waste are” keeps the impersonal, structural register and matches the spiritual sense: the remaining ways are wholly defiled and driven into the hells.

**¶3** You have explained [that the three] paths that belong to waste, the ones that are drawn to the gehennas, [a]re drawn from the fleshes. O[n]e of them is the appetite for [lawl]essness, that of people and of all flesh. The second is the [destruction of] all bodies, which is slaughter, [with] which all flesh is consumed. The third is the damaging action, which [wounds] the Cross of Light and every body; together with the rest of the error and the blasphemy that wounds the gods.

> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* The second waste-path is defined as a universal consuming violence: it pertains to “all bodies”, is “slaughter”, and is the means “with which all flesh is consumed”. Correspondentially this is the annihilating hatred that destroys receptive form. Thus the missing phrase must name not a mere act but the principle: “destruction of” (i.e., the destroying of all bodies).
> *¶3 #7 [strong]:* The third path is “the damaging action” and immediately continues: “which … the Cross of Light and every body”; then later in the same sentence: “the blasphemy that wounds the gods.” The verb required is the same functional idea: injury to what is Divine and to every form of life. “wounds” maintains the oldest-layer impersonal causality (injuring) and coheres with the later clause without shifting into devotional phrasing.

**¶4** Th[ese th]ree paths have you taught us, that they lead to the gehennas. And you have also [told] us that every person shall follow after hi[s] de[e]ds, whether to life or indeed to death.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter or word completions and are recoverable with high certainty. Two gaps require conceptual restoration: ¶2 #7 and ¶3 #5. Both are tightly constrained by the correspondential sequence (ascent and purification of Light through mixed ways; separation of refuse; the three wholly defiled ways to the gehennas) and by the internal parallelism of the third path’s action (“damaging… which wounds the Cross of Light… blasphemy that wounds the gods”).

---

## Chapter 91: Also concerning the Catechumen; shall he be saved in a single Body?

**¶3** The sign of that perfect catechumen is this: You shall find the woman in the house with him, and beside him she is like these strangers. His house, in his reckoning, shall be like these lodging houses. He says: I am living in a house for rent by days and months. His brothers and his relatives shall be, in his reckoning, necessary as foreign people who take up with him, while travelling on the road with him. He [knows that, after a time,] they will separate from him, and each one [of them] may [take] the gold and the silver and the utensils of the [same] house. To him, they shall be like borrowed vessels; [he] takes them, is served by them, and afterwards gives them to their owner. He does not set his trust on them, nor his treasure.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The correspondential thread here is that outward associations are temporary and will be loosed; the text needs a transition from present companionship to the certainty of separation. A cognitive verb in the impersonal register fits the oldest substrate: it is knowledge of order, not exhortation.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Required object of “each one … may …” in the sense of companions taking what pertains to their own use and returning it to order; also keeps the focus on temporary fellow-travellers as the ones who withdraw.
> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* The action demanded is the removal of external supports and knowledges when the temporary conjunction ends; “take” matches the later comparison to borrowed vessels that are taken and then returned.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The utensils belong to the same ‘house’ (the temporary external arrangement). This maintains the internal logic: goods of the external life are treated as belonging to that outward state, not to the person.

**¶8** When, however, they come forth from their body, they travel on their way and pass by in the place above, and go in to the life. They shall be purified in the heavens, and they are plucked just as a fruit that ripens is plucked from the tree. In just the same way, this alms-offering that passes over to the elect is given likeness in many forms. It is purified and goes in to the land of the living. Similarly, the souls of the catechumens who shall not enter (another) body resemble (the alms).

**¶13** Every person who has received the hope and the faith and has separated the light from the darkness, and has perceived the mysteries of the living soul, he has received the right hand of peace from the Light Mind

**¶20** As for the rest, he shall be questioned about a single part; and receive blows for (those sins) and retribution. Afterwards he is purified, whether indeed above or below. He shall be purified according to the worth of his deeds, and cleansed and washed and adorned. Afterwards, he is sculpted a light image; and [he glides]es up and reaches the land of rest, so that the place where his heart is, his treasure also will be there.

> *¶20 #1 [strong]:* The image is elevation after purification into rest; ‘glides up’ expresses smooth ascent when impediments have been removed. This matches the spiritual sense: elevation into a state of peace after correction and cleansing.

**¶24** the first light, the one that brings understanding through the Mind and [the] riches of its knowledge.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the chapter’s governing correspondence: outward relations and resources are temporary, then withdrawn and returned; purification precedes ascent into rest; illumination proceeds through Mind into fullness of knowledges. Exact particles around ¶3 are less constrained by surviving syntax, but the demanded sequence (certainty of separation → companions withdraw → they take back externals belonging to the temporary house) is recoverable with moderate confidence.

---

## Chapter 92: The Apostle is asked: Why when you drew every thing in the Picture (- Book), did you not draw the Purification of the Catechumens who shall be cleansed by Transmigration?

---

## Chapter 93: A Catechumen asked the Apostle: When I would give an Offering to the Saints, shall I inflict a Wound on the Alms?

**¶4** For this matter is like to two people, if a personal enemy and an opponent of theirs will hit one first and make a sore and a wound on his body. Afterwards he smites him again and [presses him] and he hits over his blows and [renews] blows that have made sores on him and [the] ⟨p.237⟩ strikes him these two times [the] accuses his enemy who has struck him. And this judge questions him to his face about the [and the injured one] of the sores that he has left as marks on his body. And they [quality] on him as if he had split his head. And he is led to judgement and sentence, and is punished before the judge of the world. He makes retribution for a sore in the sore place, and adds to him the wound that he had left on this person who had not yet sinned against him. He shall not omit the retaliation of the opponent, as he is judged [in this way] according to enmity.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The enemy strikes again and then continues the assault; the substrate needs a verb of furthering the injury before repeating ‘hits over his blows’.
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Corresponds to ‘renewing and intensifying’ the prior harm: he ‘hits over his blows’ and renews them.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Introduces the repeated blows as a definite set already mentioned.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Marks the sores as the specific standing marks left by the assault.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* The narrative requires the harmed party to become the subject who goes to judgement and makes the accusation before the judge.
> *¶4 #6 [strong]:* The judge examines not merely the fact but the nature and origin of the marks; ‘quality’ matches the correspondential emphasis on state.
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* Judgement is rendered according to the form of enmity; ‘in this way’ links the retaliation rule to the principle stated after it.

**¶5** After the end, this enemy is condemned for the retaliation [and for the] sin against him. Afterwards, the other one is sick [again] the one whom this enemy has struck, and a wound comes out on him [from the wound] and he becomes a sick person by cause of his illness, and he [seeks help] He calls the doctor and shows him his wound. However, that doctor has no [enmity] nor [retaliation] and he takes the knife to him and cuts this wound. Nevertheless, when he cuts it the blood shall pour out of his wound. That person shall be pained, but he accepts those and these things. This doctor has not retaliated nor was harm imposed on him; because he did this thing to him for healing his wound. All that he did to him was done for the good; and not directly for evil.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Condemnation is for retaliation and for establishing sin against the other; the lacuna likely joins the two causes.
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* The victim becomes sick afterward; ‘again’ signals a subsequent state after the first judgement sequence.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The disorder manifests from the opened injury; the wound ‘comes out on him’ from that source.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* In the spiritual sense he turns to one skilled in restoring; in the natural figure he ‘seeks help’ before calling the doctor.
> *¶5 #5 [strong]:* The doctor is distinguished by lacking hostile will; ‘no enmity’ matches the opposition between malice and healing use.
> *¶5 #6 [strong]:* Pairs naturally with ‘nor …’; the doctor has neither retaliatory intent nor harmful purpose.

**¶6** After the completion, he cuts this wound and lets blood from it and heals it. He shall even make [him] gifts and compliments. That illness and the healing become a pride for this doctor, and he is given honour.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* After healing, the gratitude and honour are directed to the doctor: ‘He shall even make him gifts…’.

**¶7** This is also how this matter of [healing] before this living soul. [It is] At the time ⟨p.238⟩ [when truth first approaches it,] it [seems as though] you have struck [it,] it is well and [afterwards] ease shall exist for it. [and] A glory comes about for this [rest and] healing [thereafter] it exists [work of] it is painful [for the moment, though]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* The matter is about corrective action that seems like harm but is restoration; ‘matter of healing’ fits the ensuing explanation.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the application: ‘It is at the time…’.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual logic demands the moment when ordering truth touches the disordered life, appearing as a blow.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Sets up the apparent injury: it ‘seems as though you have struck…’.
> *¶7 #5 [moderate]:* Object is the living soul/living principle spoken of in the paragraph.
> *¶7 #6 [strong]:* Rest and ease follow correction; the temporal sequence is essential in the correspondence.
> *¶7 #7 [strong]:* Joins ‘it is well’ to the resulting rest.
> *¶7 #8 [strong]:* Matches ‘ease shall exist for it’; rest is the correspondential outcome of order restored.
> *¶7 #9 [moderate]:* Links the immediate pain to the later settled state.
> *¶7 #10 [moderate]:* ‘Glory’ belongs to the use itself: the work of healing/restoration, not to assault.
> *¶7 #11 [moderate]:* Holds the paradox: glory and good end, yet present pain during separation and correction.

**¶8** Also, [this] is the other person [who] struck the comrade of the [enemy] sick with his [living soul, being] he struck in [cruelty;] he being cruel according to [envy] living soul [and he struck the] upon it [he laid hands] that [the] accusation of [enmity]

> *¶8 #3 [moderate]:* The ‘other person’ here is the opposite kind: not healer but hostile striker; ‘enemy’ keeps the correspondence with cruelty and contempt.
> *¶8 #4 [tentative]:* The comrade belongs to the community of life; the assault is ‘sick with his’ cruelty, i.e., a diseased will acting upon the living principle.
> *¶8 #5 [moderate]:* The motive is cruelty in the spiritual sense; the natural figure needs the named cause of the striking.
> *¶8 #6 [tentative]:* Envy is a common substrate form of hostile opposition to life in another; it fits ‘he being cruel according to …’.
> *¶8 #7 [tentative]:* Supplies the continued assault upon the living soul, matching the parallel with repeated striking earlier.
> *¶8 #8 [tentative]:* Natural idiom for violent action; spiritually it corresponds to willful assault rather than corrective incision.
> *¶8 #9 [moderate]:* Introduces the standing charge that follows.
> *¶8 #10 [strong]:* The conclusion is that the accusation stands because the act is from evil opposition, not healing use; ‘enmity’ names that ruling state.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored to a coherent oldest-layer comparison between hostile retaliation and corrective healing, because the correspondential structure tightly constrains sequence (assault → judgement → retribution; then disorder → physician → incision → bleeding → healing → honour; then the contrary case of cruelty). Exact connective particles and some naturalistic idioms in ¶7–¶8 remain less tightly constrained, so several fills there are tentative even though the underlying spiritual functions are clear.

## Chapter 94: Concerning the Purification of these Four Elements [that have been place]d in the Flesh.

**¶2** The four elements [are what is] purified. From the beginning they are holy ones, living ones, and enlighteners. However, when they undertood [the assault of the enemy] in that first struggle, the waste was [mixed] in with them, and the Darkness of the enemy was mixed with them. And when they were come into the body, stripping off these polluted garments, they set them in the flesh. Their pollutions, which they have stripped off, shall be displayed in a [lower place] and become a danger to the body.

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The clause defines the four elements by function: they are that which is purified (i.e., the purifiable principles). Keeping the impersonal substrate register.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially this is the first collision of understanding with invading evil; the substrate typically speaks of the ‘enemy’ and its ‘assault’ rather than moral exhortation.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The stripped pollutions are not annihilated but set for manifestation in a lower plane; ‘lower place’ keeps the cosmological, non-institutional register (rather than moralizing terms).

**¶3** The discharge of this pollution, that [the fir]e shall disgorge and place in the body, is [blo]od. The discharge of the blood is anger. The discharge of anger is the humours.

**¶4** ⟨p.240⟩
Also, the emissions that the waters discharge from them are lust. The discharge of lust is bitterness. The emission of bitterness is f[e]ver.

**¶5** The emission that light shall disgorge is flesh. The emission of flesh is gloom. [The] emission of gloom is obstinacy.

**¶6** The emissions that the tree shall disgorge, they are the voices of the winds of shame. The emission of winds of shame [is hardness of heart]

> *¶6 #1 [tentative]:* The ‘voices of the winds of shame’ are persuasive self-condemning impulses; their next discrete effect is a fixed internal binding, here rendered as ‘hardness of heart’ (obstinate closure), matching the chain-pattern of emissions producing a more fixed state.

**¶7** The emission of the [tear] is the [completion] of these twelve emissions.

> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* The tear corresponds to sorrow in the lower self; its emission is said to be the finishing-term of the twelve, so ‘completion’ fits the structural function better than a new symptom-name.

**¶8** [Afterwards] these four elements shall [be cleansed] what are gathered in are [f]ound by the management of this soul food [that] enters the body. When they enter the body, they are cleansed and purified and established [i]n their living image, which is the new man. They shall live [according to their order] and receive the Light Mind and be purified in their image; and they come forth, being cleansed and holy. They attain their first rest.

> *¶8 #1 [moderate]:* Marks the turn from the twelve corrupt outgoings to the re-ordering and purification cycle; a simple temporal pivot suits the impersonal substrate.
> *¶8 #2 [moderate]:* The sentence requires the stated end of the elements’ re-entry into the body: purification. ‘Be cleansed’ matches the repeated ‘cleansed and purified’ that follows.
> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires that the regenerated principles ‘live’ in a restored arrangement; ‘according to their order’ matches the management and establishing language and avoids later devotional phrasing.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are short and are recoverable by the chapter’s strict causal chain (element → emission → more fixed disorder) and by the structural pivot in ¶8 from defilement to re-ordering. Two places remain only moderately constrained: ¶2 #2 (the exact phrasing for the first conflict) and ¶6 #1 (the specific final effect of the shame-winds). The restorations there follow the correspondential demand for ‘enemy assault’ and for a hardening or binding terminal state.

---

## Chapter 95: The Apostle asks his Disciples: What is Cloud?

**¶2** Happen you know that it shall be released from five places and ascend over this great earth and [in the sea and in the rivers] and it is revealed and seen in the atmosphere between.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The release spreads over the whole natural degree: earth (solid), sea and rivers (waters) before it is ‘revealed… in the atmosphere between’. This keeps the oldest cosmological register of the zone: earth, waters, air.

**¶3** So, the cloud shall ascend from fire to the heavens towards the likeness of the Virgin of Light that she shall display to it. Its sign is the flashes that occur with lightning storms [and]ts exchange [in the cloud]. And they shall be purified by her towards [the place of the Virgin of Light] the light that she shall reveal [to it]. The rulers shall be released by the lightning storms and they are freed and sent.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* Completes the clause: ‘storms and its exchange…’ i.e., lightning storms and their reciprocal disturbance.
> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* The ‘exchange’ belongs to the cloud’s internal agitation under the flashes; matches the spiritual sense ‘reciprocal disturbances’.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Purification is ‘by her’ and therefore directed ‘towards’ her own station, the manifest form of Wisdom in the intermediate region.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* She reveals the light ‘to it’ (the cloud and the lives in it). Tight syntactic demand.

**¶4** However, [these] angels shall immediately be summoned to them, so that they will seize them, because they know that they shall never do good. Every place they will touch they shall make dead and a loss. Whenever the angels will make towards them and come beside [them], they shall flee in their constraint before them. They go into whatever they will meet upon and assume it, like a robber being abandoned and fleeing before one stronger than him, as he runs after him to arrest him.

> *¶4 #2 [strong]:* The angels ‘come beside’ the rulers; the rulers then flee ‘before them’ (the angels).

**¶5** Now, those rulers that shall be freed from these clouds and assume whatever they will meet upon [and enter] into the [trees] that they did not [find the way,] will seize this thing, whether indeed a tree to which they have found the way, or animals, or people. Simply, every place that they will assume they shall burn and destroy by the fire of their body. Namely, the rulers, [the] children of fire, they that shall be stripped of the fire.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The rulers ‘assume whatever they meet upon’ and then ‘enter into’ the next host-form named by the lacuna.
> *¶5 #2 [strong]:* Immediate context below lists ‘whether indeed a tree… or animals, or people’. The plural general heading fits: ‘enter into the trees’.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* Makes the ensuing relative clause cohere: ‘into the trees that they did not find the way, will seize… whether indeed a tree to which they have found the way…’ i.e., ranging until they find an entry. Exact wording is constrained but not certain.

**¶6** In contrast, [the] cloud that shall be raised up from water, and comes above towards the Virgin of Light; its sign is the thunder storms and the booms that shall come in the atmosphere in various forms and are heard. [And] Again, there are times if the rulers that belong to them, which exist in the cloud, shall on occasion be constrained [in a] water [clou]d. And they flee from it and abandon it.

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* A connective is required after the description of the sign before ‘Again, there are times…’.

**¶7** Now, [they] too are so, in that every place that they will [reach], they shall make frost and hail and snow. [They rui]n the seed-corn and the fruits and the plants; and they [waste] every place [that] they will reach.

> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Carries the same devastation formula as elsewhere (‘burn and destroy’, ‘wreak destruction’): here in the opposite-thermal sense, chilling and barrenness, so ‘waste’ fits the correspondential effect.

**¶8** Again, [ang]els shall be summoned to them who seize them. Still, while the angels [ru]sh after them in order to seize them, they shall make this destruction.

**¶9** Also, [the cl]oud that will be raised up from wind ascends to the atmosphere between, towards the likeness of the Virgin that she shall display to it. Its sign is [the s]torm wind that shall blow with bitterness.

**¶10** So, in those winds and tempests shall the lives be purified. Again, the rulers will be stripped of the cloud of wind and they descend from it, safe in their image. All the rivers and the seas that they will reach and arrive at, they shall make waves in them and raise [billows] and they [overturn] the ships. They wreak destruction [in the seas] and they seize and bring them [downwards].

> *¶10 #1 [strong]:* Wind-rulers agitate waters: ‘make waves… and raise billows’. This matches the spiritual sense ‘raise them into commotion’.
> *¶10 #2 [moderate]:* Opposite of ‘carry’: they overturn ships, matching ‘overturn what carries the mind in outward uses and doctrines’.
> *¶10 #3 [moderate]:* Keeps the natural vessel-image aligned: destruction occurs upon the waters where ships are.
> *¶10 #4 [moderate]:* They ‘seize and bring them…’ i.e., drag down what they can, per spiritual sense.

**¶11** Also the cloud, the one of light, immediately they will be stripped of it [before] the Virgin. She shall display its sign too [as well]. And its sign is this, in that you shall find it sailing in stillness and calm. The lives in it shall be cleansed in stillness and calm. Again, the rulers that will be stripped of it [in the atmosphere] that will come forth [are those] they shall put [from it,] and they take [upon] there [whatever they will meet upon;] the angels seize them.

> *¶11 #1 [strong]:* ‘Stripped of it before the Virgin’ is the fixed cosmological motif: approach to her likeness dissolves the cloud-covering.
> *¶11 #2 [moderate]:* Balances ‘She shall display its sign too…’.
> *¶11 #3 [moderate]:* The ‘sailing’ sign is observed in the intermediate region, consistent with the chapter’s repeated ‘in the atmosphere between’.
> *¶11 #4 [tentative]:* Introduces the next clause about the rulers: ‘Again, the rulers… are those that will come forth…’.
> *¶11 #5 [strong]:* Completes the emergence: rulers stripped of the light-cloud ‘come forth from it’.
> *¶11 #6 [moderate]:* They ‘put upon’ and ‘take’—language of assuming a host-form/disposition.
> *¶11 #7 [strong]:* Matches the repeated formula in ¶5 and ¶4: they seize any encountered form to inhabit.

**¶12** Again, the cloud that shall be raised up from the mingled air comes above towards [the image] of the Virgin. Its sign is this, in that [the lives] are purified in it in stillness and [repose. Again,] that will be stripped of that cloud and come from it; every place that they will find [upon it,] they shall make it [dead] one of [another] until [the angels come upon them] they are seized and taken to their midst.

> *¶12 #2 [strong]:* The sign concerns purification: ‘the lives are purified in it in stillness…’.
> *¶12 #3 [strong]:* Carries the twin term ‘stillness and repose’ from the spiritual sense; then turns to the rulers.
> *¶12 #4 [moderate]:* ‘Every place that they will find upon it’—i.e., in the outer domain they can reach after stripping.
> *¶12 #5 [moderate]:* Corresponds to profanation and damage in the outer life: making regions ‘dead’ is the chapter’s established effect-language (¶4).
> *¶12 #6 [moderate]:* Prepares ‘one of … until …’: successive modes of harm.
> *¶12 #7 [moderate]:* Required to close the sequence before ‘until … they are seized’: the seizing is by angels throughout the chapter.

**¶13** Their strippings, that they shall leave behind them in various places they will have reached, whether the produce of the trees or the offspring of the fleshes, they shall be taken midst [their place, and they] wither away. They shall inflict great dangers, but again those rulers also the angels shall send to their midst. They bring the light from them and cast the demons down to these seven outer ditches of the great sea; which is the vessel of the waters that are outside the world.
