> *¶13 #1 [tentative]:* The remnants are ‘taken midst … wither away’: i.e., into the rulers’ own domain; then they wither. The connective restores flow.

**¶15** Those ditches and the [descents continue] until they reach the earths below [the] feet of the Porter.

> *¶15 #1 [strong]:* Spiritual sense: ‘These prisons and their descents continue…’. Keeps impersonal cosmological description.
> *¶15 #2 [strong]:* Needed to complete ‘below the feet of the Porter’.

**¶16** Hear also [again,] for sometimes these angels shall be sent to them, to seize the rulers. Whenever the rulers [flee] for the angels rush after them. They beseech them [to let them go,] they that are given the command. However, [t]hose [rul]ers shall be freed from them alone and assume [tree] and [the fle]sh. They are consumed by this offspring [of] tree and that of flesh. The angels shall set a [guard] over them, that they might guard them until the hour when [those r]ulers will be stripped of tree and [flesh; a]nd they go up above. They shall be gathered [together] above, and they are poured out [again] in their first for[ms and their first im]ages in which they first were. They take the light from them, which had remained behind in them. And they too are cast down to these seven outer [path]s that are in the great sea; the place to where these former ones [were c as]t. They shall reach their midst [again,] and the light that is in them is taken.

> *¶16 #1 [moderate]:* Resumes the instruction: ‘Hear also again…’.
> *¶16 #2 [moderate]:* When pursued they flee; then ‘the angels rush after them’. Matches the chase motif in ¶4.
> *¶16 #3 [moderate]:* They ‘beseech’ the angels/commanded powers to release them. Fits spiritual sense ‘plead and attempt to avert the restraint’.
> *¶16 #6 [strong]:* Oldest-layer concrete host-forms: ‘assume tree and the flesh’.
> *¶16 #8 [strong]:* ‘offspring of tree and that of flesh’.
> *¶16 #9 [strong]:* Directly required by ‘set a … over them, that they might guard them’.
> *¶16 #12 [moderate]:* ‘They shall be gathered together above’—standard collecting formula before judgment/stripping.
> *¶16 #13 [moderate]:* ‘poured out again’ into first forms: return to original states before final casting-down.
> *¶16 #14 [moderate]:* Restores ‘first forms… images in which they first were’ in the natural layer’s paired terms (form and image).
> *¶16 #17 [moderate]:* ‘They shall reach their midst again’ completes the repetitive cycle motif and matches the spiritual sense ‘They enter into their midst’.

**¶17** Behold now, I have instructed you about this cloud: How it shall ascend above, and the lives that are in it are purified. I have also taught you of the rulers that are in it: how they shall make these {rebellions} and how they shall be {caught}. And they are cast to the outer prison by the angels, when the command is given to them through the power of the Virgin of Light. She has authority over the entire zone, and cleanses the life that is in it.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are recoverable because the chapter is highly formulaic: each cloud-type follows the same correspondential sequence (ascent toward the Virgin, sign, purification of lives, stripping and descent of rulers, devastation in ultimates, angelic seizure, casting to outer prisons). Where the damaged spots sit inside these repeated templates, restorations are tight. A few places (notably ¶5 #3, ¶11 #4, ¶13 #1, ¶12 #7) remain only moderately or tentatively constrained because multiple synonymous connective phrases could fit without changing the spiritual structure.

---

## Chapter 96: The Three Earths that Exist, they bear Fruit.

**¶1** Three earths exist in the universe, and are being toiled over. At first they [resist] the farmer in them. Afterwards they bear [true] fruit to they who toil over them.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The three ‘earths’ are discrete degrees. In the beginning each degree is unreceptive and opposes formative influx; thus the soil ‘resists’ the farmer’s work before it is opened.
> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* Change from editor’s ‘good’. In the oldest cosmological register the first yield of an opened degree is not moral goodness but the right product of order, i.e., what is genuine and according to form. Correspondentially, after resistance ceases it bears ‘true fruit’ for the operators of use.

**¶2** One of them is this great earth that people are living upon. They are ploughing in it [digging], it with iron hoes, they cultivate, [cutting] the furrows, turning it over [in order. Afterwards they harvest fruit from it] Afterwards they harvest fruit from [it ...

> *¶2 #1 [strong]:* The outer earth is worked by hard implements (iron hoes): this corresponds to firm truths acting on stubborn natural states. The missing participle should name the heavy breaking-up action preceding cultivation.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Change from editor’s ‘making’. The sequence names distinct operations: striking with iron, then forming the furrows. ‘Cutting the furrows’ matches the correspondential idea of truth dividing and arranging the compacted natural plane into order.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* The gap completes the cultivation sequence with an ordering result, then resumes the stated outcome (harvest). Correspondentially, once the outer plane is arranged into order it can yield effects (‘fruit’) from that impressed order.

**¶3** ⟨p.245⟩
The second field is the living soul, the holy one. It is mixed in the entire zone, above and below. The farmers who toil over it are these two great light-givers, which journey through the heavens. They are toiling and cultivating it, so that they will draw it towards their aspect. And it leaps up and comes to the heights. It goes upwards, out from every place wherein it is, and it adds to them.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are tightly constrained by the three-degree schema: initial resistance, then receptivity and yield; and the natural-field operations corresponding to the ordering action of firm truths. Exact verbal choices in ¶2 #3 remain the least constrained, but the required sense is clear: the work results in an ordered state, then fruit is gathered.

---

## Chapter 97: Concerning the Three Creations of the Flesh: the ones that were brought forth [...]; and the ones that were begotten by the Abortions; and the ones that were formed of themselves.

**¶2** “All these animals that you see, and also the rest of the [crea]tures [creatures] of the earth, confirm them in three likeness[es]: some from [three archetypes] abortions. Others, however, form their own selves in their shape [miscarriages].

> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* The outer-degree register here is the full formed set in the natural: “animals … and the rest of the creatures of the earth”. The spiritual sense speaks of “every other formed quality in the outer degree,” which in the substrate is stated as “creatures of the earth.”
> *¶2 #4 [strong]:* The spiritual translation names “the three universal patterns of life.” In the oldest substrate this is regularly “archetypes,” i.e., the three degrees as universals stamped into ultimates.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Matches “abortions” already present: some formations come from disordered beginnings (influx not rightly received), hence ‘miscarriages’ as the substrate term for distorted birth of forms.

**¶3** “Also, their nourishment is thre[e] light in them, if they shall wish [by the wisdom]. [But if they do not wish] they eat see[ds and] fr[uit]; they eat seeds and fruit. The [also] it entirely, and they [swallow] the three archetyp[es] from their nature and the [remain] the time when they are on the ea[rth]; they are seized [and while they are there] in their [by the lusts]. The Ambassador [members] all powers [of Light is established over].

> *¶3 #2 [moderate]:* Correspondentially this is nourishment from within: ‘light in them’ = interior truth and intelligence. Thus ‘by the wisdom’ fits the clause ‘light in them, if they shall wish’.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense explicitly contrasts willingness to receive wisdom vs. taking only lower things in potency and outwardly pleasing works.
> *¶3 #5 [strong]:* Completes “fruit”; the continuation is a repetition in the text, so only the missing letters are required.
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* Balances the two foods: seeds and fruit as two outward forms of intake (truth in potency and pleasant works).
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: they “appropriate these things into themselves.” Natural correspondence: ‘eat’ and ‘swallow’ = appropriation into life.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: “they remain within those patterns during their state in the outer degree.”
> *¶3 #11 [moderate]:* Bridges to the next clause about being seized in their own impulses while in the outer state (“on the earth”).
> *¶3 #12 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: “taken hold of by their own ruling impulses.” Oldest-layer diction often uses “lusts” for dominant loves in the opposite sense.
> *¶3 #13 [moderate]:* Natural correspondence for operative faculties in ultimates: ‘members’. Fits “they are seized … in their …”.
> *¶3 #14 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense identifies “the directing influx … gathers the operative abilities.” In the Kephalaia register this is phrased as the Ambassador (sent-out directing principle) setting order “over all powers.”

**¶4** “[N] it [ow when it was ordered], first [the first planting]. ⟨p.247⟩ Plant them generally to this name: shoots and grass. It formed the bad, bitter trees, the reeds and the thorns and the [briers] and these others that are like them.

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* Introduces the first ordering of outer growth, matching the spiritual sense: “Then, in the first ordering of these outer things…”.
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Keeps the impersonal cosmological tone: the first establishment of rudiments in the natural degree.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* Completes the list of harmful growths with a standard correspondent of prickly defensive falsity, aligning with “prickly defenses of selfhood.”

**¶5** “For that thought belongs to the flesh-eating rulers. Still, these [that were] planted (belong to) grass and gourds, since they are in their essence. Nevertheless, [this t]ho[ugh]t that came down from the rulers, as they are in fruit and grass first and shall not be planted in flesh and blood, it formed the trees that shall give nourishing fru[it], as their gourds are sweet.

**¶6** “Again, this thou[ght] that came down from the powers that eat m[ea]t and also eat grass and gourds, it has formed the trees that shall not produce any nourishment. Their fruits belong neither to good nor evil.”

**Assessment:** Most gaps are recoverable because the chapter is a tight correspondential schema: three degrees, two modes of reception (willing vs unwilling), and three vegetal outcomes (harmful, nourishing, non-nourishing) traced to distinct descending ‘thoughts’ from ruling powers. The smallest lacunae are certain. Several mid-sized restorations (¶3 #2–3, #7, #12–14; ¶4 #2–4) are constrained by the spiritual logic but remain somewhat uncertain in exact surface wording; they are restored in the oldest-layer impersonal cosmological register rather than later moral or devotional phrasing.

---

## Chapter 98: What is Virginal; or, otherwise, what is Continent?

**¶3** What is called ‘virginal’ is assigned to the mystery of the aeons of greatness. And they called it ‘virginal’ by the category of all the light that did not [taste] of death, nor come to the enemy’s war.

**¶4** However, what is called ‘continent’ occurs to the mystery of the light that came and was mixed with the darkness. It was defiled in bodies [in the garments of the flesh] and went up to the light. [They] called [it ‘co]ntinent’.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate contrast is between unmixed Light and Light that entered mixture and was defiled in bodily forms. The needed correspondence is external vestures that receive defilement: ‘garments’ as ultimate forms enclosing the Light in bodies.

**¶5** Furthermore, the holy vir[gin]als [when they stand in their purity] should they come out against the en[emy and shall ... not] taste death, nor [be overcome by him].

> *¶5 #2 [tentative]:* This line resumes the category ‘virginal’ as Light untouched by death/war. The spiritual sense requires a state-description (standing in purity) before ‘come out against the enemy’.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* The clause must express opposition to the Enemy with the invariant result ‘not taste death’. The missing matter is a connective prediction (‘shall’) rather than moral exhortation, keeping the impersonal cosmological register.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* In correspondential terms: resisting hell without being mastered by it. This complements ‘not taste death’ as the second effect of virginal Light.

**¶6** ⟨p.249⟩ On the other hand, what is called ‘continent’ [is this li]ght that mixed with the darkness, was purif[ie]d, came [up f]rom all the creatures, and was confirmed in the great[ness]. They called it ‘continent’ since it tasted [of the l]ust; but afterwards was cleansed from it and [become an] holy one.

**¶7** Furthermore, what is called [‘vi]rginal’ is this light that was purified by the ima[ge] of the [A]mbassador. It wen[t up and] attained [the] he[ights. It was] confirmed in the image of the gods.

**¶8** Again, what is [called] ‘continent’ is the remainder of this light th[at] remained behind, from what was cleansed. It goes up and comes down in transmigration. Al[so], there is a part [of] it that shall come and attain the form of [hum]an [fl]esh. Again, that one too shall be chosen in the flesh, [accept] the hope, and become an holy one. And they name it ‘continent’.

**¶9** Furthermore, what is named ‘virginal’ is the Light Mind, should he come and assume the images [of] the ele[ct]. However, on the other hand, what is called ‘contin[ent]’ is the new man, should it be purified from this old man. And it is cleansed and strips off the Sin that is compounded with it, and it becomes a continent one.

**¶10** And also, what is called ‘virgi[n]al’ in the flesh, is a man if he has [ne]ver joined himself to woman, has not been defiled by intercourse. However, [i]n contrast, what is called ‘continent’, is the man who has a wom[a]n in the world. Afterwards, he cleanses himself from her [and] renounces her. And because of this he [turns back from her] and he becomes an holy contin[en]t one.

> *¶10 #6 [moderate]:* The substrate sense is a change of state: separation of the man’s Light from the defiling conjunction. ‘Turns back’ matches the correspondential act of reversal from lust toward purification, without importing later penitential diction.

**¶11** Behold, I have taught you the categories [of] the v[ir]ginal and the continent in a great many [as]pects. For what is virginal, or otherwise what is [con]tinent, is in many forms.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are trivial letter-restorations tightly constrained by surrounding words. Three gaps required semantic reconstruction (¶4 #1, ¶5 #2–4, ¶10 #6). These were restored by the chapter’s fixed correspondence: ‘virginal’ = Light never entering mixture; ‘continent’ = Light that entered mixture, was defiled in bodily ultimates, then purified and separated. The reconstructions keep an impersonal cosmological register and avoid later institutional or devotional phrasing; confidence is moderate to tentative where multiple Coptic turnings are possible though the spiritual function is clear.

---

## Chapter 99: Concerning Transmigration

**¶5** For [he established] ⟨p.251⟩ his [thro]ne in the light, which [and set]hich he had also uttered [as a word of command.] When that one will be muzzled, they shall [be cut off] cut limb [from them,] there is [s]till a [measure of order]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate sense here is the impersonal setting of order: the Divine sets a governing arrangement before mention of the throne. This matches the spiritual context ‘established governing order within wisdom’ and avoids later exhortatory tone.
> *¶5 #3 [tentative]:* The clause must express the fixing of authority in the Light. ‘And set’ gives the required governing placement that the spiritual sense calls ‘holding authority in clear discernment’. The surviving final ‘w’ is satisfied by the following ‘which’.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* The context says what was inwardly willed was ‘uttered’ into determinate expression. In the oldest register this is not preaching but decree or command: a word that structures the lower. This fits immediately after ‘he had also uttered’.
> *¶5 #5 [moderate]:* After ‘When that one will be muzzled’ the dependent allies lose function. ‘Be cut off’ corresponds to separation of supporting powers from their head, matching the spiritual sense ‘allied powers that depend on it begin to fail’.
> *¶5 #6 [tentative]:* The next phrase ‘cut limb ...’ implies dismemberment from a body. ‘From them’ supplies the needed relational break: coherent action is broken into fragments, limb from limb.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* The closing needs a remainder within the interior that can still receive restoration. In the oldest cosmological idiom this is a remaining ‘measure’ or ‘portion’ of order within the mixture, consistent with the spiritual sense ‘something remains… that can still receive further ordering’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the correspondential sequence: establishment of governing order, authoritative setting in Light, decree as utterance, then restraint of dominant falsity leading to severing and dismemberment, yet a remaining measure receptive to re-ordering. Exact connective particles and some brief phrases remain tentative due to minimal surviving natural-sense syntax.

---

## Chapter 100: Concerning the Dragon with Fourteen Heads; what it is and [...]

**¶2** There is never a dragon in the flesh with fourteen heads, as the Magi say! Rather, this is a spiritual lesson. It was uttered in virginity [in a state of purity] the Magi did not understand [because they remained in outward knowing] all their words that are written [spiritual]tual (?).

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate contrast is between a virginal receptivity (unmixed, unappropriated influx) and the Magi’s externalizing literalism. “State of purity” keeps the impersonal cosmological register while matching the spiritual sense: “untouched reception of influx.”
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation requires the reason for non-understanding: fixation in externals. This is stated as a condition of cognition, not a moral rebuke, consistent with the oldest layer.
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The surviving letters “spiri” constrain this strongly; the sense demands that the written words are about spiritual realities, not a corporeal monster.

**¶3** Again, this is how this earth [is likened] to it, as it is accounted a corporeal dragon [and a great deceit is joined] I will instruct you [about this] the dragon is this doctrine [of life] of the flesh [that is from the self] also the fourteen heads that are distributed over the dragon are these.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The sentence needs a verb of comparison: the earth, taken in the natural sense, “is likened to it” (to a dragon) as a correspondential lesson.
> *¶3 #2 [tentative]:* The spiritual context: the natural degree taken as the whole is “counted” a persuasive ruling falsity. “Deceit joined” conveys the persuasive falsity attached to the natural appearance.
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* Introduces the didactic turn (“I will instruct you about this”), matching the impersonal expository style without adding Mani’s dialogic framing.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* The dragon is identified as “this doctrine”; the spiritual sense specifies it is a doctrine of life from the self-sense, not a mere opinion.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Completes “of the flesh” in the substrate register: “flesh” corresponds to proprium, self-derived life. This keeps the impersonal cosmological causality.

**¶4** The seven sense organs on the head of the body are the two of sight by which one sees; the two of sound by which one hears; the two of scent by which one smells; also the tongue [which is in the mouth] by which one selects, receiving there the different tastes that occur in every form.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The list of seven upper ‘heads’ ends with the tongue; specifying its placement (“in the mouth”) fits the natural description while preserving the correspondence (sensuous discrimination).

**¶5** These are the seven heads of the dragon, the seven sense organs that are on the upper part of the person; [and the seven are] below. The seven of the torso are these: two [of drawing in and expelling]; the two [of moving and acting] ruler [in the midst, the] the seven heads that are above; so with the ones below they total and make fourteen heads of the dragon.

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* Transitions from the seven above to the seven below: an enumerative connective is demanded by structure (“These are…; and the seven… below”).
> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual sense: “twofold power of drawing in and expelling as to the life of the natural,” corresponding naturally to inhalation and exhalation.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* Matches the second lower pair in the spiritual sense: “moving and acting as to its strength,” corresponding naturally to locomotion and manual action.
> *¶5 #4 [moderate]:* Introduces the “central governing power” (the ruler) as midmost/presiding, consistent with the correspondential structure: a single governor over the lower domain.

**¶6** ⟨p.253⟩
Conversely, the five dens about which they have spoken, where the dragon is congregated and protrudes, are these: the first is the tongue; the second is the lungs; the third is the heart; the fourth is the spleen; the fifth is the blood that dwells in them. Again, if it shall look out from them [and reveal its likeness and its observation above and below, it wages war by] its fourteen heads.

> *¶6 #1 [strong]:* Kept as given. It expresses the correspondential mechanism: from interior ‘dens’ it looks out, manifests in higher and lower functions, and then combats by the full set of agencies (fourteen heads).

**¶7** Whoever will recognise them with the dragon, which is the thought of the body, and struggles with it and is victorious and kills it in them [and uproots it from his members.] He receives the victory without suffering on the day of his coming forth.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The required outcome is not merely killing a creature but ending dominion within the embodied faculties. “Uproots… from his members” matches the spiritual sense: “until its dominion in him is ended,” in an impersonal, structural idiom.

**¶8** So, behold, I have taught you of the dragon that exists with its fourteen heads and five dens, wherein it is concealed and hidden. It does through them [its desire to rule]ire all the time.

> *¶8 #1 [strong]:* The spiritual translation ends with “craving to rule.” Restoring “desire to rule” fits the visible fragment “des” and keeps the oldest-layer cosmological focus on dominion rather than moral passion.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are structurally constrained by the correspondential mapping already present: seven upper inlets, seven lower executors, five interior seats, then outward manifestation and combat. Exact physiological naming for the ‘seven of the torso’ is less tight in the surviving natural text, so those restorations are moderate and expressed in functional (correspondential) terms rather than later institutional or devotional language.

---

## Chapter 101: [Concer]ning why, if the Person shall look down into Water, [ ... ]

**¶1** instruct me about this lesson: When the person is above water and looking down [into the wat]ers, if he shall see his face reflected why shall you find his head turned upside down and also his feet upwards? Yet, when he stares down into the waters, his face shall not be visible, nor can he see it there?

**¶3** [And] this: The face of people and the shape of trees shall appear turned upside down in the water as this is signified in [the] mystery of the summons, when it was sent down to the worlds of darkness towards the First Man. Sin[ce] in this way it cast itself down [to the worlds of darkness] like a person [who] plunges headlong into water; so it is also with the summons. It cast itself headlong to the worlds below, with strength and diligence.

> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Completes “Sin[ce ...]” as “Since”, which matches the causal turn in the correspondential sense: the summons appears inverted because it was sent down and therefore ‘cast itself down’ into the lower degree.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* The spiritual translation requires the incitement or summons to descend into states of falsity and darkness to meet the First Man; the natural-layer phrasing in this substrate regularly names that target explicitly as “the worlds of darkness.”

**¶4** And this too: Shall the person lift his head up from the water, his face shall not be visible to him, being bent below. This characterises the mystery of the obedience that ascended from below, from with the First Man his father to the Living Spirit. This is a spiritual mystery that I have [revea]led to you so that you would understand it.

**¶5** If you wish to understand another corporeal mystery on this subject, listen and I may teach you to understand it. The face of people and of animals and of trees is visible in the water, hanging upside down. This occurs to the mystery of the stars and the zodiac, which hang upside down and are visible in the great sea. For the face of people and beasts and all trees hang on the root of the stars and the zodiac, being begotten from them. Just as their fathers, who are spread out above, hang upside down; so it is also that the mystery of their fathers is being revealed to them whenever they stare down into the water. This is, that you shall find their shapes inverted.

**¶6** ⟨p.255⟩
Not only this, but [the] child who is born; as the ho[ur] approaches for him to be born, he hangs upside down and is born head first. Indeed, due to this, his mystery shall be revealed in these waters people stare down into.

**¶7** Furthermore, I reveal something else to you: Behold, you will find the shadow of the person who walks along is joined to the earth all the time. If the person is troubled [in his mind] his shadow up upon the earth [becomes strong] above. It is impossible for him to do [anything good] on the earth. See, this mystery is [a] great sign, signifying that the entire body came from the earth and had ascended from the abyss. Again, due to this, its shadow is joined on the earth, turned downwards, and hanging from above.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* The correspondential sense speaks of being “troubled and disordered,” i.e., an inner disturbance; the oldest-layer register expresses this as trouble occurring ‘in his mind,’ which then changes how the shadow (mere appearance) dominates the lowest level.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Needed to match the spiritual sense: when disturbed, the appearance becomes more dominant upon the lowest plane. “Becomes strong” keeps the impersonal, structural register and links naturally with “up upon the earth … above.”
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense constrains the result: he “cannot act rightly in outer life.” In the oldest substrate this is commonly cast as inability to do “anything good” upon the earth (the lowest degree).

**Assessment:** Most gaps are either trivial letter-restorations or tightly constrained by the descent-as-inversion correspondence governing the whole chapter (summons cast down; obedience rising; higher causes appearing inverted in lower truth). The three substantial restorations in ¶7 are moderately constrained by the shadow-correspondence: inner trouble increases the dominance of mere appearance on the lowest plane, yielding inability to do good in outward life; exact wording remains somewhat flexible but the required relations are fixed.

---

## Chapter 102: Concerning the Light Mind, why it does not exercise Foreknowledge for the Saint as for the Apostle?

**¶2** Happen you know that the Light Mind, who dwells in the elect, has the power to perform all these wonders among the faithful, all these signs that you have recounted to me; but the elect are not able to persist in the greatness that may be unveiled to them.

---

## Chapter 103: Concerning the Five Wonders that the Light Mind shall display in the Elect

**¶1** The Light Mind shall enact five light signs in the elect.

**¶7** The Light Mind puts these five signs in the new man, [whom he has] purified, cleansed, supported and chosen [from all mixture with Darkness]

> *¶7 #1 [strong]:* Accept the existing restoration. In the substrate register the Light Mind (Divine understanding) first forms the ‘new man’ by prior acts of separation: purification and cleansing. The relative clause marks that the subject acted upon is already made receptive before the five signs are implanted.
> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* The sequence ‘purified, cleansed, supported and chosen’ demands an additional clause that names what is being removed in the oldest cosmological logic: not moral guilt or ecclesial status, but admixture. In the Persian correspondential substrate, purification and cleansing are the unmingling of Light from Darkness; support is sustaining the separated Light by influx; choice is distinction for conjunction. Thus the missing object is the remaining ‘mixture with Darkness’ from which the new man is separated before the signs are set within him.

**Assessment:** One lacuna is an editor’s secure small restoration. The larger gap is constrained by the correspondential sequence: separation (purify, cleanse) must specify removal of admixture with Darkness prior to implantation of the five signs. Exact wording is not recoverable, but the demanded cosmological content is tight.

---

## Chapter 104: Concerning Food: It shall be allocated to Five Products of the human Body.

**¶2** This sustenance of various kinds that people gather in and eat, and enters the body; it shall be distributed to five productions.

**¶3** The first product is this that shall issue from the person in rapture and rises up in the Mind, and it comes out from all his limbs, and is immeasurable.

**¶4** The second is this that shall issue from the person in voice and word.

**¶5** The third is this that shall spring from them in strength and activity.

**¶6** The fourth is what is engendered by the pleasure of lu[s]t in men and women.

**¶7** The fifth is this that shall be formed and sculpted in the flesh, and be engendered and come from them. It is this corporeal creature; only its parents can recognise this creature that they beget. They are attentive to its desire and its thought and its [lo]ve every day, all the time. However, these other four forms they neither notice nor are concerned about; because they are not displayed.

**Assessment:** Both lacunae are trivial mid-word restorations tightly fixed by the surrounding text: ¶6 requires the single letter to complete “lust,” and ¶7 requires the two letters to complete “love.” No alternative readings fit the correspondential structure or the natural phrasing.

---

## Chapter 105: Concerning the Three Things that are great with Mankind, as they are running all the time, in that they [ ... ]

---

## Chapter 106: There is no Joy that shall remain in the World till the End.

**¶1** There is not [o]ne person [existing in] the world, standing firm [i]n mixture, for whom joy remains to the end.

> *¶1 #2 [strong]:* The substrate point is universal: no embodied subject in the created order (the mixed domain) has unbroken joy while mixture persists. The phrase marks being-in-the-world as being in the mixed state, not a moral condition.

**¶2** Not only people, even the sun and the moon, the light-givers of the heavens, [are set in their courses] nor are they oppressed, they know not [to be set]et firm in a summons and an [obe]dience. Nevertheless, they too, they do not rejoice completely.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The light-givers function as ordered governors; the oldest-layer register tends to describe them as ‘set’ or ‘appointed’ in a fixed course, not as morally struggling. This matches the spiritual sense: maintained in appointed order without oppression.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The contrast is: they are not oppressed, and they do not ‘know’ how to resist being fixed in their assigned ordinance. The surviving final ‘s’ fits the infinitival construction (‘to be set’).

**¶3** So, one: Their eyes stare at their enemy, they [perceiv]e him and also look at his ugly shapes. They [s]ee him, that he is alive and established and is king above and below, in secret and manifest. He does every thing that he wants according to his wil[l].

> *¶3 #3 [strong]:* Completes ‘will’. The enemy acts from its own love as will.

**¶4** [The s]econd: They perceive the living soul, they see [i]t and that it is ensnared and set in a great [fetter] above and below, in the tree and in the flesh, [bound] with every oppression. It is being pressed, drawn near to [and] sliced and eaten as it comes up and down; from above below and from below above. It [is] despoiled and moved from body to body.

> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* After ‘in the tree and in the flesh’ the substrate typically adds a binding term to restate the constraint across inner and outer forms. This supports the spiritual sense of continual compulsion across levels.

**¶5** Now, because of their living soul that is loved by them and honoured of them, when they see it in oppression th[ey can] not [re]joice completely.

**¶6** So, one about the soul; and one about their enemy whom they see ruling [in] what is not his own. He is proud and satisfied with a wealth that is not his own. Indeed, due to this, they do not rejoice in total; but bear up and swallow their heart until they cleanse the living soul and take it from the enemy's hands. Then they obliterate the death that is their enemy and they gather it in [and shut it up in the lump] for ever and ever.

> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* Adjusted from the editor’s ‘fetter’ to ‘shut up’: the spiritual sense here is not merely binding but confinement so it can no longer go forth into operation. ‘Lump’ (the final compact mass) is retained as the oldest-layer cosmological image of the inert aggregate at the end.

**¶7** When they will chain their enemy in the bond at the [las]t, then they will rejoice and no longer grieve from this t[ime]. Also, they become the very same as they were from the [start] for ever!

**Assessment:** Most gaps are either single-letter or already tightly constrained by the chapter’s twofold structure (grief for the bound soul; grief for the reigning enemy). The only freer restorations are in ¶2 and ¶4 where the cosmological-register phrasing must express appointed order and constraint; and ¶6 where the end-state requires not just binding but final confinement of Death’s impulse into the inert ‘lump'.

---

## Chapter 107: Concerning the Form of the Word, that [ ... 30 ... ]

**¶1** [The mouth takes it and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips gather it!] and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips g[at]her it! The word shall come forth through the power of th[ese five] members and be heard outside.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence is a correspondential inventory of five operative instruments by which what is interior becomes utterance in ultimates. The missing opening must name the first member that receives and contains what will be articulated, i.e., the mouth, completing the fivefold series that culminates in hearing outside.

**¶2** Simila[rly] the coin: One shall pour it out and an [othe]r beat it and another trim it as it is turned, and a[nother] put the stamp on it and another wipe it in the sieve (?). [Behold], these five craftsmen shall shape and beautify [their] coin, and it comes amongst mankind. It becomes a posse[ssion] to be received and given.

**¶3** This is also the case with the [wor]d, as it is formed and embellished by five [member]s. It comes forth and is heard by the ears [of] others.

**Assessment:** Nearly all gaps are trivial mid-word restorations with fixed readings. The only substantive lacuna (¶1 #1) is constrained by the correspondential fivefold anatomy of utterance; restoring “The mouth takes it” completes the series in the impersonal, technical register of the oldest substrate.

---

## Chapter 108: Concerning the Seed Grain that shall be formed by the Elements, and also be destroyed by them

**¶2** “This seed grain and this barley grain that you see shall be formed and [be gathered in] and beautified by the five elements. Now, the warmth and the cold nourish the seed grain and the entire tree. So, just as these things nourish it [and increase, and just as they strengthen it] it is destroyed [when they withdraw from it] through [the lack of nourishment] hunger.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The sequence “formed … and beautified by the five elements” requires an intermediate act of collecting and arranging prior to adornment. In the substrate register, the elements first receive, collect, and compact what is potential (seed) into a stable outer form before it is ‘beautified’ (perfected in its own degree).
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* Warmth and cold here are the alternating states that both nourish and (by right order) cause growth. The parallelism demands a second clause matching “nourish” with a further effect (“increase” or “strengthen”), preparing for the reversal in the next lacunae (what can nourish can also ruin).
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The spiritual sense requires the failure-mode of alternations: when sustaining influx is absent, what was being nourished collapses. In the oldest layer this is expressed as the elements or their qualities ‘withdrawing’ rather than as moral fault.
> *¶2 #4 [strong]:* The destruction is explicitly said to be “through … hunger.” In the substrate register, the corresponding natural cause is deprivation of sustaining supply: ‘lack of nourishment’ is the direct, impersonal complement to “hunger,” keeping the cosmological-causal tone rather than exhortative language.

**¶4** “In this way too, a lack and a shortfall shall occur from place to place through them; and they who have chosen it have gathered it in and beautified and attained it, while an affliction shall arise for it through those.

**¶5** “People also shall be caused to stumble on their account, through the energy of Matter that dwells in them. It has poured its shadow over them, just as it shall also pour its shadow over the elements and destroy the fruits of all the trees.”

**Assessment:** All four gaps are recoverable at the level of correspondential structure: (1) an arranging or gathering act must precede ‘beautifying’; (2) nourishment must be paired with growth-strengthening before the reversal; (3) the reversal is best expressed as withdrawal of sustaining qualities; (4) the causal phrase must name deprivation of nourishment as the ground of ‘hunger.’ Exact Coptic equivalents could vary, but the demanded cosmological logic is tight.

---

## Chapter 109: Concerning the Fifty Lord's Days; to what Mysteries do they correspond? Or the Second (Fifty); to whose Sign (are they)?

**¶2** Happen you know that [in each]h one of these five garments that the [First] Man summoned there are five powers. They shall be counted to the [to each] other, these five, and five times [is] their number. However, at the time [when] the summons went down. The answer wished to ascend with it. And the summons with the obedience [constitute] twenty-five characteristics in their twenty-five limbs, [which] are the sons of the Man. The summons and [the obedience]ience were raised to the heights through them.

> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The mutual ordering of the five interior powers is expressed as being “counted to each other,” matching the spiritual sense: faculties ordered in mutual relation.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The line is arithmetical: five counted in relation, and five times is their number (25). The copula best matches the completion into sufficiency.
> *¶2 #5 [strong]:* The spiritual sense pivots on the moment of descending directed influx; this requires a temporal hinge: “at the time when the summons went down.”

**¶3** ⟨p.263⟩ Just as their fa[ther] the living Man raised himself up through them, by these these other ones have set up all things a[ove]e and below. Indeed, these are what are called pentecost, the fifty great days that are the [archetypes of the] holy [d]ays. They fasted from their [foo]d, [w]hich is their own.

**¶4** So, from that time until [the time] of [the advent]ent of Jesus the Splendour, the glorious one who came [of]heir food which is the [living food] of sou[l], which is the summons and the obedience [life] that spiritual food. They rele[ased from] all these fifty days, which [through] this living food suffices for all of them. It filled them all with power and richness and life. Light [for] this living food that [by] became rich.

> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Restores the clause as “who came of their food…”, i.e., the coming is characterized by the disclosure of the true inner nourishment (summons and obedience) rather than an external meal; this fits the spiritual sense of revealed Divine truth unveiling the living provision of the soul.
> *¶4 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense requires the identification of the soul’s nourishment as living provision, explicitly equated with summons and obedience.
> *¶4 #6 [moderate]:* Completes the phrase “the … of soul” as “life of soul,” matching the correspondential teaching: nourishment is what sustains inner life.
> *¶4 #7 [moderate]:* Gives “They released from … all these fifty days,” i.e., abstained or loosened themselves from what is their own across the whole completed period, aligning with the fasting-from-their-own in ¶3 and the spiritual sense.
> *¶4 #8 [moderate]:* Marks extension across the whole pentecostal period: “through all these fifty days,” matching the spiritual translation’s “through the whole fullness of that complete process.”
> *¶4 #9 [strong]:* Introduces the sufficiency clause: “for this living food suffices for all of them,” directly required by the spiritual sense.
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* Completes “Light … this living food that … became rich” as illumination occurring by means of that nourishment, matching: “by it the understanding was illuminated and all within them was enriched.”

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are minor and can be restored tightly from the arithmetical structure (5×5=25) and from the correspondential identity of “food” with the living provision of the soul (summons and obedience). The harder gaps are in ¶4, where the syntax is damaged; there the spiritual constraints strongly require (1) a statement of living nourishment, (2) its sufficiency through the full fifty-day period, and (3) illumination and enrichment by means of it, but the exact connective particles remain somewhat uncertain.

---

## Chapter 110: Concerning the Nourishment of the Person, for there are Powers in it [ ... / ... ]

**¶1** exists [a power] daily to the body of the person [and] there are two hundred and fifty thousand seals in it [and] there are another two hundred and fifty thousand rulers [into the Interior] they are cleansed and purified and enter [rest] ⟨p.265⟩ by the voice, by the word, and by the [sound of the hymn] quiet with the silence at the time when his heart [is turned toward] of God the Father.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate is describing a continual operation that “exists daily” for the body: an impersonal sustaining function rather than a person or office. “Power” matches the cosmological register (an ordered efficacy) demanded by the spiritual sense (“continual ordered operation that sustains the outward life”).
> *¶1 #2 [strong]:* The clause needs a simple connective to continue the description of what is within the body (seals, rulers). The spiritual sense treats these as paired fullnesses (confirmations and determinations), so coordination is required.
> *¶1 #3 [strong]:* Balances the prior enumeration: the text sets two equal totals (seals; rulers). A simple conjunctive is demanded by the parallelism in the spiritual sense.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* After purification they “enter” not merely a place but a degree. The spiritual translation explicitly says “admitted inwardly,” which in the oldest-layer register is best rendered as entering “the Interior” (a discrete degree).
> *¶1 #5 [moderate]:* After “enter” a state-word is needed: the spiritual sense speaks of a tempering peace that stills motions. “Rest” is the natural correspondent of that peace and matches the impersonal cosmological tone better than moralized terms.
> *¶1 #6 [tentative]:* The sequence “by the voice, by the word, and by the …” calls for a third mode of living influx into ultimates. In the oldest layer, this triad is not ecclesial but correspondential: voice (living utterance), word (discerned doctrine), hymn (ordered sounding that composes and harmonizes). This fits the spiritual sense: influx as doctrine and discernment plus a tempering peace.
> *¶1 #7 [strong]:* The phrase “at the time when his heart … of God the Father” requires a verb of direction/intent, not confession or prayer. The spiritual translation specifies “when the inmost intention turns toward the Divine Source.”

**¶2** Also, the twelve [great archontic] rulers that belong to the Darkness shall be swept out outside in wandering and in transmigration [in the wheel of births] some of them [are destroyed through] this transmigration, and others among them shall be brought back.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The twelve are a complete organized set of governing falsities; the oldest-layer term is “rulers” (archontic powers). “Great” marks the totality-structure (twelve as a full order) without importing later pastoral language.
> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* The natural expression of “wandering and transmigration” in this substrate is the cyclic re-embodiment schema. “Wheel of births” conveys the instability of state described in the spiritual sense (successive changes of reception, displaced from form to form).
> *¶2 #3 [strong]:* The syntax requires a passive verbal phrase linking “some of them” with the effect of transmigration. The spiritual translation says some falsities are “dissolved and carried away by the very changes they undergo,” which corresponds naturally to being “destroyed through this transmigration.”

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be constrained by the chapter’s correspondential structure: (1) a continual sustaining power in the outward degree, (2) paired plenitudes (seals and rulers), (3) purification leading to entry into an inner degree and a state of rest, (4) a triadic means of influx (voice, word, ordered sound), and (5) expulsion of a complete archontic order of Darkness into cyclical instability (wandering, transmigration), with differential outcomes (dissolution vs return). Two restorations remain tentative because the exact natural phrasing for the third term after “voice” and “word,” and the specific idiom for transmigration, can vary across recensions.

---

## Chapter 111: Concerning the Four Archetypes that occur in the Eye, and the Fifth that is hidden in them; to whom do they belong?

**¶1** There are four aspects among mankind that perceive, as they perceive [in them] without these four that are hidden in them [and perceive] they belong [to the rest of mankind]. So, [if one is lacking, perception is broken].

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The four interior faculties are said to be hidden within the human; the perceiving is 'in them' rather than external.
> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts true perception with the loss of it when the four are absent; repeating the verb fits the correspondential emphasis on perception as a faculty.
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation: without the full interior set, the rest of the mind cannot truly belong to heavenly order. The natural phrasing commonly contrasts the interior four with 'the rest of mankind'.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* Correspondential constraint: the point is structural completeness; any missing faculty makes perception fragmentary.

**¶2** [The desire] that is in the [heart] belongs to the fire. The darkness belongs to the abyss; but the light that perceives what is hidden in all these things, being clear, is the living air.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual translation indicates the inmost acting from love (will). In the oldest substrate register this appears as an impersonal force: desire as inner heat.
> *¶2 #2 [strong]:* The will’s heat corresponds to the heart region (love as fire). Thus 'that is in the heart belongs to the fire'.

**¶3** How shall the pupil of the eye perceive? While it perceives by day, yet come the night it shall not perceive. What mystery does this matter signify? The pupil of the eye shall not [enter into] the mystery of the [hidden things] is the door [of the mystery] below [that is] the other four cardinal points. For in the [the compass of] above. He has [place] great [a] they came down [power] living [powers]. The First Man came forth from it and knew all things.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The pupil cannot 'enter into' hidden things by itself; this matches the spiritual sense of not penetrating the deeper degree without an opening.
> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* Pairs with 'what is hidden' and keeps the impersonal, cosmological register (not moral exhortation).
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The door is specifically the access-point to the deeper degree; 'door of the mystery' expresses that fixed correspondence.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* Grammatically links 'the door' with its location; also preserves the impersonal expository tone.
> *¶3 #5 [strong]:* Spiritual translation: 'beneath the whole compass of the outer mind'. Natural text already moves to 'the other four cardinal points', so 'compass' is demanded.
> *¶3 #6 [moderate]:* The deeper degree is 'above' the external arrangement; in natural terms this is 'in the place above'.
> *¶3 #8 [moderate]:* What descends from the higher degree is a fullness of living powers; singular 'power' fits 'great' as a collective force in the oldest layer.
> *¶3 #9 [strong]:* Completes the descent formula: 'living powers' proceeding downward as influx, matching the spiritual translation’s ‘great fullness of living powers’.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are recoverable because the chapter is a tight correspondential exposition: four hidden faculties (completeness), heart-fire (will as heat), abyss-darkness (lowest disorder), living air (clear discernment), and the ‘door’ beneath the compass of the four quarters (an opening to the higher degree). Exact connective wording remains moderately constrained, but the required images and their sequence are strongly determined by the spiritual logic.

---

## Chapter 112: The Human is less than all the Things of the Universe, and he is rebellious beyond them all.

**¶2** ⟨p.267⟩
After he had stood [in] that place in the midst of the river, he shook his head. He [sa]ys: If one of all these five wo[r]ds of the f]lesh [should appear] today on the surfaces of the earth [were gathered together] out from [the abyss] which is deep. They will make it like [a single body] and they set them in order [and they set them in order]ne another, one [wi]th one upon the earth. They all [will be lifted] up to the heights against the elevation of this mountain [in proportion to] its height; they will neither come to breadth corresponding to [its] breadth, nor to width corresponding to its width, [nor to] depth corresponding to its depth.

> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense says “outward expressions … were set forth in the outermost plane.” The substrate register prefers impersonal manifestation-language (appear, be shown) rather than moralizing terms.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* Corresponds to assembling the “five words” into the outer plane as a complete set; fits the following “set them in order … with one another.”
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense explicitly: “out from … which is deep.” In the Iranian-cosmological substrate, the deep source opposed to the heights is regularly expressed as an abyssal depth.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* The next clause: “They will make it like [ … ] and they set them in order …” demands a collective form (one corpus) made from the five, before ordering.
> *¶2 #8 [strong]:* The visible remnants include “with o”; the sense requires arrangement into an apparent harmony in the external plane.
> *¶2 #10 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: they “fail when compared” and “could not rise.” The natural image is attempted ascent “up to the heights.”
> *¶2 #11 [strong]:* The comparison-series height, breadth, width, depth demands a repeated proportional formula rather than a unique phrase.

**¶3** Not only this, b[but each] one, all of them from their smallest to their g[greatest] a single sea [and were brought into] single [a] but [whole depth] that [s]ea and they are swallowed an[drowned] is in it [their form is destroyed] they are in [this] earth [yet on] many [m]ountains [and even if there is] sea in it, they [they stand forth] are very [gre]at in their fa[fashion, but]hey did not guard against him [in their better condition] the silence and they [do not oppose] blasphemed hi[m] not, neither against his l[igh]t that is displayed, no[r] do they utter blasphemies [a]gainst his power that supports the totality, nor against the [e]ssence that is displayed indicating the totality, [nor] against the good works of righteousness. [They attribute]count no cause of evil after Go[d].

> *¶3 #2 [strong]:* The phrase “from their smallest to their greatest” is fixed by the spiritual sense (“from the least to the greatest”).
> *¶3 #3 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts multiplicity with being made “a single sea … single …”: thus a gathering into one generality.
> *¶3 #4 [strong]:* Supplies the needed article before “single sea,” matching the repeated “single …”.
> *¶3 #5 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: “one general body of knowledges … a single comprehensive doctrine.” The natural correspondent is a sea as a total mass; “whole depth” preserves the impersonal cosmological register.
> *¶3 #7 [moderate]:* The clause “they are swallowed and …” requires the consequent extinction of distinct things within the sea; “drowned” matches the sea-correspondence (life extinguished in mere generality).
> *¶3 #8 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: “absorbed and lost … and their life would be extinguished.” Natural counterpart: loss of distinct form in the sea.
> *¶3 #9 [moderate]:* Governs the following “earth … many mountains … sea in it”: i.e., within this same outer plane.
> *¶3 #10 [moderate]:* Turns from drowning-in-the-sea to the contrasting fact: “Yet within that outward plane there still exist many elevated states.”
> *¶3 #12 [tentative]:* Links “many mountains … sea in it” to the concession “even if” in the spiritual sense (better condition within the same plane).
> *¶3 #13 [moderate]:* Mountains correspond to elevated states; “stand forth” keeps the impersonal descriptive tone and prepares “very great in their fashion.”
> *¶3 #15 [strong]:* The surviving letters constrain “fashion”; the spiritual sense requires a contrast (“These … can be made great in form. These … do not set themselves against …”).
> *¶3 #16 [moderate]:* Directly matches the spiritual translation’s qualifier, distinguishing these mountains from the blaspheming idol in ¶4.
> *¶3 #17 [moderate]:* Prepares “the silence” as the stillness of Divine order; corresponds to not setting themselves against holy order.
> *¶3 #24 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense: “they do not fabricate an origin of evil from the Divine.” Substrate register prefers “attribute” (impersonal causal assignment) over moral exhortation.

**¶4** However, [the i]dol of ill-fated human[it]y [set itself up] the one that will be unlike anything, that is equal [to the power and the essence] great works; and it was found evil [by] God, the Lord of all. It even blasphemed again[st the] power that supports the totality, against its hol[y] light; [and it] profaned its glorious wisdom, bringing forth the cause of every sin. They say that every disgraceful wickedness and defilement, the rulers and the demons and the fiends and the satans, they say that they have come from God, that he is the one who [es]tablished them [in the beginning] they came not from him, and they give false testimony about him.

> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: “self-made religious principle … exalts what is unlike the Divine.” The natural text needs an action of self-exaltation introducing the comparison “unlike … equal … great works.”
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* This paragraph immediately repeats “power … light … wisdom.” The idol claims equality with what in the substrate are the Divine sustaining power and manifest essence; hence this pairing fits the correspondential structure.
> *¶4 #10 [moderate]:* Closes the claim “he is the one who established them …”: the substrate polemic is about attributing the origin of evil to the Divine as first cause.

**¶7** Jesus did not come and s[a]ve the world because of mankind alone, but [but when he] came and revealed on earth [what was strong] was strong outside in [the great expanse] And [whe]n he had finished doing his [t]ask outside [i]n the great u[ni]verse, he came [and when he went]t further with Adam and Eve and revealed [to them]. Even so, he sent the apostles to the good, [g]enera- tion by generation, and revealed to them five great thin[gs].

> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* Balances “not … because of mankind alone, but …” and transitions to purpose: revealing and ordering, as in the spiritual translation.
> *¶7 #3 [strong]:* Spiritual sense: “reveal … what had power beyond it.” This matches the extant “was strong outside …”.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Corresponds to “the whole expanse of created degrees” and fits “outside in …” without devotional coloring.
> *¶7 #9 [strong]:* The broken “... he wen” plus the spiritual sense “he advanced further” indicates forward movement after completing the outer work.

**¶9** [Se]cond: He unveiled to them about the aeons [of the greatness], how they occur; and he taught them about [the manner of] the darkness, how it t[o]o exists.

**¶10** [The th]ird time: He proclaimed to them about the [l]ight great-[ness], how it has been active against the pow[er of] darkness and conquered it.

**¶11** Fourth: He taught them that he came to the province of death [that rules in the body] that is flesh, until he should find profit and bring them from the gate of the underworld below, for [they had] been swa[ll]owed into it.

> *¶11 #1 [moderate]:* Spiritual sense: “state of spiritual death that reigns in the merely natural self.” The natural correspondent given by the text is “flesh”; this fill supplies the reigning quality (province) without later institutional language.

**¶13** All the faithful who believe this tr[u]th shall live by three great mighty things.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are small letter-restorations with strong constraint. The larger gaps in ¶2–4 are recoverable to a moderate degree by the chapter’s fixed correspondence-series: (1) assembling outward expressions, (2) arranging them externally, (3) failed comparison with the mountain’s discrete measures, (4) drowning of particulars in a single sea (mere generality), (5) the contrasting non-blaspheming mountains, and (6) the idol’s self-exaltation and false attribution of evil’s origin to the Divine. Two larger fills (¶3 #12 and ¶4 #4) remain tentative because the surviving English synopsis does not preserve enough of the underlying Coptic syntagms to lock a single phrasing beyond the demanded spiritual function.

---

## Chapter 113: The Chapter on whether any [Lig]ht comes from the Three Vessels.

**¶2** [If one should wish to ascend,] b[u]t should the [mind] ascend [upwards, it shall reach the] light of the rays that [comes from above,] which are received, which shine and go in through these great open gates, through these doors, firmament by firmament.

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The paragraph opens with the condition for ascent in discrete degrees. The substrate voice is impersonal and technical (not exhortative), setting the premise for ordered passage upward.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* What ascends correspondentially is the inner faculty. In the oldest cosmological register this is treated as the ‘mind’ or inner man, the subject that can be raised through firmament-levels.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The sequence requires upward motion into contact with the incoming Light; the wording keeps the impersonal, law-like tone of the substrate.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The rays’ Light is defined by its origin (the higher realm). This matches the spiritual translation’s ‘received from above’ and preserves the cosmological causality of influx.

**¶3** For four [gates open]pen in each world, distributed at the four points of the compass, opening downwards to the vessels. So, due to this, the [light of the] rays shall go in through these openings and it shines on [that] place, and pulls through them the light and the little pi[llar that ex]ists there.

> *¶3 #4 [tentative]:* The sense demands a small established support of what has been made firm in the recipient level (a ‘little pillar’ as minimal confirmed stability). The uncertain marker is removed because the correspondential function is clear enough to keep the phrase continuous.

**¶4** From that place [it] shall be poured [out to the] firmaments. It shall also be clarified in these firmaments [until it becomes distinct, and] a part there is cleansed and it is [all] taken up with the living ones. However, its other part [which is not able to be clarified,] shall all [be cast] down.

> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* After being poured into the next firmaments, the influx undergoes discrimination and purification. The added clause supplies the required intermediate process before ‘a part there is cleansed’.
> *¶4 #5 [moderate]:* The rejected portion is precisely what cannot be purified or made to agree with the higher order; this matches the spiritual translation’s ‘cannot be purified because it does not agree’.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are tightly constrained by the chapter’s discrete-degree scheme: ascent, influx of rays, transmission through fourfold openings, illumination, extraction of a small confirmed stability, then further purification with a bifurcation into what can be taken up and what must be rejected. The only materially uncertain point is the exact phrasing around the ‘little pillar’, where the function is clear but the idiom could vary.

---

## Chapter 114: Concerning the Three Images that are in the righteous Person.

**¶2** Three images occur in the elect person.

**¶3** The first is the spiritual image, which is the new man, which the Light Mind shall form in him; and it goes into him and dwells in him.

**¶4** The second image is the remnant and remainder of the new man, which is the psychic image that is bound in the flesh, as the old man [is being preserved in the flesh] times of this psychic image.

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* The intermediate degree is described as the remainder of the new human held within the natural; correspondentially it is ‘kept’ or ‘preserved’ under the constraint of the fleshly level. This also matches the causal clause that follows: it is bound “as the old man” because earlier states formed it.

**¶5** The other one is the corporeal image [that is] added to them all.

**¶6** These three images [operate] entirely. When it will [be elevated] through the nourishment of food. If it shall sink [into it] down to the corporeal image. [And] the corporeal, [that] is in it, shall divest itself of it and place it in the [psychic] image. [Then] again it rises up and falls to the [psychic] image; it shall divest itself of the psychical of the enemies, which [inflame and stir up] anger. The lust [also clings and is carried along] with it.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* These three images are not three unrelated things but three discrete degrees acting as one process; thus the verb must express coordinated functioning.
> *¶6 #2 [moderate]:* The motion described is the alternation of raising and lowering according to reception; ‘elevated’ matches the spiritual translation’s ‘raised toward the inmost’.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The elevation is into the spiritual image; the next clause gives the contrary motion ‘down to the corporeal image’, so this must be the upward terminus.
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* The corporeal level strips off what is not proper and places what can be reordered into the intermediate degree; that recipient is the psychic image.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* Marks the cycle described in the spiritual translation: after transfer into the intermediate, the movement repeats (‘then again it rises up and falls’).
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* The repeated falling is between degrees; here the text says it “falls to the … image” right before divesting “the psychical of the enemies”, so the target degree is again the psychic image where such admixtures are present.
> *¶6 #9 [moderate]:* Hostile admixtures in the middle degree are especially the excitations that kindle contentious states; correspondentially this is ‘stirring’ and ‘inflaming’.
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation requires that disordered craving remains attached through the process until separated; thus it ‘clings’ and is ‘borne with it’.

**¶7** Then shall a light virgin come and reveal the spiritual image that is [there], which [is] the new man. That virgin acts as a guide. [She g]oes on before and it is extended to the heights above, and received into this spiritual image. And she [sculpts] it and adorns it with the new man [wi]thin. It is sealed with all the limbs of this light virgin who is present and dwells in the new man.

**¶8** So, this is [h]ow this living limb shall be [puri]fied and live, the one that comes into the body of [the ri]ghteous one from without through the administration of food of various ki[nds], like this. The living soul shall be clea[n]sed entirely every [d]ay and traverse these three [im]a[ges].

**¶9** So, it shall divest itself of the body, which is not its own, in the corporeal. It shall also divest itself of the souls that are not its own, these that are mixed with it in the psychical [especially in] anger and desire and [dull]-ness and foolishness and envy and strife; and these other wicked teachings that are not its own.

> *¶9 #1 [moderate]:* The list that follows begins with anger and desire; the correspondential sense specifies these as the notable excitations in the intermediate degree, so the phrase should introduce them as the chief instances within that level.
> *¶9 #2 [moderate]:* The series ‘anger and desire and …-ness and foolishness’ needs a state of obscuration in the natural mind that pairs with foolishness; ‘dull-ness’ fits the correspondential idea of torpor and loss of spiritual perception.

**¶10** However, in [the] spiritual image itself it shall live and be joined with [lo]ng-sufferingness, the perfection of faith and love that reigns over them all. It is the virgin of light who robes the new man and who shall be cal[l]ed ‘the hour of life’. S[he] is the f[irst], but s[h]e is also the last.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are either trivial letter-restorations or are tightly constrained by the correspondential sequence of discrete degrees (spiritual, psychic, corporeal) and the cyclic raising and lowering described in the provided spiritual translation. The most uncertain points are the exact verbs in ¶6 for coordinated operation and the precise idiom for the hostile excitations; these were restored to match the required spiritual mechanics (elevation, descent, stripping, transfer, repeated alternation) in an impersonal cosmological register.
