## Chapter 115: The Catechumen asks the Apostle: will Rest come about for Someone who has come out of the Body, if the Saints pray over and make an Alms-offering for him?

**¶5** As to the question for which you ask me, the entreaty occurs and the true prayer occurs! For every elect person, if he beseeches charity in total faith, he is claiming a request from our compassionate Father. So, if he is beseeching over himself alone, he shall be favoured his entreaty; but if again he is beseeching on behalf of someone else, he shall be granted his request.

**¶6** For this entreaty has been since the beginning [with the first ones, when] the gods first made entreaty of their Father. They were granted their entreaty and their request.

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate logic requires continuity: this entreaty is primordial, beginning with the first ordered beings turning to the Source. The phrase links “since the beginning” with “the gods first made entreaty”.

**¶7** I will instruct you how it is that they shall beseech their entreaty from the first ones; or in what form they shall entreat their request, they who have asked for it [for the ordering of] every thing.

> *¶7 #1 [moderate]:* In the spiritual translation, the asking concerns “the ordering of all things in itself.” This fill supplies the object of the request and keeps the impersonal, structural register.

**¶8** ⟨p.272⟩ The Great Spirit, the Mother of Life, she claimed and prayed and besought and glorified and praised the first established one, who is the Father. She besought him an entreaty. She claimed of him a request. She received a great gift; she and the many powers with her.

**¶9** She besought an entreaty on behalf of the First Man, because of how he had come forth and thrown himself down. He became distant from the Mother of Life. He separated from her and gave himself; in great affliction. He joined war on earth with the destruction and the grief and the weakness; in the worlds of darkness; in the abysses made of fear; in the midst of the demons' ditch; amongst the powers of the devil!

**¶10** Indeed, he is like a king standing in the midst of his enemies. And when the Mother of Life petitioned and prayed and glorified and praised the Father, the first established one, she did petition him that he might send a power; a protector and redeemer and helper for the son of God who is set in affliction.

**¶11** ⟨p.273⟩ Now, just as she had besought and interceded, so likewise was her prayer fulfilled and received into the presence of the Father of Greatness. He turned and gave to her her entreaty: the great power, the Living Spirit, the giver of ease. The Mother of Life and the Living Spirit came with great power to the borders of the territory. They brought the First Man up from that war and struggle, wherein he is set.

**¶12** He gave himself as a grace for the Mother of Life. He became estranged and far from her, the one whom his enemies (place) in great affliction, the one who is in the land of darkness since he became weak.

**¶13** The Living Spirit grasped and took him in. He freed him. He set him right and gave him ease in the land of rest [by the will of the Father and the Mother and the Living Spirit.] the Living Spirit, the first giver of ease [The Welcomer of the Lights.] the will of these three. The welcomer [the living soul that is brought up from the war may remain in its place of rest.] find with the welcomer that [...

> *¶13 #1 [moderate]:* The passage explicitly speaks of “the will of these three” in the spiritual sense: Divine love, wisdom-from-love, and living truth-in-act. In the oldest-layer vocabulary this is Father, Mother of Life, Living Spirit.
> *¶13 #2 [moderate]:* The sequence needs the technical cosmological role that makes reception and conjunction possible (“welcomer… find with the welcomer”). In the oldest substrate this is a light-being function, not ecclesial language.
> *¶13 #3 [tentative]:* The broken sentence is about what is ‘found with’ the Welcomer: a stabilized reception so the rescued principle remains conjoined in rest, matching the correspondential explanation of welcoming and remaining.

**¶14** [And then she brought near again] a great power [and she clothed him] of the loved one [the power] the First Man who had been far away. He had become distant. She had besought on his behalf. He was set fast in affliction. (The Living Spirit) went to the place of destruction and weakness, the one that freed and redeemed him and gave him ease from the affliction. He set him right with rest and joy. He has himself become rested, with him for ever.

> *¶14 #1 [moderate]:* The fragment resumes the narrative-result of the first entreaty: the previously distant beloved power is brought near again.
> *¶14 #2 [tentative]:* Old-layer myth regularly expresses restoration as reinvestment or clothing (a correspondence for re-formation in truths). It also anticipates the later broken ‘garment’ motif in ¶25.
> *¶14 #3 [moderate]:* Keeps the clause coherent: “a great power … of the loved one …” The loved one is the restored First Man; what is described is the rescuing power operative in him.

**¶15** See now, I have taught you the first entreaty that the Mother of Life besought by her prayer. They granted her her entreaty.

**¶17** [Then they took counsel together] and they built the universe and completed it with its firmaments, with its wheels, with the ships of light that are in the heights, with its fastenings, with its earths and its walls, with its vessels. They completed the universe and set it right by the fathers of light.

> *¶17 #1 [moderate]:* The construction of the ordered universe (firmaments, wheels, ships, fastenings, walls, vessels) presupposes deliberative ordering by the light-powers; this is the impersonal, cosmogonic transition expected here.

**¶18** The Great Spirit stood with the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder with the Living Spirit and the First Man. These five stood to prayer; they glorified and praised the Father, the first established one. They besought of him an entreaty. They claimed of him a request, that he might give them the power [of the purifier,] the leader, a guide to all the things of activity that they had constructed. That he might come and purify the living soul that is set in affliction.

> *¶18 #1 [moderate]:* They ask for “the power … the leader, a guide… That he might come and purify the living soul.” Thus the missing apposition identifies the power as purifier as well as leader and guide.

**¶19** They prayed and besought their Father, and he received from them their prayer [and] he gave them their entreaty and their request [and] he summoned from him [the great power] which is the Third Ambassador. He, he came from the power of his greatness. He saw the entire soul, how it is entangled in a chain and set fast. It sees the things of activity that his brothers have constructed, that have come from the heights. Again, he saw the living soul, that it is entangled and set fast in a great torture. It is oppressed in the stink of the abyss that is joined with it.

> *¶19 #1 [strong]:* Connects “he received from them their prayer” with the consequent gift; required by English syntax.
> *¶19 #2 [strong]:* Balances the parallelism “he gave them their entreaty and their request … he summoned…”
> *¶19 #3 [moderate]:* The Father ‘summons from himself’ the mediating saving presence; in the substrate this is called forth as a ‘great power’ before being named the Third Ambassador.

**¶20** And therefore, he, the Ambassador [had compassion] in his charity. So, he revealed his way, before this underworld that is dug deep down, to free the living soul. He redeemed it from all the fleshes of death wherein it is pained. He gave it ease. He set it right in the place of rest and joy.

> *¶20 #1 [strong]:* The Ambassador acts ‘in his charity’; correspondentially this is compassion as an operative motive of rescue. The verb is required to complete the clause.

**¶21** And again, he too, the Ambassador, he fulfilled the will of these three; the will of his brothers who had besought the Father for him. They claimed of him a request, together with the [command] of the Father, the one that [hearkened to them;] he gave the entreaty that they had besought of him.

> *¶21 #1 [moderate]:* “together with the … of the Father” must be an impersonal divine act that effects the granting: a command or decree. ‘Command’ suits the oldest-layer cosmological tone.
> *¶21 #2 [moderate]:* The clause needs the Father’s receptive act before “he gave the entreaty.” ‘Hearkened’ keeps the archaic, impersonal register and matches the ‘received their prayer’ motif.

**¶22** The third is the transferral of the living soul that he had freed. He released it and gave it ease from the affliction. He became king and first to the entire body.

**¶23** Behold, I have instructed you as to the second entreaty by which they first besought the Father. They were given the Third Ambassador.

**¶25** Again, let us come to the third entreaty when they besought him the third time [when the Third Ambassador] displayed his image [and] he hid his image from the [to the rulers;] he stood firm [in the midst of the heights;] they revealed [and] the light [he showed] the First Man [to them] mock [of the Mind;] to him in [they remembered] which is exalted [the demons] garment [the assembly] they were freed [the place] to him [of glory] their voice [a shining] the body [of light;] prayer [and] it [the souls] beseech [and came] the body [praising] beseech [in] she besought [their] in [and they returned to] be free [beseeching] Ambassador [for] and also [release.]

> *¶25 #1 [moderate]:* The third entreaty, in Manichaean sequence, is tied to the Third Ambassador’s revelatory display that draws forth the soul; the fragment’s “displayed his image” aligns to him.
> *¶25 #2 [strong]:* Minimal connective demanded by the broken parataxis.
> *¶25 #3 [moderate]:* The ‘image’ is hidden from those not prepared: correspondentially the hellish governors. Old-layer term: rulers, not moralized language.
> *¶25 #4 [tentative]:* “he stood firm” needs a location/state of stability; ‘heights’ correspond to the higher degree where the divine stands unshaken.
> *¶25 #5 [strong]:* Paratactic link.
> *¶25 #6 [moderate]:* Balances “he hid his image” with a contrary act: revealing to those who can receive; required by the spiritual translation’s ‘unveiling’.
> *¶25 #7 [strong]:* Completes “they revealed … the light …” with the recipient implied; minimal syntactic repair.
> *¶25 #8 [tentative]:* Old substrate often treats the revealed ‘light’ here as a noetic manifestation that orders and draws up the soul. ‘Mind’ fits the correspondential ‘image’ and ‘figure’ motifs used later in ¶28.
> *¶25 #9 [tentative]:* The fragment includes “the First Man … mock …”; the turning-point is recollection/recognition of the First Man as archetypal good in ultimates, preceding opposition’s mockery.
> *¶25 #10 [moderate]:* Mockery is attributed to opposites; in this myth those are demons and rulers of Darkness.
> *¶25 #11 [tentative]:* Needs a collective subject for mockery and shouting; ‘assembly’ preserves impersonal cosmological style.
> *¶25 #12 [moderate]:* “which is exalted” most naturally qualifies a ‘place’ or ‘region’ of height corresponding to an elevated state.
> *¶25 #13 [moderate]:* Exalted place is characterized by ‘glory’ in the light-register without importing later devotional tone.
> *¶25 #14 [moderate]:* Modifies ‘garment’ to express correspondence: outward truth investing the saved as luminous form.
> *¶25 #15 [strong]:* Completes the common collocation “garment of light,” a standard substrate motif for investiture in truths.
> *¶25 #16 [strong]:* Paratactic link.
> *¶25 #17 [moderate]:* Those ‘freed’ in this context are the living soul-principles being extracted from bondage.
> *¶25 #18 [tentative]:* Gives motion toward the revealer after freeing; matches spiritual sense: raising and return to confession.
> *¶25 #19 [moderate]:* Pairs with ‘their voice’ as confession; keeps cosmological liturgical action without church overlay.
> *¶25 #20 [strong]:* Required preposition before “the body”.
> *¶25 #21 [strong]:* Possessive for “body,” referring to those praying from within embodiment.
> *¶25 #22 [tentative]:* Matches “prayer … it … beseech …” as a renewed act of asking after revelation.
> *¶25 #23 [strong]:* Fills verbal noun consistent with the repeated ‘beseech’ sequence.
> *¶25 #24 [strong]:* Minimal connective for the object of beseeching.
> *¶25 #25 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation explicitly ends the fragment with “asking for release—together with the mediating saving power.”

**¶26** ⟨p.276⟩ [He joined them] with the living ones and [the redeemed ones] for ever [in rest] the first [saving act] from the [from the opposites;] he gave him the hope [to endure.]

> *¶26 #1 [moderate]:* The fragment’s spiritual sense: the living are conjoined in permanence. ‘He joined them’ states the conjunction effected by the saving power.
> *¶26 #2 [moderate]:* Those conjoined ‘for ever’ are the living ones who have been freed; keeps the substrate term ‘redeemed’ as cosmological extraction, not moral preaching.
> *¶26 #3 [moderate]:* Adds the state-character of the conjunction (rest, stability), matching repeated ‘place of rest and joy’.
> *¶26 #4 [moderate]:* “the first … from the …” needs a noun of operation; ‘saving act’ is neutral, structural.
> *¶26 #5 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation: ‘effected out of what is opposed’. Old-layer term: opposites (powers of darkness).
> *¶26 #6 [tentative]:* Completes “he gave him the hope …” with enduring character of hope.

**¶27** Also, Jesus the Splendour perfected the will [of the Father,] the will [of the lights;] they found the light [Living Soul;] the will of the [elements,] that he had [ordered] he gave them the power that is Jesus the Splendour. He granted the will of the elements. [and established them] for ever. This first [and they remained] he was humbled, he was made free [This first saving power,] the [when]-ness is displayed to his enemies. He received the hope [from the bonds] reveal [likeness] Jesus the Splendour, the light power [and he made it manifest] and the entreaty of the [to those who are bound.] of light [the beings]

> *¶27 #1 [moderate]:* Jesus the Splendour ‘perfected the will’ in alignment with the Divine Source; ‘of the Father’ supplies the governing referent in oldest-layer terms.
> *¶27 #2 [moderate]:* The will is also ‘of the lights’ as the collective of light-powers aligned to the Father; fits the cosmological register.
> *¶27 #3 [tentative]:* “they found the light …” is for the living soul held in the mixture; naming it stabilizes the referent in the broken line.
> *¶27 #4 [moderate]:* The text already mentions “will of the elements”; this earlier lacuna likely introduces them as the lower constituents needing ordering.
> *¶27 #5 [moderate]:* “that he had … he gave them the power” needs a past participle: he had ordered or arranged the elements, matching correspondential ‘ordering’.
> *¶27 #6 [tentative]:* Balances ‘ordered’ with fixation into stable forms; corresponds to ‘set firm’ motif.
> *¶27 #7 [moderate]:* Prepares “for ever”; shows permanence of the ordering.
> *¶27 #8 [moderate]:* “This first … he was humbled” needs identification: a saving power that can descend. Keeps impersonal cosmology.
> *¶27 #9 [strong]:* Connective for the descent motif.
> *¶27 #10 [moderate]:* He was ‘made free’ from bonds; corresponds to liberation from compulsion and chains.
> *¶27 #11 [tentative]:* “the ...-ness is displayed to his enemies” calls for an abstract quality; ‘likeness’ fits the display of an image-form even to enemies.
> *¶27 #12 [tentative]:* Completes the display-action and links to “He received the hope”.
> *¶27 #13 [moderate]:* Hope is received by the bound soul; keeps cosmological focus.
> *¶27 #14 [strong]:* Completes “the entreaty of the … of light” as ‘beings of light’, matching the spiritual translation.

**¶28** Now, just as this entreaty [was fulfilled,] the figure [took] that they have [received within,] this is also how [you also shall take] the figure of [within you] you are set firm [the figure of order;] Happen you know this: Every one [and] knows, he has perceived [by it] all the things [in your mind.] out from [who] within him [and] upon him [that flow]

> *¶28 #1 [moderate]:* “just as this entreaty …” needs completion: it was fulfilled in a particular form.
> *¶28 #2 [strong]:* Pairs with “the figure” as “took the figure,” matching the spiritual translation.
> *¶28 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense: stability by an image of order received within. This supplies the interior reception.
> *¶28 #4 [moderate]:* Applies the cosmological pattern to the formation of mind without preaching; describes analogical formation.
> *¶28 #5 [strong]:* Interiorization is essential to correspondence: the image within gives firmness.
> *¶28 #6 [strong]:* Directly corresponds to the spiritual translation’s ‘image of order’.
> *¶28 #7 [strong]:* Connective.
> *¶28 #8 [strong]:* Instrument of being set firm.
> *¶28 #9 [moderate]:* The ‘you are set firm’ is in the mental form, matching the spiritual translation’s focus.
> *¶28 #10 [strong]:* Begins the relative clause “Every one … knows”.
> *¶28 #11 [strong]:* Connective between knows and has perceived.
> *¶28 #12 [moderate]:* Supplies the verb for “all the things … out from … within him … upon him”: influx is the theme; ‘flow’ matches correspondential influx.

**¶31** The first: [the redemption] of the living soul that is entangled and bound in the entire universe. For it shall be freed and cleansed and purified and redeemed by cause of him.

> *¶31 #1 [strong]:* The spiritual translation: “The first and chief effect … is the liberation.” In substrate diction: redemption as extraction and purification of the living soul.

**¶37** He redeemed the living person who is entangled [in the body] entirely. He has [come from] his father, the first [established one;] he made redemption for the soul [also,] that he had freed [by the power] the light has pity [and opened] the way also [therefore they beseech] a request [for it in] affliction. Again, just as he has [And again,] of the light [asked] give ease for his [the powers] grant the entreaty and the request [that he might] He besought for the soul [brothers.] him, that had come forth from the world. They shall make [to them.] ease. It shall be freed from affliction and come forth [and bring it forth into the land of light] for ever.

> *¶37 #1 [moderate]:* He redeems the living person entangled ‘in the body’—the natural ultimate where bonds are felt.
> *¶37 #2 [moderate]:* “He has … his father” needs a verb of procession; correspondentially all saving power proceeds from the First Source.
> *¶37 #3 [strong]:* Fixes the title already used: the Father as ‘first established one’.
> *¶37 #4 [strong]:* Connective in the chain of statements.
> *¶37 #5 [moderate]:* Redemption is ‘by the power’ of the saving manifestation; keeps impersonal causality.
> *¶37 #6 [strong]:* Matches spiritual translation: ‘the way of salvation is opened’.
> *¶37 #7 [moderate]:* Leads into repeated asking; keeps the impersonal “they” of the light-powers.
> *¶37 #8 [moderate]:* Connects ‘request’ with ‘affliction’: they ask for relief for the soul in affliction.
> *¶37 #9 [strong]:* Marks the renewed parallel petition.
> *¶37 #10 [moderate]:* Completes “just as he has … of the light”.
> *¶37 #11 [strong]:* “of the light” modifies ‘powers’ in this mythology.
> *¶37 #12 [strong]:* Introduces purpose clause “give ease… grant…”.
> *¶37 #13 [moderate]:* “give ease for his …” refers to his companion powers as brothers (as in ¶19).
> *¶37 #14 [strong]:* Completes “grant the entreaty and the request” with indirect object.
> *¶37 #15 [moderate]:* The closing movement is extraction from the world into the light-realm, matching the spiritual translation’s “brought forth into enduring life.” Old-layer place-name: land of light.

**¶43** Indeed, this living soul that was freed by cause of this other soul; it too, that living soul, it shall be redeemed in the name of that person. It is redeemed and purified and attains its first essence. Also, it shall become his co-helper and beseeches for the soul of the one who has been freed from his body. He shall petition charity for it and forgiveness of sins from the powers of light.

**¶44** Just as ease has come about for the living soul by cause of this soul, this is how this soul shall find ease and escape that body. It goes into the land of light [and the] soul that was redeemed because of it [and salvation] comes about for it; and it goes to the land of life and rest [for ever.]

> *¶44 #1 [strong]:* Connects “It goes into the land of light” with the clause about the redeemed soul.
> *¶44 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation: “because a soul was redeemed through it, salvation is effected for it as well.” This provides the noun of effect.
> *¶44 #3 [strong]:* Closes with the enduring state of life and rest; matches chapter’s repeated ‘for ever’.

**Assessment:** Much of the chapter’s early section (¶6–¶23) is tightly constrained by the standard cosmogonic sequence (Mother of Life → Living Spirit → Third Ambassador) and by the correspondential roles named in the spiritual translation, so restorations there are mostly moderate-to-strong. The long broken record of the third entreaty (¶25–¶28) is only loosely constrained; fills there are necessarily more conjectural and were kept generic, structural, and in oldest-layer cosmological diction (image, light, garment of light, rulers, bonds, order). The final doctrinal applications (¶31, ¶37, ¶44) are again fairly constrained by the stated spiritual outcomes (redemption, opening of the way, relief in affliction, elevation to land of light).

## Chapter 116: Concerning why if a [Nail] is cut the Person shall not be sick; but if a Limb is cut he shall immediately be pained.

**¶5** Happen you know th[at the] body is like the earth, wh[ile these] limbs als[o also] of it are like the trees. There are varieties [of] of tree you shall find growing on the earth. They ar[is]e and grow again and stand firm. They have not [their root in the earth] Again, there are others that if they are cut shall not [grow again] and they grow, as they are li[ke the reeds] which shall be cut and grow [again] and [others] they too that occur [upon the earth] they shall be cut and not grow, resembling [like the dry trees that neither] grow nor shall they [put forth shoot] it [sh]all please him on [what is defiled] and grows [strong in it] in impurity [and draws nourishment] from him. This flesh that is corrupt [and its corruption passes over] in the liv[in]g flesh and they [become weak and] are somewhat sick.

> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The sense requires the limbs to be included as part of the body’s likeness to earth; simplest restoration is the coordination “also”.
> *¶5 #4 [strong]:* Varieties “of” tree is the fixed natural correspondence for diverse faculties rising in the ultimate plane.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* Correspondentially, what makes a faculty recover is an inward root in living ground; in the natural image this is the root in the earth.
> *¶5 #7 [strong]:* Matches the class of states that, once cut off, do not return; the contrast requires “shall not grow again”.
> *¶5 #8 [tentative]:* These are the externally-stimulated growths that spring up and return while conditions last; reeds are a common natural image for such unstable vitality rooted in wetness rather than firm ground.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* The sequence needs: cut and grow “again”, i.e., apparent recovery while a supporting medium remains.
> *¶5 #10 [moderate]:* Introduces a further distinct class in the series (recovering, not recovering, mixed, not recoverable).
> *¶5 #11 [moderate]:* Keeps the correspondence in the ultimate plane: the occurrences are “upon the earth”, i.e., in the outward level where such faculties appear.
> *¶5 #12 [moderate]:* The opposite class must be those without living moisture, i.e., no inward life; “dry trees” fits the correspondence for states without living truth.
> *¶5 #13 [moderate]:* Completes the natural image required by the spiritual sense: they neither grow nor produce new life after being cut.
> *¶5 #15 [moderate]:* Correspondentially the delight is in impurity; the oldest layer states the structural fact (delight in defilement) without exhortation.
> *¶5 #16 [moderate]:* Carries the idea: the outward level grows powerful in disorder when it takes pleasure in impurity.
> *¶5 #17 [moderate]:* The corruption ‘grows’ by taking in sustaining influx from an unclean source; “nourishment” fits the tree-body correspondence.
> *¶5 #18 [moderate]:* Restores the needed transition: corruption in the outer flesh does not stay isolated but spreads into what should be living.
> *¶5 #20 [moderate]:* Completes the consequence: infected faculties meant to live become weakened, hence “somewhat sick”.

**Assessment:** Most gaps are constrained by the sustained correspondence body:earth and limbs:trees, and by the required series of four tree-types (regenerative, non-regenerative, seemingly regenerative, irrecoverable). Exact botanical terms are least constrained (notably #8), but the structural logic of inward root versus external stimulation tightly governs the restorations.

---

## Chapter 117: Concerning why Some shall delay to come forth from the Body, and Others die in an instant.

**¶2** The [sec]ond [order comes to him] if he shall fall [from his place,] one [then they set] one hour [one measure for] others who shall [to stand apart,] and they remain. They shall delay until they [have permission until] he is freed, and they delay to come toward [his boundary.]

> *¶2 #2 [tentative]:* The surviving clause begins “The second … if he shall fall …”. In the oldest cosmological register, what “comes” to a secondary power is not a person speaking but a law or ordering influx. This also prepares the later verbs of restraint and delay as regulated permissions within order.
> *¶2 #3 [tentative]:* The fall is best taken as a collapse from an appointed degree or station (a correspondential ‘place’), not yet specifying moral cause. This matches the spiritual translation’s ‘state of collapse’ and keeps the substrate impersonal.
> *¶2 #4 [tentative]:* The text immediately moves into measured delay and coordinated action by ‘others’. A procedural verb (“they set”, i.e., establish a measure) fits the cosmic-mechanical tone and introduces the next “one … one hour …” as a fixed permission.
> *¶2 #5 [moderate]:* The repeated “one … one hour …” calls for a unit-of-state framing. In correspondences, “measure” is the determination of a state by order; it avoids later devotional language and makes “hour” a qualified state-duration.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* “Others who shall … and they remain.” The ‘others’ are assisting powers held in readiness; “stand apart” expresses separation without rupture—present but restrained, matching the translation’s ‘stand by in readiness, holding their position’.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* “They shall delay until they … he is freed,” requires an intervening condition that authorizes their restraint. In the substrate, delay is governed by permission under order; ‘permission until’ bridges the waiting and the release without importing institutional terms.
> *¶2 #8 [tentative]:* “they delay to come toward …” points to a limit of approach. In correspondences a ‘boundary’ is the fixed margin of a state into which higher powers may not yet enter. This matches ‘approaching only so far as the restored freedom allows’.

**Assessment:** The chapter is extremely lacunose, so restorations can only be made at the level of correspondential function: fall as loss of station, ‘hour’ as a measured state, ‘others’ as restrained assisting powers, and approach as limited by a boundary. Exact idiom is uncertain; the fills aim to keep the impersonal, law-like tone of the oldest cosmological layer.

---

## Chapter 118: [ ... ]

**¶2** ⟨p.284⟩[these five aeons]ll of them [were commanded] that they shall [be made ready] seven people [of the Light] Again, he too, this per[son] the time when [the fullness] of the light [and the wisdom] the universe [and all its orders] is revealed [to him] take to the light [wholly]erly, the way that he exists [in it] from [not from himself] these other three [degrees within him]

> *¶2 #1 [moderate]:* The fragment begins with “all of them”, and the spiritual context speaks of a complete ordering under a single governance. In the oldest Iranian substrate this is normally expressed as the collective of primary Light-powers (aeons) being brought into order before a work of conjunction.
> *¶2 #2 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense indicates an ordering being commanded for all things. The natural-layer verb expected here is a directive act establishing order, not exhortation.
> *¶2 #3 [moderate]:* Matches the stated aim: brought into a sufficient state for conjunction. Old substrate phrasing tends to be impersonal: readiness, fitting, establishing.
> *¶2 #4 [moderate]:* The “seven people” in Manichaean-Coptic dress usually covers the seven luminaries or seven ruling powers; in the oldest cosmological register they belong to the Light-order rather than a moral or ecclesial group.
> *¶2 #6 [moderate]:* The spiritual context requires an “appointed state of readiness” at which influx is opened. In the cosmological idiom, that appointed completion is expressed as fullness or completion of the time.
> *¶2 #7 [moderate]:* The phrase “of the light …” is followed by an opening to disclosure and manifestation. In correspondential terms this is influx of wisdom from Light; thus the Light is qualified by its wisdom.
> *¶2 #8 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense: “the whole domain of knowledges and their connections could be made manifest.” The natural-layer counterpart is the universe with its orders, ranks, or arrangements.
> *¶2 #9 [moderate]:* The disclosure is specifically into the understanding of the particular recipient brought into view; the natural text likely marked the dative recipient at the point “is revealed …”.
> *¶2 #10 [strong]:* The surviving “utt]erly” demands an intensifier completing a word like “wholly, utterly”. This matches the spiritual requirement: turned entirely toward wisdom.
> *¶2 #11 [moderate]:* “the way that he exists … from …” in the spiritual reading is living from that wisdom as the ruling principle. The natural layer commonly expresses this as existing ‘in it’ (in the Light, in Wisdom) rather than from self.
> *¶2 #12 [moderate]:* Directly required by the spiritual sense: the state and power are not from the proprium. The oldest layer states this as an impersonal negation of self-origin.
> *¶2 #13 [moderate]:* The closing contrasts “these other three” with what is above them. The spiritual sense specifies three discrete degrees beneath which must receive in order. Thus the missing phrase names them as degrees, not persons or rules.

**Assessment:** Much of the chapter is too broken for verbatim recovery, but the correspondential structure is tight: an initial ordering of the Light-powers, an appointed fullness of time, opening of Light-wisdom revealing the ordered universe to a recipient, an injunction to turn wholly to that wisdom, and a final denial of self-origin contrasted with three lower discrete degrees. Several fills remain moderate because the exact Coptic lexeme cannot be fixed from the fragment, but the required cosmological functions are strongly constrained.

---

## Chapter 119: Chapter 119: [ ... ]

**¶1** ⟨p.285⟩[Hear now,] and the [righte]o[ousness of the Light] is more than [voice that is uttered] the voice through them.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The opening must introduce the doctrinal point in an impersonal didactic register. The spiritual sense begins with a general axiom about interior righteousness surpassing outward utterance; a simple summons-to-attend fits the substrate style better than a hagiographic frame.
> *¶1 #3 [moderate]:* The sentence contrasts interior righteousness with outward voice. In the oldest cosmological register, “righteousness” here is not moral exhortation but the pure right-order of the Light; adding “of the Light” anchors it in the aeonic ontology implied by the rest of the chapter.
> *¶1 #4 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense demands “outward utterance” contrasted with interior reality. “Voice that is uttered through them” completes the natural-sense correspondence: lower forms as instruments of speech.

**¶3** This one, whom they called “approved” [by the multitudes of Light,] they approved him in the midst of the ae[ons of] light. These numerous multitudes set him a[part], and he came forth against the enemy. They gave this name [upon him from the beginning,] this matter [from the concord of the Living Ones,] who are numerous, that are in the aeons of light and who [are without] measure or number.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The approval is performed by the illuminated communities. This fill supplies the agent in the oldest layer: not an apostolic voice, but “multitudes of Light” acting as ordered societies.
> *¶3 #4 [moderate]:* After “They gave this name …” the correspondential logic requires a confirmation of quality and authority as inherent order, not a later bestowal. “From the beginning” matches the substrate’s emphasis on primordial order.
> *¶3 #5 [tentative]:* The spiritual sense points to “abundant concord of all the enlightened societies.” In the oldest register, these are “Living Ones” (living truths) within the aeons of Light; this avoids later ecclesial language while preserving the society-structure.

**¶4** This one, [whom they called] “righteous”, because he is the first righteous one who had no sin amongst all of the aeons that belong to the light and the thought [of the thought of the Light,] they knew him, so that [ ...

> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The passage grounds his sinlessness not only in the aeons but also in “the thought …”. In the substrate, “Thought” is a cosmic faculty of the Light (ordering intelligence). This fill keeps it impersonal and cosmological.

**¶5** This one also [whom they called] “only begotten”, because he was established [in one alone,] pour [and was poured out against the] enemy.

> *¶5 #2 [moderate]:* “Only-begotten” corresponds to a unique proceeding from a single origin. “Established in one alone” expresses unity of origin without importing Christian begetting formulas.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The verb “pour” is already present; the spiritual sense says “sent forth for the work of resisting.” “Poured out against the enemy” matches the combat-proceeding motif typical of Iranian-Manichaean cosmology while staying in the older impersonal register.

**¶7** Again, just as the only begotten ones from your [only] begotten race and you [are begotten] according to flesh [made manifest]nly begot-ten also from gold and silver and all the [treasures] of the universe.

> *¶7 #2 [moderate]:* The analogy requires a verb of derivation: derived proceeders in lower degrees. “Are begotten” keeps the same natural image while carrying the correspondence of generated truths.
> *¶7 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual sense explicitly speaks of being made manifest in the outer person. This provides the needed clause before “according to flesh,” without shifting into moral exhortation.
> *¶7 #4 [moderate]:* Gold and silver correspond to external riches and knowledges; the missing noun should be a general container-term. “Treasures of the universe” fits the cosmological register and the correspondence to exterior goods and truths.

**¶8** ⟨p.286⟩Just as your father, [the F]irst Man, himself stood firm in the [midst] of all the worlds of darkness. He did not love [the] worlds of darkness, nor did he lust for them! Rather, h[is] thought and love for the household of his people is in h[i]m. Anything else that the enemy [planned against him, he did it] with his violent de[e]ds. Behold, (the First Man) toils and struggles; so that (the enemy) would be separat[e from] them and be cast out from them. And he purifies them and ma[ke]s them f[r]ee, and makes them as they were from the beginning.

> *¶8 #6 [moderate]:* The clause must cover “Whatever else the enemy attempted…” in the spiritual sense. “Planned against him” matches the will-and-intelligence opposition; “he did it with his violent deeds” then resumes the given text smoothly.

**¶10** [So that those who are being purified] may they discover the remembrance of the household of his peo[ple], as he remains in this [place night]ght and day.

> *¶10 #1 [moderate]:* The closing petition in the spiritual sense is about the delivered coming into remembrance. This fill supplies the purpose clause leading into “may they discover the remembrance…,” staying in an impersonal cosmological mode (purification as process).
> *¶10 #3 [strong]:* The spiritual sense equates “night and day” with every state (obscurity and enlightenment). The natural text needs the literal pair; “place night” yields “this place night and day.”

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are small letter-restorations and are secure. The larger gaps are constrained mainly by the chapter’s correspondential structure: (1) interior righteousness versus outward voice; (2) approval and naming by the innumerable societies of Light; (3) ‘only-begotten’ as unique proceeding poured out against the enemy; (4) the First Man’s unwavering combat to separate and purify. Where exact phrasing is not fixed by surviving letters, the restorations were kept in the oldest cosmological register (aeons, Light, Living Ones, Thought) and avoid later institutional or devotional idiom.

---

## Chapter 120: Concerning the Two Essences

**¶1** [There are] two essences that are present at the beginning [in the first beginning,] the light and the darkness, that which is good and that which is evi[l], life and] death.

> *¶1 #1 [moderate]:* The opening must state an impersonal cosmological given: at the first beginning two primal realities are co-present. The substrate characteristically begins with a bare assertion of the two roots rather than narration.
> *¶1 #2 [moderate]:* The list that follows (light and darkness, good and evil, life and death) requires an explicit temporal-cosmological marker: the primordial boundary condition where the two essences stand together.

**¶5** For if God has hi[mself] created them, the one who does them [h]as [no] sin therein! If they did not come [about] from him, nor did he command them to be d[on]e, [y]ou are the one who will speak a lie against God, saying [that] all [these e]vil things come about from him [by his command.]

> *¶5 #8 [strong]:* The demonstrative plus qualifier must point back to the enumerated opposite-quality complex (darkness, evil, death). Retaining the editor’s surface reading preserves the correspondential force: attributing the whole evil series to the Divine.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* The spiritual logic demands the closing clause sharpen the contradiction: to claim evils originate from the Divine is to make them proceed as commanded order. The oldest substrate speaks in causal terms (source, command, coming-about) rather than devotional polemic.

**Assessment:** Most lacunae are either single-letter word completions (certain) or require a short primordial framing clause and a brief causal closure. The correspondential constraints are tight: the chapter turns on the co-presence of two opposed essences at the first beginning and the impossibility of making the Divine the cause and commander of the evil series.

---

## Chapter 121: Concerning the Sect of the Basket

**¶3** So, [the uni]verse in which you stand is like a tree; while you are like the fruit that hangs from the tree. Now, if you yourself had been plucked from the universe with the basket, then well could you call yourself the son of the basket! However, if up till now you are entangled in the universe; how then do you call yourself the son of the basket, before you are plucked from the universe like a fruit? For as long as it hangs from the tree, it shall not be called ‘alms’. And you too, as long as you are entangled in [the] universe you [shall not be] well called the son of the basket.

**¶5** The [Father] and the great [Greatness] and the great Mind that is [the first Understanding] this Youth that is with the [with] thirty-six angels [Mind] twenty-two angels [standing] but [and] of the twenty-two [the chiefs] ⟨p.290⟩ the seven pillars that stan[d in the midst] the sea that surrounds the universe [and] four angels that are distributed to the [and] universe, supporting the mightiness [whole] below, which is the earth of all the heavens [of the world] these other five gods, the sons of the Man, they that [and] the universe is stood firm [order] the summons and the obedience [by]

> *¶5 #1 [moderate]:* The series begins with the highest interior principle before “great …” and “great Mind”; in the oldest cosmological register this is the primal source, not a personal savior-frame. “Father” fits the impersonal Zoroastrian-Manichaean substrate triad (Father, Greatness, Mind) as governing origin.
> *¶5 #2 [strong]:* Corresponds to the ‘superior governing principle’ and the standard oldest-layer designation paired with “great Mind.” It is the magnitude of the First Principle, the plenitude that contains the ordered powers.
> *¶5 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation points to “a ruling understanding that gives form and direction.” The oldest-layer idiom prefers an abstract function over later hagiographic titles; “first Understanding” matches that function and pairs naturally with “great Mind.”
> *¶5 #4 [strong]:* The clause needs the copular link: “the great Mind that is with this Youth …”. The Youth is the initiating vigor/first affection, attendant upon Mind.
> *¶5 #5 [strong]:* Restores the governing noetic principle explicitly named in the paragraph’s opening triad.
> *¶5 #6 [moderate]:* The Youth is described as ‘with’ the Mind and attended by ministering orders; “standing” preserves the static cosmological descriptiveness of the oldest layer rather than dialogic action.
> *¶5 #7 [strong]:* Connects the two angelic enumerations as coordinated ministering orders under the higher set.
> *¶5 #8 [moderate]:* The break “but … of the twenty-two …” suggests a subset within the 22. In the substrate register these are ‘chiefs’ or ‘heads’ of an order rather than ecclesial offices.
> *¶5 #9 [moderate]:* The seven pillars must be described as fixed/standing in the central axis of the cosmos. “stand in the midst” matches the correspondential idea of stable supports of order at the center.
> *¶5 #10 [strong]:* Links “seven pillars …” to “the sea that surrounds the universe …” as two structural features (axis and circumambient expanse).
> *¶5 #11 [strong]:* Introduces the next distribution: the four angels assigned through the extent of the universe.
> *¶5 #12 [strong]:* “distributed to the … universe” calls for a totalizing term; the spiritual translation says “distributed through the whole extent.”
> *¶5 #13 [moderate]:* “supporting the mightiness … below” points to the lowest degree, the natural plane; “world below” is the substrate way to denote the earth-plane under the heavens.
> *¶5 #14 [strong]:* Connects the supporting function to the identification of the lowest as “the earth of all the heavens.”
> *¶5 #15 [moderate]:* “the universe is stood firm … the summons and the obedience …” needs the concept that firmness comes from ordered responsiveness of lower to higher. “Order” supplies the governing cosmological principle.
> *¶5 #16 [strong]:* Gives the causal bridge: the cosmos stands firm ‘by’ the summons and the obedience (influx and response).

**¶6** The third also, [in the earth] below, there are twelve powers standing firm there. However, the number of the twelve images [and] the three great pillars of mightiness that [is broken] pillars supporting the disk [are set there] the other three [of the earth] they are the garments that the Living Spirit [great pillars] he put them at that time [of truth] that threw them, together with [clothed himself in] which is displayed therein [when] this [he descended] perfect before [the forms] great Porter [what is within them] in his power [arrangement] these are the three great [the] against [the] are in the midst of people [are set] knows [powers] entrusts his soul to them [disorder].

> *¶6 #1 [moderate]:* The third domain is explicitly said to be ‘below’; the twelve powers ‘standing firm there’ correspond to fixed operative forces in the natural degree. “In the earth” matches “below, which is the earth of all the heavens.”
> *¶6 #2 [strong]:* Links the placement of the third domain to the enumeration of the twelve powers.
> *¶6 #3 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation notes that the description of the representative forms ‘is broken and cannot be wholly recovered.’ This lacuna is exactly that editorial notice in the oldest-layer voice: the account of the images is broken.
> *¶6 #4 [moderate]:* The three pillars ‘of mightiness’ are fixed supports; “are set there” keeps the static architectural register.
> *¶6 #5 [moderate]:* “pillars supporting the disk …” in the lowest degree points to the earth-disk, the outermost perceptible plane. This matches the correspondential ‘outermost and perceptible.’
> *¶6 #6 [moderate]:* Balances the prior triad: three great pillars of mightiness, and ‘the other three great pillars’ as the next set extending stability outward.
> *¶6 #7 [moderate]:* The spiritual translation identifies these ‘other three’ as ‘external truths by which living influx is clothed for action.’ Naming them “of truth” keeps the oldest layer impersonal and functional.
> *¶6 #8 [moderate]:* Instead of later devotional phrasing, the substrate speaks of influx being clothed in ultimates. “garments that the Living Spirit clothed himself in” matches that correspondential mechanism.
> *¶6 #9 [strong]:* Temporal connector needed: “he put them at that time when …”
> *¶6 #10 [moderate]:* Matches the spiritual translation’s “at the time when it descends,” i.e., living influx taking outer forms.
> *¶6 #11 [moderate]:* “that threw them, together with … which is displayed therein” implies what is cast forth are forms that manifest interior things. “the forms” supplies the object without importing later polemical terms.
> *¶6 #12 [strong]:* Corresponds directly to “what is displayed within them” and the idea that interior realities are manifested in external truths.
> *¶6 #13 [moderate]:* “this … perfect before … great Porter …” calls for a term of ordered completeness/structure established. “arrangement” preserves the impersonal cosmological register.
> *¶6 #14 [strong]:* Grammatical support before “great Porter.”
> *¶6 #15 [strong]:* Connects: “great Porter … in his power …” i.e., the Porter as regulating function.
> *¶6 #16 [moderate]:* Introduces the triad of resisting agencies as established in the Porter’s power.
> *¶6 #17 [strong]:* The resisting agencies are functional protections, not persons in a devotional frame; “powers” matches the chapter’s own terminology (twelve powers) and keeps register consistent.
> *¶6 #18 [moderate]:* The opposition is against what is disorderly; the oldest substrate speaks in terms of order versus disorder rather than moralizing sin-language here.

**Assessment:** Most small lacunae can be restored by the chapter’s architectural-correspondential scheme: triads of interior principles, numbered ministering orders, structural supports (pillars, sea), and the law of influx-and-response (“summons and obedience”). Larger gaps remain underdetermined in exact proper names and some numeric sub-order detail (especially around the 22 angels), but the demanded functions are clear enough to restore in the oldest, impersonal cosmological register.

## Chapter 122: Concerning the 'Assent' and the 'Amen'

**¶2** The assent and the amen have come about from these great ones of the powers. They were given the name as the assent belongs to the summons and the amen belongs to the answer.

**¶3** Just as the summons and the obedience are the ones that opened the portals of mercy and entreaty for the First Man, so they also became portals for the Mother of Life and the Father of the living. The First Man sought and found his redeemer. And also the Mother of Life and the Father of the living sought [and found] this runner, who came forth from them, who is the First Man; but the summons and the obedience became the portals for them.

> *¶3 #1 [moderate]:* The substrate logic is parallelism: First Man “sought and found his redeemer”; likewise Mother of Life and Father of the living “sought and found this runner.” The runner is the active proceeding (the same good in operation), so the text requires successful access, not mere seeking.

**¶4** ⟨p.293⟩
Now, in this way they have become portals for the aeons of greatness towards the First Man, and the soul that was conjoined with death. They became helpers [and supports] of the First Man during his ascent. They gave him [remembrance] the Father of Life and the Mother of Life [according to his measure]. They are purifiers of the living soul, being helpers and bestowers of remembrance for it, be it either in [in the ship, or] in the tree or in the creation of flesh [or in the formed body].

> *¶4 #1 [moderate]:* They (summons and obedience) are being defined as functional means: first “portals,” then immediately as assisting agencies during ascent. “helpers and supports” keeps the impersonal cosmological register and matches the spiritual sense ‘supports for that good during its elevation.’
> *¶4 #2 [moderate]:* The next sentence explicitly names them “bestowers of remembrance,” so the preceding clause naturally anticipates this: they “gave him remembrance” in ascent, i.e., restored right orientation to the source (a standard function of these mediating powers in the oldest cosmological layer).
> *¶4 #3 [moderate]:* Correspondentially, influx is always received ‘according to reception’ and degree. The spiritual translation indicates ‘so far as it could receive.’ This phrasing preserves the substrate’s impersonal law of reception without later devotional coloring.
> *¶4 #4 [tentative]:* The closing triad lists states where the living soul may be held: a contained perceptive state (ship, as a containing vessel), a more fixed vegetative formation (tree), and the ultimate embodied formation (flesh). This matches the spiritual sense’s progression from interior perception to outermost embodiment.
> *¶4 #5 [tentative]:* The clause contrasts ‘tree’ and ‘creation of flesh’ with an intermediate/summary term for ultimates. “formed body” keeps the oldest-layer cosmological register (formation into ultimates) and fits the spiritual sense ‘outermost forming of life in acts and embodied states.’

**Assessment:** Most gaps can be restored by the chapter’s strict parallelism (seek and find; portals then helpers; influx received by measure). The final clause lists receptacles or states for the living soul; its exact natural images are less constrained, so restorations there remain tentative though aligned with the discrete-degree progression implied by the spiritual sense.
